# Changelog

Versions follow semantic versioning. Every project file records the tool version that saved it.

## Unreleased

### Added

- **The examples in two groups, and the first feature example, `full-connectors`** (pull request 1 of `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.1_FEATURE_TEMPLATES.md` sections 4.1, 5.3, 6 and 14; REQ-EX-01; decision D-268, proposed; owner questions T1, T3, T4, T6, T9 and T10, by their recommendations). `core/templates.py` holds the one list of the examples: `TemplateInfo` has a `group`, `start` (the five projects of 1.0.0: `blank`, `first-steps`, `minimal-satellite`, `small-satellite`, `flatsat`, unchanged and first) or `feature` (small projects that show one feature each); `tools/build_examples.py` takes the titles from it instead of a second list of its own, and the help of `new` says *create a new project from an example (`new --list` shows them)* instead of naming five. `new --list` prints a heading per group (*Start:*, *Feature examples (small projects, one feature each):*) and pads the names to the longest name plus two; `new --list --json` and `list_templates()` of the scripting interface give `group` (a field added: `API_VERSION` stays 1, the API snapshot is made again). The New project dialog shows a heading row per group, which cannot be chosen and which the arrow keys pass over, each with its own accessible name; *New project from an example…* still chooses `first-steps`. Home lists the group *Start* as before and ends with one line for the feature examples and a button *Show the feature examples…* that opens the dialog with the first of them chosen. The new example `full-connectors` (*Full connectors*): `PCDU1`, `OBC1` and `TTC1` with six nine-way Micro-D device connectors whose 54 pins are all fixed and named (power ports `MAIN`, `KA`, `SURV`, the heater and ground pins; RS-422, CAN, a discrete line and ground on the data link), the pin rows 1 to 5 over 6 to 9 on both parts, the keying `A` and `B` on the two identical connectors of a device, fourteen interfaces with both connectors named and each put on its port by hand (Generate alone gives the ports of one type in name order), generated: 3 harnesses, 27 wires, no spare pin. It is built by `tools/feature_examples.py` with the tool's own edit functions; `check` gives 0 errors and 0 warnings, `drc` only `part-unapproved` and `unchecked-config.*`, and `verify --outputs` 0 errors. `tests/test_feature_examples.py` runs these checks for every example and `tests/test_cli_new_list.py` the list. The files of the five existing examples do not change, nor does any written output of them (`tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` say *identical*).
- **Two feature examples that show how to make a spliced harness: `splice-by-hand` and `splice-from-connection`** (pull request 2 of `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.1_FEATURE_TEMPLATES.md` sections 4.3, 4.4, 5 and 14; REQ-EX-01; decision D-268, proposed; owner questions T5, T6, T8 and T10, by their recommendations; the owner's request of 2026-11-24: *examples that show how to create spliced harnesses*). `splice-by-hand` (*Splice by hand (EXAMPLE)*): `PCDU1`, `OBC1` and `TTC1`, each with a nine-way Micro-D whose pins 1 and 2 are `PWR` and `RTN` (out on PCDU1, in on the two loads), and the harness W001 *Power Y* drawn by hand: a plug on each device connector, the splices `W001-X1` (PWR) and `W001-X2` (RTN), crimped, of the EXAMPLE part `EX-SPLICE-1`, six wires (AWG 22 red or black to the splice, AWG 24 beyond it) and four segments with EXAMPLE lengths (0.4, 0.02, 0.6 and 0.8 m), so the wire list shows the column *Via* and `tests.csv` the tests through each splice. `splice-from-connection` (*Splice from a connection (EXAMPLE)*): `PCDU1` and `OBC1`, the EXAMPLE values of `templates/config-demo-values/` (still placeholders), the connection L1 *Power of OBC1* that joins `PWR` (4 A) and `RTN` (4 A) of `PCDU1-J01` to two pins each of `OBC1-J01` (2 A per pin): two junctions, so its harness W001 has the splices `W001-X1` and `W001-X2`; the wires carry 4 A on the trunk and 2 A on each branch, worked out from the pin currents; the data connection L2 and its harness W002 with two shielded pairs; EXAMPLE contact ratings on `EX-DSUB-9-F` (10 A) and `EX-MICROD-9-M` (5 A). Both templates hold the unverified, unapproved EXAMPLE part `EX-SPLICE-1` in their own library; the starter library does not change. Each gives 0 errors and 0 warnings in `check`, nothing for Generate to do, only `part-unapproved` and `unchecked-config.*` in `drc`, and 0 errors in `verify --outputs`. The manual page *Splices* names the two templates. The files of the six existing examples do not change.
- **Four feature examples of routing: `branching-harness`, `loose-cable-end`, `shields-and-ends` and `lengths-to-the-face`** (pull request 3 of `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.1_FEATURE_TEMPLATES.md` sections 4.5 to 4.8, 5 and 14, #371; REQ-EX-01; decision D-268, proposed; owner questions T5, T6 and T10, by their recommendations). `branching-harness` (*Branching harness (EXAMPLE)*): `PCDU1`, `OBC1`, `TRX1` and `RW1` with the segmentation `per_connector_group`, power from `PCDU1-J01` to OBC1 and TRX1 and RS-422 from `OBC1-J03` to TRX1 and RW1, generated: W001 branches at `W001-B1` and W002 at `W002-B2` (branch points are numbered across the project), with EXAMPLE segment lengths. `loose-cable-end` (*Loose cable end (EXAMPLE)*): `OBC1` and `PCDU1` with a generated power harness, and the *Bench cable* W002 drawn by hand from the test port `OBC1-J03` (`TM+`, `TM-`, `TC+`, `TC-`): its TM wires end in the loose end `W002-P2`, a new leg of the TC wires in the loose end `W002-P3` behind the branch point `W002-B1`, each pair twisted, with an EXAMPLE gauge and lengths, so that only the loose ends hold back `release` until `--accept-loose-ends` gives a reason. `shields-and-ends` (*Shields and their ends (EXAMPLE)*): `OBC1`, `TTC1` and `RW1`, RS-422 in two shielded pairs and CAN under an overall shield, the EXAMPLE values with the EXAMPLE grounding concept pigtail at end A and 360 degree backshell at end B, the EXAMPLE backshell part `EX-BACKSHELL-MICROD` (30 mm, unverified, not approved) on three cable connectors, and each kind of place of a shield end: a pigtail on the shield contact pin 5, a pigtail on the lug of a backshell, 360 degree bonds in the backshells, and one end left without a place (the info `shield_place_not_stated` of `check`). `lengths-to-the-face` (*Lengths to the face (EXAMPLE)*): `PCDU1` and `RW1`, the EXAMPLE values, `model.length_reference` `face` with `W002-P2` set back to its rear, EXAMPLE depths on the plug parts (18, 19 and 20 mm) and on the backshell of `W001-P1`, and the length model `path_loops_allowance_twist` with an EXAMPLE cut allowance and take-up of twisting, so each wire length is the path from rear to rear plus loops, take-up and allowance. Each gives 0 errors and 0 warnings in `check`, nothing for Generate to do, only `part-unapproved` and `unchecked-config.*` in `drc`, and 0 errors in `verify --outputs`. The manual pages *Branching harnesses*, *Build a harness by hand*, *Enter the build data* and *Lengths to the face* name them. The files of the eight existing examples do not change.

### Fixed

- **The note `isolated` no longer names a device that a harness drawn by hand plugs into** (part E of `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.1_FEATURE_TEMPLATES.md`, sections 8 and 15, item 1; owner question T8, by its recommendation). It said *OBC1 is not connected to anything yet* and *they will not appear in any harness plan* of the devices of a harness drawn by hand or of a cable with a loose end, which are in one; the note counted only interfaces and connections. It now counts the device connectors a cable connector of a harness plugs into (`checks.find`), and its explanation lists the harnesses that plug into the device. The words of the note do not change; *A device has no interface, connection or harness* in `docs/RULES.md`. A project whose devices are joined only by harnesses drawn by hand has one note less per such device in `drc`, `system/drc_findings.csv` and the DRC report; no example and no project of the output-hash baseline is such a project. Regression test in `tests/test_isolated_drawn.py`.
- **The gauge explanation of a wire drawn by hand without an interface no longer asks for an interface current** (section 15, item 3 of the same design). `explain DIR W001-001` on a wire of a harness made from a connection said *Gauge the search gives now = not decided [... interface current (set Max current on the interface)]*, though Generate never sizes such a wire; it now says *drawn wires are not sized; set the gauge*. Test in `tests/test_gauge_derivation.py`.

## 5.0.0 (2026-11-24; not a statement of compliance)

Version 5.0.0 is a **major release** and the **latest full release**. It is the first release that writes **format 5**, a new version of the project file format (decision D-264, the design `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.0_FORMAT_5.md`, decided with this release; the release, D-266); that is why the number is 5.0.0. It brings milestone E5 of the plan for the feedback of an electrical engineer (`docs/ait/ELEC_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`, points 1, 3, 7 and Ca of the feedback): **lengths to the mating face**, with the depth of a connector and its backshell taken off each wire (`length_to`, `depth_mm`, the setting `model.length_reference`; pull request 357), **a current per pin**, from which the current of a wire is worked out for `current-over-wire`, `bundle-current`, `voltage-drop` and, per pin, `current-over-contact` (`current_a`; pull request 359), **a splice in a harness made from a connection** with a junction (pull request 360), **where a shield end lands** (`at_a`, `at_b`; pull request 361) and **the checked state of a library device** against its ICD (`checked`, the command `device-checked`; pull request 362); pull request 353 made the step to format 5, pull request 363 the rest of the editor, and pull request 364 the end-to-end walk `tests/test_e2e_500.py` and the documents (it found one fault, the help of `import-build-data`, and fixed it). Every new key is sparse and set only by a person or an import: Generate reads the depths and the face ends, but never a pin current, never makes a splice, and never fills in a shield place. **No new design rule** (*Rules run* stays 43): the new warnings are codes of `check`, reported as `model-inconsistent`. **Format 5 is frozen with this release:** from the tag `v5.0.0` it does not change any more, and a later change of the file format is a new format with its own upgrade. **How to upgrade from 4.x:** install 5.0.0 over it, then upgrade each project once: answer **Upgrade** when the editor asks, or run `harness-design-studio migrate DIR --dry-run` to look first and then `harness-design-studio migrate DIR`. The step writes the number 5 in `project.json` and nothing else, keeps the original files in `.migration-backup-v4/` and checks itself; it changes no model hash. If your segment lengths were already taken to the mating faces, set `model.length_reference` to `face` after the upgrade and give your parts their depths. After the upgrade, versions 4.x open the project read-only, and once it holds data of format 5 they set each object with a new key aside as a damaged file, read a junction as `link_pin_reused` and refuse `generate`, `export` and `drc` (tried with the code of 4.4.0); so the whole team updates at the same time, and the main Git branch is upgraded first. A device library file with a depth, a pin current or a check is written as version 4, and a subsystem file as version 3, which 4.x does not read. **Written files:** a project without data of format 5 (every example, template and earlier fixture) writes every file of `export` and the harness plans as 4.4.1 did, apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it; **`export-data` writes 37 files for every project (35 before)**, because of the new table `devices`, and lists one setting more. The command line adds `device-checked` and the new columns and tables of the imports, the `--json` keys of the other commands are unchanged, and the scripting interface only adds (`API_VERSION` stays 1): `set_length_reference`, `set_shield_end`, `set_device_checked`, `clear_device_checked`. Every new key, column and code is listed in the release notes (`docs/releases/5.0.0.md`, *For scripts*). **Known limits:** a wire that gets its current only from its pins and has no interface type gets no voltage-drop check (the note `voltage-drop-wires` names it); nobody on a team has entered a depth, a pin current or a shield place in this tool yet, and no real datasheet or ICD with a current column was read; unbalanced currents in an ICD leave a net not worked out; the starter library has no backshell or splice part; the known limits of 4.0.0 to 4.4.1 stand; nobody has looked at the screens on a real display; the Windows packages were tried on GitHub's runners only. **Not done:** the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; and the owner's sign-off. There was no release candidate first (D-266). `5.0.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, the version with which the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot were written, so they stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0).

### Added

- **The end-to-end test and the documents of 5.0.0** (pull request 8 of `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.0_FORMAT_5.md` sections 13, 14 and 18, #364; decision D-264, proposed; REQ-MIGRATE-10, REQ-WIRE-09, REQ-WIRE-10, REQ-HARN-05, REQ-OUT-17, REQ-LIB-08, REQ-GUI-33). `tests/test_e2e_500.py` walks the milestone on one project, with made-up data: the sample `mini3` as 4.x saved it is refused by `generate`, `migrate --dry-run` names `project.json` only and writes nothing, `migrate` writes format 5 (backup `.migration-backup-v4/`), and `export --drawings` writes the golden set of `mini3`. Then part A (`settings --set model.length_reference="face"`, the four `length_depth_missing` warnings, the depths and a backshell part by `import-parts`, W002-P2 back to the rear by a table of cable ends, a backshell by the accessories table: the wires of W002 are 0.8 m less 52 mm, in `explain` and the wire list), part B (`import-pinout` with *Current* and *Current (mA)*, `current_unbalanced` in `check` and `drc`, then balanced; `explain` of a wire carrying the current of its pins against its gauge and voltage drop; `verify` stays clean, as Generate does not read a pin current), part E (`device-checked` refused without an ICD, then checked; a later pinout makes it stale in `check`, `drc` and `explain`; checked again with `--json`), part C (a connection with a junction, *Create harness* refused without a splice part and made with `EX-SPLICE-1`, `verify` by nets, the currents 3, 1.5, 1.5 and 3 A, `current-over-contact` on the pins that do not share the line and `current-over-wire` on a trunk too thin), part D (the grounding concept, `shield_place_not_stated`, a table of shield ends refused whole for a pin on a 360 degree end, then the places, which Generate keeps), and the outputs (`export --harness-plan --drawings`, `verify --outputs` with 0 errors, the Production and Review sheets, `box_pinouts.csv`, `export.json` with the keys of format 5, a second export byte-identical, `export-data` with 37 files). What 4.x does is tested where it can be: the loader's version check opens the project read-only and `generate` and `export` refuse it, and the verbatim copies of 4.x read the connection as `link_pin_reused`, measure the whole segment and see no overloaded contact. The editor's walk (offscreen) opens the format 4 project read-only, then upgrades it, and uses *Segments are measured to*, *Depth (mm)*, *Lengths measured to*, *Mark as checked…*, the pin current column, the Link window with a pin paired twice, *Create harness* with the splice part, the *Splices* list, *Change group* with the places and the Problems list. The documents: a new page of the user manual, [lengths to the face](docs/user-manual/lengths-to-the-face.md); the current of a pin in *define a device*; the tables of cable ends and shield ends in *enter the build data*; section 9 of *upgrade a project* (format 4 to 5, and what 4.4.0, run on the project of the walk, does with it: read-only, and every object with a key of format 5 set aside as a damaged file); the assumptions of format 5 in *see what the tool assumes*; six words of the glossary; the in-app guide, the learning path, the status pages, the agent notes, the design's status and section 19, and the E5 status of the plan. A project without data of format 5 keeps every written file.
- **The rest of the editor of format 5** (pull request 7 of `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.0_FORMAT_5.md` sections 4.5, 5.3, 6.1, 7.3 to 7.5, 8.3 and 18, #363; REQ-GUI-33; decision D-264, proposed). **Settings > Project** has *Segments are measured to* (rear or mating face, the project setting `model.length_reference` of part A; one undo step; a choice equal to the one in force writes nothing; off with the reason in a read-only project or when a release froze it); before, only `settings DIR --set` and `config/model.json` set it. **The Problems list** shows the infos of format 5 that are no finding of a rule as Info rows, each with its own sentence, and counts them: *the currents of the net of … were not worked out* (`current_not_worked_out`, part B), *where the pigtail … lands is not stated* (`shield_place_not_stated`, part D) and the review's junction (`link_junction`, part C, under the filter *Connections* too). A click on a warning of format 5 goes where it is put right: a face end without a depth to the cable connector's Inspector (*Lengths measured to*, its part), a stale check to the Devices tab (*Mark as checked…*), a shield group (a place that does not fit, or a pigtail without one) to its harness with its lowest wire in the Edit wire tab (*Change group…*) and to **the Inspector of the shield group**, new: its harness, kind and wires, and how each end is terminated and where it lands (*pigtail to pin 9 at W001-P1*), a pigtail without a place and a place on another connector marked. Before, a finding of a shield group showed *nothing to show*. The screenshots `docs/ux/qt/31-devices-current-and-check.png` and `32-change-group-places.png` (`tools.gui_screenshots`). Editor only: **no written file changes** (the output-hash baseline, the numbers snapshot and every golden say identical; *Rules run* stays 43).
- **The checked state of a library device** (part E of 5.0.0, pull request 6 of `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.0_FORMAT_5.md` section 8, #362; REQ-LIB-08; decision D-264, proposed; owner questions F12 and F13, by their recommendations; point Ca of the feedback of an electrical engineer: *the effort to create the database and to check it*). A sparse key of format 5: a device may say who checked it, on which day, against which ICD and its version, and the SHA-256 of what was checked (`checked` in its file under `logical/units/`: `by`, `on`, `icd`, `sha256`). What a check covers is the device's library-owned fields: its kind and mass relevance; each box connector, read by the part of its ID after the device (`J01`, so a copy and a renamed device read as the device), with its name, part, gender, keying, the types it carries, its row layout and its ICD (`source_doc`, `source_version`, `source_link`); each pin with its signal (not one Generate gave it for an interface), fixed, function, contact size, termination, contact part, role, direction, level and current; as canonical JSON, without notes and labels (`core/device_check.py`). Only a person sets it: the new command **`device-checked DIR DEVICE --by NAME [--icd TEXT] [--date YYYY-MM-DD]`** (no name is taken from the computer; without `--icd` the ICD every box connector names, else it is refused with *name the ICD with --icd*; without `--date` today; `--clear`; `--json`; one undo step, journaled; a read-only project refuses), **Mark as checked…** and **Clear the check** in the Devices tab, **Mark as checked…** in the Library window for the project's device that follows an entry, and the scripting functions `set_device_checked` and `clear_device_checked`. Anyone may mark a device checked. When the device changes, the check stays and `check` warns `device_check_stale` (*TTC1 was checked by … ; its connectors or pins changed since*), a finding of `model-inconsistent` that does not stop a release (no new design rule, *Rules run* stays 43); a release does not lock the check and Generate does not read it. The library carries it: `save-device` writes it (device library file version 4), `add-device` and `import-devices` bring it, and `update-device` shows it as the field `checked` (*A. Example, 2026-11-23, ICD-TTC Issue 3*) and `--apply` takes the library's, also its *not checked*. The Devices tab says *Checked by … on … against …*, *…; changed since the check* or *not checked*; the Library window has the column **Checked**; `explain DIR DEVICE` says it; the Review page and the *Review* sheet list each device a harness plugs into (row *Device*) while some device of the project is checked, and `verify --outputs` words that row again with its own copy; `system/export.json` holds the key; `export-data` has a new table `devices` (*Checked by*, *Checked on*, *Checked against*, *Check*) for every project. A 4.x tool sets aside a device with a check. A project without a check keeps every written file (the output-hash baseline and the numbers snapshot say identical; only the goldens of the fixture `format5` changed).
- **Where a shield end lands** (part D of 5.0.0, pull request 5 of `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.0_FORMAT_5.md` section 7, #361; REQ-OUT-17; decision D-264, proposed; owner questions F9, F10 and F11, by their recommendations; point 7 of the feedback of an electrical engineer: *shielding and backshell: where the shield is connected and where not*). Each end of a shield group may name its place (`at_a`, `at_b` in the harness file, sparse): the cable connector at that end, the pin of it a pigtail lands on, and the backshell (an accessory of kind backshell on that connector) for a 360 degree bond or a pigtail to its lug. No place on a twisted pair or a floating end; a pin only for a pigtail. Only a person sets it: the window **Change group…**, now also for a group Generate made with a shield (its kind, wires and ends stay off there), with **Lands on pin** and **Backshell** under each end and **Add a backshell…**; the scripting function `set_shield_end`; and `import-build-data` with a table of shield ends (`Shield group`, `End`, `Pin`, `Backshell`). Generate keeps the place of a group it makes again with the same wires, kind and end, says in the explanation when it drops one, and never fills one, not even from a pin of role shield. An end that becomes floating loses its place, one that becomes a 360 degree backshell its pin, and deleting a backshell takes it out of the places and says so. `check` warns when a place no longer fits (`shield_end_moved`: the ends follow the lowest wire ID and can swap), when its pin is missing, carries a wire, or mates with a device pin of the stored role power, return, signal or signal ground, and when its backshell is not on that connector (warnings, so findings of `model-inconsistent`; no new design rule, *Rules run* stays 43); a pigtail without a place is an info only, and a 360 degree backshell without one is not reported. The *Production* sheet of the build workbook and of the plan says *here pigtail to pin 9*, *here pigtail to backshell W001-A1* or *here backshell 360 (W001-A1)* and gives the pin a pigtail lands on a row **pigtail**; the *Connectors* sheet says *pigtail of W001-S1* under *Goes to*; the drawing names the pin or backshell at the end of the sleeve; `system/export.json` holds the keys; `verify --outputs` checks them with its own copy. The import record of a table of shield ends has the kind `shield-ends`, and a table of cable ends (part A) now gets its kind `cable-ends` too: before, `import-build-data` on the command line refused such a table when it wrote the record. A 4.x tool sets aside a harness with a place, and such an import record. A project without a place keeps every written file, and an unstated pigtail changes none (the output-hash baseline and the numbers snapshot say identical; only the goldens of the fixture `format5` changed).
- **A splice in a harness made from a connection** (part C of 5.0.0, pull request 4 of `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.0_FORMAT_5.md` section 6, #360; REQ-HARN-05; decision D-264, proposed; owner question F8, by its recommendation; point 3 of the feedback of an electrical engineer: *solder joints and their positions: signal or power lines merged or split inside the harness*). A connection may now pair one pin with several pins, a **junction** (*pin 1 of PCDU1-J01 to pins 1, 2 and 3 of OBC1-J01*: each pin of one side of the net with every pin of the other), and may give a pair twice, a **parallel pair** (two wires between the same two pins). A pin is still in one connection only. The review of a connection names each junction (`link_junction`, an info: *Pin 1 of PCDU1-J01 is joined to pins 1, 2 and 3 of OBC1-J01 (a junction); the harness made from the link joins them in a splice.*) and keeps the error `link_pin_reused` for a pin in two connections and for a junction a harness cannot be made from (a pair of it missing, or given twice); the proposal still pairs a pin once, and `check` no longer calls a pin in two pairs of one connection an error. **Create harness** makes, for a connection with a junction, one splice per junction (`W003-X1`, `W003-X2` …) of a splice part the person chooses (the window asks for it; without one it says *Choose a splice part (category splice) for the joint*; the program ships none), with a wire from each pin of the junction to node 1 and the signal of that pin; a parallel pair gives two wires on the same two cable pins (`pin-shared` warns, as for any pin with two wires). No length, gauge or colour is set, as before. A splice is still never placed by hand in such a harness, and **Generate makes no splice** (B8). The verifier compares a connection with its harness **by nets** (`link_issues`): a net through a splice joins each pin of one cable connector in it with each of the other, and a pair it joins that the connection does not is *Harness W003 joins pin 1 of PCDU1-J01 and pin 2 of OBC1-J01 through W003-X1, which link L1 does not pair.*; a harness without a splice gives the issues of 4.4.0 word for word (`tests/reference/link_issues_440.py`, compared on random connections). The harness has the splice outputs of a harness drawn by hand (the column *Via*, a test per pair of ends of a net, the splice block of the build workbook), the wires of a junction get their current from the pin currents (part B), and the editor's *Splices* list shows the pin currents of the net of each node and whether they add up. No key is new, but the reading of the pairs is, so it belongs to format 5: **a 4.x tool reads a junction or a parallel pair as `link_pin_reused`**, an error of `check`, and refuses the project as damaged (it opens a format 5 project read-only in any case). **A project without a junction or a parallel pair keeps every written file** (the output-hash baseline, the numbers snapshot and every golden say *identical*; `tests/test_links_same_as_181.py` stays green apart from the findings about a pin in two pairs of one connection, which it leaves out on both sides); the golden set `format5` gains the connection L1 and its harness W003. See [the file format](docs/FILE_FORMAT.md#a-splice-from-a-connection-part-c).
- **A current per pin** (part B of 5.0.0, pull request 3 of `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.0_FORMAT_5.md` section 5, #359; REQ-WIRE-10; decision D-264, proposed; point 3 of the feedback of an electrical engineer: *signal or power lines merged or split inside the harness (not enough single wires, or current limit rules)*). A sparse key of format 5: a pin of a device connector may state the current through its contact in operation, in amperes, as the device's ICD gives it (`current_a`, above 0 and at most 1000; only on a device pin, so a cable connector with one is set aside). The current of a wire now comes from the pin currents of its net when every end has one and they add up (the largest is the sum of the others, A-ONE-SOURCE; the wires between two pins share it when they have one gauge and one length, A-PARALLEL-SHARE), else from its interface's *Max current* as before, else it is not known; nothing is assumed. `current-over-wire`, `bundle-current` and `voltage-drop` judge a wire with it, so the wires of a harness made from a connection or drawn by hand are checked for the first time, and `current-over-contact` judges a pin with a current on its own (*Pin 1 of OBC1-J01 carries 2 A but its contacts allow 1.5 A after derating*) instead of giving each contact the whole current of the interface. `check` warns `current_unbalanced` when the currents of a net do not add up (a finding of `model-inconsistent`) and says `current_not_worked_out` (an info) for a loop or parallel wires that differ; no new rule (*Rules run: 43*). **Generate does not read pin currents** (owner question F5): it sizes from the interface, and a pin current does not make the harnesses out of date. Set a current in the Devices tab (the column *Current (A)*, shown while a pin of the connector has one, and from the column menu; `2`, `2 A` or `500 mA`), or with `import-pinout` (a column *Current*, *Current (A)*, *Pin current* or *Max current*, or *Current (mA)*; a value read in mA is kept in the import record), which before 5.0.0 left such a column out with a warning. The written files of a project with a pin current hold the column *Current (A)* in `box_pinouts.csv` and `export-pinout`, *Current [A]* in the `CON_…` sheets of the harness plan, and `current_a` with its unit in `system/export.json`; `verify --outputs` checks each. `explain` names the pin currents of the net, the branch and the share (`explain DIR CONNECTOR.PIN` the contact limit of a pin); the extension field `wire.current_a` and the table `wire_sizing` of `export-data` take the same current. A device library file holding a pin current is written as version 4, and `update-device` compares the current. **A project without a pin current keeps every finding and every written file** (the output-hash baseline, the numbers snapshot and every golden but `format5*` say *identical*; `tests/test_currents_compat_440.py` compares the current rules with those of 4.4.0 on random projects); the golden set `tests/fixtures/outputs/format5*` gains the currents. See [wire current](docs/models/wire-current.md).
- **Lengths to the mating face, and the depth of a connector** (part A of 5.0.0, pull request 2 of `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.0_FORMAT_5.md` section 4, #357; REQ-WIRE-09; decision D-264, proposed; point 1 of the feedback of an electrical engineer: *connector face to connector face, backside to backside, or mixed*). Two sparse keys of format 5: a cable connector says where the routing segments that end at it are measured to (`length_to`: `face` or `rear`; unset follows the new setting `model.length_reference`, `rear` by default, the meaning of every length before), and a connector or backshell part has a depth (`depth_mm`, millimetres, above 0 and at most 1000). At a face end the depth of the connector part and of each backshell on the connector is taken off each wire, so every length stays rear to rear and every heading stays true; Generate does so before it adds the service loops and keeps `length_to` on a cable connector it rebuilds. **A depth is never assumed:** a face end without one leaves its wires without a length, Generate says why in a note, and `check` warns `length_depth_missing` (and `length_below_depth` when the segments are not longer than the depths), which the design rules report as `model-inconsistent`; no new rule (*Rules run: 43*). The written files of a harness with a face end name them: a fifth sentence of the length note, `W001-P1 (face)` in `segments.csv` and the *Segments* and *Routing* sheets, *lengths to the face* in the drawing, `length_to` and `depth_mm` in `system/export.json`; `verify --outputs` words them again with its own copy. `explain` lists each depth and the step *Path from rear to rear*. Set them in the Inspector (*Lengths measured to* of a cable connector, *Depth (mm)* of a connector or backshell part), with `settings DIR --set model.length_reference=face`, `import-parts` (a column *Depth (mm)*), `import-build-data` (a table of *cable ends*: `Connector`, `Lengths to`) or the scripting function `set_length_reference`. A device library file holding a part with a depth is written as version 4. Assumption A-LENGTH-TO. **A project without data of format 5 keeps every length and every written file** (the output-hash baseline, the numbers snapshot and every golden say *identical*; `tests/test_lengths_compat_440.py` compares the lengths with those of 4.4.0 on random harnesses); the new golden set is `tests/fixtures/outputs/format5*`. See [wire length](docs/models/wire-length.md#a-face-end-and-the-depth-of-a-connector).

### Fixed

- **`import-build-data --help` names the table of shield ends** (found by the end-to-end test of 5.0.0, pull request 8, #364). The command reads a table of shield ends since pull request 5 of 5.0.0 (`Shield group`, `End`, `Pin`, `Backshell`), but its help, and so `docs/CLI.md`, named only strip lengths, pin contacts, accessories, crimp tools and cable ends. Regression test in `tests/test_shield_places.py`.

### Changed

- **Documents corrected for part B of 5.0.0** (pull request 8, #364): *connect two connectors* said that the wires of a harness made from a connection get no current check, and *branching harnesses* that nothing reads the depth of a connector; both now say what 5.0.0 does. `CLAUDE.md` names the 32 pictures of `tools.gui_screenshots`.
- **Projects are saved in format 5; a format 4 project is upgraded only by `migrate` or the editor's question** (decision D-264, proposed; pull request 1 of 5.0.0, #353, `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.0_FORMAT_5.md` section 3). The step from 4 to 5 (`V4_TO_V5_PARTS`) writes the number in `project.json` and nothing else, so it changes no model hash, no stamp and no written file (the safety net checks the same hash, findings, baselines and full export before it keeps the upgrade; the backup is `.migration-backup-v4/`). A format 3, 2 or 1 project goes through every step in one upgrade, as before. Pull request 1 added no key: the keys of milestone E5 (where a length is measured to and the depth of a connector, a current per pin, the place of a shield end, the checked state of a device) came, each sparse, with the code that reads them (pull requests 2, 3, 5 and 6), so a project without data of format 5 keeps its files apart from the number. **Older tools open a format 5 project read-only**: a 4.x tool says the project was saved by a newer version and changes nothing, so the whole team upgrades together. A device library file is written as version 4, and a subsystem file as version 3, only when it holds a key of format 5 (`depth_mm` of a part, `current_a` of a pin, `checked` of a device); a 4.x tool refuses such a file and still reads the others. See [the file format](docs/FILE_FORMAT.md#format-5-the-fields-of-the-electrical-feedback-500).

## 4.4.1 (2026-11-23; not a statement of compliance)

Version 4.4.1 is a **patch release** of 4.4.0 and the **latest full release**. It fixes one thing (pull request 350): **`update-device` sees a new ICD issue of a library device.** The comparison of a project device with its library device left out the fields that say which ICD a box connector came from (`source_doc`, `source_version`, `source_link`) and the level of a pin (`level`), all of format 3, so a library file whose only change was one of them read *up to date*, `update-device` showed no row and `--apply` took nothing. In 4.4.1 they are library-owned fields like the others: such a device reads *library changed* (in the editor, `libraries --devices` and `update-device --check`), the review lists a row for each, and taking the library's version takes them. The fault was found by the design of 5.0.0 (pull request 351, part F, owner question F14). **No format change:** 4.4.1 writes format 4 as 4.4.0 does, and format 4 stays frozen. Every file that 4.4.0 wrote has the same content with 4.4.1, apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it; the command line, its `--json` keys and the scripting interface are unchanged (the new rows name the fields as they are). **What you may see after the upgrade:** a linked device whose library file changed only in these fields reads *library changed*, and one whose ICD fields or pin levels were changed in the project and not in the library reads *changed here*; 4.4.0 said *up to date* for both. **How to upgrade from 4.4.0:** install 4.4.1 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. Each version writes its number into `project.json` and the generation record, so update the whole team at the same time. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 4.4.1, because it compares them with the version that runs. From 4.3.x or older, read the notes of 4.4.0 first, and from 3.x those of 4.0.0: a format 3 project is upgraded once to format 4. **Known limits:** the known limits of 4.4.0 stand (nobody on a team has named pins after devices in this tool, the convention is read from one example, the signal map renames at import only, there is no importer of a team's ICD format); nobody has used the library update with a real team's ICD issues; nobody has looked at the screens on a real display; the Windows packages were tried on GitHub's runners only. **Not done:** the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; and the owner's sign-off. There was no release candidate first (D-265). `4.4.1` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, the version with which the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot were written, so they stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0; decision D-265).

### Fixed

- **`update-device` sees a new ICD issue of a library device** (pull request 350; REQ-LIB-09; part F of the design of 5.0.0, owner question F14). The comparison of a project device with its library device (`core/library_link.py`) left out the fields of format 3 that say where a box connector came from (`source_doc`, `source_version`, `source_link`) and the level of a pin (`level`). So a library file whose only change was one of them (a new issue of the device's ICD) read *up to date*, `update-device` showed no row and `--apply` took nothing; when another field changed as well, `--apply` took the whole connector and these fields came along without a row. They are now library-owned fields like the others: such a device reads *library changed* (in the editor, and on the command line in `libraries --devices` and `update-device --check`), the diff lists a row for each (`RW1-J01  source_version  -  Issue 2`, `RW1-J01  1  level  -  3V3`; the connector's fields after `row_layout`, the pin's after `direction`), and taking the library's version takes them, one undo step as before. A device whose library-owned fields are equal gives the same rows as 4.4.0 and stays *up to date*; a level row of a pin that a released harness uses names that harness, as the other fields of the pin do. **No format change** and no change of a written file (`tests/test_library_icd_fields.py`).

### Changed

- **Records for 4.4.1.** The version is 4.4.1 (decision D-265). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 4.4.0 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `4.4.1` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.4.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 4.4.1 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 4.4.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_4.4.0_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 4.4.0, and they differ from the 4.4.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 4.4.1. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the designs of 3.0.0 and 4.0.0, the plan for the engineer's feedback, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 4.4.0 say that 4.4.1 is prepared. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

- **The test of the tarball on the smallest machine no longer waits on one slow package mirror** (pull request 354; `packaging/ubuntu/container/minimal-machine-test.sh`, the job `package-on-24-04` of `.github/workflows/harness-ci.yml`). The script prepares its bare Ubuntu image while online; it now takes an optional `APT_MIRROR` for that step, and apt retries and gives up on a stalled download (`Acquire::Retries=5`, `Acquire::http::Timeout=30`). CI passes the mirror its own runner uses (`azure.archive.ubuntu.com`), and the job may run for 25 minutes instead of 15. On `43cefeb`, the merge of the preparation of 4.4.1 (pull request 352), the job ran out of its 15 minutes twice while `archive.ubuntu.com` answered with time-outs, before the tarball was started; the tag workflow does not tag a commit with a check that did not pass, so 4.4.1 is tagged on the commit that carries this change and these records (decision D-265). The test itself, its four packages and `--network none` are unchanged.
- **The design of 5.0.0** (pull request 351, `docs/ait/DESIGN_5.0_FORMAT_5.md`, decision D-264 proposed): a document only; the program is unchanged.

- **CI can run the full suite by hand** (`.github/workflows/harness-ci.yml`, `workflow_dispatch`). A run started by hand always runs the full suite, also on a commit whose change was documents only, where a push runs only the `docs` job and skips `test (ubuntu-24.04)` (D-138). The tag workflow needs that job to have passed on the commit it tags; `352c108`, the merge of pull request 355, changed documents only, so 4.4.1 is tagged on the merge of this change, which runs the full suite because it changes a workflow (decision D-265). The tests and what they check are unchanged.

## 4.4.0 (2026-11-23; not a statement of compliance)

Version 4.4.0 is a **minor release** of 4.3.0 and the **latest full release**. It brings milestone E4 of the plan for the feedback of an electrical engineer (`docs/ait/ELEC_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`, points 10, 11 and Cb of the feedback; the design `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.4_NAMES_PORTS.md`, decision D-262; the release, D-263): **names and ports**, so that the names a team gives its pins take the signals of a device to that device. **Both settings are opt-in**, keys of `config/naming.json` that no example, template or new project sets, **and a project that sets neither keeps its bytes.** **`naming.far_device_names`** (off by default; `config DIR --far-device-names on|off`, or *Settings > Project*) says that a pin or port name that starts with a device ID and `_`, `.` or `-`, or ends with them and an ID, names that device: Generate then gives an interface on a fixed pinout the port named after its far device and never a port named after another device, the verifier reports such a port (`port_far_device`: an error, a warning for locked pins and released harnesses) and `explain` names the far device (pull request 343); the pairing of a connection reads the other device's name off a pin name and leaves out the pins of a third device, which the review of connections names (pull request 344). **`naming.signal_map`** (not set by default; the tool ships no name, D-11; `config DIR --signal-map FILE|none`, or *Settings > Project*) holds a team's own signal names: `import-pinout` and `import-netlist` write the type's name for the team's, also inside a port (`TTC1_TXP` becomes `TTC1_TX+`), and list and record each rename (pull request 345). Pull request 342 wrote the reading of device names, the map and the two settings; pull request 346 the editor's check box, the section *Signal map*, the marks of *Choose port…* and the Inspector's warning. ***Unassign from interface* keeps the signal name of a fixed pin**, so two ports can be swapped by hand (pull request 340). Pull request 347 adds the end-to-end walk `tests/test_e2e_440.py` and the documents; it found no fault of the others. It is a minor release because it adds two settings, two options of `config`, a check of the verifier and a key of `--json`; no command or option is removed or renamed. **No format change:** 4.4.0 writes format 4 as 4.3.0 does, and format 4 stays frozen; the two keys are free values of `config/naming.json`, which a 4.3.x tool keeps when it saves. **Written files:** for a project that sets neither key (every example, template and test fixture) every file has the same content as with 4.3.0, apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it; with `naming.far_device_names` on and ports named after devices, the pins of the device connectors, `generated/generation.json` (the reasons and the note) and every output that shows the interface of a pin change, and the design rule report lists `port_far_device` as `verify-mismatch`; `config/naming.json` of a project that sets a key gets it; an import with a map writes the mapped names and keeps each rename in `imports/records.json`. The command line keeps its names and the `--json` keys of every command, apart from the new key `renamed_signals` of `import-pinout --json` and `import-netlist --json` (an empty list without a map), and the scripting interface is unchanged (`API_VERSION` 1). **How to upgrade from 4.3.x:** install 4.4.0 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. Export the outputs once with 4.4.0 if you use `verify --outputs`, because it compares them with the version that runs. **Update the whole team at the same time before a project sets a key:** a 4.3.x tool keeps the keys when it saves but ignores them: its Generate gives a new interface the first free port in name order, which the 4.4.0 verifier then reports, its imports do not apply the map, and its `settings --set` refuses both keys (`docs/releases/4.4.0.md`). From 3.x or older, read the notes of 4.0.0 first: a format 3 project is upgraded once to format 4. **Known limits:** there is no importer of a team's ICD format or of its README until an example is given (owner question N8; `import-pinout`'s table is the generic form, `docs/IMPORTS.md`, *Reading an ICD*); the convention is read from the one example of the feedback, and an ID is compared exactly, with its case, so `TTC-1_TX` names no device `TTC1`; a device ID that looks like a port name (`RS422`) takes the ports `RS422_n`; the map renames at import only, so a changed map renames nothing already in the project, and the library imports do not read it; a connection made before the setting was on keeps its pairs, and the review names them; with the setting off, the editor's *Add interface* can choose a fixed connector without the type's names, as 4.3.0 does (section 17, item 13 of the design; a backlog item, not a fix of this release); nobody on a team has named pins after devices in this tool yet; the known limits of 4.0.0, 4.1.0, 4.2.0 and 4.3.0 stand; nobody has looked at the screens on a real display; the Windows packages were tried on GitHub's runners only. **Not done:** the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; and the owner's sign-off. There was no release candidate first (D-263). `4.4.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, the version with which the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot were written, so they stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0; decision D-263).

### Added

- **The core of names and ports: the two settings of 4.4.0 and the reading of device names, with no caller yet** (pull request 2 of the design of 4.4.0, #342; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.4_NAMES_PORTS.md` sections 3.1, 5.1, 5.4, 8, 10 and 11, decision D-262 proposed, owner questions N1 to N12 as recommended). **`core/device_names.py`** reads which device a pin or port name names: `named_device` takes a name that is a device ID and a separator (`_`, `.`, `-`) and a rest that is not empty (`TTC2_TX` names `TTC2`, rest `TX`), or a rest, a separator and an ID (`TX.TTC1`), the ID compared exactly, with case (N2); a port name also names a device when it is the ID itself (port `TTC1`); when several IDs fit the longest wins (`OBC1-R_TX` names `OBC1-R`, not `OBC1`); a name of two devices names neither and carries the note *TTC1_TX_TTC2 can be read as a name of TTC1 or of TTC2, so it names neither. Rename the pin.* `far_device` gives the device at the other end of an interface from a connector, none when both ends are on one device; `link_reading` reads a pin name in a connection, the far device's name taken off, its own device's name kept whole (N10), a third device's pin not offered (N9). **`core/signal_map.py`** holds the project's signal map: `check` (the table of 5.1), `apply` (the whole name, else one reading inside a port by the rule of ports: `TTC1_TXP` becomes `TTC1_TX+`, `TXP.TTC1` becomes `TX+.TTC1`; a name that reads in two ways, `TXP_RXN`, is not renamed and gets a warning; N7) and `read_file`, the reader of `import-netlist --signal-map FILE` moved out of `cli/data.py` without a change (a test compares it word for word with the reader of 4.3.0, and the command writes the same pins and prints the same text). **Two settings of `config/naming.json`:** `far_device_names` (*Signal names name the far device*, a flag, `false` by default, assumption `A-FAR-DEVICE`; N1) and `signal_map` (*The team's signal names*, a table, not set by default: the tool ships no name, D-11). `validate` checks both (`config_invalid`: *config/naming.json: far_device_names must be true or false.* and *config/naming.json: signal_map must be a table of a team's signal name to the signal name of an interface type, each 1 to 200 characters, no name mapped to itself and no signal name that is also a team name.*); `configcheck.plan_set_far_device_names` and `plan_set_signal_map` set each in one undo step and take the key out for *off* and an empty map. `config DIR --far-device-names on|off` writes `true` or takes the key out, so a project that tried it has its bytes back; `config DIR --signal-map FILE|none` reads the file as `import-netlist --signal-map` does (the template `signal-map.csv` is one: *Signal map: 6 name(s) in config/naming.json.*). `settings DIR --set naming.far_device_names=true`, which 4.3.0 refused with exit 2 because the key was no setting of the registry, now works, and so does `--set naming.signal_map=…` as JSON. `signal_map` is in `generation_status.NOT_READ_BY_GENERATION`, so a changed map leaves the harness plans current; `far_device_names` is not, because Generate reads it (pull request 3): switching it on makes the plans out of date, and `config` says so. **No caller yet:** Generate, the pairing, the review of connections and the imports read names as in 4.3.0; pull requests 3 to 5 use the reading. `settings DIR`, `export-data`'s settings table and the Assumptions window list two settings and one assumption more; no example, template or new project sets either key, so a project without the keys keeps its bytes: `config DIR` prints the same text, and the output hashes, the numbers snapshot and the goldens are identical. `docs/CONFIG.md` (the two keys), `docs/CLI.md`, `docs/models/pin-allocation.md` and `docs/models/README.md` (`A-FAR-DEVICE`); `tests/test_device_names.py` (with Hypothesis against the rules of section 3.1 as written; the verifier's copy comes with pull request 3) and `tests/test_signal_map.py`.

- **Generate gives an interface the port named after its far device, and the verifier checks it (opt-in)** (pull request 3 of the design of 4.4.0, #343; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.4_NAMES_PORTS.md` sections 2.1, 3.2 to 3.5, 10 and 11, decision D-262 proposed, owner questions N1 to N5 as recommended; REQ-PIN-08). With `naming.far_device_names` on, a port of a fixed pinout belongs to the device its name names (`TTC1`, `TTC1_TM`; `core/device_names.py`), and the pin allocation (`generate/pins.py`) runs one more step and changes two: **step 0**, after the locked pins, gives each interface with no locked pin the port of its far device that it held before while it is free, else the first free port of that device in name order; **step 2** keeps a port held before only when it names no device other than the far device; **step 4** leaves out every port named after another device, and when nothing is left Generate stops with *IF-003: OBC1-J01 has a fixed pinout and no free pin named TX+, TX-, RX+, RX-, and no free complete RS-422 port for unit TTC3 (port TTC1 is named after unit TTC1; port TTC2 is named after unit TTC2).* (N3). An interface with no far device sees every port; a person's lock wins over every name. On the run of section 2.1 of the design (OBC1-J01 with the ports TTC1 and TTC2, IF-001 from OBC1 to TTC2 added before IF-002 to TTC1), 4.3.0 gives IF-001 the pins named `TTC1_*` and reports nothing; with the setting on, IF-001 gets `TTC2_*` and IF-002 `TTC1_*`, in whatever order the IDs are. `PinRules` gets `far_devices` and `units` (`engine.pin_rules`), `Request` gets `far` (filled by `engine.request` while the setting is on); the reason of such a pin says *…, the TX+ of port TTC2 of OBC1-J01; the port named after unit TTC2 at the other end (naming.far_device_names)*, and the generation record gets the note `naming:far_device_names` = *on: a port named after the unit at the other end first*. **The editor's dry run** follows (`edit.connector_room(..., far_unit=)`, `shared_connectors`, `choose_connector`, the Guided choice, `ops_add_interface`, `ops_complete_chain`, the fix of `no-connector`): a connector whose free ports are all named after other devices has no room (*OBC1-J01 cannot take another RS-422: its free RS-422 ports TTC1 and TTC2 are named after other devices*), and Guided mode goes on to the next connector. **`explain DIR CONNECTOR.PIN`** says *the port named after TTC2, the device at the other end of IF-001, comes first (naming.far_device_names), then ports in name order, interfaces in ID order* and lists the input *Signal names name the far device = yes* and the assumption `A-FAR-DEVICE`. **The independent verifier** (`core/verify.py`) reads the device a port names with its own copy of the reading (`device_naming`, compared with the core's by Hypothesis, no import of it) and reports `port_far_device`: an error for pins nobody locked (*Interface IF-001 goes to unit TTC2 but uses port TTC1 of OBC1-J01, which is named after unit TTC1 (naming.far_device_names). Regenerate, or choose the port of TTC2.*; in the DRC as `verify-mismatch`, fix *Regenerate harnesses*), a warning for locked pins or a released harness (*It is kept because a person locked the pins.*, *It is kept because harness W001 is released; a new version takes the port of TTC2.*), which does not reach the DRC (N5). **With the setting off nothing of this runs:** `tests/test_ports_compat_430.py` compares the allocator with `tests/reference/pins_430.py`, the module of 4.3.0 word for word, on random connectors with named ports (some named after devices), bare names, locks and earlier allocations, and finds the same pins, reasons, errors and warnings; with the setting on and no port named after a device it finds them too. No example, template or fixture sets the key, so a project without it keeps its bytes: the output hashes, the numbers snapshot and the goldens are identical. `docs/models/pin-allocation.md` (*Ports named after the far device*), `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` (REQ-PIN-08); `tests/test_ports_far_device.py`, `tests/test_ports_compat_430.py`, `tests/test_verify_far_device.py`, and the comparison of the two readings in `tests/test_device_names.py`.

- **Pairing reads the far device off a pin name (opt-in), and the link review names a pair of a third device** (pull request 4 of the design of 4.4.0, #344; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.4_NAMES_PORTS.md` sections 2.3, 4, 10 and 11, decision D-262 proposed, owner questions N1, N9 and N10 as recommended; REQ-PIN-09). The fault was not the one the plan named: `TTC1_TX` on OBC1 and `RX` on TTC1 pair already (a prefix may be missing on one side), but the proposal for **TTC2** paired the same TTC1 pins, and `TTC1_PPS` never met `PPS`. **With `naming.far_device_names` on**, `links.propose_pairs_between` reads each free pin's name with `device_names.link_reading` before it pairs: on a connector of OBC1 in a link to TTC2, `TTC2_TX` is compared as `TX`; a name of OBC1 itself (`OBC1_TX`, N10) or of no device is compared whole; a pin whose name names a third device (`TTC1_TX`) is not offered, with one note per connector and device: *Pins 1, 2 and 3 of OBC1-J01 name device TTC1, so they are not offered in a link to TTC2 (Settings > Project: Signal names name the far device).* (N9); a name of two devices is read whole with the note of section 3.1. Then the pairing runs as before on the names so read (counterparts, then equal names, never transmit to transmit, directions first), and pins whose whole names are equal pair last, as they did (`TTC2_PPS` on both ends). The proposal keeps the pins' names. So OBC1 to TTC1 gives `TTC1_TX=RX, TTC1_RX=TX, TTC1_PPS=PPS` and OBC1 to TTC2 gives `TTC2_TX=RX, TTC2_RX=TX, TTC2_PPS=PPS`, in either order of the links, and with `OBC1_RX`, `OBC1_TX` on the transceivers `TTC2_TX=OBC1_RX, TTC2_RX=OBC1_TX`, where 4.3.0 pairs nothing; `best_connectors` and the one-click connection count these pairs, so they start with the connector that has the far device's pins. **The link review reads the names the same way** (`_Review` carries the device IDs): `link_signal_mismatch` compares the names so read (`TTC2_PPS` and `PPS` match in a link to TTC2) and names a pair whose pin names a third device (*Link L1 pairs pin 1 of OBC1-J01 with pin 2 of TTC2-J01, but TTC1_TX names unit TTC1, not TTC2. Pair it in a link to TTC1, or rename the pin.*), and `link_pin_unpaired` points such a pin, when no link pairs it, to its device (*… is named TTC1_TX (unit TTC1) but no link pairs it. Link OBC1-J01 to a connector of TTC1, or set its role to Spare if it is not used.*). A loose cable end reads names as before. **With the setting off (the default) the proposal and the review are those of 4.3.0:** `tests/test_links_same_as_181.py` stays green, and `tests/test_links_far_device.py` compares `propose_pairs`, `check_links` and `check_link` with `tests/reference/links_430.py`, a word-for-word copy of the module of 4.3.0, on random connectors whose pins also name devices (Hypothesis). No example, template or fixture sets the key, so a project without it keeps its bytes: the output hashes, the numbers snapshot and the goldens are identical. `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` has REQ-PIN-09.

- **The project's signal map renames a team's names at import, also inside a port (opt-in)** (pull request 5 of the design of 4.4.0, #345; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.4_NAMES_PORTS.md` sections 5.2, 5.3, 10 and 11, owner questions N6, N7 and N11 as recommended; REQ-IMPORT-02). With `naming.signal_map` set (`config DIR --signal-map FILE`), `import-pinout` and `import-netlist` write the signal name of the interface type for a team's name: the whole name (`TXP` becomes `TX+`) and a name inside a port, read by the rule of ports (`TTC1_TXP` becomes `TTC1_TX+`, `TXP.TTC2` becomes `TX+.TTC2`, the port and the separator kept); `import-pinout` maps the *Signal* (or *Function*) cell before it writes the *Port* column in (`RXP` with port `TTC1` gives `TTC1_RX+`). A name that reads in two ways is not renamed: *warning: TXP_RXN on OBC1-J01 pin 9 can be read as TXP or RXN of the signal map, so it is not renamed.* Both commands list the renames after their rows (*Renamed by the project's signal map (config/naming.json signal_map): 4 signal(s)*, then `  TTC1_TXP -> TTC1_TX+: RW1-J02 pin 1` …), `--json` has them as the new key `renamed_signals` (`connector`, `pin`, `read`, `stored`; an empty list without a map, before `import`), and the import record keeps each one as a converted value (field `signal`, unit `signal map`), so `imports DIR` counts them; the import wizard's check page shows the same lines and its preview the stored names. `import-netlist --signal-map` keeps its meaning (whole names) and wins per name; a locked pin is not changed, as before; a mapped name longer than 200 characters refuses its row, as a long net name does. The library imports (`import-devices`, `import-subsystem`, `add-device`, `update-device`) read no map (N11), and neither do Generate, the rules and the verifier (N6). **Without a map every import prints and stores what 4.3.0 did**, the same lines, the same pins and the same import record, compared with 4.3.0 on the shipped templates, the examples and the test fixtures; the one change for a script is the key `renamed_signals` (empty) in `--json`. The output hashes, the numbers snapshot and the goldens are identical. `core/kicad.py`, `core/pinout_import.py`, `core/import_reports.py`, `core/import_record.py` (`lines(listed_apart=…)`), `core/signal_map.py` (`Renamed`, `renamed_lines`), `cli/data.py`, `cli/jsonout.py`, `gui/import_kinds.py`; `docs/IMPORTS.md`, `docs/KICAD.md` (its open question 2 answered), `docs/CLI.md`, `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`; `tests/test_import_signal_map.py`.

- **The editor sets names and ports: *Settings > Project* has the check box *Signal names name the far device* and the section *Signal map*; *Choose port…* marks the ports named after another device, and the Inspector warns under such an end** (pull request 6 of the design of 4.4.0, #346; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.4_NAMES_PORTS.md` sections 3.4, 3.5, 4.3, 5.4, 8 and 11, owner questions N1, N3 and N5 as recommended; REQ-GUI-32). **Settings > Project** gets the check box *Signal names name the far device* (`settings-far-device-names`, `naming.far_device_names`: on writes `true`, off takes the key out, so the file is as it was) and the section *Signal map* (`naming.signal_map`): a box with one `TEAM NAME = SIGNAL` per line (read as the netlist page of the import wizard reads its pairs), *Take the names of a file…* (a CSV, spreadsheet or JSON file, read by `core/signal_map.read_file` as `import-netlist --signal-map FILE` reads it) and *Save*; nothing is saved until *Save*, an empty box takes the map out, and a line that is not a pair or a table the check refuses says why under the box (*config/naming.json: signal_map must be a table of a team's signal name to the signal name of an interface type …*). Each change is one undo step through `configcheck.plan_set_far_device_names` and `plan_set_signal_map`; a read-only project turns both off with the reason. **Choose port…**, while the setting is on, lists the ports named after the device at the interface's other end first (*TTC2 (pins 5-8), named after TTC2 at the other end*), then the ports that name no device, then the ports named after another device, marked (*TTC1 (pins 1-4), named after TTC1*, with a tooltip that says Generate would not give it); such a port can still be chosen and is locked, as before (`devices.port_choices` reads the names with `core/device_names`; `PortChoice` has `device` and `far`). **The Inspector** then shows under the end the verifier's `port_far_device` in the screen's words (*⚠ Warning: IF-001 goes to device TTC2 but uses port TTC1 of OBC1-J01, which is named after device TTC1 (Settings > Project: Signal names name the far device). It is kept because a person locked the pins.*; for a released harness *It is kept because harness W001 is released; a new version takes the port of TTC2.*; for pins nobody locked an error, *Regenerate, or choose the port of TTC2.*), from `devices.far_port_warning`. The window *Link connectors* shows the notes of the pins that name a third device, as before. With the setting off and no map, every screen is as in 4.3.0. **Written files:** none changes (`tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` *identical*, the goldens unchanged). `core/devices.py`, `gui/settings_page.py`, `gui/main_window.py`, `gui/devices_dialogs.py`, `gui/panels.py`, `gui/strings.py` (the block *4.4.0 E4*); `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md`; `tests/test_ui_names_ports.py`, a case of the layout sweep (`tests/layout_cases.py`).

- **The end-to-end test and the documents of 4.4.0** (pull request 7 of the design of 4.4.0, #347; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.4_NAMES_PORTS.md` sections 11, 12 and 16, decision D-262 proposed, owner questions N1 to N12 as recommended; REQ-PIN-08, REQ-PIN-09, REQ-IMPORT-02, REQ-GUI-32). `tests/test_e2e_440.py` walks the engineer's case of section 2.1 through the command line, with made-up devices: a blank project, `import-pinout` of OBC1 (J1 with the ports `TTC1_*` and `TTC2_*`) and of the transceivers TTC1 and TTC2, two RS-422 interfaces added in the wrong ID order (IF-001 to TTC2 first). With the settings off it shows what 4.3.0 does: `generate`, `verify` and `drc` report nothing, and `explain`, `export` and `system/box_pinouts.csv` show IF-001 on the pins named for TTC1. Then `config --far-device-names on`: `verify` reports both ports as `port_far_device` errors and `drc --json` as `verify-mismatch`; `generate` moves them (`0 added, 1 changed`), a second one changes nothing, `verify` and `verify --outputs` find 0 errors, the generation record has its note and the pin its reason, `explain DIR OBC1-J01.5` names the far device and `A-FAR-DEVICE`, and the box pinouts show IF-001 on `TTC2_*`, in either order of the IDs; `--far-device-names off` gives `config/naming.json` its bytes back and the ports stay. A second test walks a connection: the proposal for TTC2 pairs the TTC1 pins with the setting off, the review then names the link (`link-signal-mismatch`, `link-unpaired` with its device), and after the link is deleted the proposals and the one-click connection pair each transceiver's own pins with the note of the third device's, and the review is quiet. A third takes the shipped `signal-map.csv` with `config --signal-map` (6 names), imports OBC1 and TTC1 with the team's names (`TTC1_TXP` …; 8 and 4 renames in the echo and in `--json` `renamed_signals`) and TTC2's names from a KiCad netlist (`/TXP` …; 4 renames, kept in the import record and counted by `imports`), and ends with the right ports after `--far-device-names on`; `--signal-map none` renames nothing back. A fourth swaps two ports by hand with the setting off (*Unassign* keeps the names of the fixed pins, *Choose port…* TTC2, `generate` keeps `4 locked pin(s)`), and a fifth shows a lock on another device's port kept as a warning while Generate refuses the other interface a port of another device (*… no free complete RS-422 port for unit TTC1 (port TTC1 is used by IF-001; port TTC2 is named after unit TTC2).*). The editor walk ticks *Signal names name the far device* under *Settings > Project*, reads the Inspector's error under the end, the marks of *Choose port…* before and after Generate, takes the template's names into *Signal map* with *Save*, and the command line then reads what the editor saved. **No fault of pull requests 1 to 6 in the program was found.** Found in the documents: `docs/CONFIG.md` still said that Generate, the pairing and the imports read names as in 4.3.0 (written with pull request 2); corrected. Recorded in section 17 of the design, not changed: the editor's *Add interface* with the setting off can choose a fixed connector without the type's names, as 4.3.0 does (a backlog item; with the setting on, pull request 3 already leaves it out). **The documents:** `docs/releases/4.4.0.md`; the user manual (`generate-harnesses.md`: ports named after the far device and the swap by hand; `define-a-device.md`; `connect-two-connectors.md`: pins named after a device and the review; `import-data.md`: the team's signal names; `import-kicad-pinouts.md`; `naming-and-title-block.md`; `see-what-the-tool-assumes.md`: the two settings and `A-FAR-DEVICE`), the guide (*Settings > Project*, *Choose port…*, *Link connectors*, `config`, `import-pinout`, `import-netlist`; `python -m tools.build_guide`), `docs/IMPORTS.md` (the section *Reading an ICD*, part D), `docs/CONFIG.md`, `GLOSSARY.md` (*Far device*, *Signal map*), `LEARNING_PATH.md`, the templates' `README.md` and `docs/examples/README.md` (`signal-map.csv` for `config --signal-map`), the agent notes `core.md` and `gui.md`, and the status of the design (built, not released; section 17, items 8 to 14) and of E4 in `docs/ait/ELEC_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`. **Written files:** none changes (`tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` *identical*, the goldens unchanged).

### Fixed

- ***Unassign from interface* keeps the signal name of a fixed pin** (pull request 1 of the design of 4.4.0, #340; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.4_NAMES_PORTS.md` sections 2.7 and 7, part E, owner question N12 as recommended). `devices.ops_unassign_pins` set the signal of every pin it freed to none. On a fixed pinout the signal is the device's own name, from an ICD, an import or the Devices tab, so the pins lost their port: after *Unassign* of pins 5 to 8 of `OBC1-J01` (`TTC2_TX+` ... `TTC2_RX-`), *Choose port…* said `OBC1-J01 has no RS-422 port TTC2.`, and two ports could not be swapped by hand. Now a fixed pin keeps its signal and loses only its interface and its lock; a pin that is not fixed is freed as before (no signal, no interface, no lock). The command of the Devices tab keeps its words and its undo step. With it, the swap of the design's section 2.7 works: *Unassign* pins 5 to 8, *Choose port…* TTC2 for IF-001, Generate. **Written files:** no example, template or fixture unassigns a pin, so the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the golden outputs are as before. New: `tests/test_unassign_fixed.py`.

### Changed

- **The Windows test job of CI may run for 150 minutes instead of 90** (#338; `.github/workflows/harness-platforms.yml`). On `master` it also runs the slow tests, and it took between 48 and 88 minutes on the last nine merges; it hit the limit of 90 minutes and was cancelled on `ec68c12` (#334) and on `20bf8f1`, the merge of the preparation of 4.3.0 (#337), before any test failed. The tests and what they check are unchanged; the time limit of each test (`--timeout=300`) stays.

- **Records for 4.4.0.** The version is 4.4.0 (decision D-263). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 4.3.0 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `4.4.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.4.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 4.4.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 4.3.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against the `SHA256SUMS` of the publication record of 4.3.0; no dependency changed since 4.3.0, and they differ from the 4.3.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 4.4.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the designs of 3.0.0, 4.0.0, 4.1.0, 4.2.0, 4.3.0 and 4.4.0, the plan for the engineer's feedback, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the record of the publications say that 4.4.0 is prepared, and decision D-262, which the design proposed, is recorded. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

## 4.3.0 (2026-11-21; not a statement of compliance)

Version 4.3.0 is a **minor release** of 4.2.0 and the **latest full release**. It brings milestone E3 of the plan for the feedback of an electrical engineer (`docs/ait/ELEC_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`, point 9 of the feedback; the design `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.3_REVIEW_VIEW.md`, decision D-260; the release, D-261): **the review view**, one place per harness where a reviewer finds what to check and where each fact comes from. **A project's review checklist** is the new key `review_checklist` of `config/naming.json`, a list of points; **it is empty by default and the tool ships no point** (D-11): `config DIR --review-checklist FILE` takes the list items of a file (the shipped template `design-review-checklist.md` gives 26 points), and *Settings > Project* edits it (pull request 332 wrote the checklist, `core/review.py` and `vcs/release.harness_findings`, the one selection of the findings of a harness, which the release gate now uses with the same blockers). **The build workbook gets the sheet *Review*** before *About* (pull request 334): the release entry of the version, the checklist with an empty tick column, every finding of the harness with its state and what it means for the release, the findings of the whole project, the values still open, the sources (the ICD of each device connector it plugs into, each part with its approval and link) and how a number is explained; the independent check of the outputs words it again (`out_review`). **The editor gets a *Review* page** per harness, the same blocks with *Show*, *Explain*, *Copy link*, the library state of each device and tick boxes kept on this computer only; *Settings > Project* also shows the label format (pull request 335). **The ticks reach the release comment** from the person releasing (`release DIR HARNESS --ticked POINTS`, or the release dialog), as one note with the list hash and the points not ticked; **there is no gate**: points not ticked never stop a release (pull request 333). **`explain DIR FINDING`** explains one finding by its ID, as `drc --json` gives it (pull request 331). Pull request 336 adds the end-to-end walk `tests/test_e2e_430.py` and the documents; it found no fault of the others. It is a minor release because it adds a setting, a sheet, a page and two options, and `explain` takes a finding; no command or option is removed or renamed. **No format change:** 4.3.0 writes format 4 as 4.2.0 does, and format 4 stays frozen; the checklist is a free value of `config/naming.json`, which a 4.2.x tool keeps when it saves. **Written files:** every `<ID>_build.xlsx` gets the sheet *Review* and its line under *How to use this workbook* on *Start here*, and *About* is one sheet later; the comment of a release (the change log, the baseline, `system/changelog.csv`, `system/version_report.md`) gets the note in a project with a checklist, or with `--ticked`; `config/naming.json` of a project that sets a checklist gets the key; every other file has the same content as with 4.2.0, apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it. The command line keeps its names and the `--json` keys of every command, and the scripting interface is unchanged (`API_VERSION` 1). **How to upgrade from 4.2.x:** install 4.3.0 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. Export the outputs once with 4.3.0, because every build workbook changed and `verify --outputs` compares them with the version that runs. **Update the whole team at the same time:** a 4.2.x tool keeps the checklist when it saves, but it shows no Review page, writes no Review sheet and adds no note to a release, and its `verify --outputs` refuses the build workbooks of a 4.3.0 export (*does not have the sheets*). A script that reads the sheets of the build workbook by position from the end must count *Review* (`docs/releases/4.3.0.md`, *For scripts*). From 3.x or older, read the notes of 4.0.0 first: a format 3 project is upgraded once to format 4. **Known limits:** the ticks are not kept in the project (a field for them is a format change, milestone E5 of the plan), and the page keeps them on one computer; a ticked sheet is not read back; no setting makes a release wait for every tick; a long sheet is not cut (W002 of `flatsat` has about 80 rows without a checklist, about 105 with the template's points); a release made in the editor writes no output file, so the sheet shows the release and its note after the next export; `config DIR --review-checklist FILE` keeps the exit code of `config`, so it ends with 1 while an engineering value is missing; the library state of a device is on the page only; a device has no *checked* state of its own (format 5); a `single` type that shields per pair still gets no shield (owner question R11); nobody at a review has used the page or the sheet yet; the known limits of 4.0.0, 4.1.0 and 4.2.0 stand; nobody has looked at the screens on a real display; the Windows packages were tried on GitHub's runners only. **Not done:** the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; and the owner's sign-off. There was no release candidate first (D-261). `4.3.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, the version with which the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot were written (again for the build workbooks with pull request 334), so they stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0; decision D-261).

### Added

- **The core of the review view: the project's review checklist, the one selection of the findings of a harness, and the view of one harness with no caller yet** (pull request 1 of the design of 4.3.0, #332; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.3_REVIEW_VIEW.md` sections 3, 4.1 to 4.4, 6.2 and 10, decision D-260 proposed, owner questions R1, R2, R5, R6 and R12 as recommended). **The review checklist** is the new key `review_checklist` of `config/naming.json`: a list of points in the order a reviewer goes through them, each one line of 1 to 300 characters (the spaces around it left out), each point once (compared after trimming and folding the case), at most 100; not set, `null` or `[]` is a project without a checklist, and no example, template or new project has one, because the tool invents no review content (D-11).
