It is in the registry of settings (`naming.review_checklist`, kind *names*, no default), checked by `validate` (code `config_invalid`: *config/naming.json: review_checklist must be a list of points, each one line of 1 to 300 characters, each once, at most 100.*; a refused list is no list, and `config-invalid` stops every release as for any refused value), not on the hand-over list (so `config DIR` and its exit code do not change), and in `NOT_READ_BY_GENERATION`, so a changed list does not make the harness plans out of date; it is part of the content hash, so the outputs are out of date until the next export. `configcheck.plan_set_checklist` sets it in one undo step and takes the key out for no point. **`config DIR --review-checklist FILE`** reads the list items of a UTF-8 text or Markdown file of at most 64 KiB (`review.points_from_text`: lines that start with `- [ ] `, `- [x] `, `- `, `* `, `+ ` or a number and `. ` are the points and every other line is skipped; a file without list items gives each line that is not empty and not a heading; the list mark, `**` and backticks are taken away), prints the number of points and the list hash (`review.list_hash`, the first 8 hexadecimal characters of the SHA-256 of the list as canonical JSON), and refuses a file whose points the check refuses, with the text of the check, or a file with no point; `--review-checklist none` takes the key out, so the file is as it was. The shipped template `design-review-checklist.md` gives its 26 points. `settings DIR --set naming.review_checklist='[...]'` takes the list as JSON. **`vcs/release.harness_findings(project, h, findings)`** is the one selection of the findings that belong to a harness (its object is one of `related_ids`, or `drc.locate` places it in the harness or on an interface it carries, or places it nowhere and its object is a part the harness uses; the rules `config-invalid` and `unchecked-config` are the whole project's, `release.PROJECT_RULES`), taken out of the loop that `release_blockers` wrote inline. **The release gate gives the same blockers as before:** `release_blockers` now takes its rule errors from `harness_findings` and adds the errors of `config-invalid` for every harness, in the order of `drc.run` as before; a test compares its blockers with the selection of 4.2.0, word for word, on the five examples, `mini3`, `sat15`, the fixture projects, `loose_ends`, `splices`, `row_layout`, `mini3-released` and a copy of `mini3` with errors, a waiver and a wrong engineering value, and on random findings (the only findings the new clause adds are those of `part-unapproved`, a warning). **`core/review.py`** has `points`, `list_hash`, `points_from_text`, `parse_ticked` (`all`, `none` or numbers and ranges such as `1-5,7`; anything else raises `TickedError` with the text of the design: *--ticked must be all, none, or point numbers from 1 to 26 such as 1-5,7 (got '27').*) and `review_view(project, h, findings)`, pure, which gives the head and the six blocks of section 4.2 of the design as plain values: the checklist, every finding of the harness with its state (`open`, `open: its waiver no longer applies (the finding changed)`, `waived: …`, `accepted as a deviation: …`) and what it means for the release (`stops the release`, `needs a written reason`), the findings of the whole project, the values still open (placeholder files, engineering values not set, each open check of *Start here* with its count and its first five items, the interface types of example data it carries, overrides, the as-built records while that gate is on), the sources (each device connector it plugs into with its ICD, each part it uses with its approval and link) and how a number is explained; on the examples it gives the counts of section 2.8 of the design. Where the design left a wording open: an empty block of sources says *W002 plugs into no device connector and uses no part.*, an as-built row of a harness without records says *no records*, the counts in a heading are singular for one (*1 error*, *1 accepted as a deviation*), and a file of `--review-checklist` without any point is refused (`none` takes the list out). **Nothing calls the view yet:** no screen and no written file changes (the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the golden outputs are unchanged); pull requests 2 to 5 of the design add the ticks in the release comment, `explain DIR FINDING`, the Review sheet and the Review page. The key is new in `config/naming.json` only, read as a free value, so the project format stays 4, every existing project stays valid, and a 4.2 tool keeps the key on save without a word. `docs/CONFIG.md` describes the key, `docs/CLI.md` the option. New: `tests/test_review_checklist.py`, `tests/test_review_view.py`.

- **The ticks of the review checklist in the release comment** (pull request 2 of the design of 4.3.0, #333; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.3_REVIEW_VIEW.md` sections 6.1 to 6.4, 10 and 11, decision D-260 proposed, owner questions R4, R5 and R12 as recommended; REQ-CC-11). **`release DIR HARNESS --ticked POINTS`** says which points of the project's review checklist the person releasing says were ticked: `all`, `none`, or point numbers and ranges such as `1-5,7,9-14` (read with `review.parse_ticked`). `plan_release` and `release_blockers` take `ticked=`: the point numbers (as the editor's Review page will hand them) or the text of `--ticked`. Two blockers are new (`about="ticked"`, so the command line names `--ticked`): `ticked_invalid` (*--ticked must be all, none, or point numbers from 1 to 26 such as 1-5,7 (got '27').*) and `ticked_no_checklist` (*This project has no review checklist, so there is nothing to tick (config/naming.json: review_checklist).*). **No gate on the checklist** (R5): points not ticked, or no ticks given, never stop a release; without `--ticked` a release goes ahead as before. **`vcs/release.review_note`** adds one note last to the comment, after the notes of today, so their text and order stay: nothing in a project without a checklist; ` [Review checklist (26 points, list 1a2b3c4d): no ticks recorded]` when a project has one and no ticks are given; else ` [Review checklist (26 points, list 1a2b3c4d), recorded by NAME: all ticked]`, `none ticked]`, or `ticked 1-5, 7, 9-26; not ticked 6 (…), 8 (…)]`, the ticked points as ranges (`number_ranges`) and at most five points not ticked named, each cut to 60 characters (with `...`, as the note of deviations cuts a reason), then `and N more`. The note is in the change-log entry and the baseline, so `log`, the Releases tab, `system/changelog.csv` and `system/version_report.md` show it; the check of `comment_long` (2000 characters) counts it. A tick is a person's statement: the note says *recorded by*, never *checked*. **The comment of every release in a project without a checklist stays byte for byte as it was** (a test holds the comment of a release of `mini3`), and no example, template or recorded project sets a checklist, so no written file changes (the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the golden outputs are unchanged). `--json` of `release` keeps its keys (R12); the note is in the comment. A blocker whose text starts with its option (`--ticked must be …`) is printed without the option a second time. `docs/CLI.md` lists the option; `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` has REQ-CC-11. New: `tests/test_release_ticks.py`.

- **`explain DIR FINDING`** (pull request 3 of the design of 4.3.0, #331; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.3_REVIEW_VIEW.md` section 7, owner questions R9 and R12 as recommended). `explain` takes a finding ID as `drc --json` gives it (`voltage-drop.W001-001`, `part-unapproved.EX-MICROD-15-M`, `unconfirmed.IF-001`, a note of the checks not run such as `unchecked-config.emc`; a long ID cut and hashed by `checks.finding_id` alike, waived findings too) and prints what the editor's *Explain* shows (`derive.explain_finding`): the limit the rule checked and how it was derived, or, for a yes/no rule, what it compares, the statement of the finding, the configuration it reads and what it saw (the `rule-check` derivation of `derive_rules`), with the model page; `--json` prints the derivations as a list, as for an object, with no new key. An ID is looked up among the findings only when it names no object (`explain_object` raises the new `NotAnObjectError`), so every object keeps its meaning. An ID that is neither says `error: '…' is not a wire, a harness or a part used in a harness, nor an interface, a connector, a pin, a shield group, a test or a finding of this project.` and exits with 2; `api.explain` keeps its own words. The help and `docs/CLI.md` name the finding; `tests/test_explain_finding.py`. No written file changes, and the scripting interface is unchanged.

- **The build workbook has a *Review* sheet: what a reviewer of the harness checks and where each fact comes from** (pull request 4 of the design of 4.3.0, #334; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.3_REVIEW_VIEW.md` part B, sections 4.1 to 4.7, 9 and 11, decision D-260 proposed, owner questions R3 and R6 as recommended; REQ-OUT-16). `<ID>_build.xlsx` gets the sheet *Review* before *About*, after *Tests* and, for a harness with as-built records, *As-built*, so every other sheet keeps its place; *Start here* lists it in *How to use this workbook* before *About*. The sheet writes the review view of pull request 1 (`core/review.review_view`) and works nothing out again: five rows of head (`Review of W002  aocs to bus`, the version, status, stamp and notice, the release entry of the version or `Release: not released yet.`, what the column *Release* means, and where to tick: a printout or a copy outside `outputs/`), then six blocks, each a heading row, a column row and its rows, or one sentence when it has none: **Checklist** (the points of `naming.review_checklist` with **No.**, **Point**, an empty tick column **Done (x)** that offers an `x` and turns green, and **Note**; the heading names the count and the list hash), **Findings of W002** (every finding about the harness or what it carries, the selection the release gate uses, with its severity, rule, ID, object, text, state and *stops the release* in red or *needs a written reason* in amber), **For the whole project** (`config-invalid` and `unchecked-config`), **Values still open** (placeholder files, engineering values not set, each open check of *Start here* but the parts with its count and first five items in natural ID order (so the sheet does not depend on the order in which the wires are stored), interface types of example data, overrides, the as-built records while the gate is on), **Sources to look at** (each device connector the harness plugs into with its ICD, version and link, each part with its approval and link) and **How the numbers were made**. It prints like every sheet (A4 landscape, one page wide, the stamp in the footer) with no repeated header row, *Point* and *Finding text* up to 80 characters wide, and a page break before a block that would start in the last five rows of a page. `build_outputs` runs the logical checks and the design rules once and hands them to every build workbook and to `system/drc_findings.csv` (`build_workbook(project, h, stamp, findings)`; a call without the findings runs them). The independent check of the outputs words the sheet again (`core/outputs/verify_review.py`, which imports neither the view, the workbook, the tables nor the release gate) and reads it back block by block: a wrong release entry, point, tick, finding (missing, not about the harness, a wrong state or cell), count, open value, source, heading or sentence is `out_review`; a missing sheet or *Start here* line is `out_build`. The proof of 3.0.0 gets step 10 (`tests/format3_support.review_sheet`): the 2.x build workbook with the sheet *Review* as written and its line on *Start here*. **Written files:** every `<ID>_build.xlsx` of every project changes, for this one reason, and no other file. The stored hashes of the written files were made again on purpose (`docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json`: the 45 build workbooks of the four examples, the only differences of `--check`), and so were the numbers snapshot (`tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json`: 129 build workbooks of 11 scenarios, the only differences) and the golden outputs (`tests/fixtures/outputs/*.json`: 69 build workbook digests of `mini3`, `sat15`, `sat15_full`, `loose_ends`, `splices` and `row_layout`; the harness plan goldens are unchanged). A script that reads the sheets of the build workbook by position from the end must be updated: *About* is one sheet later. A 4.2.x tool refuses a 4.3.0 export in `verify --outputs` (*does not have the sheets*), so a team updates at the same time. `tests/test_review_sheet.py`, `tests/test_review_verify.py`.

- **The editor has a *Review* page: one harness at a time, the checklist with tick boxes, its findings, what is still open and the sources; *Release…* records the ticks; *Settings > Project* edits the checklist and the label format** (pull request 5 of the design of 4.3.0, #335; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.3_REVIEW_VIEW.md` part C, sections 5.1 to 5.4, 6.1 and part F, section 8, decision D-260 proposed, owner questions R2, R4, R7, R8 and R10 as recommended; REQ-GUI-31). **The page** (`gui/review_page.py`, the area `review` with the icon `nav-review`, after *As-built*) shows the review view of pull request 1 (`core/review.review_view`) over the findings the window already has, so nothing is worked out twice: a harness picker shared with the Versions, the state, a summary line (*W002, version 1.0.0, draft: no checklist; 1 thing stops the release; 7 warnings and 11 notes open; 8 values still open*), *Release…* and *Edit the checklist…*, then the blocks of the sheet, each a table (an empty block shows its sentence in place of the table, as on the sheet): the checklist with a tick box per point, *Tick all* and *Clear*; the findings of the harness and of the whole project with *Show*, *Explain*, *Waive…* and *Accept as deviation…*; the values still open with *Show*, *Supply value…* and *Show all*; the sources with *Copy link* (the clipboard; the editor opens no browser), *Show* and a column *Library*, the state of each device in the device library, which a written file cannot hold; and how the numbers were made. While the check runs the page says that the list is from the last check. **It is reached** from the rail of the Electrical engineer and the AIT engineer (the Systems engineer's rail leaves it out, as Drawings and Outputs), the button of the step *Review and release* of the Next step page (*Open the Review page* in place of *Go to Releases*: the first harness in ID order that is not released), the button *Review page* of the Releases tab, *Go > Go to Review* (`act-go-review`, in every role's menu), the command palette and *Review…* in the menu of a harness in the project tree. **The ticks** are kept on this computer only, in the settings file under `layout/<hash>/review/<harness>`, with the list hash and the first 12 characters of `content_hash` they were made for; when the list or the design changed they are dropped and the page says *The checklist or the design changed after these points were ticked; tick them again*. A released harness shows its release entry and no tick boxes. *Release…*, from the page, the Releases tab, the top bar or the menu, hands the ticks to the release dialog, which shows the note it adds to the comment (*Added to the comment: [Review checklist …]*, worded with the name typed) and *Open the Review page*; the release then records them (`plan_release(ticked=…)`); a project without a checklist releases as before. **Settings > Project** gets the section *Review checklist* (a box with one point per line, *Use the template's points*, *Take the points of a file…*, *Save* as one undo step through `configcheck.plan_set_checklist`, a refused list says why under the box, and nothing is saved until *Save*) and the choice *Label format* (`model.label_format`, the five formats with *(a new project's choice)* after `harness_device_connector`, the words of the format and, for `custom`, how the two templates are set; one undo step through `configcheck.plan_set_choice`). `core/review.read_points` and `template_points` read a file and the shipped template for the editor and for `config --review-checklist`, whose texts stay as they were. **Written files:** none changes (`tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` *identical*, the goldens unchanged); the ticks are never in the project, the model hash or a written file. `tests/test_ui_review_page.py`, the role tests.

- **The end-to-end test and the documents of 4.3.0** (pull request 6 of the design of 4.3.0, #336; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.3_REVIEW_VIEW.md` sections 11, 12 and 16, decision D-260 proposed, owner questions R1 to R12 as recommended; REQ-OUT-16, REQ-CC-11, REQ-GUI-31). `tests/test_e2e_430.py` walks a reviewer through the review view on the example `flatsat`, through the command line: `new --template flatsat`, `templates`, `config --review-checklist design-review-checklist.md` (26 points, the list hash on its first line; the command ends with 1 while four engineering values are missing, as `config` does), `generate` (nothing changes) and `export`; the *Review* sheet of W001 read back (the checklist with an empty tick column, its 14 findings each one that `drc --json` prints with its state and *needs a written reason* for the two example parts, the three notes of the whole project, the six placeholder files the release gate names, 18 device connectors and 2 parts as sources, how a number is explained); a ticked workbook in `outputs/` that `verify --outputs` reports (`out_modified`, `out_review`) and that stops the release (`outputs_modified`), and a ticked copy elsewhere that does not; `release --ticked 1-20` blocked by the placeholders and the example parts with nothing recorded, `--ticked 27` refused (`ticked_invalid`), and the release with the two reasons, whose comment ends with the note; the note in `log`, `log --json`, `system/changelog.csv`, `system/version_report.md`, the baseline and row 3 of the sheet written again; `verify --outputs` with 0 errors. A second test explains a finding of each kind the sheet lists with `explain DIR FINDING` and `--json`. A third walks the editor: the Next step page's *Open the Review page* opens W001, 25 of 26 points ticked on the page, *Release…* shows *Added to the comment: [Review checklist …]* and the release records it, the released page shows no tick box and no line about ticks to make again, and the next `export` writes the release and its note on the sheet. **No fault of pull requests 1 to 5 was found.** Two facts the design did not say are recorded in its section 17: a release in the editor writes no output file (the sheet shows the note after the next export), and `config --review-checklist` keeps the exit code of `config`. **The documents:** `docs/releases/4.3.0.md`; the user manual (`release-a-harness.md`: the checklist, the Review page, `--ticked` and the note; `export-the-outputs.md`: the ten sheets, the Review sheet and where to tick; `read-and-fix-problems.md`: the findings of one harness and `explain DIR FINDING`; `choose-your-role.md` and `follow-the-workflow.md`: *Open the Review page* in place of *Go to Releases*, the Review area on the rail and in Quick mode; `naming-and-title-block.md`: the checklist in `naming.json` and the label format on *Settings > Project*; `use-git-and-ci.md`: the template as the project's checklist), the guide (the rail, *Settings > Project*, the Review sheet, the Review page, *Go to Review*, `config --review-checklist`, `release --ticked`, `explain` of a finding; `python -m tools.build_guide`), `GLOSSARY.md` (*Review checklist*, *Review page*, *Review sheet*), `LEARNING_PATH.md` (step 7), the templates' `README.md` and `docs/examples/README.md` (row 14), the agent notes `gui.md` (the Review page), `change-control.md` (`harness_findings`, `--ticked`, `review_note`) and `outputs.md`, and the status lines of the design and of `docs/ait/ELEC_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md` (E3 built, not released). **Written files:** none changes (`tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` *identical*, the goldens unchanged).

### Changed

- **Records for 4.3.0.** The version is 4.3.0 (decision D-261). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 4.2.0 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it (the build workbook digests of the golden outputs changed with pull request 334, with its own renewal). `4.3.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.4.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 4.3.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 4.2.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against the `SHA256SUMS` of the publication record of 4.2.0; no dependency changed since 4.2.0, and they differ from the 4.2.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 4.3.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the designs of 3.0.0, 4.0.0, 4.1.0, 4.2.0 and 4.3.0, the plan for the engineer's feedback, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the record of the publications say that 4.3.0 is prepared, and decision D-260, which the design proposed, is recorded. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

## 4.2.0 (2026-11-21; not a statement of compliance)

Version 4.2.0 is a **minor release** of 4.1.0 and the **latest full release**. It brings milestone E2 of the plan for the feedback of an electrical engineer (`docs/ait/ELEC_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`; the design `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.2_ELECTRICAL_CHECKS.md`, decision D-258; the release, D-259): **electrical checks.** Four design rules are new, so the tool has **43 design rules** (pull request 326 registers them; pull requests 324 and 325 wrote them): `pair-untwisted` (a warning: two wires of a signal pair that no twisted pair, shielded pair or quad holds together, the pair taken from the interface type or, for a wire without an interface, from the types of its signals or from the endings of the new setting `naming.pair_suffixes`, default `+ -`, `P N`, `H L`), `signal-unshielded` (a warning: a wire of a harness Generate did not make whose signal every interface type that has it shields, in no shield), `level-conflict` (an error that may be accepted as a deviation: a net, or a pair of a connection without a harness, that joins two device pins whose levels are a pair of the new setting `segregation.level_pairs_to_separate`, compared as whole words; **the table is empty by default and the tool ships no level word**, so the rule is silent until a project fills it in and says *not checked* where pins have levels) and `wire-direction` (an error: a net of wires drawn by hand that joins two outputs, or only inputs). Each rule notes under *Checks not run* what it could not judge, only where the project holds the data. Generate makes the overall shield of an interface type of construction `single` that shields overall, which it skipped, so that its own verifier refused the harness (pull request 323). Pull request 327 adds the end-to-end walk and the documents, and fixed a fault of pull request 4 that it found (the endings of `naming.pair_suffixes` in an explanation). It is a minor release because it adds design rules and two settings; it changes no command or option. **No format change:** 4.2.0 writes format 4 as 4.1.0 does, and format 4 stays frozen; the two settings are free values of `config/naming.json` and `config/segregation.json`. **Written files:** every `system/drc_report.md` says `Rules run: 43.` where it said `39.`; `system/drc_report.md`, `system/drc_findings.csv` and the *DRC findings* sheet of `system/system.xlsx` get the new findings and notes in a project with a harness drawn by hand or made from a connection that has a signal pair, a shielded signal or a net of known directions, or with pin levels (none of the examples); *Start here* of the build workbook lists an accepted `level-conflict`; the generated harnesses of a project with a `single` type that shields overall get the new group, and with it the files that list groups; every other file has the same content as with 4.1.0, apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it. The command line and the scripting interface keep their names (`API_VERSION` 1; `api.list_rules()` and `api.run_checks()` return 43 rules). **How to upgrade from 4.1.x:** install 4.2.0 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. **Update the whole team at the same time if you draw harnesses by hand or make them from connections:** a 4.1.x tool keeps the two settings when it saves but does not run the new rules, so it can release a harness that 4.2.0 blocks with a `level-conflict` or `wire-direction` error. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 4.2.0, because every DRC report changed. A script that reads the findings sees `rules_run` 43, and `drc` exits 1 for a harness drawn by hand with an open error where 4.1.x exited 0 (`docs/releases/4.2.0.md`, *For scripts*). From 3.x or older, read the notes of 4.0.0 first: a format 3 project is upgraded once to format 4. **Known limits:** a `single` type that shields per pair still gets no shield; pairing by endings can pair signals that are no pair (`HEATER_H` and `HEATER_L`; take `H L` out of the setting or waive the finding); levels are words, and nobody has told the tool a team's real level words yet; neighbouring pins, one connector and one bundle are not checked for levels; a shield is asked per interface type, not per signal (a field per signal needs a new format, milestone E5 of the plan); *Shielded signals* gives one finding per wire; a generated wire's directions are still judged by its interface type; `explain` takes no finding ID on the command line (`explain DIR --all --out FOLDER` and the editor's *Explain* say what each rule read); nobody at a bench or a review has read the findings yet; the known limits of 4.0.0 and 4.1.0 stand; nobody has looked at the screens on a real display; the Windows packages were tried on GitHub's runners only. **Not done:** the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; and the owner's sign-off. There was no release candidate first (D-259). `4.2.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, the version with which the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot were written (again for the build workbooks and the wire lists of 4.1.0 with pull requests 311 and 314; the count of 43 rules leaves them identical, as both read a two-digit count back as 39), so they stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0; decision D-259).

### Added

- **The end-to-end test and the documents of 4.2.0** (pull request 5 of the design of 4.2.0, #327; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.2_ELECTRICAL_CHECKS.md` sections 10, 11 and 15; REQ-DRC-06 to REQ-DRC-09). `tests/test_e2e_420.py` walks the pinout-first project of section 2.7 through the command line: `new --template blank`, two RS-422 devices from their pinouts with *Direction* and *Level* (`import-pinout`), a connection and the harness made from it (through the plans the editor runs, as no command makes them), `generate`, which keeps it, and `drc --json` with `rules_run` 43, two `pair-untwisted`, four `signal-unshielded` and the note `levels`; then the level table through `settings --set`, a harness drawn by hand between the same device connectors and `drc --json` with the counts of the corrected section 2.7 (two `pair-untwisted`, seven `signal-unshielded`, one `level-conflict`, two `wire-direction`, the notes `level-unknown` and `wire-direction-unknown`, and the texts of the findings); `export`, whose `system/drc_findings.csv` and `system/drc_report.md` (*Rules run: 43.*) hold them; `explain --all --out`, whose `derivations.json` says what each of the four rules read at one of its findings; the fixes a person makes (two wires moved to the pins that fit, a gauge and a length, *Group wires* as a shielded pair); `review` and `release`, refused for the level conflict alone; `deviation`, after which the release goes on, the findings file reads `deviation` and *Start here* of the build workbook lists it; `verify --outputs` with 0 errors; and an exported `flatsat` with no finding and no note of the four rules. The documents of section 11 that pull requests 1 to 4 left: the release notes `docs/releases/4.2.0.md`; the user manual (`build-a-harness-by-hand.md` and `connect-two-connectors.md`: what the four rules check on a harness drawn by hand or made from a connection; `edit-a-wire.md`: which group answers `pair-untwisted` and `signal-unshielded`, and why a pair that needs a shield is one shielded pair; `see-what-the-tool-assumes.md`: the two settings and the four assumptions; `define-a-device.md` and `import-kicad-pinouts.md`: the *Level* and *Direction* of a pinout now feed two rules); the in-app guide (`python -m tools.build_guide`); `docs/GLOSSARY.md` (*Level (of a pin)*, *Signal pair*, and *Direction* read by `wire-direction`); the status of E2 in `docs/ait/ELEC_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md` and of the design page (built, not released). A fault of pull request 4 found by the test is fixed: the explanation of a `pair-untwisted` finding showed the setting `naming.pair_suffixes` as `++-, P+N, H+L`; it now shows it as `settings` and the Assumptions window do, `+ -, P N, H L` (`derive_rules.yes_no_derivation` uses `settings.display_of`). **Written files:** none change (the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the golden outputs are as before; only `derivations.json` and `derivations.csv` of `explain --all`, and the editor's *Explain*, of a project with a `pair-untwisted` finding read the endings in the new way).
- **The four electrical rules run: 43 design rules** (pull request 4 of the design of 4.2.0, #326; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.2_ELECTRICAL_CHECKS.md` sections 7, 8 and 10, decision D-258 proposed, owner questions C1 to C10 as recommended; REQ-DRC-06 to REQ-DRC-09). `pair-untwisted`, `signal-unshielded`, `level-conflict` and `wire-direction` (`core/drc/electrical.py`) are in the registry `drc.RULES` after `direction-conflict`, so `drc`, the editor's background check, the *Problems* panel and the *Checks* tab, `export` (the findings files) and the release gate run them, and `api.list_rules()` and `api.run_checks()` have the four more (`rules_run` 43). Their notes (`levels`, `no-level`, `level-unknown`, `wire-direction-unknown`) are under `unchecked-config` (`rules._unchecked` calls `electrical.unchecked`), only where the project holds the data. The *Checks* tab: `pair-untwisted` reads `naming.pair_suffixes`, and `level-conflict` is *pending* while `segregation.level_pairs_to_separate` is not set (on screen only; the note `levels` is its gate's), the other notes make a rule *not checked* (`drc/by_rule.py` `READS`, `GATES`, `NOTES`). The editor's *Explain*, `explain DIR --all --out FOLDER` (`derivations.json`) and `api.explain_finding` say what each rule saw (the pair and where it came from with the groups; the types of the signal and how each shields; the levels at the device ends; the direction of each end and whether it came from the pin or the types; `derive_rules.INFO`), and send the reader to `docs/models/shields.md` (the first two) or `rule-limits.md`. `level-conflict` may be accepted as a deviation (`deviation DIR FINDING --reason TEXT` or *Accept as deviation*), which puts a line under *Overrides and deviations* of *Start here* in the build workbook and lets the release of its harness go on; an open one blocks it; the help of `deviation`, the refusal of `checks.deviate_op` and the user manual name *signal levels* among the rules that take a deviation. Every *39* that counted the rules says 43 (the tests, `docs/RULES.md`, the user manual, the guide, `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, the developer notes); `tools/output_hashes.py` keeps `BASE_RULES` 39, the count of its baseline. **None of the four fires on the five examples** (no example has a link, a drawn wire, a pin direction or a pin level), and the counts of section 2.7 of the design hold with the registered rules, but one: the pinout-first project with the drawn harness and the level table gives **7** `signal-unshielded`, not 8, because W002-003 runs from the fixed pin `PWR_RTN` (signal RTN of port PWR of the type `discrete`, which shields nothing) to TX-, so the types at its ends disagree; the design page says so. **Written files:** every `system/drc_report.md` says `Rules run: 43.` in place of `39.`; a project with a harness drawn by hand or made from a connection that has a signal pair, a shielded signal or a net of known directions, or with pin levels, gets the findings and notes in `system/drc_report.md`, `system/drc_findings.csv` and the *DRC findings* sheet of `system/system.xlsx`; nothing else changes. The stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot are **identical** (both read a two-digit count back as 39). The golden outputs were renewed on purpose for this reason (`python -m tools.gen_output_goldens`, read file by file): the digest of `system/drc_report.md` of `mini3`, `sat15`, `sat15_full`, `row_layout`, `loose_ends` and `splices` (only the count line), and for `loose_ends` also `drc_findings.csv` and `system.xlsx` (3 `pair-untwisted`, 4 `signal-unshielded` and the note `wire-direction-unknown`) and for `splices` the two findings files (the note `wire-direction-unknown`); the texts `tests/fixtures/outputs/mini3/system/drc_report.md` and `tests/fixtures/drc/sat15.md` differ only in the count line; the plan goldens do not move. The proof of 3.0.0 has **step 9** (`tests/format3_support.py` `rule_count`): the one line `Rules run: 39.` of the DRC report of 2.x reads as the count of this tool, and a fast test shows that the step changes that line only and still reports a finding added by hand, another count or the line twice; the slow tests pass. New: `tests/test_electrical_registered.py`; `POSITIVE` of `tests/test_drc.py` has a case for each of the four.
- **The rules `level-conflict` and `wire-direction` exist, and are not run by `check` or `drc` yet** (pull request 3 of the design of 4.2.0, #325; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.2_ELECTRICAL_CHECKS.md` sections 5, 6 and 7, decision D-258 proposed, owner questions C1, C2, C3, C8 and C10 as recommended). `core/drc/electrical.py` gets the two rules with the texts of the design (in `ELECTRICAL_RULES`, beside the two of pull request 2), their checks and `unchecked`, the notes of what they could not judge. `level-conflict` (an error that may be accepted as a deviation) finds a net of a harness, or a pair of a connection without a harness, that joins two device pins whose levels (`Pin.level`) are a pair of the new setting `segregation.level_pairs_to_separate`, compared as whole words with the case and the outer spaces left out and no number read out of a word (*Wire W002-003 in W002 joins pin 6 of DEV1-J01 (level 18V) and pin 4 of DEV2-J01 (level RS422-3V3), two levels the project keeps apart*; through a splice *The wires … (through splice W003-X1) join …*; *Link L1 pairs … with …*); a connection with a harness is judged through its harness. **The table is empty by default and the tool ships no level word** (D-11): while it is not set the rule is silent, and the note `levels` (*Signal levels were not checked: 9 pin(s) have a level (18V, RS422-3V3), but no pairs of levels to keep apart are set …*) is given only where a pin has a level; with a table, `no-level` and `level-unknown` say what was not judged. `wire-direction` (an error) finds a net of wires without an interface whose ends are all device pins and that joins two outputs (*… two outputs drive against each other*) or only inputs (*… nothing drives the wire*), by the directions the review of connections reads (`links.pin_direction`: the pin's own, else the interface types'); a net of two ends that a connection pairs stays with `link-direction`, a generated wire with `direction-conflict`, a net with a loose end is not judged, and a net with an end of unknown direction is counted in the note `wire-direction-unknown`. `links._direction` and `links._conflict` have the public names `pin_direction` and `direction_conflict` (and `_signal_index` `signal_index`); the old names are kept. The setting `segregation.level_pairs_to_separate` is in the registry of settings (kind *pairs*, no default), is an optional value of `config` (not on the hand-over list, so `config DIR` says what it said), has the field *Signal levels kept apart* in *Settings > Engineering values* (`28V + 3V3`), is checked by `validate` (pairs of two different words, each pair once, at most 200 characters, `["3V3", "3v3"]` is one word twice; `config_invalid`; a refused table keeps the rule silent), set with `settings DIR --set` (any word, `+5V` too) and described in `docs/CONFIG.md`. Two assumptions are new: `A-LEVEL-WORDS` (a setting) and `A-DIRECTION-AT-DEVICE` (built in), on `docs/models/rule-limits.md`. **The rules are not registered:** the registry still holds 39 rules, so `check`, `drc` and every written file are as before (the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the golden outputs are unchanged); pull request 4 of the design registers them, with the notes, the deviation and the release. The key is new in `config/segregation.json` only, read as a free value, so the project format stays 4 and every existing project stays valid. New: `tests/test_rule_level_conflict.py` (with the nets compared with the verifier's own `verify.splice_nets` on random harnesses), `tests/test_rule_wire_direction.py` (with Hypothesis: on a harness made from a connection with random pin directions, its findings once the connection is removed are the `link_direction_conflict` findings of the connection); `tests/test_configcheck_levels.py` covers the new setting too.
- **The rules `pair-untwisted` and `signal-unshielded` exist, and are not run by `check` or `drc` yet** (pull request 2 of the design of 4.2.0, #324; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.2_ELECTRICAL_CHECKS.md` sections 3, 4 and 7, decision D-258 proposed, owner questions C4, C5 and C6 as recommended). A new module `core/drc/electrical.py` holds the two rules with the texts of the design (`ELECTRICAL_RULES`), their checks and helpers (`signal_pairs`, `shield_asked`, `signal_types`, `signal_end`). `pair-untwisted` (a warning) finds two wires of a signal pair that no twisted pair, shielded pair or quad holds together: the wires of one interface whose signals have one `pair` in the type and run from the same connector to the same connector, or two wires without an interface that join the same two connectors and carry, at each of the two where both have a signal, two signals that every interface type that has both pairs, or, where no type has both, two names that differ only by the endings of a row of the new setting `naming.pair_suffixes` (default `+ -`, `P N`, `H L`, compared exactly); a name shared by three wires is no pair, and a wire part with two conductors does not twist (*Wires W010-001 (1PPS+) and W010-002 (1PPS-) in W010 are a pair by their names but are not twisted together*). `signal-unshielded` (a warning) finds a wire of a harness Generate did not make whose signal every interface type that has it shields (overall, per pair for a signal of a pair, or a coax), in no shielded pair, overall shield, coax or quad (*Wire W011-001 in W011 carries SIG+, which the interface type analog shields (overall), but is in no shield (it is in twisted pair W011-S1)*); a generated harness stays with the verifier's `shield_missing`. The setting `naming.pair_suffixes` is in the registry of settings (kind *pairs*; the Assumptions page shows it as `+ -, P N, H L`), checked by `validate` (pairs of two different endings of 1 to 3 characters, letters, digits, `+` and `-`, no ending in two pairs; code `config_invalid`; a refused table pairs no names), set with `settings DIR --set`, and described in `docs/CONFIG.md`; it is not on the hand-over list of `config` and has no field in the editor. Two assumptions are new: `A-PAIR-NAMES` (a setting) and `A-SHIELD-BY-TYPE` (built in), on `docs/models/shields.md`. **The rules are not registered:** the registry still holds 39 rules, so `check`, `drc`, the editor and every written file are as before (the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the golden outputs are unchanged); pull request 4 of the design registers them. The key is new in `config/naming.json` only, read as a free value, so the project format stays 4 and every existing project stays valid. New: `tests/test_rule_pair_untwisted.py`, `tests/test_rule_signal_unshielded.py` (they call the checks directly; on every example the pairs found are the twisted and shielded pairs that `generate/shields.plan` makes, and the drawn harnesses of `loose_ends` give the 3 and 4 findings of section 2.7 of the design), `tests/test_configcheck_levels.py`.

### Fixed

- **Generate makes the overall shield of an interface whose type has construction `single` and shields overall** (pull request 1 of the design of 4.2.0, #323; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.2_ELECTRICAL_CHECKS.md` sections 15 and 16.1, owner question C7 as recommended; REQ-GEN-04). `generate/shields.plan` passed over a type of construction `single` before it read its `shielding`, so an interface of such a type that shields overall got no shield group. The independent verifier then reported `shield_missing` for its wires, `generate` did not save (*Not saved: fix the errors above first*), and `drc` listed a `verify-mismatch` error whose fix, *Regenerate harnesses*, changed nothing. Reproduced on a copy of `flatsat` with the type `discrete` set to `shielding` `overall`: IF-040 (W002-025, W002-026) and IF-041 (W006-011, W006-012). The fault is as old as the generator: the skip is in its first version, before 1.0.0, and was moved unchanged into `generate/shields.py` with the transparency work of 1.12.0. Now such an interface gets one `overall_shield` over all its wires, made as the overall shield of every other construction is made: after the other groups of the interface, with the next number of the harness (`W002-S20`), and with the ends of the grounding concept (`config/generation.json` `shield_end_a`, `shield_end_b`). A `single` type still twists no pair, even when its signals name one, and a `single` type that does not shield still gets no group. As whenever a type's groups change, the groups that Generate makes after the new one in the same harness take the next numbers (in that copy of `flatsat`, W006-S6 and W006-S7 become W006-S7 and W006-S8). `explain` of the group says why (*the type discrete shields overall: one shield over all the wires of IF-040 (construction single: no pair is twisted)*); `docs/models/shields.md` says what a `single` type gets. **Written files:** none of the examples, templates or fixtures has a `single` type that shields, so no file of theirs changes: the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the golden outputs are as before. In a project with such a type, Generate now saves its harnesses, and their wire lists, cut lists, *Production* sheets, drawings and plans name the new group. New: `tests/test_shields_single_overall.py`.

### Changed

- **Records for 4.2.0.** The version is 4.2.0 (decision D-259). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 4.1.0 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it (the DRC reports and the findings files of `loose_ends` and `splices` changed with pull request 326, with its own renewal). `4.2.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.4.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 4.2.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 4.1.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_4.1.0_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 4.1.0, and they differ from the 4.1.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 4.2.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the designs of 3.0.0, 4.0.0, 4.1.0 and 4.2.0, the plan for the engineer's feedback, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 4.1.0 say that 4.2.0 is prepared, and decision D-258, which the design proposed, is recorded. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

## 4.1.0 (2026-11-20; not a statement of compliance)

Version 4.1.0 is a **minor release** of 4.0.1 and the **latest full release**. It brings milestone E1 of the plan for the feedback of an electrical engineer (`docs/ait/ELEC_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`; the design `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.1_PRODUCTION_SHEET.md`, decision D-256; the release, D-257): **the production sheet.** The build workbook (pull request 314) and the harness plan (pull request 317) get a sheet *Production*: one block per cable connector in natural ID order, the splices last, and one row per pin and wire with where it goes, the wire part, the colour, the gauge, the length, where the length comes from, the label at this end and every shield group with its end here and at the far end; a run of spare pins is one row. Every length heading of the build workbook and of the plan says what it is measured from (*rear to rear*; on *Routing*, *rear or branch centre*), and each file says it once more in words taken from the project's settings. The plan's harness sheet prints at a fixed scale, one page wide, with rows 6 to 9 on every page and page breaks between the blocks, where it was fitted to one page (pull requests 313 and 317). A new label format, `harness_device_connector`, labels a cable connector by harness, device and device connector name (`W001:MTQ1:J01`), and **a new project starts in it** (pull request 315); a project that names no format is read as `standard`, as before, so no existing project changes its labels. The wire list and the cut list name every shield group of a wire, not only one (pull request 311). Pull request 312 laid the core, pull request 318 fixed a fault that the end-to-end test found, and pull request 319 adds the end-to-end walk and the documents. It is a minor release because it adds to the written files and adds a value of a setting; it changes no command or option. **No format change:** 4.1.0 writes format 4 as 4.0.1 does, and format 4 stays frozen. **Written files:** every build workbook and every harness plan changes once; `wirelist.csv`, the *Wire list* of `<ID>.xlsx` and the *Cut list* change for a harness with a wire in two shield groups; the labels change only in a project that names the new format; every other file has the same content as with 4.0.1, apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it. The command line and the scripting interface are unchanged (`API_VERSION` 1). **How to upgrade from 4.0.x:** install 4.1.0 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. **Update the whole team at the same time, before anyone starts a new project with 4.1.0:** a 4.0.x tool refuses the value `harness_device_connector` (`config_invalid`; Generate does not save, and `export` writes the standard labels), so it cannot work on a project made with 4.1.0. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 4.1.0, because every build workbook changed. A script that reads the sheets of the build workbook by position must read them by name (`docs/releases/4.1.0.md`, *For scripts*). From 3.x or older, read the notes of 4.0.0 first: a format 3 project is upgraded once to format 4. **Known limits:** nobody has printed the new sheets at a bench (the scale, the rows per page and the page breaks are worked out from the column widths and an estimate of the row heights, so a program may break a page elsewhere); a length reference per connector end, a connector depth, the pin a shield pigtail lands on and the backshell of a shield end need new fields, so a new format (milestone E5 of the plan); the known limits of 4.0.0 stand; nobody has looked at the screens on a real display; the Windows packages were tried on GitHub's runners only. **Not done:** the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; and the owner's sign-off. There was no release candidate first (D-257). `4.1.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, the version with which the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot were written (again for the build workbooks and the wire lists with pull requests 311 and 314), so they stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0; decision D-257).

### Added

- **The core of the production sheet exists, with no output change yet** (pull request 1 of the design of 4.1.0, #312; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.1_PRODUCTION_SHEET.md` section 14). `core/outputs/production.py` makes the rows of the coming *Production* sheet (`production_blocks`: one block per cable connector in natural ID order, the splices last; one row per pin and wire, a run of spare pins on one row such as `3 to 9`, a pin with two wires on two rows; the far end with what it plugs into, the wire's part, colour, gauge, length, where the length comes from, the label at this end and every shield group of the wire with its end here and at the far end, or *to fill in*). `core/shield_ends.py` names the rule the drawings have used for end A of a shield (the `from` end of the group's wire with the lowest ID in the harness; `end_at`, `reference_wire`), and the drawing takes it from there. `core/generate/lengths.py` gets `reference_text` (what a length is measured from, worded from the project's settings) and `length_origin` (*on the wire*, *locked*, *segments only, no service loops*), which `explain` of a wire's length now starts from. The independent verifier has its own copy of the rows (`core/outputs/verify_production.py`, which imports none of these builders), and its copy of the label texts moved, word for word, from `core/outputs/verify.py` to `core/outputs/verify_labels.py`. Nothing calls `production_blocks` yet, so no written file changes: the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the golden outputs are as before, and so are the texts of `explain`. `tests/test_production_sheet.py`, `tests/test_production_verify.py`, `tests/test_shield_ends.py`, `tests/test_length_reference.py`.
- **The `.ods` writer can print a sheet at a fixed scale, with heading rows repeated on every page and page breaks** (pull request 4 of the design of 4.1.0, #313; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.1_PRODUCTION_SHEET.md` section 5.1). `core/outputs/ods.py` has `Page.scale` (a whole percent from 10 to 400, written as `style:scale-to`; with `fit` it is refused), `Sheet.repeat_rows` (the rows written inside `table:table-header-rows`) and `Sheet.breaks` (the rows before which a page starts, each with a row style `fo:break-before="page"`); equal rows are not merged across the heading rows or a break. Each is off by default and has no caller yet, so no written file changes (the digest of the writer's sample, the output-hash baseline, the numbers snapshot and the plan goldens stay). New: `tests/test_ods_print.py`.
- **The build workbook has a *Production* sheet, and its lengths say what they are measured from** (pull request 3 of the design of 4.1.0, #314; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.1_PRODUCTION_SHEET.md` sections 3 and 6). `<ID>_build.xlsx` gets the sheet *Production* after *Cut list* (and *Routing*, for a harness with a branch point) and before *Connectors*: one block per cable connector in natural ID order, the splices last under their own title, one row per pin and wire with the far end and what it plugs into, the wire part, the colour (filled in, as on the cut list), the gauge, the length in m (*Length (m), rear to rear*), *Length from*, the label at this end and the shield groups with their end here and at the far end; a run of spare pins is one row; a value the design does not hold reads *to fill in* on an amber cell; a tick column *Done (x)*. It prints like every other sheet (A4 landscape, one page wide, the headings on every page), with a page break before a block that would pass 42 rows. *Start here* lists the sheet in *How to use this workbook* and has a row *Lengths* under *From the design* with the four short sentences that say what every length is measured from, worded from the project's settings, one row each (`lengths.reference_lines`; `reference_text` joins them), so a printout never cuts one long cell at the last column. The heading *Length (m)* of the *Cut list* is now *Length (m), rear to rear*, and of *Routing* *Length (m), rear or branch centre*; both sheets and *Production* end with the same four short sentences, a row each (the cut list after its totals). `segments.csv`, `wirelist.csv`, the `<ID>.xlsx` tables workbook and every other CSV and JSON file keep their headings. The independent check of the outputs reads the sheet back and compares every heading, every block heading and every row with its own wording (`verify._build_production`, `verify_production.production_rows`), refuses a tick in *Done (x)*, and checks the new headings, the *Lengths* row, the sentences and the line of *Start here*. **Written files:** every `<ID>_build.xlsx` of every project changes, and no other file. The stored hashes of the written files were made again on purpose for this reason (`docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json`: 45 build workbooks, the only differences of `--check`), and so were the numbers snapshot (`tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json`: 129 build workbooks of 11 scenarios, the only differences) and the golden outputs (`tests/fixtures/outputs/*.json`: 69 build workbook digests; the harness plan goldens are unchanged). A script that reads the sheets of the build workbook by position, or the cut list's length heading, must be updated. `tests/test_production_sheet.py`, `tests/test_production_verify.py`.
- **The harness plan prints over several pages and has a sheet *Production*** (pull request 5 of the design of 4.1.0, #317; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.1_PRODUCTION_SHEET.md` sections 3.1, 5 and 6.2; REQ-OUT-15). The harness sheet of `<ID>_plan.ods` prints on A4 landscape at a fixed scale, the largest whole percent at which its print range is one page wide (61 % for W001 of `flatsat`, where the one page it was fitted to printed at about 22 %), with rows 6 to 9 at the top of every page and a page break before a near block that would not fit in what is left of the page; the print range is as before. A new sheet *Production* after the harness sheet lists one block per cable connector, the far ones and the splices too, so every wire is in two rows, one at each end: where it goes, its part, colour, gauge, length in mm, where the length comes from, the label at this end and its shield groups with their end here and at the far end (the rows of `core/outputs/production.py`, plain values only), at 100 % with its title, length note and headings on every page; while the plan colours by pin code it has a column *Pigtail colour* (owner question P8, the design's recommendation). Every length heading of the plan reads *Length [mm] rear to rear*, and `H9` and row 2 of *Production* say what the lengths are measured from, in four sentences from the project's settings (`lengths.reference_lines`; the words of `reference_text` are those sentences joined). A harness named `Production` gets the sheet `Harness_Production`. The independent check (`core/outputs/verify_plan.py`) states the new sheet, `H9` and the headings again with its own copy (`verify_production.production_rows`, `_length_lines`) and now checks the print set-up of these two sheets: no page fitted to one page, the scale worked out from the column widths as the file states them, the heading rows, the print range and every page break, with its own estimate of the row heights (`out_plan`). The page breaks are an estimate from the column widths and the text lengths: a program can break a long text elsewhere. **Written files:** only the plans change (`export --harness-plan`): every plan gets the new sheet, `H9`, the headings and the print set-up; the plan goldens `tests/fixtures/outputs/harness_plan/` (the digests of the seven projects and the four text dumps) were written again for this reason, and every difference of the dumps is `H9`, a length heading or the new sheet. The plans are not in the output-hash baseline or the numbers snapshot, which stay *identical*, and the digest of the writer's sample in `tests/test_ods.py` stays. New: `tests/test_plan_print.py`.

- **A new label format, `harness_device_connector`, and a new project starts in it** (pull request 6 of the design of 4.1.0, #315; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.1_PRODUCTION_SHEET.md` part B, section 4; owner questions P4, P5 and P9 as recommended; REQ-LABEL-02). The setting `model.label_format` gets the value `harness_device_connector`: a cable connector's label is the harness, the device and the name the device gives its connector, `W001:MTQ1:J01` (a loose end `W009:W009-P2 open end`; a connector that plugs into nothing, or into no connector of the project, `W001:W001-P3`; a splice has no label, as in every format), and a wire's labels are the standard text. The label a person gave a connector is not part of it, so that the bench can rely on the form; a template field `{mates_name}` (the name of the device connector, `J01`) lets `custom` print both, `{name} {harness}:{device}:{mates_name}`. Two plugs of one harness on one device connector (a harness drawn by hand) read the same in this format, so Generate warns `label_not_unique` for it as for `custom`, now also for the harnesses made by hand. In the harness plan, while the format is in force, each `CON_…` sheet has *Connector Name* in `A4`/`B4` and the header of a near block reads it, `[W001:MTQ1:J01]`, by a formula over `B4` in place of `B2`; in every other format the plan is as before. `explain DIR W001-P1` names the device and the connector name and says that the person's label is not printed. The independent verifier words the format, the field and the plan's cells again on its own (`core/outputs/verify_labels.py`, `verify_plan.py`). **A new project starts in it:** `new FOLDER`, `new FOLDER --template blank` (the blank example gains `config/model.json`), the editor's *New project*, `create_project` of the scripting interface and the empty project of `tools.usability_setup` write `config/model.json` with `"label_format": "harness_device_connector"`, not as a placeholder. The default stays `standard`, what a file that names no format is read as, so **existing projects keep their labels** byte for byte, and so do projects made from the other templates; no example, fixture or golden names the format, and the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the golden outputs are unchanged. **A 4.0.x tool refuses a project in the new format:** `validate` and `generate` report `config_invalid` (*label_format must be one of standard, compact, descriptive, custom*) and Generate does not save, and `export` writes the standard labels; a template with `{mates_name}` is refused the same way. So update the whole team at the same time, before anyone starts a new project with 4.1.0. No key is new and the project format does not change. `tests/test_new_project_labels.py`; `tests/test_label_format_outputs.py` now exports every example in every format, the new one included.
- **The end-to-end test and the documents of 4.1.0** (pull request 7 of the design of 4.1.0, #319; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.1_PRODUCTION_SHEET.md` sections 9, 10 and 14; REQ-OUT-13, REQ-OUT-14). `tests/test_e2e_410.py` walks the release through the command line: `new FOLDER` (whose `config/model.json` names `harness_device_connector`), two devices from their pinouts (`import-pinout`), an RS-422 interface (two shielded pairs; through the plan the editor runs, as no command adds an interface), `generate`, a segment length (`import-lengths`) and a length a person sets (`override`), a cable drawn from a port and left with a loose end, `export --harness-plan` and `verify --outputs` with 0 errors; then it reads the written files back: the *Production* sheet of the build workbook (both ends of every wire, the label at each end, the shield group with its ends, *Length from*), the *Lengths* row of *Start here*, the heading *Length (m), rear to rear* of *Cut list*, the labels `W001:DEV1:J01` and `W002:W002-P2 open end` in `labels.csv`, the plan's *Production* sheet with the same rows in mm, its print set-up and `B4` of a `CON_…` sheet with the formula of the near header; a second export is byte-identical, and an exported `flatsat` keeps the `standard` labels. The documents of section 10: the release notes `docs/releases/4.1.0.md`; the requirements REQ-OUT-13 (the Production sheet in both files, checked by the verifier) and REQ-OUT-14 (every length of a document says its reference) in `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` and `compliance/traceability.csv`; in `docs/OUTPUTS.md` what the lengths of the tables mean; `docs/models/wire-length.md` (*What the written files say*, with *Length from*), `docs/models/shields.md` (which connector is end A), `docs/GLOSSARY.md` (*End A of a shield*, *Production sheet*), `docs/user-manual/enter-the-build-data.md` (the position of a label accessory in the block heading); the status of the design and of `docs/ait/ELEC_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`; the in-app guide made again. No program code changes, and no written file: the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the golden outputs are as before.

### Fixed

- **The wire list and the cut list name every shield group of a wire** (pull request 2 of the design of 4.1.0, #311; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.1_PRODUCTION_SHEET.md` section 15, owner question P7). A wire of a twisted pair inside an overall shield is in two groups, but the column *Shield group* of `wirelist.csv`, of the *Wire list* sheet of `<ID>.xlsx` and of the *Cut list* of the build workbook showed only the last one, and the editor's wire table showed the same; the independent check of the outputs used the same rule, so it could not see the loss. Now the column holds every group in natural ID order, joined by `;` (`W002-S2;W002-S3`), and the check words it on its own (`verify._shield_groups`). A wire in one group keeps its one ID. **Written files:** only the three files of a harness with a wire in two groups change (14 wires of W002 and 6 of W004 of `flatsat`; harnesses of `small-satellite`); the output-hash baseline (`docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json`, 36 files), the numbers snapshot (105 files of its scenarios) and the goldens `loose_ends`, `sat15` and `sat15_full` were written again for this reason, and every difference is one of those three files. The harness plan already listed every group (its *Shielding* column) and does not change. New: `tests/test_shield_group_columns.py`.
- **A wire locked by an override of its gauge, part or colour is no longer said to have a *locked* length** (a fault of pull request 1 of the design of 4.1.0, found by its end-to-end test; #318). Generate gives such a wire the length of its path again, so `lengths.length_origin` now says *locked* only for a length Generate keeps: a locked wire with no override, or an override of `length_m` (`lengths.length_is_kept`); the independent verifier's copy (`verify_production._length_origin`) says the same. Nothing writes the origin yet, so no written file changes. `tests/test_length_reference.py`.

### Changed

- **Records for 4.1.0.** The version is 4.1.0 (decision D-257). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 4.0.1 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it (the build workbooks, the plans and the wire lists changed with pull requests 311, 314 and 317, each with its own renewal). `4.1.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.4.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 4.1.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 4.0.1, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_4.0.1_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 4.0.1, and they differ from the 4.0.1 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 4.1.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the designs of 3.0.0, 4.0.0 and 4.1.0, the plan for the engineer's feedback, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 4.0.1 say that 4.1.0 is prepared, and decision D-256, which the design proposed, is recorded. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

## 4.0.1 (2026-11-20; not a statement of compliance)

Version 4.0.1 is a **patch release** of 4.0.0 and the **latest full release**. It fixes one thing (pull request 306): **`export` passes its own self-check in every label format.** Since 1.15.0 (D-198), a project whose labels are in the format `compact`, `descriptive` or `custom` (the setting `model.label_format`) could not be exported: the independent check of the outputs knew only the standard text of a label, so `export` stopped with `out_labels` and `out_build` and wrote nothing, and `verify DIR --outputs` refused such a set the same way. In 4.0.1 the check words the label of a wire and of a cable connector for the format in force, so such a project is exported, and a wrong label text is still refused. **No format change:** 4.0.1 writes format 4 as 4.0.0 does, and format 4 stays frozen. Every file that 4.0.0 wrote has the same content with 4.0.1, apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it; the command line and the scripting interface are unchanged. **How to upgrade from 4.0.0:** install 4.0.1 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. Each version writes its number into `project.json` and the generation record, so update the whole team at the same time. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 4.0.1, because it compares them with the version that runs. From 3.x or older, read the notes of 4.0.0 first: a format 3 project is upgraded once to format 4. **Known limits:** the known limits of 4.0.0 stand (the row layout: no datasheet was read, the face rule takes the rows as centred on each other, Generate does not read the rows and no face is drawn; nobody has looked at the new screens of 4.0.0 on a real display; the Windows packages were tried on GitHub's runners only). **Not done:** the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; and the owner's sign-off. There was no release candidate first (D-255). `4.0.1` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, the version with which the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot were written, so they stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0; decision D-255).

### Fixed

- **`export` passes its self-check in every label format** (pull request 306). Since 1.15.0 (D-198), a project whose labels are in the format `compact`, `descriptive` or `custom` (`model.label_format`) could not be exported: the independent check of the outputs knew only the standard text of a label, so `export` stopped with `out_labels` (the labels table) and `out_build` (the Labels sheet of the build workbook) and wrote nothing; `verify DIR --outputs` refused such a set the same way. The check now words the label of a wire and of a cable connector for the format in force and fills a template in with its own copy of the template rules (`core/outputs/verify.py`; it still imports neither `core/label_format.py` nor `tables.py`, and a test compares the copy with the core's). A wrong label text is refused in every format, also the standard text where another format is in force. The labels table and the Labels sheet are the only outputs that carry the texts; the harness plan, the drawings and `system/export.json` do not. Nothing changes in a written file: the files of a project in the standard format are the same bytes. `tests/test_label_format_outputs.py`.

### Changed

- **Records for 4.0.1.** The version is 4.0.1 (decision D-255). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 4.0.0 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `4.0.1` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.4.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 4.0.1 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 4.0.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_4.0.0_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 4.0.0, and they differ from the 4.0.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 4.0.1. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the designs of 3.0.0 and 4.0.0, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 4.0.0 say that 4.0.1 is prepared. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

## 4.0.0 (2026-11-20; not a statement of compliance)

Version 4.0.0 is a **major release** and the **latest full release**. It is the first release that writes **format 4**, a new version of the project file format (decision D-251; the release, D-254); that is why the number is 4.0.0. It brings the owner's answer of 2026-11-19 (D-250): **the row layout of a connector.** A connector part, or a device connector where it differs from its part, can hold the rows of pins on its face, for example a 21-pin Micro-D with the rows 1–11 and 12–21 (pull request 294). `check` and `validate` compare the rows with the pins, and the rule `power-return-adjacent` reads the face of a connector with rows (pull request 295); the pinouts, `export-pinout` and the harness plan show the row of each pin, and `system/export.json` the rows (pull request 296); `pin-rows` and `import-pinout` set them on the command line (pull request 297), and **Edit rows…** in the Devices tab and the Inspector in the editor (pull request 298); pull request 300 adds the end-to-end walk and the documents. A project without rows gives the same findings and writes the same files as with 3.1.0, apart from the version stamp, the model hash that includes it, `system/export.json` and one more sentence in the advice of the rule `power-return-adjacent` (*On a connector with a row layout, a neighbour in the next row counts too; …*). **The older names of 3.0.x are gone** (owner answer L4; pull request 299): `system/export.json` has `format_version` 2 and no `revision` beside `version`, and `log --json` has no `revision`. For extensions this is the one breaking change: the expression field `harness.revision` is removed, so a rule or a table that names it is refused; use `harness.version`, which holds the same value. **Format 4 is frozen with this release:** from the tag `v4.0.0` it does not change any more, and a later change of the file format is a new format with its own upgrade. **How to upgrade from 3.x:** install 4.0.0 over it, then upgrade each project once: answer **Upgrade** when the editor asks, or run `harness-design-studio migrate DIR --dry-run` to look first and then `harness-design-studio migrate DIR`. The step writes the number 4 in `project.json` and nothing else, keeps the original files in `.migration-backup-v3/` and checks itself; it changes no model hash. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once afterwards, because `system/export.json` changed. After the upgrade, versions 3.x open the project read-only, and once a part or a connector has rows they set it aside as a damaged file and refuse `generate`, `export` and `drc`; so the whole team updates at the same time, and the main Git branch is upgraded first. A project of 2.x or older goes through every step in one upgrade. A script that reads the written files or the output of the commands finds every new key, column and command and every dropped name in the release notes (`docs/releases/4.0.0.md`, *For scripts*); the scripting interface only adds (`API_VERSION` stays 1). The guides are [Pin rows of a connector](docs/user-manual/pin-rows-of-a-connector.md) and [Upgrade a project](docs/user-manual/upgrade-a-project.md#8-from-format-3-to-format-4-400). **Known limits:** nobody has looked at the new screens on a real display (they were tested offscreen); no datasheet was read, so the rows in the documents are examples of the form; the rule takes the rows as centred on each other and does not judge pins of different rings; Generate does not read the rows (owner answer L2), and no face is drawn; the starter parts and the example projects have no rows (L3); the Windows packages were tried on GitHub's runners only. **Not done:** the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks), the Windows packages on a real computer, and the owner's sign-off. There was no release candidate first (D-254). `4.0.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, the version with which pull request 299 wrote the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot again for `system/export.json` (`BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0), so they are not written again.

### Added

- **Format 4: the row layout of a connector** (decisions D-250 and D-251, pull request 1 of 4.0.0, #294; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.0_ROW_LAYOUT.md` sections 3 to 5). A connector part (`library/connector.json`) and a box connector (`physical/connectors/`) may hold `row_layout`: the rows of the insert from the top of the face, each the pin names in order along the row (a 21-pin Micro-D: `{"rows": [["1", …, "11"], ["12", …, "21"]]}`), and `rings: true` for the rings of a circular insert. Both are left out of the files while unset. A file that breaks a rule of a layout (an empty row, a pin twice, more than 64 rows or 1000 names, more names than the part has pins, a layout on a part that is not a connector, an own layout on a cable connector, a name that is not a pin of the connector) sets the part or the connector aside with the reason. Renaming or deleting a pin of a box connector keeps its own layout in step, in the same undo step; a part's pin count cannot go below the names of its layout. `core/row_layout.py` works out the layout of a connector (its own, else its part's, else the box connector it plugs into) and reads and writes the short text form `1–11 / 12–21`. The checks, the rule, the outputs, the command line and the editor that read a layout are in the entries below. A device library file is written as version 3 and a subsystem file as version 2 only when a box connector or a part in it has a layout; the other files keep their version. See [the file format](docs/FILE_FORMAT.md#format-4-the-row-layout-of-a-connector-400).

- **The row layout is checked, judged and edited in the core** (decision D-251, pull request 2 of 4.0.0, #295; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.0_ROW_LAYOUT.md` sections 4.3, 8 and 10; owner answer L1). `check` and `validate` report three new codes, none of them an error: `row_layout_pin_missing` (a warning: the rows of a part, or of the device connector a cable connector plugs into, name a pin the connector does not have), `row_layout_pins_outside` (a note: pins in no row, judged in the list of pins) and `row_layout_mate_differs` (a warning: two connectors that mate, or two parts linked by their mating part, hold other pins in a row; the order inside a row is not compared, since a mating face is seen mirrored); the two warnings are also findings of `model-inconsistent`. The rule **`power-return-adjacent` reads the face** of a connector with a row layout: two power or ground pins that are both in rows count the free contacts between them along the row (the shorter way round a ring), and two pins of rows next to each other are neighbours with no contact between when their places differ by one place or less, the rows taken as centred on each other (*J1: +28V (pin 1) and RTN (pin 12) are neighbours on the face of the connector (rows 1 and 2), with no unassigned contact between them*); a pair with a pin in no row is judged in the list of pins as before; pins of different rings are not judged and `unchecked-config` names them for each connector. The rule ID, its setting `generation.power_return_gap_pins` and the count of 39 rules stay; its help says that a neighbour in the next row counts too and that Generate does not read the rows. **A project without a row layout gives the same findings as before**: the DRC golden and the output-hash baseline are unchanged, and a test compares the rule with a word-for-word copy of the rule of 3.0.0 on generated projects. The plans `plan_set_row_layout`, `plan_clear_row_layout` and `plan_share_with_part` (`core/row_layout_edit.py`) are what the editor and `pin-rows` call: one undo step each, journaled on save, refused with the reason when a layout breaks a rule of the files; a release does not lock the rows of a part or of a box connector. **For scripts:** `harness_design_studio.api` adds `row_layout_of`, `set_row_layout` and `clear_row_layout` and the result type `RowLayoutRecord`; `API_VERSION` stays 1. The assumptions A-RETURN-GAP and A-PIN-ORDER, the explanation of the rule (`explain`), `docs/RULES.md` and the pages of `docs/models/` say how neighbours are read. REQ-PIN-06.

- **The row layout shows in the written outputs** (decision D-251, pull request 3 of 4.0.0, #296; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.0_ROW_LAYOUT.md` section 7).
