Where a connector has a row layout, `system/box_pinouts.csv` (and the Devices tab's pinout table) gets a last column *Pin row* with the row of each pin (empty for a pin in no row), and so does the pinout table of a harness with such a connector (`pinouts.csv` and its sheet of `<ID>.xlsx`; a cable connector has its part's rows, else those of the device connector it plugs into); the rows stay in pin order. `export-pinout` writes the column while a connector of the device has a layout, and `import-pinout` reads it back as no change. In the harness plan the `CON_…` sheet of such a device connector has *Pin row* in column F, and a near block on it lists the pins row by row in the order of the layout, then the pins in no row, with *Pin row* in column A copying column F. `system/export.json` carries `row_layout` of the parts and device connectors that have one (an added key; its `format_version` is 2 for the older names that went, under *Removed*). `verify --outputs` reads the rows again on its own and refuses a wrong row, a missing or extra column and a wrong order. A project without a layout writes the same bytes as before (the output-hash baseline and every golden set are unchanged); the new golden set is `tests/fixtures/outputs/row_layout*`.

- **The row layout on the command line and in the imports** (decision D-251, pull request 4 of 4.0.0, #297; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.0_ROW_LAYOUT.md` sections 5.5, 6 and 10). A new command `pin-rows DIR (--part ID | --connector ID)` shows the rows of a connector part or of a connector and where they come from (*own rows*, *from part …*, or, for a connector of a harness, read only, *from DEV2-J1, the device connector it plugs into*), and sets them through the same plans as the scripting interface: `--set "1–11 / 12–21"` (the short form, `(rings)` at the end for a circular insert), `--lengths 11,10`, `--clear`, `--rings`/`--no-rings`, and `--to-part`, which moves the own rows of a device connector to its part and clears the equal own rows of the other connectors of that part. Each change is one step, recorded in the change journal when the project keeps one; a pin twice, a name that is not a pin of the connector or more names than the part has pins are refused with the reason, and a read-only project refuses it. `--json` prints `ok`, `changed`, `object`, `kind`, `rows`, `rings`, `source`, `source_id` and `text` (`docs/CLI.md`). `import-pinout` reads an optional *Pin row* column (also *Row* or *Contact row*): rows that differ from the ones a connector has become its own rows, keeping the ring mark, the import record lists them under *Row layouts set* with a note when the part has no rows (*the rows are stored on J1 only; …, or run `pin-rows --connector J1 --to-part`*), a gap in the numbers is closed up and noted, a cell that is not a whole number from 1 refuses its row, and its document lists the connectors in `row_layouts`; a sheet without the column imports as before. `save-device`, `add-device`, `update-device`, `export-devices`/`import-devices` and the subsystem file carry the rows of the parts and of the device connectors (file versions 3 and 2 while they hold rows), and `update-device` shows a change of a connector's own rows in its diff, in the short form. `explain` of a connector names its rows and where they come from, while it has rows.

- **The row layout in the editor** (decision D-251, pull request 5 of 4.0.0, #298; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.0_ROW_LAYOUT.md` section 9). In the Devices tab the line of the pin table says *Pins of J1 Rows: …*: the rows in the short text form and where they come from (*1–11 / 12–21, from part EX-MICROD-21-F*, *own rows*, or *none*; cut when the window is narrow, the whole text as the tooltip), with **Edit rows…** before **Add pin**; the pin table has a column **Pin row** while the connector has a row layout. So that the pin table keeps at least four rows at 1440 x 900, the count of the pins and the notes share one line under the table, and *Source version* sits beside *Source* on the connector card. The window **Pin rows of J1** takes one row per line (`1–11`, `12–21`), **Row lengths** with **Fill** (`11, 10`), the check box **Rings of a circular insert**, and says a mistake on a line under the fields while **Set rows** stays off (*the row layout names pin 22; J1 has no pin 22*, *pin 7 is in row 1 and in row 2*); **Use for every connector of this part** moves the rows to the part and clears the equal own rows of its other connectors, **Back to the part's rows** clears the connector's own rows, **Clear** empties the box (Set rows then removes the rows). The Inspector of a connector part shows its rows with **Edit rows…** (the same window, which names the connectors of the part that lack a pin of the rows); the Inspector of a cable connector shows the rows it takes and where from, read-only. The Problems list shows the three checks of the row layouts: the two warnings, and the note *pins in no row* as a row of severity Info; a click goes to the connector, and the row menu has **Edit the pin rows of …**. Every change is one undo step through the plans of the core, journaled on save. Pictures `docs/ux/qt/29-devices-pin-rows.png` and `30-pin-rows-dialog.png`.

- **The end-to-end test of 4.0.0 and the documents** (decision D-251, pull request 6 of 4.0.0, #300; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.0_ROW_LAYOUT.md` section 14). `tests/test_e2e_400.py` walks the whole path through the command line on example data: a project as 3.0.x saved it (the sample `mini3`, generated, in format 3) refused by `generate` until `migrate --dry-run` names `project.json` only and `migrate` writes format 4 and changes no other file; its export without a layout is the golden set of `mini3` (which differs from the set of 3.1.0 in `system/export.json` only), and that file is the one of 3.0.0, byte for byte, once `revision` is put back, `format_version` 1 read and the version and the model hash read as 3.0.0's; `pin-rows --lengths` on a part (`row_layout_pin_missing`), `--set` on the part and own rows on a device connector (`row_layout_mate_differs`); `drc` with `power_return_gap_pins` judging the face (a pair across the rows warned, a list pair two rows apart no longer); `export-pinout` read back by `import-pinout` as no change, and a sheet with *Pin row* for another connector (`row_layout_pins_outside`); `explain`; `export --harness-plan --drawings` with the *Pin row* column, the plan's column F and the near block row by row; `verify --outputs` with 0 errors; a second export byte-identical. About 2 s, so it is not marked slow. The user manual has a new page [Pin rows of a connector](docs/user-manual/pin-rows-of-a-connector.md) (the rows on a part and on a device connector, the editor, `pin-rows`, the *Pin row* column of the sheets, the three checks, the rule on the face, the written files, what 3.0.0 does with a project that has rows), and [Upgrade a project](docs/user-manual/upgrade-a-project.md#8-from-format-3-to-format-4-400) the step from format 3 to format 4 with the output of 3.0.0 on an upgraded project; the in-app guide (the Devices tab's *Pin rows*, `migrate`), `docs/OUTPUTS.md` (format 4, the *Pin row* column, the plan's column F and order, `row_layout` in `export.json`, the section *A row layout*) and the glossary (*Row layout*, *Pin row*) follow. The release notes are `docs/releases/4.0.0.md`, with the section *For scripts*.

### Changed

- **Projects are saved in format 4; a format 3 project is upgraded only by `migrate` or the editor's question** (decision D-251, pull request 1 of 4.0.0, #294). The step from 3 to 4 writes the number in `project.json` and nothing else, so it changes no model hash, no stamp and no written file (the safety net checks the same hash, findings, baselines and full export before it keeps the upgrade). A format 2 or format 1 project goes through every step in one upgrade, as before. **Older tools open a format 4 project read-only**: a 3.x tool says the project was saved by a newer version and changes nothing, so the whole team upgrades together. `merge-fix` also rewrites the keys and letters of format 2 that a merge brought into a format 3 project, so that `migrate` can then upgrade it.
- **Records for 4.0.0.** The version is 4.0.0 (decision D-254). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those before only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `4.0.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*: pull request 299 wrote both again for `system/export.json` with `BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0 ([`docs/changes.md`](docs/changes.md), 2026-11-19), and they are not written again. The inventories and SBOMs of 4.0.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 3.1.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_3.1.0_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 3.1.0 (the row layout uses the standard library, pydantic and PySide6 only), and they differ from the 3.1.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 4.0.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the designs of 3.0.0 and 4.0.0, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 3.1.0 say that 4.0.0 is prepared. The user manual, the file format page and the release notes say *3.x* where they said that 3.0.0 sets a part or a connector with rows aside: 3.1.0 does the same, and its `drc` refuses such a project too. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is as the pull requests of 4.0.0 left it.

### Removed

- **The older names that 3.0.x kept for scripts are gone; `system/export.json` has `format_version` 2** (decision D-251, owner answer L4 of 2026-11-19, pull request R of 4.0.0, #299; `docs/ait/DESIGN_4.0_ROW_LAYOUT.md` section 11). A breaking change for scripts. 3.0.0 renamed *revision* to *version* and kept the old name beside the new one *through 3.0.x* (D-242, Q29). In 4.0.0: each harness of `system/export.json` has `version` and no longer `revision`, and the file's `format_version` is 2 (it was 1); each entry of `log --json` has `version` and no longer `revision`; the expression field `harness.revision` of the extensions is removed, so a rule or table that names it is refused with *unknown field 'harness.revision' (did you mean harness.version?)*. A script reads `version`, which holds the same value. `verify --outputs` reads an `export.json` written by 3.0.x as *does not match the design* until the project is exported again. The command `revise` stays as the older name of `new-version` (Q30), and `diff --from` still reads an old letter. Only `system/export.json` changes in the written files: the output-hash baseline (`docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json`, 4 entries), the numbers snapshot (`tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json`, 11 entries) and the output goldens (`tests/fixtures/outputs/`) were written again once, with the tools, for that reason (`docs/changes.md`). The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) had no older name; `API_VERSION` stays 1.

## 3.1.0 (2026-11-19; not a statement of compliance)

Version 3.1.0 is a **minor release** of 3.0.1 and the **latest full release**. It adds one thing (decision D-250, the owner's answer of 2026-11-19; pull request 290): **the search boxes of the harness plan.** The harness sheet of each plan has the two boxes of the users' template, in `I2:I3` over Function and in `M2:M3` over Shielding; a text typed into a box shows in red bold every cell of that column, in the rows of the near connectors, that contains it, without regard to case. The boxes use `SEARCH` and `ISNUMBER` in a conditional format, and every `.ods` the tool writes turns regular expressions and wildcards off, so LibreOffice reads a search text as typed. It is a minor release because it adds a function to a written file; it changes no command, option, setting or field. **No format change:** 3.1.0 writes format 3 as 3.0.1 does, and format 3 stays frozen. The harness plans (written with `--harness-plan` only) get the boxes, their formats, one line of the Notes sheet and the calculation settings; every other written file has the same content as with 3.0.1, apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it. The command line and the scripting interface are unchanged. **How to upgrade from 3.0.x:** install 3.1.0 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. Each version writes its number into `project.json` and the generation record, so update the whole team at the same time. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 3.1.0: it compares them with the version that runs, and a plan written by 3.0.x has no search boxes and no calculation settings, so 3.1.0 reports it (`out_plan`, `out_plan_formula`) until it is written again; a 3.0.x tool in turn reports the formats of a plan of 3.1.0 as outside its list of functions. From 2.x or older, read the notes of 3.0.0 first: a format 2 project is upgraded once to format 3. **Known limits:** Excel reads `*`, `?` and `~` in a search text as wildcards (it ignores the calculation settings); a plan saved with a typed search text is a changed plan, which `verify --outputs` reports, so search in a copy; *Used in Row* of a far pin inside a joined text still uses `MATCH`; nobody has looked at the boxes in LibreOffice or Excel on a real display (a test checks LibreOffice's values over the formula where LibreOffice is installed); the known limits of 3.0.1 stand (the tool never reads a harness plan back, and an export overwrites it; Generate makes no splice; the Windows packages were tried on GitHub's runners only). **Not done:** the row layout of a connector (format 4, the next planned release, 4.0.0; its design is D-251, and nothing of it is in 3.1.0); the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; and the owner's sign-off. There was no release candidate first (D-253). `3.1.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, the version with which the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot were written for format 3, so they stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0; decision D-253).

### Added

- **The search boxes of the harness plan** (the owner's decision D-250 of 2026-11-19; #290; `docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md` section 25). The harness sheet of each plan has the two search boxes of the users' template: *Column Search String* and `#` in `I2:I3` over Function and `M2:M3` over Shielding. A text typed into a box shows in red bold every cell of that column in the rows of the near connectors that contains it, without regard to case; an empty box marks nothing. The conditional format is `IF([.$I$3]="";0;ISNUMBER(SEARCH([.$I$3];[.I34])))`: `ISNUMBER` and `SEARCH` join the closed list of functions, in a conditional format only, in the writer, the verifier (`verify --outputs`) and the tests' evaluator; a cell formula with either is still refused. Every `.ods` the tool writes now turns regular expressions and wildcards off (`table:calculation-settings`), so LibreOffice reads a search text such as `TX+` as typed; Excel reads `*`, `?` and `~` as wildcards. The verifier reports a plan without that setting as `out_plan_formula`. The Notes sheet says how to search. Only the plans change (written with `--harness-plan`); no format change, and the output-hash baseline is unchanged.

### Changed

- **Records for 3.1.0.** The version is 3.1.0 (decision D-253). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 3.0.1 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it (the golden plans got the search boxes with pull request 290). `3.1.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.4.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 3.1.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 3.0.1, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_3.0.1_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 3.0.1, and they differ from the 3.0.1 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 3.1.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the status line of the design of 3.0.0, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 3.0.1 say that 3.1.0 is prepared. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

## 3.0.1 (2026-11-19; not a statement of compliance)

Version 3.0.1 is a **patch release** of 3.0.0 and the **latest full release**. It fixes one thing (decision D-250, the owner's answer of 2026-11-19; pull request 291): **the editor opens nothing with the system's default program.** 3.0.0 copied the links of parts and sources to the clipboard, but **F1** still handed the user guide to the system, which started a browser, and *Open* in the Export window handed an exported file to the program the system uses for it. In 3.0.1 **Help > User guide** (F1) shows the guide in a window of the program, **Copy path** takes the place of *Open*, and **Reveal in folder** hands only a folder to the file manager. **No format change:** 3.0.1 writes format 3 as 3.0.0 does, and format 3 stays frozen. Every written file has the same content as with 3.0.0, apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it; the command line and the scripting interface are unchanged. **How to upgrade from 3.0.0:** install 3.0.1 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. Each version writes its number into `project.json` and the generation record, so update the whole team at the same time. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 3.0.1, because it compares them with the version that runs. From 2.x or older, read the notes of 3.0.0 first: a format 2 project is upgraded once to format 3. **Known limits:** the guide window shows the guide as Qt's text browser reads it, so the guide's own colours, its table of contents that stays at the top and its width limit do not show; nobody has looked at the guide window or the new *Copy path* on a real display (they were tested offscreen); the known limits of 3.0.0 stand (the tool never reads a harness plan back, and an export overwrites it; the search boxes of the users' template are not written; Generate makes no splice; the Windows packages were tried on GitHub's runners only). **Not done:** the row layout of a connector (format 4, released as 4.0.0, with a design to follow, D-251) and `SEARCH` and `ISNUMBER` for the search boxes of the plan (3.1.0), both answered by the owner on 2026-11-19 (D-250); the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; and the owner's sign-off. There was no release candidate first (D-252). `3.0.1` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, the version with which the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot were written for format 3, so they stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0; decision D-252).

### Fixed

- **The editor opens nothing with the system's default program, the user guide included** (decision D-250, the owner's answer of 2026-11-19; #291). In 3.0.0 the editor stopped opening the links of parts and sources in a browser, but **F1** still handed the guide to the system, which started a browser. **Help > User guide** (F1) now shows the guide in a window of the program, beside the editor and moving on its own: the links of its table of contents move within that window, **Back** (Alt+Left) returns and **Contents** (Alt+Home) goes to the top; a web link that leaves the guide is copied to the clipboard and the window says so, and any other link is not followed. The guide is the same page as before, read by Qt's text browser: headings, lists, tables, code and the anchors show; the guide's own colours, its table of contents that stays at the top and its width limit do not, and the window gives the page the theme's colours instead.

### Changed

- **Copy path in place of Open, in the Export window and in Exported files** (decision D-250, #291). *Open* handed an exported file to the program your system uses for it; it is removed. **Copy path** (also Enter and a double-click on a file) puts the full path of the selected file on the clipboard and says *Path copied to the clipboard*, so that you open it where you choose. The picture of a selected drawing stays. **Reveal in folder** stays and shows only a folder: every place that hands something to the system's file manager (*Reveal in folder*, *Reveal output folder*, *Open the folder after the export*, *Show folder* after *Export diagram*) goes through `gui/folders.py`, which hands over an existing local folder and refuses a file or a web address (`tests/test_security.py`). No format change, and no written file changes (`tools.output_hashes --check` says *identical*).
- **Records for 3.0.1.** The version is 3.0.1 (decision D-252). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 3.0.0 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `3.0.1` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.4.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 3.0.1 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 3.0.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_3.0.0_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 3.0.0, and they differ from the 3.0.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 3.0.1. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the status lines of the design of 3.0.0, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 3.0.0 say that 3.0.1 is prepared. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

## 3.0.0 (2026-11-18; not a statement of compliance)

Version 3.0.0 is a **major release** and the **latest full release**. It is the first release that writes **format 3**, a new version of the project file format (decision D-242; the release, D-249); that is why the number is 3.0.0. It brings three things, each in format 3: a harness has a **version MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH** (`1.0.0`, `1.1.0`, `2.0.0`) in place of a revision letter, and the screen and every written file say *Version* where they said *Revision* (pull requests 274 and 280; their first part, one order of the revisions, was pull request 272 in 2.4.0); the export can write the **harness plan** of each harness, an `.ods` spreadsheet with formulas in the users' layout, which `verify --outputs` checks cell by cell (pull requests 277, 278, 279, 281, 284 and 283); and a harness you drew can join wires at a **splice** (pull request 282; decision D-243, section 6). The editor opens no browser: it copies the link of a part or of a source to the clipboard (the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, "Never open browser at all"). **Format 3 is frozen with this release:** from the tag `v3.0.0` it does not change any more, and a later change of the file format is a new format with its own upgrade. **How to upgrade from 2.x:** install 3.0.0 over it, then upgrade each project once: answer **Upgrade** when the editor asks, or run `harness-design-studio migrate DIR --dry-run` to look first and then `harness-design-studio migrate DIR`. The upgrade gives each revision letter the version of its place (A is `1.0.0`, B is `2.0.0`), keeps the original files in `.migration-backup-v2/`, checks itself and writes nothing if anything else would change; a revision that is not a letter is given with `migrate --set-version`. Unlike the step to format 2 it changes the model hash once, so export the outputs once afterwards. After the upgrade, versions 2.x open the project read-only, so the whole team updates at the same time; upgrade on the main Git branch first. A project of 1.17.1 or older goes through format 2 to format 3 in one step. A script that reads the written files or the output of the commands finds every renamed heading, key, file and command in the release notes (`docs/releases/3.0.0.md`, *For scripts*); the scripting interface only adds (`API_VERSION` stays 1). The guides are [Upgrade a project](docs/user-manual/upgrade-a-project.md#7-from-format-2-to-format-3-300) and [Versions](docs/user-manual/versions.md). **Known limits:** nobody has looked at the new screens on a real display (they were tested offscreen); the tool never reads a harness plan back, and an export overwrites it; the search boxes of the users' template are not written; Generate makes no splice, and a splice is placed in the editor only; the Windows packages were tried on GitHub's runners only. **Not done:** the owner's open questions: whether the Help guide (F1, which opens the local `index.html` through the system's opener) and the *Open* of the Export window should also stop using the system's opener; the row layout of a connector (for example Micro-D rows 1–11 and 12–21), which is not in format 3 and could come with a later format; `SEARCH` and `ISNUMBER` for the search boxes of the plan. Also not done: the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks), and the owner's sign-off. There was no release candidate first (D-249). `3.0.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, the version with which pull request 274 wrote the stored hashes again for format 3 (`BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0), so they are not written again.

### Added

- **Format 3: harness versions MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH and the fields of the harness plan** (decision D-242, pull request 1 of 3.0.0, #274; `docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md` sections 4, 5 and 12). A harness now has a version `1.0.0`, `1.1.0`, `2.0.0` in place of a revision letter; a new harness starts at `1.0.0`. `harness-design-studio new-version DIR HARNESS --kind major|minor|patch` starts the next version of a released harness from the released one (from `1.4.2`: `2.0.0`, `1.5.0` or `1.4.3`); a version that is not higher than every baseline of the harness is refused. The *New version* dialog asks for the kind, none chosen at the start. Format 3 also holds, all left out of the files while unused: a pin's `level`, the pin role `signal_ground`, a part's web `link`, a box connector's `source_doc`, `source_version` and `source_link`, the document data of the plans (`meta.document`) and the optional `config/harness_plan.json` (colour rule, colour codes, notes). The harness plan shows them (see *The harness plan, one `.ods` per harness*). A device library file is written as version 2 only when a device uses one of the new fields. See [the file format](docs/FILE_FORMAT.md#format-3-versions-and-the-fields-of-the-harness-plan-300).
- **`migrate --set-version HARNESS:OLD=VERSION`** (pull request 1 of 3.0.0, #274) gives the version of an old revision that the upgrade cannot map by itself (anything but an upper-case letter or a version); it can be given more than once.
- **The notes on the kind of a new version, and the kind chosen again** (pull request R2 of 3.0.0, #280, D-242; owner questions Q20 to Q22; [Versions](docs/user-manual/versions.md)). `review` and `release`, and their windows, add a note when the version is minor or patch and the change against the last baseline reaches an end that plugs into a device (a cable connector's part, gender, keying or mate, or a wire added, removed or moved at such a connector): *This version changes the pinout at a device (…). The harness plan template calls that an incompatible change.* Choosing major says that the template gives an incompatible harness a new ID instead. Neither note blocks anything; `--json` lists them as `notes`. `new-version` on a draft after a release, before its review, chooses the kind again: the version is worked out again from the released one and the change log gets the event *version changed*; in the editor **New version…** on such a draft opens **Choose the kind of version of W001 again**.
- **`diff --from` and `--to` take an old letter** (R2, #280) and read it as its version by the rule of the upgrade (`B` is `2.0.0`), and say so once; `--json` adds `read_as`.
- **A merge with a branch still in format 2** (R2, #280). The loader names a key `revision`, a letter, the kind `new_revision` or the title block field `revision` in a file of format 3 with `revision_not_version` and the version the letter maps to; `merge-fix` rewrites them (A is `1.0.0`), moves a baseline to its new name, prints each place (`--json`: `versions`) and does not guess a value that is not a letter.
- **Splices** (milestone M6a, #282; `docs/ait/DESIGN_2.3_BRANCHING.md` section 6, decision D-243, owner answers B3 and B8). A splice joins several wires inside a harness you drew, for example a power line from one device to two others. In format 3 it is a connector of the harness with the new role `splice` and a library part of the new category `splice` (`library/splice.json`, written only while the library holds one); each of its pins is a node that joins every wire on it. In the Harness tab, under the routing, the **Splices** list has **Add splice**, **Splice this wire** and **Delete splice** (a node with two wires joins them back), each one undo step; Generate makes no splice. The written outputs of a harness with a splice follow its nets: one continuity test per pair of ends of a net through a splice (`W003-P1.1` to `W003-P2.1 via W003-X1`) and one isolation test per net in place of the tests of its wires, a last column **Via** in the wire list, no label for a splice, the splice part in the BOM, its mass only under the model `full_bom`, a block of its own in the *Connectors* sheet of the build workbook, a node in the drawing and a connector of style `simple` in `wireviz.yaml`. The design rule `pin-shared` and the independent verifier leave the nodes out, the verifier counts an interface per pair of ends of a net, and the output check words every new cell again with its own nets. New integrity checks: `splice_unused`, `splice_one_wire`, `splice_signals_mixed`, `splice_stub` (warnings), `splice_mated`, `splice_in_generated_harness` (errors). `explain DIR W003-X1` shows the nets of a splice, and `routing` lists the splices (the JSON key `splices`). The template `splice-parts-EXAMPLE.csv` holds one example splice part; the tool ships none. A project without a splice keeps every file, hash and output byte for byte.
- **The harness plan, one `.ods` per harness in the users' layout** (decision D-242, pull request 3 of 3.0.0, #278; `docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md` sections 3, 6, 7, 9 and 10). `build_outputs(project, harness_plan=True)` writes `harnesses/<ID>/<ID>_plan.ods` for each harness and lists it in `manifest.json` with its SHA-256; the default is off, so every set written without it is byte for byte as before (`tools.output_hashes --check` says *identical*). Each plan has the sheets *Cover* (the document data of `meta.document`, *to fill in* on amber where it is empty; the Version is the person's plan version, else the harness version), *Change_Register* (the change log of that harness only), the harness sheet (named after the harness: the header with `VERSION: v1.0.0 (draft)` and `[no SGND*]`, the title by formula, the used connectors, cable types and backshells, one block of rows per connector of the near end with the device side and the harness side of every pin, a yellow row for a pin with no wire, the far pins and the far device side, the wires that end on no near pin, the sums of length per cable type divided by the wires per cable, and the reference pinout of each far device connector with *Used in Row*), *Notes* (the colour codes the harness uses, the project's note lines, a legend), one `CON_<connector>` sheet per device connector the harness plugs into (the same in every plan that has it) and *About* (the stamp, the model hash and the notice, also on the Cover and in the footer of every sheet). The near and far ends are split by the rule of section 9 (through splices as well: see *Splices in the harness plan*). Formulas only where section 7.1 says, each with its stored value, from the closed list `IF`, `ISNA`, `VLOOKUP`, `MATCH`, `SUMIF` and `&`, `=`, `/`, `+`; no `INDIRECT`, `OFFSET`, `ADDRESS` or `HYPERLINK`. **The colour rule by pin number** (section 6): with `"colour_rule": "pin_code"` in `config/harness_plan.json`, an end whose connector part is listed in a colour code gets the colour of its pin from the code's table on the *Notes* sheet (`VLOOKUP`, exact match; a pin the code does not list reads *to fill in*); otherwise each end shows the wire's colour. `validate` refuses a part named in two codes, and the plan's settings do not make the harnesses look out of date. **The example colour code** `templates/pigtail-colour-code-EXAMPLE.csv` (pins 1 to 37, from the users' template): an example, not a standard and not a supplier's table, applied only when a person takes it; while it is marked as an example the *Notes* sheet says so. Goldens in `tests/fixtures/outputs/harness_plan/`; `tests/test_harness_plan.py` holds every region, the stored value of every formula against the tests' own evaluator, the bytes, and (with LibreOffice installed) the values a real reader shows.
- **Splices in the harness plan** (decision D-242, pull request 6 of 3.0.0, #284; `docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md` sections 9 and 23). The plan of a harness with splices: the sides rule leaves the splices out and joins two connectors when wires reach both through a splice (`core/nets.py`), so a Y harness through a splice puts its two branches at one end and the trunk at the other. A near row reaches every far pin that its wire reaches through splices: a row that reaches two far connectors fills a pin in each far column, two pins of one far connector are joined by ` + `, and the far cells *Cable / Type*, *Color*, *Direction* and *Function* list the values of each far wire in column order, joined by ` + `; a row through a splice to one far pin shows that far wire's type and colour. *Further wires* lists the wires beyond a splice with the other wires that end on no near pin, so every wire of the harness is in exactly one row and the sums per cable type count both blocks. The independent check (`core/outputs/verify_plan.py`) has its own copy of the rule and finds a wire beyond a splice that is missing and a far pin that is missing. A harness without a splice gets the same plan as before, byte for byte; the plan of the splice fixture is a new golden. `tests/test_plan_splices.py` holds a Y harness through a splice, a ring of three connectors, a harness of one connector and a splice on a branch point's leg, and the random harnesses of the agreement test now have splices.
- **The independent check of the harness plans** (decision D-242, pull request 4 of 3.0.0, #279; `docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md` section 7.5). `verify --outputs` (and the check an export makes of its own files) now reads each plan that `manifest.json` lists, with `core/outputs/verify_plan.py`, which imports neither the `.ods` writer nor the plan's layout: it works out every sheet and cell from the project with its own copies of the sides rule, the Direction words, the mirrored directions and the colour lookup. It reports `out_plan_package` for the package (the entry order with `mimetype` first, every entry stored with the fixed date, `create_system` 3 and the mode 644, the manifest listing exactly the entries, `meta.xml` with the title and the generator) and for an XML entry with a DOCTYPE or entity declaration, which is refused before it is parsed; `out_plan` for a sheet missing, extra or out of order, a cell whose value type, value, shown text or link differs from the design (named by its sheet and address), a pin row missing, a wire in two rows, a plan of a harness that is no longer in the design, and a harness without its plan in an export that has plans; and `out_plan_formula` for a formula that is not the text the design gives, is outside the closed list (`IF`, `ISNA`, `VLOOKUP` and `MATCH` with exact match, `SUMIF`, `&`, `=`, `/`, `+`, direct references only; `AND` and `OR` only in the conditional format) or does not give its stored value, which a small evaluator of that list computes over the stored cells. The stamp is read from the About sheet (`out_stamp`); a plan changed after the export fails `out_modified` as every file does. At most ten findings of one kind are listed per plan, and the rest are counted. Not checked: the styles, the column widths, the print set-up and how a program shows the file. `tests/test_verify_plan.py` holds the mutation tests of section 11, a plan of every example as written with no error, and the agreement of the verifier's copies with the core's on random harnesses (Hypothesis). A set written without the plans gives the same findings as before.
- **The harness plans on the command line, and the imports of the users' sheets** (decision D-242, pull request 5 of 3.0.0, #281; `docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md` sections 4, 6 and 10). `harness-design-studio export DIR --harness-plan` writes the plan of each harness, `harnesses/<ID>/<ID>_plan.ods`, and `--json` lists them under `harness_plans`; an export without the option into a folder whose last export had the plans removes them and says so (`harness_plans_removed`), as for the drawings, and the re-export at a release keeps the choice (`harness_plan_exported`). `api.export(..., harness_plan=True)` does the same; the result type `ExportResult` gets the fields `harness_plans` and `harness_plans_removed` (an addition: `API_VERSION` stays 1). The new command `harness-design-studio document DIR [--harness ID] [--set FIELD=VALUE] [--contact LABEL=VALUE] [--clear FIELD] [--json]` shows and sets what the Cover of the plans shows (the system code, title, company and contact lines of the project; the number, version, release and title of one harness's plan), as one undo step recorded in the change journal (`core/document.py`, which the editor will use too). `import-pinout` reads the users' headings and words: *Description* is the pin's function, *Level* its level, the Direction words `PWR`, `PWR-RET`, `GND`, `SGND`, `SHIELD` and `SPARE` set the role (`IN`, `OUT` and `IN/OUT` the direction), any other word such as `TEST` sets neither and is listed in the import record under the converted values (it stopped the import before), *Pinout* is read as the pin with `--column pin=Pinout`, and *Source* (also *ICD*), *Source version* and *Source link* say where the pinout of a connector comes from. `import-parts` reads *Link* (also *URL*), a web link only. `export-pinout` writes *Level*, *Source*, *Source version* and *Source link* while the device uses them, and `import-pinout` reads them back. `check` gives the note `plan_colour_example` for a colour code that is still marked as the example and the warning `plan_colour_missing` for a harness connector with a pin its code does not list, and warns about a plan of the document data whose harness is gone (`plan_document_unknown`); none of them stops generation or an export. Every output written without `--harness-plan` is byte for byte as before (`tools.output_hashes --check` says *identical*). User manual: [Export the harness plans](docs/user-manual/export-the-harness-plans.md).
- **The harness plans in the editor** (decision D-242, pull request 7 of 3.0.0, #283; `docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md` section 10; [Export the harness plans](docs/user-manual/export-the-harness-plans.md#6-in-the-editor)). The Export window has the check box **Include the harness plans (.ods), one per harness**, ticked at the start when the last export into the folder had the plans; an export without them into such a folder removes them and says so, as `export` without `--harness-plan` does, and an export that runs without the window keeps the set of the last export, plans included. **Settings > Document** sets the system code, title, company and contact lines of the Cover (`core/document.py`); **Settings > Harness plan** sets the colour rule, the colour codes (parts, the table of pins and colours with a paste of two columns, the example mark) and the note lines, with **Use the example colour code…** for the connector parts a person ticks, and lists the notes of `check` on the plans (`plan_colour_missing`, `plan_colour_example`, `plan_document_unknown`); `config/harness_plan.json` is made by the first value and removed when every value is back to its default (`core/plan_config.py`), and a change `validate` would refuse is refused there. The Inspector of a harness has **Plan document** (number, plan document version, release, title), the Inspector of a part **Link** with **Copy link**; the Devices tab has the pin column **Level**, the role **Signal ground** and the connector fields **Source**, **Source version** and **Source link**; the library window lists the parts of a device with their links. **The editor opens no browser** (the owner's decision of 2026-11-18, "Never open browser at all"): **Copy link**, the source link's **Copy** and the parts of the library window put the link on the clipboard, and only a link the model accepts (`https://` or `http://`) is copied (`gui/weblinks.py`). Every change is one undo step and goes into the journal. No written file changes (`tools.output_hashes --check` says *identical*).
- **Internal: a writer of OpenDocument spreadsheets** (`core/outputs/ods.py`, decision D-242, pull request 2 of 3.0.0, #277; `docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md` sections 7 and 8). The harness plan is written with it, and nothing else calls it. It turns a small model (a workbook of sheets, rows and cells, styles by value) into the same bytes for the same model: every entry stored, with fixed dates and attributes, `mimetype` first, a manifest of every entry and a `meta.xml` with the title and the generator only. It writes text, numbers without an exponent, OpenFormula formulas with their stored value, spans, links, column widths, the printed page with its header and footer, print ranges and conditional formats, and refuses a formula outside the closed list of the design (`IF`, `ISNA`, `VLOOKUP`, `MATCH`, `SUMIF` and the operators `&`, `=`, `/`, `+`; never `INDIRECT`, `ADDRESS`, `OFFSET`, `HYPERLINK`, a `COM.MICROSOFT.` function or a reference with `!`). No new dependency.
- **The end-to-end test of 3.0.0 and the documents** (decision D-242, pull request 8 of 3.0.0, #285). `tests/test_e2e_300.py` walks the whole path through the command line on example data: a project of 2.x with a release history (the letters A and B) refused by `generate` until `migrate --dry-run` and `migrate` map A to `1.0.0` and B to `2.0.0`; `log --json` with both keys; `diff --from A`; `new-version --kind minor` on the draft, which chooses the kind again (`2.0.0` becomes `1.1.0`); a harness drawn by hand with a splice whose part comes from `splice-parts-EXAMPLE.csv`; `export --harness-plan --drawings`, one plan per harness; `verify --outputs` with 0 errors and every formula of every plan evaluated; the word *Version* in `changelog.csv`, `version_report.md` and `export.json`; a second export byte-identical. It found no fault. The user manual has a new page [Splices](docs/user-manual/splices.md) and a section *A harness with splices* in [Export the harness plans](docs/user-manual/export-the-harness-plans.md#a-harness-with-splices); [Upgrade a project](docs/user-manual/upgrade-a-project.md#7-from-format-2-to-format-3-300) has the step from format 2 to format 3; [`OUTPUTS.md`](docs/OUTPUTS.md#harness-plan) says how the plan follows splices, what the editor's Export window does and what `export.json` holds since 3.0.0; the in-app guide names `migrate --set-version` and the plan of a harness with a splice. The release notes are `docs/releases/3.0.0.md`, with the section *For scripts*.

### Changed

- **Projects are saved in format 3; a format 2 project is upgraded only by `migrate` or the editor's question** (decision D-242, pull request 1 of 3.0.0, #274). The upgrade maps every revision letter to `N.0.0` by its place (`A` is `1.0.0`, `B` is `2.0.0`, `AA` is `27.0.0`) for the harness, its baselines and its change-log entries, renames the keys `revision` to `version` (and the change-log kind `new_revision` to `new_version`, and the title block field `revision` to `version`) and stores each baseline under its new ID; old journal lines keep their words. Unlike the step to format 2 it changes the model hash once, so the outputs exported before it read *out of date*: export once more after the upgrade. Before it writes, the upgrade checks on the files that only the versions change (with each version read as its place in its harness's order: the same design, the same findings, the same released harnesses) and that every version is the mapping of its revision; `migrate --dry-run` prints the mapping and both checks, the migration record and the journal entry name the mapping. **Older tools open a format 3 project read-only**: a 2.x tool says the project was saved by a newer version and changes nothing, so the whole team upgrades together.
- **The word *Version* in the written files** (owner question Q23, pull request 1 of 3.0.0, #274). The drawings say *VERSION* in the title block and `Version 1.0.0  status draft` in the header; *Start here* of the build workbook says `Version 1.0.0 · status …`; `system/changelog.csv` and the *Change log* sheet head the column *Version* and say *new version started*; `system/revision_report.md` is now `system/version_report.md` (*# Version report*), and an export removes the old file; the findings and refusals say *version* (*W001 is released (version 1.0.0); start a new version to change it.*). For scripts: `system/export.json` and `log --json` write `version` and keep `revision` beside it with the same value through 3.0.x; the expression field `harness.revision` stays as the older name of `harness.version`; `revise` stays as the older name of `new-version` and now needs `--kind`. The top bar shows `v1.0.0`.
- **The word *Version* on screen** (pull request R2 of 3.0.0, #280, owner question Q32). The navigation area **Revisions** is **Versions**; the buttons say **New version…** and **Start a new version…**, the dialog **New version of W001**; the state *Revised* is *New version*; the chips of the Harness tab say *Released · v1.1.0*; the Releases panel, the Inspector, the project tree, the lock texts and banners, the tooltip of Undo after a saved release and the Outputs list (*Version report*) say *version*. A draft is shown as a new version when it has a baseline, not by its version number. No written file changes with these words.
- **Records for 3.0.0.** The version is 3.0.0 (decision D-249). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those before only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `3.0.0` has as many characters as `2.4.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*: pull request 274 wrote both again for format 3 with `BASE_VERSION` 2.4.0 ([`docs/changes.md`](docs/changes.md), 2026-11-18), and they are not written again. The inventories and SBOMs of 3.0.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 2.4.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_2.4.0_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 2.4.0 (the `.ods` writer and the check of the plans use the standard library only), and they differ from the 2.4.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 3.0.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the designs of 2.3.0 and 3.0.0, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 2.4.0 say that 3.0.0 is prepared. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is as the pull requests of 3.0.0 left it.

## 2.4.0 (2026-11-18; not a statement of compliance)

Version 2.4.0 is a **minor release** and the **latest full release**. **A new project makes one harness that branches** (decision D-247; the release, D-248; pull request 271): `new FOLDER`, the editor's *New project* with the empty project, the scripting interface's `create_project` and the empty project of the usability kit start with `segmentation.mode` = `per_connector_group`, so a device connector that carries interfaces to several devices gets one plug, and Generate splits the harness at a branch point. **Existing projects keep their mode:** every project names its mode, and nothing switches it; a project in `per_connector_pair` with the plugs of several harnesses on one device connector gets, in the editor after Generate, a notice once with the button **Make one harness that branches**. The default of the setting stays `per_connector_pair`, so a file that names no mode is read as before. 2.4.0 also puts the revisions after Z in order (pull request 272, R1 of 3.0.0, decision D-242): the top bar shows `Z-AA` instead of `AA-Z`. **No format change:** 2.4.0 writes format 2 as 2.3.1 does, and format 2 stays frozen. A project that generates at 2.3.1 generates the same at 2.4.0: its project files are the same apart from the version, and every written file has the same content apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it. The command line and the scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) are unchanged. **How to upgrade from 2.3.x:** install 2.4.0 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. **Update the whole team at the same time:** each version writes its number into `project.json` and the generation record. A 2.3.x tool knows `per_connector_group` (it came with 2.3.0) and groups a project that 2.4.0 started as 2.4.0 does; a 2.2.x, 2.1.x or 2.0.x tool reads the mode as not known, uses `per_connector_pair` and removes a joined draft on its next Generate, so a team with such a tool updates before anyone makes a new project with 2.4.0. A script or a test that makes a project with `new` or `create_project` and counts on one harness per pair of connectors now gets `per_connector_group`: set the mode it needs first (`harness-design-studio settings FOLDER --set segmentation.mode="per_connector_pair"`). The description of the setting `segmentation.mode` now says what a new project starts in, so a project whose config files carry the comments `_about` (`settings DIR --annotate`) gets the warning `config_about_stale` for it in `validate` and `config` until `settings DIR --annotate` writes them again. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 2.4.0, because it compares them with the version that runs. From 2.2.x or older, read the notes of 2.3.0 first. **Known limits:** the notice is in the editor only, once for each project on each computer (the answer is kept in the editor's settings, not in the project); the command line keeps the warning `box_connector_shared`. The limits of 2.3.0 stand: splices, planned for 3.0.0 with format 3; joining in `per_connector_group` only, and not for connectors wired in a ring, chains kept apart or a released harness; no command-line edits of the routing tree. The versions `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` of 3.0.0 are not in use: 2.4.0 only orders the revisions with them in mind. **Not done:** nobody has looked at the notice or the *New project* window of 2.4.0 on a real display (they were tested offscreen); the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; and the owner's sign-off. `2.4.0` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.0.0; decision D-248).

### Changed

- **A new project makes one harness that branches** (decision D-247, the owner's decision of 2026-11-18: "That should be the default actually, because most connectors will be custom and bespoke."; pull request 271). A new project now starts with `segmentation.mode` = `per_connector_group`: `new FOLDER` (the blank project), the editor's *New project* and the scripting interface's `create_project`. So a device connector that carries interfaces to several devices gets one plug, and Generate splits the harness to the far connectors at a branch point: a 21-pin Micro-D with RS-422, RS-485 and CAN to three devices gives one harness with one plug on the Micro-D, one branch point and four segments, where `per_connector_pair` gave three cables with three plugs on it (the warning `box_connector_shared`). A project with no shared connector gets the same harnesses in both modes. **Existing projects keep their mode:** every project names its mode, and nothing switches it. A project in `per_connector_pair` whose device connector has the plugs of several harnesses of Generate gets, in the editor after Generate, a notice once, with the button **Make one harness that branches** (it sets the mode as *Settings > Project* does, one undo step, and offers *Generate again*); closing the notice keeps `per_connector_pair`, and the answer is remembered for the project in the editor's settings, not in the project files. The fix *Use per_connector_group* of the Problems panel stays. The examples with outputs keep their mode, so their written files are unchanged (`first-steps`, `minimal-satellite` and `small-satellite` `per_connector_pair`, `flatsat` `per_zone_pair`). A file that names no mode, or a mode this version does not know, is still read as `per_connector_pair`, the setting's default, so every project groups as before. No format change; the command line and the scripting interface are unchanged.
- **Records for 2.4.0.** The version is 2.4.0 (decision D-248). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 2.3.1 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `2.4.0` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.0.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 2.4.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 2.3.1, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_2.3.1_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 2.3.1, and they differ from the 2.3.1 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 2.4.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 2.3.1 say that 2.4.0 is prepared. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

### Fixed

- **The order of revisions after Z.** The top bar showed the range of the project's revisions as `AA-Z` when one harness was at revision Z and another at AA, because it sorted the letters as text. It now shows `Z-AA`. The list of baselines already used this order; both now use one ordering function, `core/vcs/versions.py`, which also prepares the versions `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` of 3.0.0 (decision D-242, pull request R1 of `docs/ait/DESIGN_3.0_HARNESS_PLAN.md`; pull request 272). No other change on screen or in a written file, and no format change.

## 2.3.1 (2026-11-18; not a statement of compliance)

Version 2.3.1 is a **patch release** of 2.3.0 and the **latest full release**. It brings a new logo (decision D-246; pull request 269, which carries the logo of the draft pull request 258). The owner saw on 2026-11-17 that the old icon was barely visible in the dark mode of Ubuntu: its purple tile has less than 2:1 contrast on a dark panel. The new icon shows what the tool does (a device connector, a bundle of wires that branches to two connectors, a check mark, an orbit and a star), and a lighter purple rim keeps it visible on the dark and light panels of Ubuntu and Windows. **No change of the program's behaviour.** **No format change:** 2.3.1 writes format 2 as 2.3.0 does, and format 2 stays frozen. Every written file has the same content as with 2.3.0, apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it; the scripting interface is unchanged. **How to upgrade from 2.3.0:** install 2.3.1 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. Each version writes its number into `project.json` and the generation record, so update the whole team at the same time. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 2.3.1, because it compares them with the version that runs. From 2.2.x or older, read the notes of 2.3.0 first. **Not done:** the known limits of 2.3.0 stand (splices, planned for 3.0.0 with format 3; joining in `per_connector_group` only; no command-line edits of the routing tree); nobody has looked at the new icon on a real display (its contrast was measured against the colours of the panels, offscreen); the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; and the owner's sign-off. `2.3.1` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.0.0; decision D-246).

A lighter purple rim keeps the icon visible in the dark mode of Ubuntu and Windows: the purple tile alone has less than 2:1 contrast on a dark panel, the rim at least 3:1 on light and dark panels. The window icon (`gui/appicon.py`), `docs/img/logo.svg`, the banner and `packaging/ubuntu/harness-design-studio.svg` show the same picture; `tools/make_icons.py` draws the Windows `.ico` from the same code. No change to the program's behaviour or the project format.
- **Records for 2.3.1.** The version is 2.3.1 (decision D-246). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 2.3.0 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `2.3.1` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.0.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 2.3.1 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 2.3.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_2.3.0_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 2.3.0, and they differ from the 2.3.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 2.3.1. ## 2.3.0 (2026-11-18; not a statement of compliance)

Version 2.3.0 is a **minor release** and the **latest full release**. It brings branching harnesses (decision D-243; the release, D-245): one harness with one plug on each device connector, a branch point where it splits, and a segment with a length between each two of its nodes. Generate makes such a harness when you choose the new mode `per_connector_group` of `segmentation.mode` (*Settings > Project*, *How interfaces are grouped into harnesses*); the default stays `per_connector_pair`, and existing harnesses stay as they are until you choose it. On a harness drawn by hand you draw the routing tree yourself: branch points, segments, a new leg from a plug and the join of two harnesses that plug into one device connector, in the *Routing* table of the Harness tab and on the diagram. A harness with a branch point gets `segments.csv`, a sheet *Segments* in its workbook and a sheet *Routing* in its build workbook; `routing DIR HARNESS` prints its tree, and `explain` explains a segment and a branch point. 2.3.0 also brings the owner's feedback of 2026-11-17 from a real display: the program starts on Home and asks the role there, the tour of the first start is removed, every window moves on its own, and the `.deb` has a page in Ubuntu's App Center. **No format change:** branch points and segments are lists of every harness file since format 1, and the diagram mark `branch:` is outside the model hash. 2.3.0 writes format 2 as 2.2.0 does, and format 2 stays frozen. A project that generates at 2.2.0 generates the same at 2.3.0: its project files are the same apart from the version, and every written file has the same content apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it (the bytes of the `.xlsx` and PDF files can differ, because they are compressed), with two exceptions: a harness with a branch point gets the new file and the two new sheets (in the examples, six harnesses of `flatsat`), and the advice on more than one cable plug on a device connector now names `per_connector_group`, so a waiver of that finding shows as stale once. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged; `routing` has a new `--json` document. **How to upgrade from 2.2.0 or 2.1.x:** install 2.3.0 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. **Update the whole team at the same time:** each version writes its number into `project.json` and the generation record, and an older tool reads the mode `per_connector_group` as not known, uses `per_connector_pair` and removes a joined draft on its next Generate; it reads a drawn tree as it is, with the same wire lengths, but cannot edit it. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 2.3.0, because it compares them with the version that runs. From 2.1.x, read the notes of 2.2.0 too; from 2.0.x, those of 2.1.0 first. **Known limits:** connectors wired in a ring are not joined (the warning `branch_not_built`), and neither are chains kept apart or the interfaces of a released harness; a new leg takes only the free pins of a plug, because two wires on one pin need a splice; a shield group runs between two connectors; joining is in `per_connector_group` only, not in `per_unit_pair` or `per_zone_pair`; the tree, the legs and the join are edited in the app only. **Not done:** splices (planned for 3.0.0, with format 3); a kind of branch point and backshells; in Guided mode the message after connecting does not say that a shared connector gets a second plug; the prototype counts the pins of the part, not the ports, and has no device library and no branch points; the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; a look at the screens on a real display, the new screens of 2.3.0 included; and the owner's sign-off. `2.3.0` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.0.0; decision D-245).

### Added

- **Generate can make one harness that branches: `segmentation.mode` = `per_connector_group`** (decision D-243, pull request 2 of 2.3.0; `docs/ait/DESIGN_2.3_BRANCHING.md` section 5.1). A new choice of the segmentation, opt-in; the default stays `per_connector_pair`. Generate makes the groups of `per_connector_pair` and joins those that plug into one device connector: one harness with one plug on each device connector, a branch point on an end with more than one connector (*Branch X*, *Branch Y*), the trunk and a segment to each plug. The two ends come from a walk from the first connector in natural ID order; the key is `cg|<chain>|<first connector>` and the name lists the connectors of each end (`OBC1-J01 to STR1-J01 + STR2-J01 + STR3-J01`). Connectors wired in a ring are not joined: their harnesses stay separate and Generate warns `branch_not_built`, naming the ring. Chains kept apart are not joined, and neither are the interfaces of a released harness (a new interface on its connector keeps the warning `box_connector_shared`). When the mode is chosen, the generated drafts are taken over: the one with the lowest ID keeps its ID, and the locked wires, labels, notes and strip lengths and the edited cable connectors of every taken draft carry over under its numbering; the other drafts are removed, and what cannot carry over is reported (`locked_data_lost`). Existing harnesses stay as they are until a person chooses the mode. `explain DIR HARNESS` names the join or the ring; the assumption `A-SEGMENT-MODE` and the setting describe the mode. Every project that does not choose the mode generates as before: a word-for-word copy of the segmentation of 2.2 (`tests/reference/segmentation_220.py`) gives the same groups on every example, fixture and random project, and every written file of the examples is unchanged. **Older tools:** 2.2.x, 2.1.x and 2.0.x read the value as not known, use `per_connector_pair` and remove a joined draft on their next Generate (a released harness stays), so update the whole team together.
- **A person draws the routing tree of a harness: branch points, segments, a leg, the join** (decision D-243, pull request 3 of 2.3.0, #262; `docs/ait/DESIGN_2.3_BRANCHING.md` section 5.2). A new pure module `core/branching.py` (the design calls it `core/routing.py`, a name that holds the routing of the diagram's links) has the plans, each one undo step through `History`: add, rename and delete a branch point (the next free ID of the namer, `W001-B1`, and the name *Branch 1*; a branch point on the only path of a wire is not deleted, and the wires are named), add and delete a segment (one that would close a loop is refused, so the rule `segment-loop` stays for files edited by hand), set the length of a segment (also on a harness Generate made: Generate keeps the entered length by the two nodes of its segment, as it keeps an imported one), a new leg from a plug (a loose end with a wire from each free pin of the plug that has a signal; when a leg left the plug before, a branch point is added next to it and the old leg moves onto it with its length; a pin with a wire is refused, because two wires on one pin need a splice), and the join of two harnesses drawn by hand that plug into one device connector (one plug, the other's wires moved pin by pin, its connectors, wires, shields, branch points, segments and accessories moved under their IDs, its notes added, the other harness deleted; refused, with the reason, when both wire a pin, the plugs have different parts, the harnesses share no device connector or more than one, one was made by Generate or from a connection or is released, or the other has a release history). A released harness refuses every plan with the reason. Three new integrity warnings, reported through the design rule `model-inconsistent`: `segment_tree_split` (the segments of a harness with branch points do not reach every connector), `branch_point_unused` (a branch point with fewer than two segments) and `shield_across_branch` (a shield group whose wires end on different connectors); no new design rule, the count stays 39. The diagram mark `branch:<branch point ID>` (where a branch point sits on the diagram) is dropped with its branch point and renamed by `merge-fix` with its harness, as `pos:`. **No format change:** branch points and segments are lists of every harness file since format 1, and every mark is outside the model hash. **Older tools** (2.2.x, 2.1.x, 2.0.x) read a drawn tree, a leg and a join as they are, with the same wire lengths, but cannot edit the tree; they read a `branch:` mark as a mark of a kind they do not know and keep it (their `merge-fix` does not rename it).
- **The routing of a harness that branches in the written files** (decision D-243, owner question B7, pull request 4 of 2.3.0, #263; design section 5.6). A harness with at least one branch point gets `harnesses/<ID>/segments.csv` (`Segment, From, To, Length (m), Number of wires, Wires`, one row per segment in natural ID order, the wires whose one path takes the segment), a sheet *Segments* with the same rows as the last sheet of `<ID>.xlsx`, and a sheet *Routing* in the build workbook after *Cut list*: the segments with the length or *to fill in* on an amber cell, then each branch point with the connectors beyond it on each segment, on one line; *Start here* lists the sheet. No new readiness check: a segment without a length already shows as *Wires without a length*. A straight harness keeps its files and sheets byte for byte. `verify --outputs` works the rows out from the project alone, with its own route of each wire, its own count of the wires per segment and its own walk beyond a branch point, and reports `out_routing` for a cell that differs, a row missing, extra or out of order, a sheet missing or present where it must not be, or a wrong line of *Start here*. The *Export outputs* window knows `segments.csv`. **The files of the example `flatsat` change once:** its harnesses W001, W002 and W004 to W007 have branch points, so its export writes 95 files instead of 89 and their two workbooks gain a sheet; the stored output hashes, the numbers snapshot and the goldens were written again for exactly those files. The sides of a branch point are joined by `; ` on one line, because a line break in a cell made the workbook's bytes differ on Windows (openpyxl writes a sheet through a file in text mode); a test now finds no line break in any cell of a written workbook of `flatsat` and `small-satellite`.
- **`routing DIR HARNESS` and `explain` of a segment and a branch point** (decision D-243, pull request 5 of 2.3.0, #264; design sections 5.7 and 5.9). `routing` prints the routing tree of one harness and changes nothing: each segment with its two nodes, its length, where the length came from (*imported from lengths.csv, row 2*, *entered*, *not set*; *kept by Generate* on a harness Generate made) and the wires through it, then each branch point with the connectors beyond it on each segment, the segments without a length and the wires with no single path. A harness with no branch point prints its segments and says it does not branch; an unknown harness is an error with exit code 2. `--json` gives a document (`ok`, `harness`, `name`, `origin`, `branches`, `connectors`, `segments`, `branch_points`, `lengths_missing`, `wires_without_route`; the keys are in `docs/CLI.md`). `explain DIR W001-L3` explains a segment (its nodes, its length and its source, the wires through it) and `explain DIR W001-B2` a branch point (the connectors beyond it on each segment); both rest on the model page `docs/models/wire-length.md` and are explained on demand only, so `explain --all` and `derivations.json` are as they were. The source of a length is worked out, not stored: a length that an import record of kind `lengths` lists with the same value is *imported*, any other is *entered*. The rows are those of `segments.csv` and the sheet *Routing*. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.
- **Branching harnesses in the editor** (decision D-243, pull request 6 of 2.3.0, #265; design section 5.8). **Settings > Project** has *How interfaces are grouped into harnesses* with the four modes and a sentence for each (*Per connector group*: one plug on each device connector, branching where needed); a change is one undo step and takes effect at the next Generate. The **Harness** tab has a **Routing** table under the wires: one row per segment (segment, from, to, length, the wires through it) and one per branch point (its name, its segments with their lengths); a double-click on a length enters it in metres as one undo step (on a harness Generate made the table says that Generate keeps it; a released harness shows why it cannot); for a harness drawn by hand **Add branch point**, **Add segment…**, **Rename…** and **Delete** change the tree. The **Inspector** shows a branch point with its segments and their lengths. The **Problems** panel offers, on a device connector with plugs of several harnesses, **Join into one harness** when every plug belongs to a harness drawn by hand, and **Use per_connector_group** (Settings at the choice) when every plug belongs to a draft Generate made; the row menu of the bottom Problems list offers the same. On the **diagram** the branch points of a harness drawn by hand are drawn as small diamonds, the lines of the harness run through them, and a drag moves one (the mark `branch:<ID>`, one undo step, outside the model hash, so no output goes out of date); a device connector with plugs of harnesses drawn by hand, and a plug on it, offer **Join into one harness…** in Expert mode.
- **The user manual page *Branching harnesses* and the end-to-end test of 2.3.0** (decision D-243, pull request 7 of 2.3.0). [Branching harnesses](docs/user-manual/branching-harnesses.md) says what a branch point and a segment are, how a segment is measured (from the rear of a connector to the centre of a branch point, owner question B4), how Generate makes a harness that branches, how to draw a tree, add a leg and join two harnesses, what the shields across a branch do (B5), the three warnings, the written files, `routing` and `explain`, what older versions do with such a project and what is not in 2.3.0 (splices, planned for 3.0.0), with a worked example on the command line. `tests/test_e2e_branching.py` runs that example: the users' harness *DEV2 + DEV3 to DEV1 + DEV4* from a blank project through `import-pinout`, Generate in the default mode and then in `per_connector_group`, `import-lengths`, a length typed in, `export`, the rows of `segments.csv` and of the sheets *Routing* and *Segments*, `verify --outputs`, `routing --json` and `explain`, a second Generate and a second export without a changed byte and the same project files in two folders; the RS-422 x3 case of 2.1.0 in the new mode; a harness drawn by hand with a leg, a join, undo and redo, the refusals, the three warnings and the mark `branch:` through save, load and `merge-fix`. It found no fault. [`FILE_FORMAT.md`](docs/FILE_FORMAT.md#branching-harnesses-230-format-2-unchanged) describes the `branch:` mark and says that no field of format 2 changed; [`OUTPUTS.md`](docs/OUTPUTS.md#a-harness-that-branches) the new file, the two sheets and the output check `out_routing`. The in-app guide has a section on branching harnesses.
- **A page in Ubuntu's App Center for the `.deb`** (the owner's feedback of 2026-11-17, pull request 257). App Center showed the `.deb` of 2.1.1 with *No Screenshots*, *No details for this release*, *Unknown Licence* and *Age Rating: no information*, because the package had no AppStream metainfo file. The `.deb` now installs `/usr/share/metainfo/io.github._0h3xn4.harness_design_studio.metainfo.xml` (and `install.sh` of the `.tar.gz` installs it under `~/.local/share/metainfo/`, or `/usr/share/metainfo/` with `--system`, and `uninstall.sh` removes it), linked to the desktop entry `harness-design-studio.desktop`, whose name stays. It holds the name, a summary and description, the licence `GPL-3.0-only` (the metadata itself: `FSFAP`), the developer `0h3xn4`, the repository and its issues, an empty age rating (`oars-1.1`: nothing to declare), the categories of the desktop entry, the three commands, five screenshots of `docs/img/` and the newest five releases. App Center loads the screenshots from the web (`raw.githubusercontent.com`), so on an offline computer the page shows without them; the program itself never uses the network. The releases are made at build time from the headings of this changelog and the first sentences of `docs/releases/X.Y.Z.md` (`tools/appstream.py`, called by `tools.build_installer` and `tools.build_deb`), so a release needs no edit of the file. The CI and release workflows check the file of the built `.deb` with `appstreamcli validate --no-net` and its desktop entry with `desktop-file-validate`. App Center still says *Provided by a third party* and *No Software Repository Included* for any `.deb` installed from a file; only an apt repository, a Snap or a Flatpak would change that.

### Changed

- **The advice on more than one cable plug names `per_connector_group`** (decision D-243). For harnesses that Generate makes, the generation warning `box_connector_shared` and the design rule finding `model-inconsistent` now end *…, use different connectors, or set segmentation.mode to per_connector_group for one harness that branches.* in place of *Or accept the second plug until a harness can branch (planned for 2.3.0).*; in the mode `per_connector_group` they say why Generate did not join the plugs (a ring or different chains). The editor's note on a connector in use says, in that mode, *Generate joins it into the harness on J1 as a branch*. The text of a finding is part of its ID, so a waiver of this finding shows as stale once: waive it again. No example has such plugs, so no written file of the examples changes.

- **A second cable from a port that a harness drawn by hand plugs into is a new leg of that harness** (decision D-243, pull request 6 of 2.3.0, #265). Since 1.17 such a port refused a second cable. Now a cable dragged from a port that exactly one harness drawn by hand plugs into, and let go on empty ground, adds a loose end to that harness, with a wire from each free pin of the plug that has a signal, and a branch point when a leg left the plug before (one undo step; the message says *A new leg of W001: the loose end W001-P3 from DEV2-J01. Choose its part, or plug it in.*). A plug with no such pin is refused with the reason. A port with plugs of a harness Generate made, of a harness made from a connection, or of several harnesses keeps the refusal of 1.17.
