- **A shield group runs between two connectors** (decision D-243, owner question B5, pull request 3 of 2.3.0, #262). **Group** in the *Edit wire* tab (and a change of a group's wires) refuses wires that end on different connectors: *W001-001 and W001-005 end on different connectors (DEV1-J01, DEV4-J01); a shield group runs between two connectors.* Such a group in a file edited by hand is the warning `shield_across_branch`. Generate makes only groups of one interface, which have two ends, so no generated harness changes.

- **The program always starts on Home, and Home asks the role** (the owner's feedback of 2026-11-17 on a real Ubuntu display: "The tool should not immediately start with a loaded project"; "the role window looks very bad and unfinished"; pull request 260). What loaded a project at the start was not the reopening of the last project (removed in 1.17, D-223) but the first-start path: the tour left Home for the diagram of the built-in sample, and the answer to the role window left Home too (Systems and AIT went on to the Next step page of the sample). Now nothing leaves Home by itself: the question *Who are you working as?* is a section of Home, with a short heading, the three roles as big buttons with their sentences and *You can change this later in View > Role.*; a press stores the role as before, the section goes and Home stays, with nothing opened. Until it is answered the window is an Electrical engineer, as before, and a role chosen in **View > Role** or with the **Role** button answers it too. The window of its own (`RoleDialog`), which a window manager opened taller than the main window, is removed. A settings file that holds values, the hook of tests and CI (`roles.FIRST_RUN_ROLE`) and Quick mode still skip the question. The quick path of the click budget (REQ-GUI-27) now starts on Home, where the program starts, and costs 10 clicks instead of 11.

- **Every window moves on its own** (the owner's feedback of 2026-11-17: "All breakout windows should be independently moveable"; REQ-GUI-30; pull request 260). GNOME glues a dialog that is *modal* for the window system to its parent window, so the role window, the edit windows, the export window, the review window and the questions could not be moved apart from the main window. The program now runs every dialog with `gui/dialog_base.run_dialog` instead of `exec()`: the dialog is not modal for the window system and stays in front of the window that opened it. What a dialog does is unchanged: a dialog whose answer the next step needs still waits for it, and while it waits the main window (and the library window, and a dialog that opened it) gets no clicks, keys, shortcuts or close button (`InputGuard`), as the modality did; a click there brings the dialog to the front. The glossary, the assumptions and a change log only show something and no longer wait. The questions of the window (`ask_choice`, `ask_text`) and the progress window of a job work the same way. The file and folder choosers are the system's own and are unchanged.

- **Records for 2.3.0.** The version is 2.3.0 (decision D-245). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those before this preparation only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it (the files of `flatsat` that branching changes were written again by pull request 4, #263). `2.3.0` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again for the version, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.0.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 2.3.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 2.2.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_2.2.0_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 2.2.0, and they differ from the 2.2.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 2.3.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the design of 2.3.0, the owner's checklist (F1) and the final report of the legal work say that 2.3.0 is prepared. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

### Removed

- **The tour of the first start** (the owner's feedback of 2026-11-17 on a real Ubuntu display: "Remove the guided tutorial, that is just annoying"; pull request 260). The cards *Step 1 of 6* over the built-in sample, the tour of the Systems engineer role, **Help > Replay the tour** and the code behind them (`gui/tour.py`, `MainWindow.tour`) are gone, with their texts and tests. The tour also took the first start away from Home to the sample's diagram, so the program seemed to start with a project loaded. **Guided mode** (the Guided / Expert switch) stays. A settings file that holds `tour/done` keeps it; nothing reads it.

## 2.2.0 (2026-11-17; not a statement of compliance)

Version 2.2.0 is a **minor release** and the **latest full release**. It brings the device library (decisions D-234 and D-240; the release, D-244). A device library is a folder of device files, one device each (`OBC1.device.json`), best kept as a Git repository of its own. A project names its libraries in `device-libraries.json` at its root, and a device added from a library keeps an import record of its file: the program says when the library has a newer version of the device, shows what changes, field by field, and takes it only when you ask. On the command line the new commands `libraries`, `add-device`, `update-device` and `save-device` do this; in the editor the library window's *Status* column, the line on a device in the Inspector and the Devices tab, the notice when a project opens, the review window, *From library* in the pickers, *Settings > Project* and *Save As*. **No format change:** the import record uses the kind `devices` of format 2, outside the model hash and every output, and `device-libraries.json` is outside the project format. 2.2.0 writes format 2 as 2.1.1 does, and format 2 stays frozen. A project that generates at 2.1.1 generates the same at 2.2.0: every written file has the same content apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it (the bytes of the `.xlsx` and PDF files can differ, because they are compressed), and a project without a device library keeps the same bytes and model hash. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged; the new commands have new `--json` documents, and `explain DIR DEVICE`, an error for a device before, prints one. **How to upgrade from 2.1.1 or 2.1.0:** install 2.2.0 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. **Update the whole team at the same time:** each version writes its number into `project.json` and the generation record, and a 2.1 tool saves a device to a library as `<ID>.json`, so beside the `<ID>.device.json` of 2.2.0 the device is in two files and 2.2.0 offers it from neither. Devices that the editor of 2.1 added from a library have no import record; adopt them (`update-device DIR ID --apply --adopt`, or *Take the library's version* in the review). If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 2.2.0, because it compares them with the version that runs. From 2.0.x, read the notes of 2.1.0 first: 2.0.x does not read port names. **Known limits:** only the device whose ID is the library's ID, and its copies, follows the library (a renamed device does not; owner question Q1 of D-240); with no common base, a change on both sides cannot be told apart and the review shows both values; `import-devices` is unchanged (a taken ID gets the next free one without asking); two project folders that went through the same steps differ in `journal.jsonl` only, whose entries carry the time of each change. **Not done:** a field that links a device of another ID (a format 3); the base of a change found in the library's Git history; in Guided mode the message after connecting does not say that a shared connector gets a second plug; the prototype counts the pins of the part, not the ports, and has no device library; the branching harness (planned for 2.3.0); the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; a look at the screens on a real display; and the owner's sign-off. `2.2.0` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.0.0; decision D-244).

### Added

- **A project follows its device library** (decision D-240). A device library is a folder of device files, one device each (`OBC1.device.json`), best kept as a Git repository of its own with a `library.json` that gives it a name. A project names its libraries in `device-libraries.json`, with paths relative to the project folder. A device added from a library keeps an import record of its file, which links the device and its copies to the library: the program says when the library has a newer version, shows what changes, field by field, and takes it only when you ask. A change that a released harness locks is refused, and the harness is named. The device file is the device library file of 2.1 (format `harness-device-library`, version 1), so a 2.1 tool reads it. **No format change:** the import record uses the kind `devices` of format 2, outside the model hash and every output; `device-libraries.json` is outside the project format. A project without a device library keeps the same bytes, model hash and outputs.
- **The device library on the command line** (decision D-240). `libraries DIR` lists the device libraries of a project (`device-libraries.json`, or the folders of `--library`): each with its name, its number of devices, its Git commit and branch (read without starting `git`) and the files that could not be used; `--add FOLDER` and `--remove FOLDER` change `device-libraries.json` at once, outside undo, and say so; `--devices` lists every device with its state (*not in project*, *up to date*, *library changed*, *changed here*, *same ID, not from the library*, *source not found*, *in more than one library*). `add-device DIR NAME… [--count N] [--as ID] [--dry-run]` adds a library device with the import record that links it, as one undo step; an ID the project has already is refused (exit 1) with the choices: `--count` for copies of a device that follows the library, else `--as` for a copy that does not, or `update-device --adopt`. `update-device DIR [NAME…|--all]` shows one row per field that the library changes (device, connector, pin, field, in this project, in the library, the released harness that locks it) and the impacts, and changes nothing; `--check` ends with exit code 1 while a device that follows a library differs from it; `--apply` takes the library's version, all or nothing per device and its copies, and refuses a change that a released harness locks, naming the harness; `--adopt` links a device of the same ID that was not added from the library. `save-device DIR DEVICE [--library FOLDER] [--replace]` writes the device into the library (`<ID>.device.json`, or the file that holds it), links it and says *Commit the library to share it.*; a different device of the same ID is refused without `--replace`. The command line never reads the editor's library folder. Each command has `--json` (the keys are in `docs/CLI.md`).
- **The device library in the editor** (decision D-240). The library window lists the devices of the libraries that the project names (a line *Libraries of this project* names each with its path) and of your library folder, with a *Library* column, so a project that names its libraries shows them even when you have chosen no folder. Its *Status* column says how the project's device of that ID stands to the library, in plain words, and the preview names the source of a device (the library, the Git commit, the file, *not committed*); *Review changes…* opens the review of a device that differs. The Inspector and the Devices tab show where a linked device comes from and its state (*From ACME devices, PCDU00.device.json, up to date*), with *Review…* when it differs. When a project opens, its libraries are read in the background, and a notice says when a device has a newer version in its library; it never applies anything. The review window shows one row per field (connector, pin, field, in this project, in the library, locked by), the impacts and what is kept, with *Take the library's version* (one undo step, the import record with it; a refusal says why and changes nothing), *Keep this project's* (nothing is stored) and *Save this project's to the library…*. *Edit > Add device*, *Add here* on the diagram, Ctrl+K and the Quick mode *Add device* window have a *From library* section with the project's library devices. *File > Device libraries of this project…* and *Settings > Project* edit `device-libraries.json` at once, outside undo, and say so; *Save As* copies the file and rewrites its paths for the new folder.
- **`imports` and `explain` know the device library** (decision D-240). `imports DIR` looks for the file of a device record in the project's device libraries (or those of `--library`), so `imports --check` ends with 1 when a library file changed, without `--source`; for such a file it says that `update-device` shows what changed, where it said to import the file again. `explain DIR DEVICE` names the library device a device follows, its state and the import record that links it (`--json` too).
- **Two more JSON Schemas** (decision D-240). `schema DIR` also writes `device-file.schema.json`, for a device file that holds one device (`NAME.device.json`), and `device-libraries.schema.json`, for `device-libraries.json`; `editor-settings.json` maps `device-libraries.json` to its schema.

### Changed

- **A device is saved to the library in a file of its own** (decision D-240). *Save to library* writes each device into the file of the library folder that holds it already, else into `<ID>.device.json` (it was `<ID>.json` for one device and `devices.json` for several); *Save to library…* of the Devices tab suggests `<ID>.device.json`. The library window refuses a device that is in two files of the folder, and two device files of the same name, and names the files; the files of 2.1 are still read. A 2.1 tool can still save `<ID>.json` beside `<ID>.device.json`, and the device is then offered from neither file: update everyone who saves to a library at the same time.
- **Adding a device from a library records where it came from** (decision D-240). *Add to project* of the library window and *Add from library…* of the Devices tab now write the import record of the library file (`devices:<file name>` in `imports/records.json`, with the SHA-256 of the file, the library's name, the file and its Git commit) in the same undo step, as `import-devices` does. A taken device ID no longer gets the next free one without a word: the editor asks whether to add copies of the project's device, adopt it (link it to the library and take the library's version) or add the library's device under another ID, a copy that gets no import record. Devices added from the Devices tab now get a place on the diagram as the library window gives them. `import-devices` on the command line is unchanged.
- **Records for 2.2.0.** The version is 2.2.0 (decision D-244). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 2.1.1 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `2.2.0` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.0.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 2.2.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 2.1.1, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against the `SHA256SUMS` of the 2.1.1 release page; no dependency changed since 2.1.1, and they differ from the 2.1.1 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 2.2.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the owner's checklist (F1) and the final report of the legal work say that 2.2.0 is prepared. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

### Documentation

- **The device library, from setup to update.** [Use the library](docs/user-manual/use-the-library.md) is one page: what a device library is and the states of a device, how to set one up as a Git repository (`library.json`, a `.gitignore` with `*.bak`), point a project to it, add, review and update a device, and save one, in the editor and on the command line, with a worked example that the end-to-end test of 2.2.0 runs (`tests/test_e2e_device_library.py`) and the limits: a device renamed in the project does not follow the library, a change on both sides cannot be told apart, and a 2.1 tool can write `<ID>.json`. [`FILE_FORMAT.md`](docs/FILE_FORMAT.md#import-record-optional-schema-version-stays-1) describes the import record of kind `devices` with its fixed-form notes (format 2 unchanged) and `device-libraries.json`; [`IMPORTS.md`](docs/IMPORTS.md#devices-from-a-device-library-folder-220) the commands and how they differ from `import-devices`. The in-app guide follows.

## 2.1.1 (2026-11-17; not a statement of compliance)

Version 2.1.1 is a **patch release** of 2.1.0 and the **latest full release**. It brings the three fixes of the end-to-end test of 2.1.0 (decision D-239). `explain` of a connector with several interfaces names each interface once and both tiers of the Guided choice. The advice on more than one cable plug on a device connector names `per_zone_pair` for harnesses that Generate makes. The **Port** column of the Devices tab follows **Signal**, so it is in view at 1440 × 900. **Two side effects.** A waiver of the finding `model-inconsistent` on a connector whose plugs all belong to harnesses that Generate makes, made with 2.1.0, shows as stale, because the text of the finding is part of its ID: waive it again. Column widths that you changed in the pin table of the Devices tab are set back once. **No format change:** 2.1.1 writes format 2 as 2.1.0 does, and format 2 stays frozen. Every written file has the same content as with 2.1.0, apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it, and apart from the advice in the design rule report and the generation warnings of a project with more than one plug on a device connector. **How to upgrade from 2.1.0:** install 2.1.1 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. Each version writes its number into `project.json` and the generation record, so update the whole team at the same time. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 2.1.1, because it compares them with the version that runs. From 2.0.x, read the notes of 2.1.0 first: 2.0.x does not read port names. **Not done:** in Guided mode the message after connecting does not say that a shared connector gets a second plug; the prototype counts the pins of the part, not the ports; the branching harness (planned for 2.3.0); the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; a look at the screens on a real display; and the owner's sign-off. `2.1.1` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.0.0; decision D-239).

### Fixed

The findings of the end-to-end test of 2.1.0:

- **`explain` of a connector with several interfaces names each interface once.** `explain DIR OBC1-J01` repeated the step *Connectors of OBC1 that carry rs422 …* once per interface, without naming one. The interfaces with the same choice now share one step that names them: *Connectors of OBC1 that carry rs422, for IF-001, IF-002, IF-003: …*. With another model of `model.connector_choice`, each ranking step names its interface. The equation now names both tiers of the Guided choice: *… else the first free connector of the device that carries the type; when none is free, the first connector of the device with a fixed pinout that has a free complete port of the type*. A connector with one interface keeps its step as before.
- **The advice on more than one cable plug on a device connector says what you can do today.** The generation warning `box_connector_shared` and the design rule finding `model-inconsistent` (*The device connector … has 3 cable plugs on it*) said to give the interfaces one harness, or to join the wires into one harness with a branch, which Generate does not make before 2.3.0. For harnesses that Generate makes, they now say: *For one harness with one plug on it, put the far devices in one zone and set segmentation.mode to per_zone_pair, or use different connectors. Or accept the second plug until a harness can branch (planned for 2.3.0).* When a plug belongs to a harness drawn by hand or released, the texts stay as they were: the segmentation does not join such harnesses, and a harness drawn by hand can branch. The text of the finding is part of its ID, so a waiver of this finding on harnesses that Generate makes, made with 2.1.0, shows as stale: waive it again.
- **The *Port* column of the Devices tab is in view at 1440 × 900.** It came after *Interface*, and the tab, which scrolls sideways at that size, showed it only after a scroll. It now follows *Signal*, and it is wide enough for *RS422_1 (IF-001)*. Column widths that you changed in the pin table are set back once.

### Changed

- **Records for 2.1.1.** The version is 2.1.1 (decision D-239). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 2.1.0 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `2.1.1` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.0.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 2.1.1 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 2.1.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_2.1.0_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 2.1.0, and they differ from the 2.1.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 2.1.1. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 2.1.0 say that 2.1.1 is prepared. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged.

## 2.1.0 (2026-11-16; not a statement of compliance)

Version 2.1.0 is a **minor release** and the **latest full release**. It brings several interfaces on one connector (decisions D-233 and D-237). A device connector takes another interface while it has room. On a fixed pinout whose pin names name ports (`RS422_2_TX+`), Generate gives each interface the pins of one port. The Inspector shows the port of each interface end and lets you choose it (**Choose port…**), the **Devices** tab has a **Port** column, `import-pinout` reads a Port column, and a full connector is refused with the reason. **No format change:** a port is part of the signal name of a pin, not a new field. 2.1.0 writes format 2 as 2.0.1 does, and format 2 stays frozen. A project that generates at 2.0.1 generates the same at 2.1.0: every written file has the same content apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it (the bytes of the `.xlsx` and PDF files can differ, because they are compressed). **How to upgrade from 2.0.1 or 2.0.0:** install 2.1.0 over it (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. **Update the whole team at the same time.** 2.0.x does not read port names. On a project that uses them, its `verify` and `drc` report errors for those interfaces, even without Generate, and its command-line `generate` saves nothing. Its editor's Generate drops the draft harnesses of those interfaces: a released harness stays with its pins, and a port locked with *Choose port…* keeps its pins, but the draft of its interface is dropped too. The next Generate with 2.1.0 makes the drafts again on the same ports, under new harness numbers, so the wire IDs, the labels and the output folders of those harnesses change. Each version also writes its number into `project.json` and the generation record. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 2.1.0, because it compares them with the version that runs. From 1.17.1 or older, upgrade each project once as for 2.0.0: [Upgrade a project to format 2](docs/user-manual/upgrade-a-project.md). **Until a harness can branch (planned for 2.3.0, decision D-235):** interfaces from one connector to different far connectors get one harness each, so the connector gets one plug per harness, and Generate warns `box_connector_shared`. To get one harness with one plug, set `segmentation.mode` to `per_zone_pair` and put the far devices in one zone. **Not done:** in Guided mode the message after connecting does not say that a shared connector gets a second plug (Generate and the design rules say it); the prototype counts the pins of the part, not the ports; the sparse field `Pin.port` of a format 3, which the owner can still choose instead of the names (owner question Q1); the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; a look at the screens on a real display; and the owner's sign-off. `2.1.0` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.0.0; decision D-238).

### Added

- **Generate assigns one port to each interface when the pins of a fixed pinout name ports** (decision D-237, REQ-PIN-04). A pin named `RS422_2_TX+` (also `RS422_2.TX+`, `TX+-RS422_2` and the like) is the signal TX+ of the port RS422_2. Generate gives each interface the pins of one complete port: the port its locked pins are in, else the port it used before, else the free pins named by the signal alone when they hold every signal, else the first free complete port in name order (`RS422_2` before `RS422_10`). The pins of two ports are never mixed. A port that misses a signal is not used, and the error says what it lacks. The explanation of a pin names its port (`explain DIR CONNECTOR.PIN`, step *Port of the pin*). A project that generates at 2.0.1 generates the same: only interfaces that failed with *no free pin named TX+* now get pins. A test compares the new pin allocation with a copy of the one of 2.0.1.
- **The warning `pin_port_unknown`.** When two or more interfaces of one type need signal names that repeat on one connector (`TX+` on two pins), Generate keeps the assignment of 2.0.1 and says that it cannot tell which pins form one port: name the ports or lock the pins of each port.
- **The design rules `signal-unassigned` and `direction-conflict` and the independent verifier read port names.** The verifier reports the new error `port_mixed` when one interface uses the pins of two ports of one connector. The verifier has its own copy of the reading rule; a test compares it with the one of Generate.
- **`import-pinout` and the import wizard read a Port column** (decision D-237). The headings *Port*, *Port name* and *Port ID* are matched (`--column port=HEADING` for another one). The import writes the port into the signal name: port `RS422_2` and signal `TX+` give `RS422_2_TX+`; a signal that already names the port is kept. Which interface type a port belongs to is read from the signal, against the types the connector carries (all types of the project when it carries none). A signal that no such type has is imported as it is, without the port, with a warning. A row is refused, and then nothing is imported, when it has a port but no signal, when the port name cannot be read back as that port, when the signal names another port, or when the name would be longer than 200 characters. The preview lists the ports of each connector (`OBC1-J01: RS-422 ports: RS422_1 (pins 1-4), RS422_2 (5-8), RS422_3 (9-12)` on a connector that carries RS-422; `Ethernet or RS-422 ports: …` on a connector with no *Carries*, because the two types have the same signal names) and warns about incomplete or doubled ports and pin names that read in two ways; `import-pinout --json` has a new key `ports`. `export-pinout` is unchanged: it writes the composed names (`RS422_2_TX+`), but it has no Mode column. The pins of its file are imported as not fixed, so no ports form until you set them fixed (Mode *fixed*). How: [define a device](docs/user-manual/define-a-device.md#a-connector-with-several-ports-since-210), [`IMPORTS.md`](docs/IMPORTS.md#device-pinouts-and-kicad-netlists).
- **The lists of signals that match no interface type read port names** (`import-pinout`, the import wizard and `import-netlist`): `RS422_2_TX+` is the RS-422 signal TX+ and is no longer listed. A name that reads in two ways (`TX+_RX-`) still is. The KiCad page says not to map port names away with `--signal-map`.
- **A device connector can carry several interfaces** (decision D-237); 2.0.1 allowed one interface per connector. A connector that already carries interfaces takes another one while it has room. The room is decided by a dry run of Generate's own pin allocation with every interface that ends on the connector and the new one, so the editor and Generate cannot disagree. A full connector is refused with the reason: *J01 cannot take another RS-422: its RS-422 ports RS422_1, RS422_2 are used by IF-010, IF-011*, or *… too few free pins for TX+, TX- (2 free, 4 needed)*. The answer carries a note, not a refusal, when the connector's other interfaces go to another far connector (Generate makes a second harness with its own plug on it and warns `box_connector_shared`, until a harness can branch, planned for 2.3.0), or when a released harness plugs into it (the new interface is wired only after a new revision). The dry runs are kept while the project does not change, so asking about every connector stays fast (a `perf` test at 1000 pins and 200 devices).
- **The diagram and the Inspector show several interfaces on one connector.** In Expert mode the diagram, the pointer and a click accept a connector in use that has room; a full one stays grey and a click says why. The note of a shared connector shows in the tooltip over the connector, in the message after connecting, and in the tooltip of the connector in the Inspector's list of an end. That list offers the shared connectors with room and shows a full one as *J1: Full*, not offered, with the reason. A connector that carries several interfaces is drawn on the side of the first interface on it by ID.
- **The port of each interface end in the Inspector, and Choose port…** On a fixed pinout with named ports each end shows a line such as *Port RS422_2 (pins 5-8), chosen by Generate* (or *locked*, or *Generate will choose it* before Generate has run). **Choose port…** lists the complete ports of the connector, says why a port cannot be taken, and locks the chosen port's pins to the interface in one undo step (*Assign port RS422_3 to IF-012*); their names and `fixed` stay. The pins of that connector that Generate gave the interface before lose the interface in the same step (only the interface is cleared; a locked pin keeps it), so the pin table and the Inspector show one port at once, not after the next Generate. A port is refused when it is incomplete or doubled, when a release locks one of its pins, or when another interface holds one of them. The next Generate keeps the interface on the port. Scripts use `devices.ops_assign_port`.
- **One Confirm per interface.** The Inspector of a device has one **Confirm** for each interface the tool chose a connector for; when one connector was chosen for several interfaces, each button names its interface.
- **The Port column of the Devices tab** shows the complete named port each pin is in and the interface on it (*RS422_2 (IF-012)*). It is worked out from the pin names: not stored, not exported. **Assign…** has a **Port** list on a fixed pinout with named ports; the port that the selected pins form is chosen first, and a port is assigned as with **Choose port…**.
- **The inline note CD-K9** under the pin table says what is wrong with a port: a missing or doubled signal, a port written in two ways, or a pin name that reads in two ways.
- **The assumption `A-PORTS`** names how the room on a connector is decided and how the pins of a port are read; `explain` of a connector that carries several interfaces cites it.
- **The prototype** (`prototype/`) follows: a connector in use takes another interface while the pins of its part last, and a full one is refused with *J01 cannot take SpaceWire as well: too few free pins for … (5 free, 8 needed).*

### Changed

- **The Guided choice of a connector has two tiers** (decision D-237). First a free connector that carries the type, exactly as before: every project and every example chooses the same connectors. Only when none is free, a connector with a fixed pinout that has a free complete port of the type, in the order of `model.connector_choice`. The tool never shares a connector whose pins the device leaves free. *Choose connectors automatically* (the fix of the finding `no-connector`) and the cross-strap fix follow the same two tiers.
- **Assign… (`devices.ops_assign_pins`) no longer refuses a second interface on a connector.** It refuses a pin that another interface on the connector holds (*Pin 5 carries IF-011; unassign it first.*) instead of moving it without a word. A fixed pin keeps its signal name and stays fixed (`RS422_2_TX+` is no longer rewritten to `TX+`); it must read as the signal it gets, and the pins of two ports are refused together. A pin that is not fixed is assigned as before.
- **The inline notes of a fixed pinout read port names:** a pin `RS422_2_TX+` is not an unknown signal (CD-K4), a type with a complete named port is complete (CD-K5, CD-K8), and a locked pin of a port carries a signal of its type (CD-K6). *Suggest carries* and the counts of `model.connector_choice` count complete free ports.
- **`explain` of a connector** lists every interface on each candidate connector and its free ports when another connector choice model is in force, and the free ports of a connector that carries several interfaces.

- **Records for 2.1.0.** The version is 2.1.0 (decision D-238). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 2.0.1 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `2.1.0` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.0.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 2.1.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 2.0.1, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_2.0.1_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 2.0.1, and they differ from the 2.0.1 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 2.1.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 2.0.1 say that 2.1.0 is prepared. The scripting interface (`harness_design_studio.api`) is unchanged; `import-pinout --json` has the new key `ports`.

### Documentation

- **Several interfaces on one connector, step by step.** The user manual has a worked example with three RS-422 links on one connector of an on-board computer: the Port column, the Guided choice, Generate, *Choose port…*, a full connector and the second plug until 2.3.0 ([generate harnesses](docs/user-manual/generate-harnesses.md#worked-example-three-rs-422-links-on-one-connector-since-210), [define a device](docs/user-manual/define-a-device.md#a-connector-with-several-ports-since-210)). The model page [pin allocation](docs/models/pin-allocation.md#ports-on-a-fixed-pinout) gives the full rule of the ports, a port chosen by a person and a worked example; [`FILE_FORMAT.md`](docs/FILE_FORMAT.md#ports-on-a-fixed-pinout-210-format-2-unchanged) says that a port is a naming convention on `Pin.signal` and no field, so format 2 is unchanged. The in-app guide follows.

## 2.0.1 (2026-11-16; not a statement of compliance)

Version 2.0.1 is a **patch release** of 2.0.0 and the **latest full release**. It brings two things. First, step M7b of the plan: `merge-fix` resolves a harness or a connection that two Git branches made with the same ID (decision D-231). Second, a shorter release page: the four packages, `SHA256SUMS` with its signature, and one release bundle with everything else (decision D-232); 2.0.1 is the first release with this page. It also holds the documents written after the first demo feedback (D-233 to D-235). **No format change:** 2.0.1 writes format 2 as 2.0.0 does, and format 2 stays frozen. A project saved by 2.0.0 opens and saves as before, and every written file is the same apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it. **How to upgrade from 2.0.0:** install 2.0.1 over 2.0.0 (the `.deb` or the setup program the same way as before; unpack the `.tar.gz` into a new folder and run `./install.sh` there, or unpack the `.zip` into a new folder). Projects need no step. Update everyone who edits a shared project at the same time: 2.0.0 and 2.0.1 each write their version into `project.json` and the generation record, so a team that mixes them changes these files on every save. If you use `verify --outputs`, export the outputs once with 2.0.1, because it compares them with the version that runs. From 1.17.1 or older, upgrade each project once as for 2.0.0: [Upgrade a project to format 2](docs/user-manual/upgrade-a-project.md). **Not done:** the release workflow with the bundle runs for the first time on the tag of 2.0.1; the editor does not show the renames of `merge-fix` (they are on the command line); interface, device and connector IDs made on two branches are not renamed (when both branches let the tool choose an interface ID, both get the same one and `merge-fix` changes nothing; the manual says how to fix it by hand); whether the *Merge* button of the GitHub web site honours `merge=union`; the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; a look at the screens on a real display; and the owner's sign-off. `2.0.1` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the stored hashes of the written files and the numbers snapshot stay as they are (`BASE_VERSION` 2.0.0; decision D-236).

### Added

- **`merge-fix` resolves a harness or a connection that two branches made with the same ID** (step M7b of the plan towards 2.0, decision D-231). A new harness gets the next free number (`W003`) and a new connection the next free `L1`, `L2` ..., so two people who each make one on their own branch get the same ID for two different things, and the Git merge stops on `physical/harnesses/W003.json` (both added it) or on `logical/links.json`. `merge-fix` now keeps the harness or link of the branch you are on under its ID and gives the other branch's the next free ID, with the IDs inside the harness (its connectors and wires) and every entry of the other branch that names it: the link a harness was made from, the place of a loose cable end and the label marks of a link (`diagram/marks.json`), and waivers and deviations (`waivers.json`). It prints every rename (`old -> new`), adds one journal entry (kind `edit`) that names them, and then runs `check` (and `generate` when the record of the last Generate is in conflict too). The same harness on both branches is kept once. It refuses and changes nothing when the harness has a release history on either branch (in review or released, a baseline, a change-log entry or an as-built record; released IDs never change), when a baseline holds the link, when both branches let Generate make a harness for the same group, when both branches changed an existing harness or link in different ways, or when the folder is not in a Git repository it can read. It reads which branch added what from Git's own files (the index and the commit you are on) and does not start `git`. The editor does not show these renames; this step is on the command line. How: [Use Git and CI](docs/user-manual/use-git-and-ci.md#when-both-branches-made-the-same-harness-or-connection-id).
- **`merge-fix --dry-run`** shows what `merge-fix` would do (the files it resolves, the renames) and writes nothing. `merge-fix --json` has two more keys, `dry_run` and `renamed`.

### Changed

- `merge-fix` no longer refuses when `logical/links.json`, `waivers.json` or `diagram/marks.json` is in conflict only because the two branches changed different entries: it merges them entry by entry against the version both branches started from. An entry that both branches changed in different ways is still left to you. No file format changes: projects without such a merge are read and written as before, and no written output changes.
- **The release page carries only what users need (decision D-232, amends D-213).** From 2.0.1 a release page has seven files instead of 221: the four packages, `SHA256SUMS`, its signature `SHA256SUMS.sigstore.json`, and one archive, `harness-design-studio-<version>-release-bundle.tar`, with everything else, each kind in its folder: the source of the LGPL and GPL components with `SOURCES.txt` (`sources/`), the SBOMs, the inventories, the notices, the offline-audit report, the signatures of the packages and the SBOMs (`signatures/`), and a `README.txt`. The archive is a reproducible plain tar (`python -m tools.release_bundle`). `SHA256SUMS` lists the packages and the bundle and is signed, so one signature covers both; the build provenance attestation is unchanged. The page text says what the bundle holds and where the signatures are; the notices and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` say that the source is in the bundle on the release page. The release stops before publishing when a package kind or the bundle is missing, or the bundle lacks `SOURCES.txt` or a file it lists, an SBOM, a notices file or the audit report. The pages of 2.0.0 and older are not changed, and the `notes_only` run writes them as before. Not proven yet: the release workflow runs first on the tag of 2.0.1.
- **Records for 2.0.1.** The version is 2.0.1 (decision D-236). The example projects, the test fixtures `mini3`, `sat15` and `custom_device`, the DRC golden, the golden outputs and the migration hashes were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of 2.0.0 only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. `2.0.1` has as many characters as `2.0.0`, so the check of the stored hashes (`tools.output_hashes`) and the numbers snapshot (`tools.numbers_snapshot`) replace the stamp and say *identical*; their files are not written again, and `BASE_VERSION` stays 2.0.0. The inventories and SBOMs of 2.0.1 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 2.0.0, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against `compliance/evidence/release_2.0.0_SHA256SUMS`; no dependency changed since 2.0.0, and they differ from the 2.0.0 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 2.0.1. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 2.0.0 say that 2.0.1 is prepared. No code and no written output changes, apart from the version stamp.

### Documentation

- **Documents added after the first demo feedback (2026-11-16):** the user manual explains how auto-linking and the checks work ([generate harnesses](docs/user-manual/generate-harnesses.md#how-auto-linking-and-the-checks-work)), connector IDs and tags ([naming and title block](docs/user-manual/naming-and-title-block.md#connector-ids-and-tags)), every PDF and Excel file in one table ([export the outputs](docs/user-manual/export-the-outputs.md#pdf-and-excel-files-at-a-glance)) and, on a new page, how to use the tool in an organisation ([using HDS in your organisation](docs/user-manual/use-hds-in-your-organisation.md)); the feedback is recorded in [`docs/ux/feedback-2026-11-16.md`](docs/ux/feedback-2026-11-16.md), with the owner decisions D-233 to D-235. No change of the program.

## 2.0.0 (2026-11-12; not a statement of compliance)

Version 2.0.0 is the **full release** of what the release candidate 2.0.0rc1 tested, and the **latest full release**. 2.0.0rc1 was published on 2026-11-12 as a pre-release; an end-to-end test of its packages found two small faults and two gaps in the upgrade guide, and they are fixed here (the first section below). There is no 2.0.0rc2: the fixes are small, covered by tests, and change no file format and no written file. It is the first full release that writes **format 2**, a new version of the project file format (decisions D-226 and D-228), and **format 2 is frozen with this release**: it does not change any more. **How to upgrade from 1.17.1 or older:** open each project once in the editor and answer **Upgrade**, or run `harness-design-studio migrate DIR --dry-run` to look first and then `harness-design-studio migrate DIR`. The upgrade keeps the original files in `.migration-backup-v1/`, checks itself and changes no design data. Export the outputs once afterwards. After the upgrade, versions 1.x open the project read-only, so everyone who edits a shared project needs this version. The guide is [Upgrade a project to format 2](docs/user-manual/upgrade-a-project.md). Everything that changed since 1.17.1 is listed below in short; the full text is in the section 2.0.0rc1 of the changelog, which stays as history. **Not done:** step M7b of the plan (two branches that make the same ID); whether the *Merge* button of the GitHub web site honours `merge=union`; the signature and the attestation checked from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip such checks); the Windows packages on a real computer; a look at the screens on a real display; and the owner's sign-off. `2.0.0` is shorter than `2.0.0rc1`, so the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the migration hashes were written again: no number and no written file changes apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it (`docs/changes.md`, decision D-230).

### Fixed

The findings of the end-to-end test of the published 2.0.0rc1 (pull request 226):

- **The editor names the right reason for a read-only project.** A project of format 1 opened with **Open read-only** showed the banner *Read-only: this project was saved by a newer version of the tool, or is open in another window*, and the note of the user manual was not shown. The banner now says *This project is in format 1, so it is open read-only. Upgrade it to edit it.*, and its button **Upgrade…** asks the upgrade question again. For the other causes the banner names the one that applies: a newer version saved the project, it is open in another window, or it cannot be locked for editing here.
- **`check` no longer warns `not_canonical` on `project.json` of an untouched format 1 project.** It compared the file with what a save would write now, which is format 2. A project read from an older format is now compared with the tool's formatting of its own format, as 1.17.1 did. A hand edit of the file is still found.
- **`validate` and `check` say how to upgrade a format 1 project.** The line `migrated` said *Nothing is changed on disk until you save*, but a save into the project's folder is refused. It now says that nothing was changed on disk and names `harness-design-studio migrate DIR` and the editor's **Upgrade**.
- **`migrate` and the editor's upgrade say when to export again.** When the outputs in `outputs/` were written by another version, they say to export them once with this version, because `verify --outputs` compares them with the version that runs. `migrate --json` gives that version as `outputs_version`.
- **The user manual [Upgrade a project to format 2](docs/user-manual/upgrade-a-project.md)** now says to export once after the upgrade and why, what 1.17.1's `journal` command says of a format 2 project (it does not see `journal.jsonl`, and `--start` is refused), and that the `error:` line of a refused command can come first in a log.

### Changed

- **Records for 2.0.0.** The version is 2.0.0 (decision D-230). The example projects, the test fixtures, the golden outputs, the stored hashes (`BASE_VERSION` 2.0.0) and the numbers snapshot were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of `master` only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. The inventories and SBOMs of 2.0.0 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 2.0.0rc1, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against the `SHA256SUMS` of that release (`compliance/evidence/release_2.0.0rc1_SHA256SUMS`, the publication record of pull request 225); no dependency changed since 2.0.0rc1, and they differ from the 2.0.0rc1 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 2.0.0. The status pages, the plan towards 2.0 (M4 and M7a done in 2.0.0), the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 2.0.0rc1 say that 2.0.0 is prepared. No code and no written output changes, apart from the version stamp.

### Everything since 1.17.1, in short

The full text of each change is in the section 2.0.0rc1 below.

- **Projects are saved in format 2** (D-226; M4 of the programme towards 2.0.0). Format 2 is format 1 with the number 2 in `project.json`, and the format that may hold a loose cable end. A format 1 project is upgraded once, by `migrate` or the editor's question, with a backup in `.migration-backup-v1/`, a record and a check; a command that saves refuses a format 1 project. The model hash and every written file stay the same. Versions 1.x open a format 2 project read-only.
- **The change journal merges** (D-228; M7a). The journal is `journal.jsonl`, one entry per line, each entry chained to the entry before it on its own branch; `.gitattributes` says `journal.jsonl merge=union`, so the entries of two Git branches merge without a conflict, and `merge-fix` records the merge.
- **A workflow makes the release tag** (D-227). It checks the commit, the version and the tests, makes the annotated tag and starts the release workflow on it. 2.0.0rc1 was tagged this way.
- **Text in the editor is no longer cut off** (pull request 218): the project name in the top bar, the cards of the Problems tab in a narrow panel, a message over the diagram and the hint of the Connect tool.
- **The install step of the Windows setup program on the release page reads as a sentence** (pull request 219).

## 2.0.0rc1 (2026-11-12; release candidate; not a statement of compliance)

Version 2.0.0rc1 is the **first release candidate of 2.0.0**, a pre-release. It is the **first release that writes format 2**, a new version of the project file format (decisions D-226 and D-228); that is why the number is 2.0.0. **What it means for you:** a project saved by 1.17.1 or older is in format 1. This version reads it, but saves it only after you have upgraded it once: answer **Upgrade** when the editor asks, or run `harness-design-studio migrate DIR --dry-run` to look first and then `harness-design-studio migrate DIR`. The upgrade keeps the original files in the folder `.migration-backup-v1/` inside the project and checks itself. The upgrade changes no design data: the model hash, the findings, the released baselines and a full export are the same before and after it. After the upgrade, versions 1.x open the project read-only and cannot save it, so everyone who edits a shared project needs this version. The guide is `docs/user-manual/upgrade-a-project.md`. **Still open:** format 2 is not frozen until 2.0.0 is tagged; step M7b of the plan (two branches that make the same ID) is not done; whether the *Merge* button of the GitHub web site honours `merge=union` is not checked; nobody has looked at the screens on a real display; the Windows packages were not tried on a real computer; and the owner has not signed this release off. It is no statement of compliance (decision D-229).

### Added

- **A workflow makes the release tag** (`.github/workflows/harness-tag.yml`, decision D-227). Started by hand with a version and the full SHA of a commit, it checks that the commit is on `master`, that the tag is new, that the version and the changelog of the commit fit the tag (`tools.release_notes check`) and that the Linux and the Windows tests passed on the commit; then it makes the annotated tag, pushes it and starts the release workflow on the tag. The signatures of a release made this way name the same workflow identity as before (`release.yml@refs/tags/vX.Y.Z`). A tag pushed by a person still works as before.

### Changed

- **Projects are saved in format 2** (decision D-226; M4 of the programme towards 2.0.0). Format 2 is format 1 with the number 2 in `project.json`: it is the format that may hold a loose cable end, so that a version 1.x tool opens such a project read-only instead of setting a harness aside. The step from format 1 to 2 changes nothing else, so the model hash, the 12-character stamp, the baselines of released harnesses and every written file stay the same (checked over the packaged examples, with `tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check`). A format 1 project opens upgraded in memory; it is written in format 2 only by `harness-design-studio migrate DIR` (look first with `--dry-run`) or by **Upgrade** in the editor's question, which keep the original files in `.migration-backup-v1/`, write the record `MIGRATION.json`, check themselves and add one journal entry *migrated from format 1*. **A command that saves no longer upgrades a project of an older format on the way:** `generate`, the imports, `override`, `release` and the other commands that save refuse it with *Upgrade it first: `harness-design-studio migrate DIR`* and write nothing; `validate`, `check`, `drc` and `export` still read it, and *Save as* to a new folder writes format 2. Version 1.17.0 and 1.17.1 open a format 2 project read-only (*saved by a newer version of the tool*). The shipped example projects and the test fixtures are in format 2, apart from two fixtures kept in format 1 on purpose. Format 2 is not frozen until 2.0.0 is tagged.

- **The change journal merges: `journal.jsonl`, one entry per line** (decision D-228; M7a of the programme towards 2.0.0, part of format 2). The journal is now the file `journal.jsonl`, one entry per line, and a save only adds lines at the end. Each new entry is chained to the entry before it on its own branch (`parent`), so when two people add entries on two Git branches, both sides' entries stay valid after Git has put the lines together. The `.gitattributes` of a project says `journal.jsonl merge=union`, so Git keeps both sides' lines and the journal does not conflict; whether the *Merge* button of the GitHub web site honours this is not verified. `merge-fix` adds one `merge` entry that names the last entry of each branch, and keeps both sides of the journal when it is in conflict (a project without the `.gitattributes` line). The reader shows the entries by time, then author, whatever the order of the lines; a line that is there twice is read once; a kind of entry it does not know is read and shown as `other`. A changed or removed entry is still found (`journal_damaged`). The step from format 1 to 2 moves `journal.json` to `journal.jsonl` with every entry and chain value as it was and adds the entry `migrated from format 1`; the backup keeps `journal.json`, and the upgrade adds the journal's line to `.gitattributes`. A format 2 project that a build of `master` wrote before this change (with `journal.json`) is read, and its next save writes `journal.jsonl` and keeps the old file as `journal.json.bak`. The journal is outside every hash and written output: no output, golden, output hash or number changed. The number in front of an entry is now its place in the journal, so a merge can renumber later entries.

- **Records for 2.0.0rc1.** The version is 2.0.0rc1 (decision D-229). The example projects, the test fixtures, the golden outputs, the stored hashes (`BASE_VERSION` 2.0.0rc1) and the numbers snapshot were made again with the repository's tools; they differ from those of `master` only by the version stamp and the hashes that include it. The inventories and SBOMs of 2.0.0rc1 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 1.17.1, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against the `SHA256SUMS` of that release; no dependency changed since 1.17.1, and they differ from the 1.17.1 files only by the version. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The pages that print the version as example output say 2.0.0rc1. The new guide *Upgrade a project to format 2* (`docs/user-manual/upgrade-a-project.md`) says how to upgrade a project, what changes in the folder, what the older versions do with it and how to go back; its output blocks are real output of 2.0.0rc1 and of 1.17.1. One test of format 2 (`tests/test_format2.py`) took the version of this tool for 1.17.1 and now checks the tool version on its own; because it reads the version, the component table (`compliance/sdd_components.csv`) names REQ-MIGRATE-05 for the package's `__init__`.

### Fixed

- **The install step of the Windows setup program on the release page reads as a sentence.** It said *The package is has no Windows code-signing certificate*; it now says *The package has no Windows code-signing certificate*. `tools/release_notes.py` writes that text; a published page can be written again with the release workflow's `notes_only` run.
- **Text in the editor is no longer cut off when there is room for it, and a narrow panel no longer cuts it.** The end-to-end test of 1.17.1 found three faults at the default window size of 1360 x 860 with a wide left panel. The project name in the top bar was cut (*Wheel…k E2E*) after a project was opened from Home, although the bar had room for it; it now shows whole. The cards of the Problems tab in the right panel were wider than the panel: titles and text stopped in the middle of a word, a sideways scroll bar showed and the tab row was scrolled so the first tab read *ector*. A long setting name in a card now breaks at its dots and underscores, the to-do lines and the button rows of a card wrap, and the three tabs fit the panel. A message over the diagram could be wider than the diagram, so its *Undo* and close buttons were cut off; a message now fits the diagram and follows it when a side panel is made wider. The hint of the Connect tool (*Click the first device for Primary power.*) was cut in a slot of two lines; the row now grows when the hint needs a third line. Only the screen changes; no written file does. `tests/test_ui_narrow_panels.py`.

## 1.17.1 (2026-11-11; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.17.1 is the **full release** of what the release candidate 1.17.1rc2 tested. The program is the same as in 1.17.1rc2, which was published on 2026-11-11 as a pre-release; since then only the source step of the release workflow changed (the first entry below, pull request 215). It is the **first full release with signed packages**: every package, `SHA256SUMS` and the SBOMs are signed with Sigstore without a key and have a build provenance attestation, and the release page carries the SBOMs, the inventory of the third-party components, the notices and the source of the LGPL and GPL components (decision D-213). **It supersedes every release from 1.0.0 to 1.17.0:** those packages carry no licence text, no notices of the third-party components and no signature, and they cannot be completed, so they stay as published and this release and its successors are the ones to use (decision D-218). Everything that changed since 1.17.0 is listed below in short; the full text of each change is in the sections 1.17.1rc2 and 1.17.1rc1 of the changelog (1.17.1rc1 was never published). **Not done:** nobody has checked the signature or the attestation of 1.17.1rc2 from outside the release workflow (the owner decided to skip that check); the Windows packages were not tried on a real computer; nobody has looked at the new screens on a real display; and the owner has given no sign-off for this release. `1.17.1` is shorter than `1.17.1rc2`, so the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the migration hashes were written again: no number and no written file changes apart from the version stamp and the model hash that includes it (`docs/changes.md`, decision D-225).

- **A download of the source step is tried again after a network error.** The second release run for `v1.17.1rc2` lost the network to Launchpad for a moment (`Network is unreachable`) and stopped. `tools/fetch_sources.py` now tries a download again after 10, 30 and 90 seconds when the network or the server fails (5xx); a client error such as 404 is not tried again.
- **Records for 1.17.1.** The inventories and SBOMs of 1.17.1 are made by `tools/third_party.py` from the published packages of 1.17.1rc2, after their SHA-256 sums were checked against the `SHA256SUMS` of that release; they differ from the 1.17.1rc2 files only by the version, and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` follows. The stored hashes carry the new version (D-225). The status pages, the owner's checklist (F1), the final report of the legal work and the publication record of 1.17.1rc2 say that 1.17.1 is prepared and that pull request 215 is part of it. No code and no written output changes, apart from the version stamp.

**Everything since 1.17.0, in short** (the full text is in the sections 1.17.1rc2 and 1.17.1rc1 of the changelog):

- **A licence and legal texts** (decisions D-205 to D-216). The code, tests, tools and documents are licensed under GPL-3.0-only. `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` are inside every package, and the licence text is in the About window. The disclaimer, the intended use and the project status are in the READMEs, the About page, a notice at the first start and every release page. Every written file carries a short notice (*Provided as is, without warranty. Verify before use.*), so the written files differ from those of 1.17.0 by that notice and the version. The *Independent check* is now called the *self-check*.
- **Signed packages, and what is inside them** (D-213). The release workflow signs every package, `SHA256SUMS` and the SBOMs with Sigstore, attests the build provenance, and attaches the SBOMs, the inventory, the notices and the source of the LGPL and GPL components. Its first runs failed and were fixed: the release check runs before the package is installed, the Qt source is checked against the SHA-256 sum of download.qt.io, the Ubuntu sources of an older update come from Launchpad, and a download is tried again after a network error.
- **The editor starts on Home and opens nothing by itself; Clear list for the recent projects** (D-223). Home lists New project, Open project, the examples and your recent projects, newest first.
- **No name is looked up, and the lock file names no computer** (D-222). A file holds a person's name only when the person typed it or set it in `HARNESS_AUTHOR`; `review`, `release` and `revise` need `--by`. `.harness.lock` holds a random id of the installation.
- **The upstream WireViz files are as upstream wrote them, and the text of the ECSS and ESCC standards is out of the repository** (D-220, D-221). Our own assessment stays.
- **Loose cable ends** (steps M4a to M4c of the programme towards 2.0; D-201, D-204). A cable let go on empty ground is kept as a harness drawn by hand with a loose end; the written files name a loose end as an open end.
- **Evidence that the tool works offline** (D-203). A guide to check it yourself in 30 minutes (`docs/VERIFY_OFFLINE.md`), an audit of the built package, and a switch that turns Python plug-ins off on a computer.
- **The documents were brought up to date after 1.17.0.**

## 1.17.1rc2 (2026-11-11; release candidate; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.17.1rc2 is a **release candidate**. It replaces 1.17.1rc1, which was **never published**: the release workflow ran twice for the tag `v1.17.1rc1` and stopped both times before a package was attached, so its release page is an empty pre-release. The first run stopped at its first step, because `tools/release_notes.py` imported the package before the workflow installs it (fixed, see the first entry of 1.17.1rc1); the second stopped while it fetched the source of the LGPL components, because Qt for Python publishes no `md5sums.txt` beside its source (fixed here: the source is checked against the SHA-256 sum that download.qt.io publishes). This is the first release made by the workflow that signs the packages without a key (Sigstore) and attaches the SBOMs, the third-party inventory and the source of the LGPL and GPL components (decision D-213), so its first purpose is still to test that workflow. It carries everything of 1.17.1rc1 (the section below: the licence and the legal protection, the loose cable end in the model, the written files and the editor, and the evidence that the tool works offline) and, since then, the owner's answers of 2026-11-10 (no name is looked up, the lock file names no computer, the upstream WireViz files are as upstream wrote them, the ECSS and ESCC text is out of the repository; decisions D-220 to D-222) and the Home page that the editor starts on, with Clear list for the recent projects (D-223). **It supersedes every release from 1.0.0 to 1.17.0** (decision D-218), as 1.17.1rc1 was meant to. **Still open:** the signing, attestation and source-fetch steps have never run to the end; the Windows packages were not tried on a real computer; nobody has looked at the new screens on a real display; and the owner has not signed this release off. `1.17.1rc2` has as many characters as `1.17.1rc1`; the stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the migration hashes were written again with the new stamp: no number and no written file changes apart from the stamp and the model hash that includes the version (`docs/changes.md`, decision D-224).

- **The Ubuntu sources of an older update are fetched from Launchpad.** The first release run for the tag `v1.17.1rc2` stopped while it fetched the source of the Ubuntu libraries: `apt-get source libpcre2-8-0=10.42-4ubuntu2.1` found no such version, because the archive index lists only the newest version of each pocket, and the build machine had an older update installed. `tools/fetch_sources.py` now maps each library to its source package and source version with `dpkg-query`, fetches it with `apt-get source` while the index lists it, and otherwise from Launchpad, which keeps every published version; every file is checked against the SHA-256 sums in its `.dsc`. A new workflow, `harness-design-studio sources`, runs this step on a pull request that changes it, so it no longer needs a release tag to be tried. That run made no release page with packages.
- **The source of Qt for Python is checked against the SHA-256 sum that download.qt.io publishes.** The release run of 1.17.1rc1 stopped while it fetched the source of the LGPL components: Qt for Python has no `md5sums.txt` beside its source, so the run got a 404. `tools/fetch_sources.py` now checks every Qt file against its own `<file>.sha256` from download.qt.io. No release page got packages from that run.
- **No name is looked up any more, the lock file names no computer, the upstream WireViz files are as upstream wrote them, and the ECSS and ESCC text is out of the repository (the owner's answers of 2026-11-10; decisions D-220 to D-222).** **Names:** the run record, the change journal and the change log of a review, release or new revision hold a person's name only when the person typed it (`--author`, `--by`, the name field of the dialogs) or set it (`HARNESS_AUTHOR`). The tool no longer takes the user name of the operating system (`USER`, `USERNAME`, `LOGNAME`) or the name Git has (`user.name`): the journal and the run record say `unknown` when no name was given, `review`, `release` and `revise` always need `--by` (also in a project with `release_distinct_roles` on, where a missing name used to come from Git; the message says to type it), and the dialogs start with an empty name field, or the name typed there last time. **Lock file:** `.harness.lock` holds a random id of the installation (32 hex digits, made once and kept in the file `install-id` of the settings folder) instead of the computer's name; a lock file written by an older version, or by another user account, counts as a lock of another computer (as before, the message names the file to delete when nobody has the project open, and the editor offers to open the project read-only). The run record's `platform` on Windows is made without asking for the computer's name, so the program imports no network module on any system; the Windows exception for Python's socket module in the offline audit's allow-list is gone. The privacy statement says all this. **WireViz files:** every file of upstream WireViz (`src/wireviz/*.py`, `setup.py`, `.gitignore`, `docs/README.md`, `.github/workflows/main.yml` were changed after the fork) is again byte for byte as at the fork base `e4fe099f`, and a test checks all 186; `MANIFEST.in` is gone; the documentation index of the repository is now `docs/INDEX.md`; CodeQL no longer analyses the upstream Python files (`src/wireviz/**`, `setup.py`), whose findings are upstream's. **Compliance records:** the requirement lists of the five standards and `compliance_matrix.csv`, which quoted the standards, are no longer in the repository (they stay in its history); our assessment in `compliance/assessment/` stays, the two tools that make the lists and the matrix from the standards stay for local use, and the checks of cited requirement IDs use the assessment. No calculation and no written file changes, except that `run_record.json` says `unknown` where it named the system user or Git's name before.
- **The editor starts on Home and opens nothing by itself; Clear list for the recent projects (decision D-223, REQ-GUI-29).** Users said the tool loaded whatever was worked on last. The program now always starts on the **Home** page as a landing page: *Nothing is open*, with **New project…**, **Open project…**, **New from an example…**, the examples and your **Recent projects**, newest first, so continuing is one click on **Open**. The panels, the top bar, the status bar and the sample's notice are put away on that page; opening or making a project, going to another area, the tour or an edit brings them back as they were. A project opened from Home opens on the diagram (Electrical engineer) or on the Next step page (Systems and AIT engineer). The window still keeps the built-in sample in memory behind the page (no folder is open, nothing is locked), as at a first start before; the **Model** area shows it if you go there without opening a project. **Clear list** on Home asks first, then empties the recent list (the names and folders kept in the settings file); it touches no project folder and is hidden while the list is empty. **Closing** releases the project's lock and keeps only the window's own settings and the recent list: the settings key `project/last` is no longer written, and an old settings file that has it starts without an error and loses the key. The packaged `--audit-run PROJECT` starts on the landing page too and opens its project as before; `--selftest` is unchanged. **Tests:** no existing test held the old reopening (the start was not tested through `app.main()`); the new `tests/test_ui_landing.py` plays the start through `app.main()`, and the layout sweep (`tests/layout_cases.py`) looks at the landing page too. The click budget of the four actions (REQ-GUI-27, 11 clicks and 5 dialogs) is unchanged: the test sets the role and Quick mode in a running window, so it still clicks **Home**. Window only: no project file, model hash, number or written output changes (`tools.output_hashes --check` and `tools.numbers_snapshot --check` print `identical`). Nobody has looked at the page on a real display.
- **Records brought up to date for 1.17.1rc2.** `docs/SECURITY.md` and the reason on the allow-list of the offline audit (`tools/offline_audit_allow.json`) no longer call the project names in the recent-projects list an open point: the owner decided on 2026-11-10 to keep the list and to add **Clear list** (D-223). The owner's checklist and the risk register record the owner's answer of 2026-11-11: the short notes of `compliance/requirements/overrides.csv` are our own words and stay. The stored hashes, the inventories and the SBOMs carry the new version (D-224). No code and no written output changes, apart from the version stamp.

## 1.17.1rc1 (2026-11-10; release candidate; not a statement of compliance)

**1.17.1rc1 was not published:** both release runs for its tag stopped before a package was attached, and everything in this section is in 1.17.1rc2 (decision D-224).

Version 1.17.1rc1 is a **release candidate**. It is the first release made by the workflow that signs the packages without a key (Sigstore) and attaches the SBOMs, the third-party inventory and the source of the LGPL and GPL components (decision D-213), so its first purpose is to test that workflow; the signed release follows when it works. It carries everything made since 1.17.0: the licence and the legal protection (GPL-3.0-only, a notice in every written file, third-party notices), the loose cable end in the model, the written files and the editor (M4a to M4c of the programme towards 2.0), and the evidence that the tool works offline. **It supersedes every release from 1.0.0 to 1.17.0:** those packages carry no licence text, no notices of the third-party components and no signature, and they cannot be completed, so they stay as published and this release and its successors are the ones to use (decision D-218). **Still open:** the signing, attestation and source-fetch steps have never run; the Windows packages were not tried on a real computer; nobody has looked at the new screens on a real display; and the owner has not signed this release off. The stored hashes of the written files, the numbers snapshot and the migration hashes were written again because the version string is longer than before (`1.17.1rc1` has nine characters, `1.17.0` six; a PDF stores byte offsets, so the comparison cannot swap the stamp): no number and no written file changes apart from the stamp and the model hash that includes the version (`docs/changes.md`, decision D-219).

- **The release check runs before the package is installed.** The first run of the release workflow for this tag stopped at its first step, because `tools/release_notes.py` imported the package's legal texts before the workflow installs the package. The tool now reads them from the source folder. No release page was made by that run.
- **Licence, notices and legal texts (decisions D-205 to D-216; no change to a calculation).** The code, tests, tools and documents of `HarnessDesignStudio/` are licensed under **GPL-3.0-only** (`LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, a licence and copyright header in every source file, `REUSE.toml` for the other files, a check in CI). The disclaimer, the intended use, the project status, the statement of AI authorship, the licence, the non-affiliation and the privacy statement are written once (`compliance/legal/texts.md`) and appear word for word in the READMEs, the About page, a notice at the first start (closed once, blocks nothing), the packages and every release page; tests fail when a copy drifts and when promise wording appears. "Clean-room" is no longer used. **The written files changed on purpose in one way:** every file carries a notice (*Provided as is, without warranty. Verify before use.*): a second comment line in `.csv` and `.yaml`, a note line in the `.md` reports, a row on the About sheet and a print footer in the workbooks, a line in the drawings, and a `README.txt` in each outputs folder and in the open data (so every export writes one file more); the reference outputs and the output baseline were written again for this. The *Independent check* is called *self-check*. Packages: `LICENSE`, `NOTICE` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` are inside every package, the inventory is made from the built package and compared with the committed one, and the build setuptools pin is 84.2.1 (84.0.0 has an advisory). Release workflow: every release is signed with Sigstore and attested, with the SBOM, the inventory, the notices and the source of the LGPL and GPL components attached (not yet run in a release). Contributions need a sign-off (`CONTRIBUTING.md`), and `.github/` has a security policy, a support note and templates. All actions are pinned to commits.
- **Loose cable ends in the editor (M4c of the programme towards 2.0; decisions D-201 and D-204, REQ-GUI-28).** Drag a cable from a port of a device and let it go on **empty ground**: the cable is now **kept**. It is a new harness drawn by hand (*Cable 1*, origin *Drawn by hand*) with one end plugged into the port and the other end **loose** where you let it go, made by the core plan of M4a as **one undo step**; until now it was a dashed draft of the window that was never saved, and the test that held "a loose end is never saved" (`tests/test_ui_tactile_connect.py`) now holds the opposite on purpose. A cable let go **on a port or a device** still makes a **connection** exactly as before (a connection says which devices talk; a cable drawn by hand is a physical harness from the start), and **Esc** while the cable is in hand still makes nothing; the end of a *connection* picked up and let go on empty ground still waits as a draft of the window, because a connection always joins two connectors.
