In Guided and Quick mode the cable goes into the device's first connector with no cable on it; a connector that has a cable plugged in already refuses, with the reason. **On the diagram** the loose end hangs on a line out of its port as a plug with a **dashed outline** and the words *open end* (the written drawing of M4b draws it dashed too), follows its device, survives save and reload, and says *Open end of W001 (Cable 1): no connector chosen yet* in its tooltip; a click picks it, a drag **moves** it (only the mark `pos:<connector id>` in `diagram/marks.json` changes, outside the model hash, one undo step), and a drag onto a port or a device **plugs it in** (pins paired by signal name, TX to RX included, else by number; a refusal says why in the status bar); a harness drawn by hand that is plugged in at both ends is drawn as a line between the two ports. The **project tree** lists each loose end under its harness with the chip *Open end*. The **inspector** shows the **Origin** of every harness (*Interfaces (made by Generate)*, *Connection L1*, *Drawn by hand*, worked out by `core/origin.py`) and, for a cable end, its harness, pins, part and what it is plugged into, with **Rename**, **Use this part** (a connector part decides the end), **Plug into…** and **Unplug** (a harness made from a connection or by Generate refuses, with the reason). Without the mouse: **Edit > Cable ends** (*Leave a loose end…*, *Plug loose end into…*, *Unplug a cable end…*) and the right-click menus of a connector (*Leave a loose end from J01*, *Unplug W001-P1*, greyed out with the reason where it is refused) and of an end. The **Release** window of a harness with a loose end names the ends and asks for a **reason** (at least 10 characters, passed as `--accept-loose-ends` is on the command line). The problem card of a loose end says how to decide it (not *approve the part*), its **Show** goes to the end, and the Next step page no longer offers to approve the stand-in part `TBD-CONNECTOR` (`part_approval.approval_blocker` refuses it). **One fix in the core:** an end that was plugged in and unplugged again has every pin of the port, the spare ones too, and plugging it in again was refused (*Pin 5 ... has no partner*); a pin that the proposal leaves over now goes on the free port pin of its own number when the two names do not differ (a pin of another name is still refused). **Nothing changes for a project without a loose end:** the file format stays 1 (`SCHEMA_VERSION` 1), no new file, field or rule (still 39), and every written file, every number and every golden are as before (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print `identical`). **Limits:** (1) **nobody has looked at any of this on a real display**: it was built and tested on Qt's offscreen platform (the mouse tests use the helper that also works on the real-platform lane, but that lane is not a person at a monitor); (2) there is no keyboard way to move an end on the diagram (the menu chooses ends and ports from lists); (3) a cable with no end plugged in anywhere has no place on the diagram (it is in the project tree and the menus); (4) the plugged far end of a cable drawn by hand cannot be dragged to another port (unplug it, then plug it in); (5) the file format is still 1, so a 1.16 tool sets a harness with a loose end aside as unreadable (see the entry of M4a); (6) no compliance claim is made.
- **Evidence that the tool works offline, that you can check yourself, and a switch that turns Python plug-ins off (decision D-203).** Colleagues asked for proof of the offline nature and of *no secret telemetry* that does not rest on our word. **A guide, [`docs/VERIFY_OFFLINE.md`](docs/VERIFY_OFFLINE.md)** (*Verify it yourself in 30 minutes*), gives copy-paste commands for Linux and Windows to run on a package you downloaded: check the checksum, run the program in a network namespace without network (Linux) or behind firewall rules (Windows), watch it with `strace` or Windows' audit events, list the network code in the package and in the running program, and look for a word you planted in your project everywhere outside it. **An automatic audit, `tools/offline_audit.py`,** does all of it on the built Linux package in one run: a whole workflow (first start, a new project from an example, import, generate, check, export with drawings, export-data, release, the editor with F1 and an idle time, two deliberate errors) once without network and once under `strace` with the normal network, and it judges every socket, connection, program started, file written and library loaded against a reviewed allow-list in which each entry has its reason. It runs on every change of the program (workflow *harness-design-studio offline audit*, not a required check) and, from the next release, on the released Linux package; its report is attached to the release page as `offline-audit-<version>.md` and `.json`. Result for this version's Linux package: no socket of any family, no connection, no listening port, no host name looked up, no program started but the design rule check helper, and no network-capable library loaded. **The packages are smaller by what they never use:** Qt's VNC platform plug-in (which would serve the window over the network if someone set `QT_QPA_PLATFORM=vnc`), Qt's TUIO touch plug-in (a UDP listener), the Qt network library that only those two need, the Kerberos libraries, Python's TLS module with OpenSSL's TLS library, and a start-up hook that loaded Python's socket module at every start of the packaged program are no longer shipped (the Linux program folder is 4.2 MB smaller, the `.tar.gz` 1.9 MB); the build fails if one comes back. **Python plug-ins can be switched off on a computer:** the policy file `/etc/harness-design-studio/policy.json` (Linux) or `%ProgramData%\HarnessDesignStudio\policy.json` (Windows) with `{"python_plugins": "off"}`, which only an administrator can write, or the environment variable `HARNESS_DESIGN_STUDIO_NO_PLUGINS=1`. Then no plug-in is imported whatever a project or your trust list says, **Help > Extensions** and `harness-design-studio extensions` say so, and `trust` refuses. Rules and tables written as data still apply. Without the file and the variable nothing changes. **Limits, said plainly:** a test shows the absence of network use only on the paths it runs; the operating system, the desktop and the browser that opens the guide are outside the claim; a trusted Python plug-in can use the network unless plug-ins are switched off. The audit found two places outside the project that hold design data, both on your computer: the editor's recent-projects list keeps each project's **name** in your settings file, and while a build workbook is written its worksheets are in temporary files of the system's temporary folder (deleted at once). Neither is changed by this version; both are in `docs/SECURITY.md`. The Windows counterpart of the audit (firewall rules and audit events) runs in CI and cannot see files that were written and deleted again; on Windows the editor loads Python's socket module to read the computer's name for the project's lock file (a local call, no connection event). No written file, model hash or number changes.
- **The written files name a loose end as an open end (M4b of the programme towards 2.0; decision D-201, decision 2).** In a harness that has a loose end every written file now says what it is instead of listing an ordinary connector: the pinout tables, the drawing and the build workbook give its part as `TBD-CONNECTOR (loose end)` and what it mates with as `open end`; its label reads `W009-P2 open end`; the **bill of materials** of the harness and of the system has **no line for `TBD-CONNECTOR`** any more but one line `(open end)` (category connector, *Connector not chosen yet (loose end)*, approval *to fill in*, the number of ends); the **mass** never counts the end (not even a mass someone gave the placeholder part) and lists `mass of open end W009-P2 (no connector chosen yet)` as missing, so the harness mass is not complete; a **test** at the end names the pin `W009-P2.1 (open end)` and expects *test when the end is decided* instead of *continuous* (continuity with an open end, isolation from one); the **drawing** draws the end with a dashed outline and the lines `TBD-CONNECTOR (loose end), 4-pin` and `open end` instead of *gender not set*; the **Connectors sheet** of the build workbook heads it *open end, to fill in* instead of *gender not set · not plugged into a unit*, and the Parts sheet shows *to fill in* in amber; the WireViz file gives its type as `TBD-CONNECTOR (loose end)`; the **as-built sheet** asks no lot of the placeholder part and describes the end as *open end: no connector chosen yet*; `explain` says *Open end: no device connector and no part chosen yet* (not *TBD-CONNECTOR mates with None*), `explain TBD-CONNECTOR` explains the `(open end)` line, and the change reports (`diff`, `compare`, the revision report, the history) call the object *Loose end*. `system/export.json` keeps the stored data (role `loose`, part `TBD-CONNECTOR`). The independent verifier words every one of these again on its own and refuses a file that names an open end as an ordinary connector; a new golden set, `tests/fixtures/outputs/loose_ends.json` and `tests/fixtures/outputs/loose_ends/`, holds the outputs of a project with five drawn harnesses (REQ-OUT-11, `docs/OUTPUTS.md` section *A loose cable end*). **Nothing changes for a project without a loose end**: the file format stays 1, and every written file, every number and every golden are as before (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print `identical`). **Limits:** the editor still cannot draw a loose end (M4c); a wire label and the cut list name the pin of an end as `W009-P2.1` without the words *open end*; the summary line of `validate` and `check` still counts an end among the connectors; no compliance claim is made.
- **A cable can end in a loose end (the second step towards 2.0, in the core only: no screen yet).** This is the model and the rules behind "drag a cable from a port and drop it on empty ground" (decisions D-200 and D-201; the throw-away spike of it is `docs/ait/SPIKE_M4_LOOSE_END.md`). A **loose end** is the end of a cable that is not on a connector yet. It is saved like any other connector of the harness (role `loose`, with the placeholder library part `TBD-CONNECTOR`, which the tool adds when the first one is made and which the part lists do not offer), its wires and pins are the ones you drew, and it follows the same rules as every connector. What the core can do, each as one undo step: make a cable from a port with one end plugged in and the other loose; **plug** a loose end into a port (its pins are paired with the pins of the port by signal name, TX to RX included, else by pin number; where two pins would be met by number and carry different names the plug is refused and says which, so nothing is paired wrongly without a word); **unplug** a connector so that it is loose again (its pins and wires stay, its part goes back to `TBD-CONNECTOR`; a harness made from a connection keeps both ends plugged in and refuses, with the reason); and **decide** an end, by plugging it or by giving it a connector part in the Connectors tab. The **checks** say so: the design rule *Approved parts* reports *N cable end(s) have no connector chosen yet (loose end)* (a choice to make, not a part to approve; still 39 rules), and the data check refuses a loose end that says it mates, a loose end in a harness that Generate makes, and a connection that claims a harness Generate makes. The **build workbook** of a harness with a loose end gets a ninth check, *Ends not decided*, and says *Ready to build: no* until the end is decided, however complete the rest is (the independent verifier checks this again). **A harness with a loose end cannot be released** (`loose_end_unresolved`, with the ends named); `release --accept-loose-ends REASON` (at least 10 characters) releases anyway and keeps the reason in the change log and the baseline, and `--accept-unapproved-parts` does not cover it. Sending a harness for review is not blocked. Where an end hangs on the diagram will be kept in `diagram/marks.json` as `pos:<connector id>`, outside the model hash, so moving one never makes an output stale. The **origin** of a harness (made by Generate, made from a connection, or drawn by a person) is worked out from what the files already say and is never stored. **Nothing changes for a project that has no loose end:** the file format stays 1, every file, the model hash, the 12 characters on every written file, every written output and every number are as before (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print `identical`). The step from format 1 to format 2 is written and proved with the safety net of the previous entry (it changes only the number, so a project with loose ends upgrades with the same hash, the same baselines and byte-identical exports), but it is not in force. **Limits:** (1) **nothing in the editor yet**: you cannot draw, see, plug or unplug a loose end on the diagram or in the Harness tab, and the release window shows the blocker without a field for the reason (the command line has the option); (2) the written outputs name the end as an open end since the next entry (M4b); (3) the file format is still 1: a project with a loose end is written in format 1, and a 1.16 tool sets the harness that holds one aside as unreadable (the role is new) until format 2, which is what makes an older tool open such a project read-only, is switched on in a later release; (4) no compliance claim is made.

- **Documentation refresh after 1.17.0.** The pages that said 1.17.0 was prepared now say it is published (tag `v1.17.0` on `4c5a56c`, 2026-11-09), with its package names and checksums on the install page, the checksum list `compliance/evidence/release_1.17.0_SHA256SUMS` and a publication record in `compliance/SIGNOFF.md`; the test count of the developer page is the one of today (6,281 tests in 320 files); the architecture page and the notes for agents name `core/io/upgrade.py`, `core/origin.py` and the loose-end plans; the plan for 2.0 says which milestones are done; the FAQ and the in-app guide (F1) tell about the question *Upgrade?* and `migrate --dry-run`. No code, no project file, no written output and no number changes.

## 1.17.0 (2026-11-09; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.17.0 is the **safety-net release** (decision D-202). It carries the first two steps of the programme towards 2.0 (decision D-200, plan `docs/ait/PLAN_2.0.md`): before any new file format exists, the tool gets a way to upgrade a project to one, and the tests get a lane on real window systems. **A safer way to upgrade a project:** the model hash no longer depends on the format number, `migrate DIR --dry-run` shows what an upgrade would do and writes nothing, `migrate` writes a record of what it did, checks itself after the write and puts every file back from the backup if anything differs, and the editor asks once before it upgrades a project that is in an older format and no longer does it silently at the first save. **A real-platform test lane and a click budget:** a separate GitHub check runs the layout audit and the sweep over every dialog on Qt's real `xcb` platform (Ubuntu) and `windows` platform (the Windows runner), and a test holds the quick path (New project, Add device twice, Connect, Export diagram) to a budget of 11 clicks and 5 dialogs. The third entry is the documentation refresh after 1.16.0. **Nothing that the tool writes changes, and no number changes:** the calculations and the 39 design rules are as in 1.16.0. It is a minor release because of new commands and options (`migrate --dry-run`, the migration record, the question in the editor), not a patch release.

**The file format and the written files.** The file format keeps `schema_version` 1 and there is no migration for a project that is in format 1; there is no new field and no new file in a project. A project in the older format 0 (no `schema_version` in `project.json`) is the only one that has anything to upgrade: the editor now opens it read-only until you agree to the upgrade, and `migrate` does it with the backup folder `.migration-backup-v0/`, the record `MIGRATION.json` in it and the self-check. The model hash no longer includes `schema_version`; as there is only one format the hash of every project is the same as before (a test compares them). No written output of the packaged examples or of the eleven scenarios of the numbers snapshot changes, apart from the tool version in the stamp and the model hash that follows from it (the model hash includes the version of the tool that generated a project, `generation.generator_version`): `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print `identical` for 1.17.0 (`1.10.0`, `1.16.0` and `1.17.0` have the same string length, so `BASE_VERSION` of the stored baseline stays 1.10.0, D-183). **Limits:** nobody looked at the upgrade question or any other window on a real display (offscreen renderings, tests and the two real-platform jobs of GitHub's runners only); there is still only one format, so the first real migration beyond the old 0 to 1 has not been run; the real-platform check is not a required check of the repository.

### Changed for you to know

- **The layout tests now also run on a real window system, and the quick path has a click budget (no change to the tool).** This is the second step of the programme towards 2.0. Until now every test of the editor ran on Qt's `offscreen` platform, which has no screen, no window system and no desktop fonts; the layout problems the owner found on a real display (windows on top of themselves) were exactly what it could not show. **Nothing changes in the program, in a project file, in the model hash or in any written output.** What is new, in the tests and in GitHub's checks only: (1) **a separate check, "harness-design-studio real platform"**, with a job on Ubuntu (Qt's real `xcb` platform on a virtual X display, at screen scaling 100 % and 125 %) and a job on Windows (Qt's real `windows` platform on GitHub's Windows runner, whose screen the job first raises from 1024 x 768 to 1920 x 1080); both run the layout audit and the sweep over every dialog and window at the interface sizes (100 to 200 %) and font sizes (+2 and +4 pt) already in the tests, and the mouse tests of connecting two devices; they run beside the existing checks, change none of them and are **not** a required check; each job prints first what Qt got from the machine (platform, screen, scaling, desktop session, the fonts and the characters the bundled Inter and JetBrains Mono lack); (2) **a click budget for the systems engineer's four actions**: a test plays New project, Add device twice, Connect (a drag from a port to a port) and Export diagram in Quick mode with real mouse events, counts the clicks and the dialogs, and fails if either grows past the budget written once in the test (today 11 clicks and 5 dialogs: Home, New project, Browse, Create; the two Add device windows; the drag; Export diagram and Save), if a window opens that the path did not have, or if a window it opens fails the layout audit. What the real platforms showed: the Windows runner has a real, interactive desktop session; the sweeps found no layout problem on either platform that the offscreen run had not, apart from text widths that differ by 1 to 2 px (and Windows draws Inter about 1.6 % wider than Linux); four things in the tests were wrong on a real platform (a mouse move that is delivered late, a pixel test at fractional scaling, a window that Windows will not make as small as asked, window sizes larger than the screen) and were corrected in the tests. **Limits:** this finds a class of problems (overlap, clipping, a wrong minimum size); it is not a person looking at a real monitor. It covers two window systems and the fonts of two machines; the two characters `ⓘ` and `＋` are drawn by a fallback font of the machine and their room is not tested; on the Windows runner the main-window sweep at 150 % and 200 % is skipped (Windows cuts a window to its screen); no window manager runs under the virtual display, so what a desktop does to a window (a minimum size, snapping, a title bar) is not tested; the plain Systems engineer path without Quick mode is not under a budget (a new device has no connector there, so Connect first asks for one).

- **A safer way to upgrade a project to a newer file format (no format changes yet).** This is the first step of the programme towards 2.0 (decision D-200): the safety net is built and tested before any new format exists, so that no project of yours is put at risk when one does. **Nothing changes for your projects:** the file format stays 1, the model hash, the 12 characters on every written file, every written output and every number are as in 1.16.0 (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print `identical`). What is new: (1) **the model hash no longer depends on the format number**, so a later upgrade changes no hash and no stamp and leaves every released baseline matching; (2) **`harness-design-studio migrate DIR --dry-run`** shows what an upgrade would do and writes nothing: the files that would change, the objects by kind before and after, the model hash before and after, a full export before and after, anything that could not be carried over; it exits 0 only if nothing would be lost; (3) **`migrate` writes a record**, `.migration-backup-v<N>/MIGRATION.json` (from and to format, tool version, SHA-256 of every file before and after, model hash, counts), (4) **checks itself after the write** (the same model hash, the same findings of `validate` and `check`, the released baselines untouched, a byte-identical export) and **puts every file back from the backup, and says so, if anything differs or the write fails**; a run that was killed half-way is put right by the next one; (5) **the editor asks once** when you open a project in an older format (*This project is in format N. Upgrade? A copy of the original stays in .migration-backup-vN. Older tools can no longer edit it.* with *Upgrade*, *Open read-only* and *Cancel*) and no longer upgrades it at the first save without asking. A migration generates nothing and makes no new ID; it adds one entry, `migrated from format N`, to the journal of a project that has one. The tests run a fake format 2 that exists only in the tests over the examples, the fixtures and projects with released harnesses, a journal, overrides, connections, as-built records and a diagram mark, and inject failures (a kill while the backup is made or while the files are written, a read-only folder, a linked file, a difference found by the check). **Limits:** there is still only one format, so the first real migration (a step beyond the old 0 to 1) has not been run; a file that is a link, a damaged project or a count that goes down makes `migrate` refuse; other commands that save (for example `generate`) still bring a format-0 project up to date by saving, with the backup but without the record and the check; a kill in the last moment, after the files are written and before the record is completed, leaves the record at `started` (the project itself is whole); the backup of an upgrade is not in Git (`.migration-backup-v*/` is ignored); no one has looked at the question on a real display.

- **Documentation refresh after 1.16.0.** The READMEs, the user manual, the how-tos, the cheat sheet, the reference and developer pages and the in-app guide (F1) now describe the tool as it is: the tactile cable connecting, the free canvas, labels that slide along their line, the looks and the Kind of a device, the dialogs that no longer overlap, the garbage-collection change and the versions and counts of 1.16.0; stale texts (an old "current version", old screenshots, old menu names) were corrected and the screenshots of the window were drawn again. No code, no project file, no written output and no number changes.

## 1.16.0 (2026-11-06; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.16.0 is the **diagram-handling release** (decision D-199). It carries on from the owner's feedback on 1.14.0 and 1.15.0 about drawing and handling the block diagram: labels that stay on their line and slide along it, devices that can be told apart at a glance (a picture, a colour and a shape for each kind, and a Kind you can set by hand), connections made like picking up a cable (drag from a port, with snapping, refusals, loose ends and re-plugging an end), devices that are no longer trapped in the panel, and dialogs and windows whose text no longer lies on top of itself on a real screen. It also fixes a rare crash of the editor: Python's cyclic garbage collector now runs on the main thread only, which is a deliberate change of behaviour that shows nothing on the screen. **The written block diagram changes for every project, on purpose (the owner asked for it):** `system/block_diagram.svg` and `system/block_diagram.pdf`, written by every `export`, and the SVG, PDF and PNG of **Export diagram** now show the looks of the devices. The calculations, the 39 design rules and the numbers are as in 1.15.0.

**The file format and the written files.** The file format keeps `schema_version` 1 and there is no migration. There is one new optional field, `kind` of a device (`logical/units/<subsystem>.json`), written only when a kind is set: a project that sets none keeps byte-identical files and the same model hash, a project that sets one has the kind in its model hash, and a tool older than this one refuses a device that has a `kind` until the key is taken out. In `diagram/marks.json` a label is now stored as a fraction `t` along its line; a mark written by 1.14.0 or 1.15.0 is read as before, migrated when the diagram is drawn and written in the new form at the next save, and a tool older than this one sets a mark in the new form aside as unreadable. A loose end of a connection (the cable you let go on empty ground) is only on the screen and is saved nowhere. **Of the written files only the block diagram changes:** `system/block_diagram.svg` and `system/block_diagram.pdf` (and `manifest.json`, which lists the hash and size of every written file) differ from 1.15.0 for every project; every other written file and every number is as in 1.15.0, apart from the tool version in the stamp and the model hash that follows from it. The stored hashes of the block diagram were renewed on purpose with the tools of the repository when the looks were merged (D-199); `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print `identical` for 1.16.0 (`1.10.0`, `1.15.0` and `1.16.0` have the same string length, so `BASE_VERSION` of the stored baseline stays 1.10.0, D-183). It is a minor release because of new features, one new optional field and a deliberate change to a written file. **Limits:** nobody looked at any of this on a real display (offscreen renderings and tests only; the owner's first look at a real display is what found the overlapping widgets that the dialog fix addresses, so another look is asked for); loose ends are drafts and are not saved; in Guided and Quick mode a device card has one port and not one for each connector; there is no keyboard path to a port (the Connect tool and the window *Link connectors* are the keyboard way to connect); a few buttons of the rail are a few pixels narrower than their text at some sizes (`docs/developer/gui-layout.md`); there is no AC or skin-effect model of the voltage drop and no mass model that counts splices or backshells (both need engineering input and were not built in 1.15.0 either). The packages are not signed. No one has signed this release off; it is not a statement of compliance.

### Changed for you to know

- **Devices are easier to tell apart on the block diagram (owner feedback: *it would help if you could make different devices visually/graphically more distinguishable*).** A device card now has the picture of its kind in a badge at the left of its header (20 pictures: battery, solar array, power distribution, reaction wheel, magnetorquer, thruster, star tracker, sun sensor, gyro, sensor, transceiver, antenna, payload, heater, pyro unit, mechanism, computer, interface unit, ground support and the plain device), a band of the colour of its family down its left edge and a badge shape (square, circle, hexagon or octagon) for the family. There are nine families with a colour each, in the light and the dark theme. A project opens zoomed out, where a device was a plain box with its ID: the box now has the same band and badge and a faint tint, so the 23 devices of the `flatsat` example can be told apart at a glance. Colour is never the only sign: the picture and the shape say the same, and the tooltip (*Looks like: Reaction wheel (from the name Reaction wheel 1)*), the **Inspector** (under **Subsystem**) and the **legend** (it lists the kinds of device the diagram has, first) say it in words. The tool takes the kind from the words of the name, then the letters of the ID (`BAT1`), the subsystem and, last, the links the device carries (the clue the card's small grey picture already used), and a device it cannot place keeps the plain outlined box. To change a look, rename the device, or set the kind by hand (the next entries). The small grey picture of the card (`describe.unit_glyph`, 11 pictures) is replaced by the new badge. A new page, [Telling devices apart](docs/user-manual/telling-devices-apart.md), has the key to the pictures and the colours. **Limits:** nobody looked at this on a real display (offscreen renderings and tests only); nine colours cannot all stay apart for every kind of colour blindness (green and rose, blue and purple come close), which is why the picture and the shape are there too; the written block diagram and the kind set by hand are in the next entries.
- **You can set the kind of a device by hand (owner decision: *do you want to set a device's kind by hand? yes*).** Select a device and open **Kind** in the **Inspector**, under **Subsystem**: the first entry is **Automatic**, with what the tool would pick (*Automatic: Battery*), then the 19 kinds by name and, last, **Device (plain box)**, which forces the plain outlined box. The card, the tooltip, the legend and the written diagram follow at once, **Undo** takes the change back in one step, and the tooltip and the Inspector say *Looks like: Battery (set by hand)*. The kind is stored on the device as the new optional field `kind` (`logical/units/<subsystem>.json`; the IDs are in [FILE_FORMAT.md](docs/FILE_FORMAT.md#device-kind-after-1150-schema-version-stays-1) and in the JSON Schema). **A project that sets no kind is unchanged**: the field is written only when it is set, so its files, its model hash and every output are byte for byte as before (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print `identical` for the project data), and `schema_version` stays 1 with no migration. A project that sets one has the kind **in its model hash**, as it has the name of a device. A kind that is not in the list is refused with a message that names the allowed ones, when an edit is made and when a project is opened. A released harness does not lock it (like the name and the notes). The kind travels with the device in a device library file, a subsystem file and `system/export.json`. **Not done:** there is no command-line command that edits one device, so there is no `--kind`; the **Add device** window has its own *kind* (it chooses the connectors a new device starts with) and was not changed; the kind cannot be set for several selected devices at once; a tool older than this one refuses a device that has a `kind` until the key is taken out. Nobody looked at this on a real display.
- **The written block diagram and Export diagram show the device looks (owner decision: *should the exported and written diagram show these looks too? yes, without breaking anything*).** `system/block_diagram.svg` and `system/block_diagram.pdf` (every `export`, so **the written block diagram changes for every project**) and the SVG, PDF and PNG of **File > Export diagram** now draw each card with a band of the family colour on its left edge and a badge with the picture of the kind in the shape of the family, then the ID and the name, and a key to the kinds the project has beside the key to the lines. The files are paper, so the colours are those of the light theme, taken from the same table as the window's `device.*` colours, and the pictures are the same path data that the window paints, so the two cannot drift. The looks come from the model only (name, ID, subsystem, links and the kind set by hand), never from the window, its theme, zoom or marks, and every number is written with at most three decimals, so the same design gives the same bytes on Linux and on Windows. The ID is never cut: where it does not fit beside the badge it is set smaller; a long name is cut with `~` a little sooner than before (about 21 characters). **What stays byte for byte as before:** every other written file (the tables, the drawings of the harnesses, `harness_overview.svg` and `.pdf`, `export.json`, the open data), every number (wire lengths, gauges, the BOM ...) and the model hash; only the two block diagram files change, and so `manifest.json`, which lists the hash and size of every written file (checked on the four examples: the only files that differ are `system/block_diagram.svg`, `system/block_diagram.pdf` and `manifest.json`). The independent check (`verify`) still passes. The stored hashes of the block diagram were renewed on purpose with the tools of the repository (`python -m tools.output_hashes --write ...`, `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --write ...`, `python -m tools.gen_output_goldens`); [what changed](docs/changes.md) has a before and after. **Open:** the harness overview keeps its plain boxes; there is no setting to turn the looks off in the written files (they are always on). Nobody looked at the files on a real display; they were drawn offscreen and read back by Qt's SVG renderer.
- **Dialogs and windows no longer lie on top of themselves on a real screen.** On a real display the *Add device* window was too short for its text: the name field lay over the note under it, the *Kind* box over the same note, and the banner over the buttons. A window can be made as small as its layout's smallest size, and that size counted a wrapped note as one line, so a window opened small (by the window manager, or by dragging an edge) squeezed its rows into each other. Every dialog and wizard now keeps its height at what its text needs at its width, and its width at what its layout needs, and grows when a message appears; wrapped text in a split pane is counted in full. A new test opens every dialog, wizard and area of the window at five interface sizes and with larger fonts, in both themes, with long names and with the message shown and hidden, and looks for anything over anything, cut off or squeezed. This changes no project file, no model hash and no written output.
- **A form does not scold before you have typed.** The window *Add device* (and the forms that ask for an ID, a part or a library name) opened with the red banner *A device needs a name.* already showing, next to a disabled button. The banner now appears from your first change on (for example when you type a name and then delete it), and stays while the problem does.
- **Connecting devices feels like picking up a cable (owner feedback).** In the block diagram, press on a port (in Expert mode the small square of a connector, in Guided and Quick mode one port on the edge of the device) and drag: a cable follows the pointer as a line in right angles with a plug at its end. The ports and devices that can take it light up and the rest fade, the status bar says *Drop on a port to connect, ... Esc to cancel*, and the plug snaps to a port within a few pixels. Let it go on a port: the two connectors are connected (the window *Link connectors* opens already filled in with exactly those two connectors; in Quick mode the connection is made at once, as one undo step, with the message *Connected A and B*). Let it go on a device: the tool picks the connector that the signal names fit best, as when two devices are clicked. Let it go on empty ground: the cable stays as a loose end, dashed with a plug; press on it and drag it to a port, or click it and then click a port; a click on empty ground or Esc drops it. A loose end is only on the screen: it is not saved with the project, not in the model hash and in no written file, and saving drops it without a message. A port of the same device, a connector with no free pin and a connector with the cable plug of a harness on it refuse with a message that says why. Hovering a port lights it and says in a tooltip the connector, its kind and how many pins are free. The Connect tool (C), the window *Link connectors* (Ctrl+Shift+K) and Quick mode's one-click Connect work as before. Dragging a card still moves the device, dragging a label or a line still moves that, a drag on empty ground still draws the selection rectangle, and a click on a port only selects the device. The files, the model hash and every written output are unchanged.
- **The end of a connection can be picked up and plugged in elsewhere.** Every end of a connection line on the diagram has a small ring, its plug. Press on it and drag: the connection stays in the project (drawn as a ghost) until the end is put down on another port or on a device; then the same connection (same ID and name) moves to the new connector as one undo step, and its pins are paired again for the two connectors it now joins (by signal name, else by pin number, else none; **Edit pairs** changes them). On empty ground the end becomes a loose end that remembers its connection, and Esc leaves the connection exactly where it was. A connection that a harness was made from, a locked one, and an end that would join a device to itself are refused with the reason. This is the operation `plan_move_link_end` in `core/links.py`.
- **Devices are no longer trapped in the panel (owner feedback).** A device can be dragged anywhere: past the edge of the diagram, above the first lane and to the left of it (below zero). Until now every position was clamped to zero (the drag, Shift+arrow, *Add here* and *Align*), and the scene grew only after the device was let go, so the view could not follow it. Now the scene grows as the device is carried, the scroll bars follow, the view scrolls by itself when a device (or a cable) nears the edge of the window, and nothing snaps back. Pan with the middle mouse button or Space and drag, zoom with Ctrl+wheel, and **F** still fits everything in the window. A move is one undo step; the positions are written and read exactly as before (`logical/layout.json`, the model hash and every written output are unchanged), and the written block diagram and **Export diagram** still frame every device, because they lay the devices out by lane and row and never by canvas position. Nothing keeps devices from lying on each other when you move them.
- **Labels stay on their line and slide along it (owner feedback on 1.14.0).** The pill of an interface, the label of a connection and the connector box at each end of a connection can still be dragged, but they no longer float free in the diagram: wherever the pointer goes, the label sits at the point of its own line that is nearest to the pointer (round the corners too), stops short of the devices at the two ends, and the cursor shows which way it slides. It keeps its place along the line when you move a device, drag the line sideways or the tool routes again (a line that gets shorter holds it inside, a line that gets longer gives it its place back), **Undo** is one step for each drag, and **Reset label and line positions** in the right-click menu puts it back. A label you did not move is placed by the tool as before, so that labels do not lie on top of each other; one you moved stays where you put it, even over another label. In `diagram/marks.json` a label is now `{"id": "interface:IF-1", "t": 0.35}`, its place as a fraction of the length of the line from its first point; the bend of a connection and the vertical piece of a route are still an offset (`dx`, `dy`). A mark written by 1.14.0 or 1.15.0 (a free offset of the label) is read when the project is opened as the point of the line nearest to where the offset put the label, and is written as `t` at the next save. The file keeps `schema_version` 1, stays optional and outside the model hash and every written output (the block diagram that is written does not use it), and a project that was not touched keeps byte-identical files. A tool older than this one sets a mark in the new form aside as unreadable, as it does with any damaged mark, and draws that label where it puts it itself. Nobody has seen this on a real display yet; the tests and the pictures were made on the offscreen platform.
- **The editor could close without a message while a design check finished as you moved a device (found by a failing test run, not reported by a person).** When the check ended on its own thread, Python's garbage collector started there at the same moment as the diagram redrew its links on the main thread, and Python 3.14 can then fail with a segmentation fault; a Qt object in the garbage would also have been destroyed off its own thread. The garbage collector now runs only on the main thread (every half second, with the same thresholds as before), so the check, the autosave and the other background jobs never run it. Nothing changes on the screen.

## 1.15.0 (2026-11-05; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.15.0 is the **choice-and-explanation release** (decision D-198). It carries on from the final audit of the transparency work (`docs/transparency_final_audit.md`) and from the leftovers of 1.14.0. Three more things became models you can choose, each with today's method as the default: how pins are fitted on a connector, which connector the tool picks for an interface and what the labels read. `explain` now shows how the gauge of a wire was chosen and names the page of the model behind a number. Two assumptions that were built into the program (spare contacts and an allowance on the wire in the bill of materials) are settings now, and three design rules that cited no assumption now cite one. The as-built report can have a tolerance on the lengths, and a release can, if you want, require the as-built records. The editor can write the open data from the Export window, shows the model page in the Explain window and the Why tab, lets you drag the line of an interface and keeps Quick mode calmer, and `diagram/marks.json` no longer holds marks of things you deleted. The calculations, the 39 design rules and the numbers are as in 1.14.0 unless you choose another setting.

**The file format and the written files.** The file format keeps `schema_version` 1 and there is no migration. There is no new project file: the new settings are optional keys in files a project may already have (`config/model.json`: `bom_contact_spare_fraction`, `bom_wire_allowance_fraction`, `pin_fit_strategy`, `connector_choice`, `label_format`, `label_wire_template`, `label_connector_template`; `config/generation.json`: `asbuilt_cut_tolerance_mm`, `asbuilt_strip_tolerance_mm`; `config/naming.json`: `release_requires_asbuilt`), and each one defaults to what the tool did before. `diagram/marks.json` may now hold the marks `route:<interface ID>` of dragged interface lines. No written output changes for the packaged examples or the eleven scenarios of the numbers snapshot, apart from the tool version in the stamp and the model hash that follows from it: `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print `identical` for 1.15.0 (`1.10.0`, `1.14.0` and `1.15.0` have the same string length, so `BASE_VERSION` of the stored baseline stays 1.10.0, D-183). It is a minor release because of new features and new optional settings. **Limits:** nobody looked at any of this on a real display (offscreen renderings and tests only); there is no AC or skin-effect model of the voltage drop and no mass model that counts splices or backshells (both need engineering input and were not built); the choice of a connector acts when the tool picks one, not on a connector a person picked; the release window shows the as-built blocker but has no field for the reason of `--accept-asbuilt`, and a release does not freeze the as-built records. The packages are not signed. No one has signed this release off; it is not a statement of compliance.

### Changed for you to know

- **`explain` now shows how the gauge of a wire was chosen (audit R1, owner decision 14).** `explain DIR WIRE` has a new derivation of the kind `gauge`, after the length: the gauges the search tried, from the smallest conductor up, the current limit of each after derating, which carries the current, then the voltage drop of each from there on and the first that keeps it within the limit, the settings it used and the assumptions it rests on (A-AMPACITY-AWG, A-BUNDLE-ALL, A-TEMP-FACTOR, A-DC-SOLID, A-ROUND-TRIP). It replays the search with the function that `generate` calls (`size_wire` now also returns the gauges it tried), so it cannot disagree with the gauge chosen; a test compares them on the four packaged examples. For W004-003 of the `flatsat` example it shows AWG 26 to 18 too small for 5 A and AWG 16 carrying it with 5.4 A. A gauge set with a reason, a locked wire or a wire drawn by hand says so instead of showing a search, and a wire whose gauge no longer equals what the search gives now says that too. It is computed on demand: `explain --all` and `derivations.json` / `derivations.csv` are byte for byte what they were, and no number, no written output and no hash changes.
- **`explain` prints the link to the model page (owner decision 15).** The text of `explain` now ends with `Model page: docs/models/<page>.md` (one line for each page that explains a kind of number it showed, in the order of the derivations), and `explain --json` gives each derivation the key `model_page` with the same path, last in the record (additive; `docs/CLI.md` lists the row `explain OBJECT`). The new method `Derivation.to_explain_dict()` is what the command prints; `Derivation.to_dict()` and the files `derivations.json` and `derivations.csv` do not have the key and are unchanged. The editor's *Show the calculation* is not changed by this entry.
- **Optional tolerance for the lengths of the as-built report (owner decision 10).** Two new optional settings in `config/generation.json`, `asbuilt_cut_tolerance_mm` and `asbuilt_strip_tolerance_mm` (millimetres, 0 or more), both not set by default, which is what the tool did before: no tolerance, every difference listed as *differs from design*. With one set, the as-built report (`asbuilt`, `system/asbuilt_report.*`, the As-built sheet) and `explain` mark a recorded length that differs from the design by more than the tolerance as *out of limit* and one within it as *within limit*, like a test value: actual, limit (the tolerance), margin and source (assumption A-ASBUILT-TOLERANCE, new). A distance equal to the tolerance is within; a length exactly as designed stays *as designed*. A length out of tolerance makes `asbuilt` end with exit code 1, as a value out of its limit does. `explain WIRE` shows a strip length only when the strip tolerance is set. The tolerances are not read by generation, so setting one does not make the harness plans out of date; the tool supplies no value. The `asbuilt --json` document has the keys it had. A project without the keys is read, reported, explained and hashed exactly as before.
- **Opt-in release gate on the as-built records (owner decision 11).** New optional setting `release_requires_asbuilt` in `config/naming.json` (true or false, not set = false). Off, a release is exactly what it was. On, `release` is blocked with `asbuilt_incomplete` while the harness has no as-built records or a value the sheet asks of it is missing or stale (the needed values of `docs/models/asbuilt.md`), and with `asbuilt_length_out` while a recorded cut or strip length is out of the tolerance the project sets (the tolerances of the entry above; without one no length is out). `release --accept-asbuilt REASON` (at least 10 characters) releases anyway and writes the reason, with what was open, into the comment of the release in the change log and the baseline, the way `--accept-placeholders` and `--accept-unapproved-parts` do. A test value that is out of its limit is shown by the report and is not part of the gate. The records are **not frozen** by the release in this step. The key is not read by generation, so switching it does not make the harness plans out of date. The editor shows the blocker in its release window and has no field for the reason yet.
- **The label format is a model you can choose (`model.label_format`, requirement R4).** `standard` (the default, the text the tool always printed), `compact` (the wire ID and the far end only; a connector `ID mates DEVICE-CONNECTOR`), `descriptive` (adds the interface of the wire and the device of a connector) and `custom`, which fills in two templates, `model.label_wire_template` and `model.label_connector_template`, from the places `{harness}`, `{wire}`, `{signal}`, `{name}`, `{interface}`, `{emc}`, `{end}`, `{from}`, `{to}` (a connector: `{harness}`, `{connector}`, `{name}`, `{mates}`, `{device}`). A place that does not exist is refused when you set it and named; nothing is executed. Generation warns (`label_not_unique`) when two labels of a harness read the same, and `explain DIR WIRE` or `explain DIR CONNECTOR` names the format, its rule and whether the labels differ. The colour table stays the setting `generation.wire_colour_by_signal`. See [selectable models](docs/models/selectable-models.md#label-format).
- **Segmentation was a choice all along: the audit text is corrected.** `segmentation.mode` (`per_connector_pair`, `per_unit_pair`, `per_zone_pair`) and the `segregation` keys select how interfaces share a harness, and `explain DIR HARNESS` names the mode and the key. No second selector was added: one harness for everything has no meaning for a harness with two ends, and one harness for each interface cannot be built where two interfaces share a connector. The table of the keys is on [selectable models](docs/models/selectable-models.md#segmentation) and the audit (`docs/transparency_final_audit.md`, R4) says so.
- **The choice of a connector is a model you can choose (`model.connector_choice`, requirement R4).** When the tool picks the connector of an interface at a device, it takes by default the first free connector that carries the type (`first_free`, as ever). `fewest_spare_pins` takes the free connector with the fewest pins left over once the interface is on it (best fit), `most_spare_pins` the one with the most left over; ties go by connector ID, and a connector that is used, does not carry the type or is a person's pick is never touched. `explain DIR CONNECTOR` names the model in force and lists the candidates in its order with the pins it chose from. A connector already chosen does not move. See [selectable models](docs/models/selectable-models.md#connector-choice).
- **The pin fit strategy is a model you can choose (`model.pin_fit_strategy`, requirement R4).** `first_fit` (the default, what the tool always did: the first free run of pins from pin 1 up), `last_fit` (from the last pin down, power on the last pins and the signals below it) and `spread` (a free pin between one interface and the next when there is room, else first fit). Locked pins, fixed pinouts, the gaps between power and signals and between power and return hold in every strategy. A project without the key generates the same files byte for byte; with it, `explain DIR CONNECTOR.PIN` names the strategy in force and its rule, a change makes the harness plans out of date, and generating again re-places the pins (an earlier allocation is kept only while it was made by the same strategy). See [selectable models](docs/models/selectable-models.md#pin-fit-strategy).
- **The Explain window and the Why tab name the page of the model.** Under each calculation the window *How this was calculated* and the **Show the calculation** part of the Why tab show a quiet line such as *Model page: docs/models/wire-sizing.md*, the page of the documentation that describes the model behind the number (the table of kinds was in the code since 1.12.0 but no screen printed it). It is selectable text only: nothing is opened and nothing goes to a network. The text of `harness-design-studio explain` and every written file are unchanged.
- **The Export window can write the open data (GUI for `export-data`).** A new check box **Include the open data (JSON and CSV of every result)** in the Export window, off by default and remembered, writes the same 34 files as `harness-design-studio export-data` (same bytes for the same design) into the folder `data/` of the outputs folder. They are not part of the manifest and the independent check does not read that folder (`read_folder` skips `data/`); the Outputs list shows them as *Open data*; with the box off the export writes exactly the files it wrote before. The run record stays a command-line option. If the folder cannot be written, the outputs stay and a warning says so.
- **The line of an interface can be dragged sideways.** The vertical piece of the line the tool routes between two devices can be dragged left or right, like the bend of a connection, when it runs where you do not want it. It never enters a device card (it stops a few units short of it, and stays within reach of the devices), every piece stays level or plumb, it keeps its offset when devices move, and **Undo** is one step. **Reset label and line positions** in the right-click menu puts the label and the line back (the entry of an interface was *Reset label position*). Where it was moved to is kept in `diagram/marks.json` as `route:<interface ID>`; the file stays outside the model hash and every written output, and the routes of the written block diagram do not change.
- **`diagram/marks.json` no longer keeps the marks of a deleted connection or interface.** What you dragged on a connection or an interface (label, connector boxes, bend) is dropped from the file when the thing is deleted: it is left out when the project is saved and dropped, silently, when a project is opened; a project whose marks all vanish loses the file as before. While the project is open the marks wait in memory, so **Undo** of the delete gets them back. The file is still outside the model hash and every written output.
- **Quick mode is quieter (leftovers of 1.14.0).** With Quick mode on, the status bar no longer shows the automatic *Checking the design rules* progress and the project tree no longer shows the *N pending* and *Placeholder* chips of **Configuration**; both come back when you leave Quick mode. The check itself still runs in the background, and a check you ask for (Ctrl+K, *Check*) still shows its progress. Only what the window shows changes: no project file, model hash or written output does.
- **Two of the assumptions that were built into the program are settings now: spare contacts and an allowance on the wire in the bill of materials.** `model.bom_contact_spare_fraction` adds, for each harness and contact part, that fraction of the contacts used as spares (rounded up to a whole contact), and `model.bom_wire_allowance_fraction` adds that fraction of the metres of every wire part (assumptions `A-BOM-CONTACTS` and `A-BOM-NO-ALLOWANCE`). Both are 0 by default, so a project that sets nothing gets the quantities it always got (the hashes of the outputs and the numbers snapshot are identical); a value from 0 to 1 is accepted, anything else is refused with the name of the key. `explain` of a part lists the extra lines and the fraction. The other 12 assumptions that were built in stay built in, each with the reason on the page of its model and in `docs/transparency_final_audit.md` (*Stays built in, why*): they are statements about the method and not numbers or choices among alternatives. The count is now 34 assumptions, 21 of them settings and 13 built in, and 64 settings.
- **The three limit rules that cited no assumption now cite one.** `pin-shared` rests on `A-PIN-ONE-WIRE` (built in), `wire-voltage` on `A-WIRE-VOLTAGE` and `emc-class-split` on `A-EMC-ONE-BUNDLE` (the settings `derating.wire_voltage_factor` and `emc.same_class_one_bundle`), so `explain` of their findings lists the assumption under *Rests on*. Owner decision 16 of the final audit. No finding, limit or margin changes.

## 1.14.0 (2026-11-04; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.14.0 is the **quick-diagram release** (decision D-197). Workshop and owner feedback said that the tool was too much for a systems engineer who only wants a quick block diagram, that labels on the diagram lay on top of each other, and that things should be movable. This release answers with a **Quick mode** (off by default, on top of any role) with one-click **Connect**, a new **Export diagram** command, a visible **Role** button in the top bar, a diagram whose labels and connector boxes no longer overlap, and labels you can drag to where you want them. The calculations, the 39 design rules and the numbers are as in 1.13.0.

**The file format and the written files.** The file format keeps `schema_version` 1 and there is no migration. The only new project file is the optional `diagram/marks.json` (where labels were moved to; written only when something was moved, outside the model hash and outside every written output). Quick mode is a per-user setting (`ui/quick`), not part of a project. No written output changes for the packaged examples or the eleven scenarios of the numbers snapshot, apart from the tool version in the stamp and the model hash that follows from it: `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print `identical` for 1.14.0 (`1.10.0`, `1.13.0` and `1.14.0` have the same string length, so `BASE_VERSION` of the stored baseline stays 1.10.0, D-183). It is a minor release because of new features and one new optional file. **Limits:** nobody looked at any of this on a real display (offscreen renderings and tests only); the marks of a deleted connection stay in `diagram/marks.json` unused; Quick mode keeps the *Checking the design rules...* progress in the status bar and the *N pending* chips of the project tree; the sheet of the exported diagram is cut to its content only in **Export diagram** (the normal export keeps its fixed sheet). The packages are not signed. No one has signed this release off; it is not a statement of compliance.

### Changed for you to know

- **Quick mode: Add device asks only for a name, and a page for the quick way (*Make a block diagram in five minutes*).** With Quick mode on, **Add device** asks for a name and, if you want, a kind; the tool makes the ID, the place and a connector (the connectors of the kind, else one starter connector), so that **Connect** always works, and the diagram stays in front. A project opened in Quick mode lands on the diagram. The new manual page `docs/user-manual/quick-diagram.md` gives the five steps (Quick mode, New project, Add device, Connect, Export diagram), what Quick mode hides and how to leave it.
- **One-click Connect in Quick mode.** With Quick mode on, joining two devices does not open the *Link connectors* window: the connection is made at once as **one undo step**. A device with no connector gets a starter connector (a 9-pin D-sub) in the same step; the connectors are the pair the signal names fit best; the pins are paired by signal name, else by pin number, else not at all (a connection with no pair is valid: it is drawn, exported in the connection list and passes the independent check). A message says *Connected A and B* with **Undo** and **Edit pairs**. The choice of the best connectors moved from the window into `core/links.py` (`best_connectors`, `plan_quick_link`). Without Quick mode the window behaves as before.
- **Quick mode: a short layout for a quick block diagram (a view setting on top of any role, off by default).** Turn it on in the **Role** button's menu (below the three roles), in **View > Quick mode**, on the **Settings** page or with Ctrl+K. The rail then lists only the Model, Home and Settings, the top bar keeps the project name, Undo and Redo, the Role button, a *Quick* marker and **Export diagram** (no Generate, Check, Release, hash or status chip), the problem notices are forced off (your own choice comes back when you leave Quick mode), there is no tour and no first-start question, and a project opens on the diagram. It works with every role, is stored as `ui/quick` apart from the role's layout memory, takes effect at once, and turning it off gives back exactly the layout of the role. Nothing is locked: every hidden area and command stays in the menus and in Ctrl+K. It changes only what the window shows; no project file, model hash or written output changes.
- **Export diagram… (File menu, Ctrl+K): the block diagram as SVG, PDF and PNG in one step.** It asks for a file name and writes the block diagram of the devices and their connections as `<name>.svg`, `<name>.pdf` and `<name>.png`. The sheet is cut to its content (devices, lane titles, links, legend, title and stamp, plus a margin) so a small set-up fills the picture; the files of the normal export keep their fixed sheet (`core.outputs.canvas.fit_sheet`). It needs no project folder, no harness, no check, no resolved placeholder and no Generate; a project with only devices, only interfaces or only connections works, and a project with no device says *Nothing to draw yet*. It changes nothing in the project and writes no file the full export writes (the output hashes do not change); it is there in every role.
- **The role is now visible in the top bar.** A **Role** button shows who the window is set up for (*Role: Systems engineer*, *Electrical engineer* or *AIT engineer*) and a click lists the three roles. Until now the role could only be chosen in the window of the first start, in **View > Role** or on the Settings page, and a new user did not find it. It is the same choice as before: nothing else about roles changed, no project file and no written output is affected.
- **The diagram no longer draws labels on top of each other (workshop feedback).** On a connection between two connectors, the label in the middle and the connector box at each end keep clear of each other and of the devices; a label that is too long is cut with an ellipsis, and the tooltip of the line has it whole. The hint over a diagram without connections ("No links yet ...") sits below the names of the lanes and clear of the devices. A long hint of the Connect tool no longer widens the tool row and with it the smallest size of the window, so "More" stays in view down to a window width of 1000 px. Only the drawing changes: no project file, number or written output does.
- **Labels on the lines can be moved (workshop feedback).** Drag the pill of an interface, or the label of a connection, when it hides something you want to read; on a connection you can also drag the connector box at each end and the vertical piece of the line (sideways). A moved thing keeps its place relative to its line when devices move, **Undo** puts it back, and **Reset label position** in the right-click menu returns it to the automatic place. Where things were moved to is kept in the new optional file `diagram/marks.json` (written only when something was moved, so a project that was not touched keeps byte-identical files). It is not part of the model hash and no written output carries it.

## 1.13.0 (2026-11-04; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.13.0 is the **follow-up of the transparency release** (decision D-196, for D-195 and the follow-ups of D-191 and D-193). It closes three gaps that the audit of 1.12.0 left open (`docs/transparency_final_audit.md`): every number of `system/export.json` now has its unit, a config file can carry its own comments (opt-in), and a test that is about a wire with an override or a deviation says so. Nothing else is new: the calculations, the 39 design rules and the numbers are as in 1.12.0.

**The file format and the written files.** The file format keeps `schema_version` 1 and there is no migration. One written file changes on purpose: `system/export.json` gains the top-level object `field_units` (nothing is renamed or moved, `format_version` stays 1). The test outputs (`tests.csv`, the *Tests* sheet of the build workbook, the as-built report) gain a `Deviations` column and the band *Overrides and deviations* only for a harness in which a test is about a wire with an override or a deviation; a project without either writes the same bytes as before. A config file has `_about` only when someone asked for it (`settings --annotate`), and it is outside every hash. For the four packaged example projects and the eleven scenarios of the numbers snapshot, `system/export.json` is the only written file that differs from 1.12.0 (`docs/changes.md`): `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print `identical` for 1.13.0 (`1.10.0`, `1.12.0` and `1.13.0` have the same string length, so `BASE_VERSION` of the stored baseline stays 1.10.0, D-183), apart from the tool version in the stamp and the model hash that follows from it, as in every release. No one has signed this release off; it is not a statement of compliance.

### Changed for you to know

- **`system/export.json` states the unit of every number (a written file changes on purpose, D-195; first released as 1.13.0).** The file has a new top-level object `field_units` that maps the path of each numeric field to its unit: `"harnesses[].wires[].length_m": "m"`, `"interfaces[].max_current_a": "A"`, `"parts[].mass_per_m_g": "g/m"`, `"harnesses[].wires[].gauge_awg": "AWG"` (the gauge stays the plain AWG number in the file, decision D2), `"parts[].conductors": "none (count of conductors in one metre of the part)"` for a number without a unit. Nothing is renamed or moved and `format_version` stays 1, so a reader that does not know the object is not affected; the key `units` still holds the devices, which is why the new object is called `field_units`. The table behind it is one place in the code (`core/outputs/export_units.py`), held by a test against the fields of the model and against the numbers of real files. The output check reports `out_json` when a number of the file has no unit; a file from before this change (no `field_units`) is still accepted. The baseline of the written files and the golden outputs are renewed; of the files of the four examples only `export.json` differs (`docs/changes.md`).

### Added

- **Comments in the config files: `_about` (transparency work, R3, D-191 (2), open decision 13; opt-in).** JSON has no comments, so a person who opened `config/derating.json` saw a number and not its unit, range, source or use. A config file may now carry the reserved top-level key `_about`: for every setting of the file its `label`, `unit`, `range`, `source`, `what`, `used_by`, `default` and `assumption`, made from the registry (`core/settings.py`). `harness-design-studio settings DIR --annotate` writes or refreshes it in every existing config file (it creates no file and changes no value, and saves only when a file changes), `--no-annotate` takes it out, `new --annotate-config` starts a new project with it (off by default), and the scripting interface has `annotate_config(folder, remove=False)` and `create_project(..., annotate_config=False)`; `--annotate` and `--no-annotate` take `--json` (`settings --annotate` in the reference). **It is a comment, not a setting:** loading ignores it for values, `settings --set` refuses it, a damaged `_about` is ignored with the warning `config_about_ignored` and the values of the file are kept, and the JSON Schema of `config/*.json` allows it. **It is not part of the design:** it is left out of the model hash (`layout.bytes_for_hash`, also for the files hash of the journal), of the inputs that tell whether the harnesses are out of date and of every number and written output, so annotating a project makes nothing stale (a test compares the model hash, the exported outputs and the open data with and without it); the change journal records no row for it; a save writes it back unchanged and a value edit (`--set`, `set_config`, the editor, a profile) keeps it. A project without `_about` is opened and saved byte for byte as before: `schema_version` stays 1, no migration, the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical. **A stale entry is a warning:** `validate` and `config` (and `config --json`, key `comment_warnings`) name the file and the key when an entry differs from what the registry says now, has no entry for a setting, or is for a key the tool does not know; the warning never fails a command and never reaches generation, and `--annotate` fixes it. On the engineering values page of the editor the tooltip of each value now also shows the range and the source. Documented in `docs/CONFIG.md` (*Comments in the files: `_about`*), the user manual page *See what the tool assumes*, `docs/FILE_FORMAT.md` and `docs/transparency_final_audit.md` (R3 and open decision 13). **Not built:** it is not on by default; a save does not refresh a stale entry by itself; there is no place for notes of your own per key.
- **Overrides and deviations are shown on the tests (transparency follow-up, R7).** For a harness in which a test is about a wire with an override or a deviation, `tests.csv` has a last column `Deviations` (`override gauge_awg: reason; deviation current-over-wire: reason`) and the *Tests* sheet of the build workbook starts with the band *Overrides and deviations* (the lines of *Start here*) and has the same column; the as-built report repeats the mark on the line of a measured test. The output check reads all of it. A project without an override or a deviation writes the same bytes as before (see `docs/changes.md`).

## 1.12.0 (2026-11-04; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.12.0 is the **transparency release** (decisions D-191 to D-194): a glass box for systems, electrical and AIT engineers. Every number the tool makes can be opened and followed (`explain`, the Why tab, the Explain window), every setting and assumption a result depends on is listed with its unit, value and source, the calculation choices that were built into the program are settings you can change, an engineer's override of a generated value needs a reason and goes into a change journal, an error can be accepted as a recorded deviation, imports are recorded and say what they read and converted, every result can be exported as data and reproduced outside the tool, the whole tool can be scripted from Python, rules and tables can be added without editing the tool (Python plug-ins only after the user trusted the code), and AIT can capture what was built. The work was done in stages (the audit, step 0, then S1 to S10; `docs/transparency_plan.md`, `docs/transparency_final_audit.md`) and the entries below are those of the stages, kept as they were written.

**The file format and the written files.** The file format keeps `schema_version` 1 and there is no migration. Everything new is in optional files that a project may or may not have: `config/model.json`, `imports/records.json`, `journal.json`, `asbuilt/records.json` and the folder `extensions/`; a project without them opens and saves as before. For the four packaged example projects and the eleven scenarios of the numbers snapshot nothing that is written changes: `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` and `python -m tools.numbers_snapshot --check tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` print `identical` for 1.12.0 (`1.10.0`, `1.11.1` and `1.12.0` have the same string length, so `BASE_VERSION` of the stored baseline stays 1.10.0 and is not written again, D-183), apart from the tool version in the stamp and the model hash that follows from it, as in every release. The exceptions are those that the entries say: a project with a bundle-factor table of its own gets a new model hash because the note on a sized wire changed (`docs/changes.md`), and a project that uses a new feature (an override, the journal, an import record, as-built records, an extension) writes the files that feature adds. The 39 design rules are unchanged; the rules of an extension are its own and only run where the project enables them. The numbers and the findings of an existing project are as in 1.11.1: this is a release that explains and records, and it moves no number unless a project chooses a new setting. The program was not looked at on a real display by a person; nothing here is a statement of compliance.

### Changed for you to know

- **New optional project files.** `config/model.json` (the calculation choices), `imports/records.json` (what each import read), `journal.json` (the change journal), `asbuilt/records.json` (as-built records) and `extensions/` (rules, tables and plug-ins). None is needed and none is part of the model hash; a project that has as-built records also gets an as-built report and sheet in its outputs.
- **`new` starts a change journal** (`journal.json`) unless you pass `--no-journal`; the editor's New project does the same, and `journal --start` starts one for an existing project. A project without the file has no history before the day it starts one.
- **Overriding a generated wire needs a reason.** A gauge, wire type, colour or length set by hand on a generated wire is an override with a reason (the Edit wire tab, *Edit all wires*, `override DIR WIRE FIELD VALUE --reason TEXT`); an edit without a reason is refused. A wire locked in an earlier version keeps its lock and shows as *Locked, no reason recorded*.
- **The gauge shows its cross-section on screen (decision D2).** `24 (0.205 mm²)` in the tables, the Edit wire tab, the Problems panel, the explanations and the text of `generate`. The written files (drawings, CSV, workbook, reports) keep the plain gauge.
- **`derivations.json` has format number 3** (the cross-section is `area_mm2` in the inputs; a text result has a `text` key). It is new in this release; a reader that checks the format number must be told. `derivations.csv` has the column *Text*.
- **`import-*` print a read summary and `import-lengths` echoes every value.** Before the rows: `Read N row(s) ...`, the columns read, a `warning:` for each column left out and the unit read (given or assumed); `import-lengths` prints `row 2: OK W001 / W001-L1: 5.0 mm = 0.005 m` and takes `--unit mm|cm|m|in|ft` (default `mm`). A script that reads the old `row 2: OK` lines must be adjusted. The tool's own stamped CSV files can be imported again, and the apostrophe that protects text from a spreadsheet is taken away on import.
- **`validate` checks the values of `config/*.json`** like `generate` and `drc`: a value that `generate` refuses (`bundle_derating` of 1.5) now makes `validate` print `config_invalid` and exit 1.
- **Three roles: Systems engineer, Electrical engineer, AIT engineer.** *Electrical / AIT engineer* is split; the stored value `electrical` and the older `electrical_ait` both mean the Electrical engineer. The AIT engineer opens on the Next step page and has the new As-built page; the first-start question has a third button and View > Role a third entry. Nothing is locked; the command palette reaches everything.
- **A scripting interface and `--json`.** `harness_design_studio.api` (`API_VERSION` 1, frozen by a snapshot) and `--json` on every command with a result in data form; `export --out FOLDER` writes the outputs elsewhere.
- **A model hash changes for a project with a bundle table of its own** (see above); a project with the ECSS table, a single bundle factor or none is written as before.
- **Python plug-ins run with your rights and are not sandboxed.** They are never loaded unless the project enables them and you trusted that exact code; the trust is per user and computer.
- **The Windows and Linux packages are not signed** (as before; D-186). Windows SmartScreen shows *Windows protected your PC*.
- **Open for the owner:** the decisions listed in `docs/transparency_final_audit.md` ("The owner's open decisions"), among them the legacy lock, the author of a journal entry, the trust for plug-ins and the strictness of the roles; no one has signed this release off.

### Fixed

- **An explanation names the setting that counts the conductors, and a difference of two gauges has no cross-section (transparency work, S10).** The source of *Conductors counted n* in the voltage drop said "2 for power and ground, 1 otherwise" even after `model.return_path_conductors` was changed; it now names `config/model.json: return_path_conductors` or `single_path_conductors` and says whether the category is in `return_path_categories`. The text `over the limit by 2 AWG` (an extension rule on a gauge) was shown as `2 AWG (33.6 mm²)`; a margin of gauge numbers is not a gauge and gets no cross-section. Only explanations on screen change; no written file, number or finding does (the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical).
- **`validate` checks the values of `config/*.json` like `generate`, `drc` and `config` do (transparency work, D-191).** With `bundle_derating` set to 1.5, `validate` printed `0 error(s)` and exited 0 while `generate` stopped with `config_invalid`. It now prints the same `config_invalid` error and exits 1. A project whose values are valid gets the same output as before. `docs/CONFIG.md` already said the commands use the same checks. `tests/test_validate_config.py`.
- **The note on a sized wire cites "Table 6-41" only for the table of the ECSS profile.** Before, any table of bundle factors was cited as the standard's. For a table of one's own the note names `config/derating.json, bundle_factor_by_count`. No number changes; the note is stored in the generation record, so a project with a table of its own gets a new model hash (`docs/changes.md`). `tests/test_bundle_citation.py`.
