- **A CSV file the tool wrote can be imported again (transparency work, step 6, D-191, D-193).** The first line of every CSV the tool writes (`# harness-design-studio VERSION model HASH`) made `import-lengths`, `import-build-data` and `import-parts` read it as the heading row, so `system/bom.csv` could not be fed to `import-parts`, and the text the tool marks with an apostrophe so that a spreadsheet does not run it (`'+28V`) came back with the apostrophe from `import-pinout`. Every reader now skips the stamp line of the tool's own file and gives back the marked text; a file that does not start with that line is read exactly as before. `tests/test_import_records.py`. Taking the apostrophe away again is an agent decision for the owner to confirm.

### Changed

- **The cross-section in mm² is shown beside every AWG gauge a person reads (decision D2, D-193).** In the wire and pin tables of the Harness tab (`24 (0.205 mm²)` in the Gauge column; the number still sorts), the *AWG* column of the lists, the Edit wire tab (the cross-section beside the field as you type), the Problems panel (a gauge in a finding), the Why lines, the explanations (`Gauge = 24 AWG (0.205 mm²)`, in `explain`, the Explain window and the Why tab), the Assumptions page (`AWG 24 (0.205 mm²): 2.0` in the table of ampacities), the import preview of the ampacity table and the text of `generate`. **The written files keep the plain gauge**: the drawings, the CSV lists, the workbook, the design rule report and the finding texts are byte for byte as before (the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical). In data the cross-section is a number: `area_mm2` in the inputs of `derivations.json` (format 3; the other keys are as before) and a column of `wire_sizing`.
- **Every import says what it read, left out and converted; `import-lengths` echoes every value (transparency work, step 6, D-191, D-193).** `import-lengths` printed `row 2: OK` for a bare `5.0` that became 0.005 m. It now prints `row 2: OK W001 / W001-L1: 5.0 mm = 0.005 m` (the value as written, the unit, the metres stored) and `Unit: length in mm (assumed; --unit says another).`. All file imports (`import-parts`, `import-lengths`, `import-build-data`, `import-pinout`, `import-netlist`, `import-devices`, `import-subsystem`, `config --ampacity-csv`) now print, before the rows, `Read N row(s) ...`, the columns they read and a `warning:` for every column they leave out (the parts and build data imports ignored such columns without a word). The lines that were printed before are printed as before. The CLI reference shows the format (`docs/CLI.md`).

### Added

- **The workflow shown, as-built capture for AIT, and the third role (transparency work, stage S9, D-191, D-193, requirement R6).** The build workbook is no longer write-only, the steps of the tool say what they use and make, and the role *Electrical / AIT engineer* is two roles.
  - **The workflow in one place** (`core/workflow.py`): the nine steps (define the devices, connect them, set the engineering values, generate, check, enter the build data, write the outputs, release, record what was built), each with what it **uses** and **makes** (project files, configuration files, output files, `export-data` tables), the **intermediates** a person can look at and, where the tool allows it, change (the sizing of a wire, the segmentation groups, the pin allocation, the readiness checks, the derived lengths, with `explain`, `export-data` and `override`), the command and the place on screen, and the **state** worked out from the project (done, to do, pending, out of date, needs attention, blocked and by which steps). It is shown in three places that read it: the command `harness-design-studio workflow DIR [--outputs FOLDER] [--json]` (and `api.get_workflow`), the page `docs/user-manual/follow-the-workflow.md` (its two tables are made from the code by `python -m tools.gen_workflow_doc`; a test fails when the page and the code differ, and when a step names a project file, a configuration file, an output file, a table or a command the tool does not have), and, on the Next step page, a disclosure **What this step uses and makes** that is **closed by default** and worked out only while it is open. The six steps of the Next step page and the three steps of the top bar take their words from the same table (`PAGE_LABELS`, `BAR_LABELS`). **The hidden-link problem is fixed:** the status bar's *n values pending* link is a problem notice and is off for the Systems engineer, so that role saw no sign of engineering values still to set; the Next step page now shows a quiet line with the count and a button **Set the values…** in every role.
  - **As-built capture** (`core/asbuilt.py`, new optional file `asbuilt/records.json`, opt-in by existence like the journal and the import records): per harness, part lot, wire, connector, pin and test, the serial number(s), the lot of the wire and connector parts, the cut length (m) and the strip lengths (mm), the crimp tool and its calibration ID, who built and who inspected (names typed, no authentication), the result of each test of the Tests sheet and its measured continuity (ohm), insulation (Mohm) and test voltage (V), notes and a non-conformance text. It holds no time, user name or path and is **not part of the model hash**; the journal (when the project keeps one) records who saved a record and when, as entries of the new kind `asbuilt`. `export-asbuilt DIR FILE.csv|.xlsx [--harness ID]` writes the sheet to fill in (the designed values are filled in, the as-built columns are empty); `import-asbuilt DIR FILE [--dry-run]` reads it back and echoes what it read, dropped and converted like the other imports (the unit of a column is the one in its heading; an empty cell never clears a value; a row the design cannot take is refused and then nothing is changed) and writes an import record (kind `asbuilt`); `asbuilt DIR [--complete] [--json]` reports what is complete, **missing**, **out of its limit** (actual, limit, margin, against the project's three test limits) and different from the design (cut length and strip lengths, no tolerance applied); `api.get_asbuilt`, `api.import_asbuilt`, `api.write_asbuilt_sheet`. The derivation kind `asbuilt-result` (`core/derive_asbuilt.py`, page `docs/models/asbuilt.md`) explains each measured value and each cut length with `explain`. **The tool never says that anything passed or is compliant**: a result recorded as pass with a value out of its limit is shown as out of limit, and nothing is accepted automatically. No design rule is added (still 39). **Written outputs**: a project **with** records also gets `system/asbuilt_report.md` and `.csv` and an *As-built* sheet in the build workbook of each harness that has records, and its manifest has `asbuilt_hash` so that the outputs are out of date when the records change; a project **without** records writes every file byte for byte as before (the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical). Pages: `docs/user-manual/record-what-was-built.md`, `docs/models/asbuilt.md`, the format in `docs/FILE_FORMAT.md`, the schema `asbuilt` in `harness-design-studio schema`.
  - **Three roles.** *Electrical / AIT engineer* is split into the **Electrical engineer** (the role that was, with the same stored value `electrical`; `electrical_ait` is read as it) and the **AIT engineer** (build data, build sheets, outputs, as-built records, the workflow; opens on the Next step page; settings keys of its own), next to the unchanged **Systems engineer** (`gui/roles.py`, `gui/areas.py`). A role is a named set of pages on the rail and entries in the menus: the Electrical engineer lists everything but the new **As-built** page; the AIT engineer leaves out the Model area and the 30 menu entries that edit the diagram, and has a button **As-built records…** on the Next step page; the Systems engineer leaves out the same 42 entries as before plus the as-built entries. Nothing is locked and everything is reachable by the command palette. The first-start question has a third button, **View > Role** a third entry. A table-driven test (`tests/test_ui_role_pages.py`) opens the window in each role, lists the visible rail pages and menu entries, and fails if one is hidden in every role. Page `docs/user-manual/choose-your-role.md` updated. The As-built page (`gui/asbuilt_page.py`) shows the values that need attention, with **Import the filled-in sheet…** and **Save the sheet to fill in…**.
  - New: `core/workflow.py`, `core/asbuilt.py`, `core/derive_asbuilt.py`, `core/model/asbuilt.py`, `cli/asbuilt_cmd.py`, `gui/asbuilt_page.py`, `tools/gen_workflow_doc.py`. Tests: `tests/test_s9_workflow.py`, `tests/test_s9_asbuilt.py`, `tests/test_s9_asbuilt_outputs.py`, `tests/test_ui_role_pages.py`, `tests/test_ui_asbuilt.py`.
- **Every model, rule and assumption has a page, and the work is audited again (transparency work, stage S10, D-191, D-193).** `tests/test_models_docs_complete.py` ties `docs/models/` to the code: every design rule has a row in `docs/RULES.md` and an explanation that names its page (`derive_kinds.page_of_rule`), every kind of derivation a page that names it, every assumption a description and every ID a page names exists, every setting is in `docs/CONFIG.md`, every key and choice of `model.json` is on `models/selectable-models.md`, every definition is on the new page `docs/models/definitions.md`, and the contents page links every page. `tools/trace_numbers.py` traces the 16 numbers of the audit again with only `explain`, `export-data` and `settings --json`, checks that each resolution is complete and takes under a minute, and writes `docs/transparency_trace_results.md` (no timing in the file, so it stays the same bytes). `tests/test_definition_of_done.py` asks the four tests of the brief as automated tests. `docs/transparency_final_audit.md` goes through the 14 requirements: what was built, the evidence, the gaps that remain, the owner's open decisions and the limits of the verification. No design rule is added, no written file changes.
- **Open data, the run record and mm² beside AWG (transparency work, stage S5, D-191 (1), D-193 D2).** A colleague's result can now be reproduced from exported files alone, and the same design written twice still gives the same bytes.
  - `harness-design-studio export-data DIR --out FOLDER` (and `api.export_data`) writes every intermediate and final result as a JSON and a CSV file with the unit in each heading and a `format` number: `wire_sizing` (current, gauge, cross-section in mm², base ampacity, bundle size and K, temperature factor, derated limit, length, resistivity, voltage drop, limits, margins, verdicts), `lengths` (with the basis of each), `segment_lengths`, `segmentation_groups`, `segmentation_interfaces`, `pin_allocation`, `rule_results` (actual, limit, margin, verdict), `findings` (with the numbers of their rule), `readiness`, `mass`, `mass_wires`, `bom`, `bom_contributions`, `test_limits`, `parts` and `settings` (every setting in force with value, unit and origin); and `configuration.json` (the configuration files as the tool used them, with their placeholder flags) and `provenance.json` (tool version, model hash, library, the imported files when the project records them, and the SHA-256 of every other file). The numbers come from the functions the generator and the rules call. No file holds a time, a user name or a path. Page: `docs/user-manual/export-the-data.md`.
  - A run record, `run_record.json` (time in UTC to the second, operator, tool version, platform, Python version, model hash, an empty list of extensions), outside every hash: written only with `--run-record` on `export`, `generate` or `export-data` (`--author NAME`, else `HARNESS_AUTHOR`, the Git name, the user of the system), beside the outputs and not in `manifest.json`. `verify --outputs` does not call it an unlisted file. Without the option nothing is written. `api.export` and `api.generate` take `run_record` and `operator` as optional arguments.
  - `docs/examples/open-data/reproduce.py`: a script that imports only `csv`, `json`, `math`, `sys` and `pathlib`, reads an exported folder and recomputes the cross-sections, the derated current limits, the adequacy of the gauges, the voltage drops and the metres of the bill of materials to 9 decimals. A test runs it on every packaged example.
  - Two exports are read back by the importers: `segment_lengths.csv` by `import-lengths --unit m` and `parts.csv` by `import-parts`; `docs/IMPORTS.md` lists which export is read by which importer.
  - New: `core/open_data.py`, `core/run_record.py`, `cli/open_data.py`. Tests: `tests/test_open_data.py`, `tests/test_gauge_area.py`, `tests/test_ui_gauge_area.py`.
- **Extensions: rules, tables and readers without editing the tool (transparency work, stage S8, requirement R13, D-191 (5), D-193).** The audit found that the rules were a code tuple, the output list was hard-wired and nothing could be added without changing the program. Now:
  - **A registry** (`core/extensions/registry.py`) lists everything the tool can run, built-in and extension together: the 39 design rules, the fields an expression can read, the calculation models of `config/model.json`, the tables `export` writes, the lookup tables and the importers, each with its kind, name, version, source (`built-in`, `declarative`, `plugin`), origin, SHA-256, and whether it is enabled and trusted. The built-in items are registered through the same call an extension uses. `harness-design-studio extensions DIR [--all] [--json]` and `api.list_extensions` print it; **Help > Extensions...** shows it in the editor.
  - **Declarative extensions first (data, no code).** A project folder `extensions/` holds `*.json` files with rules (an ID, a title, a severity, the kind of object, a condition, a message with `{actual}`, `{limit}` and `{margin}`, why, how, optional requirement IDs), output tables (extra CSV files written by `export` to `system/extensions/`, with columns made from fields and expressions) and lookup tables (for example an ampacity table of one's own). A condition is written in a small expression language with a hand-written parser and a whitelist of fields, operators and functions (`core/extensions/expr.py`); nothing is handed to `eval`, `exec` or `ast`, and a test reads the module and fails if it did. A comparison gives the rule its actual value, its limit and its margin, so every rule gets the S1 derivation automatically (`explain`, the Explain button, `explain --all`). A rule the file defines has the ID prefix `x-`; `drc`, its report and the Checks tab list it, say where it comes from and name the file, version and hash. A value that is not known gives *not checked*, never a pass. The format is described once (`core/extensions/schema.py`), checked from that description and exported as `extension.schema.json` and `extensions-enabled.schema.json` by `schema`. A problem is reported with the file, the line, the path and the key (`validate` as a warning, `extensions`, the page, and a finding `x-extension-problem`); a broken file is not applied and never stops generation, the check or the export.
  - **Python plug-ins second, opt-in twice.** A plug-in is `extensions/plugins/NAME.py` or an installed package with an entry point in the group `harness_design_studio.plugins`; it declares its name, version, API version and kinds in a literal that is read without running the file, and registers rules, fields, tables and importers through a small API (`core/extensions/pluginapi.py`; a rule must state what it compares, which is its explanation). Python code is **never imported** unless the project lists it in `extensions/enabled.json` **and** the user trusted that exact code. The trust store is in the **user's** settings folder (not in the project, so a cloned repository cannot trust itself) and keys the plug-in's name to the SHA-256 of its source (an installed package: of its Python files); a change of one byte takes the trust away. `harness-design-studio trust DIR [NAME] [--revoke] [--yes]` shows the name, version, file, hash and number of lines and asks for the name to be typed; the editor shows a window that names the code, with the code itself on request, when it opens a project that enables an untrusted plug-in. An untrusted or disabled plug-in is listed and not imported (a test checks that a module with a side effect at import is not executed, in a fresh interpreter too); a load failure is reported and fatal to nothing. `import-plugin DIR NAME FILE [--dry-run]` runs an importer a plug-in added.
  - **Provenance.** Every enabled data file and loaded plug-in (name, version, SHA-256) is listed in `extensions --json`, in `system/provenance.json` and the manifest of the outputs, and in the DRC report; `extensions.manager.loaded(project)` is that list; the run record of S5 (`run_record.json`) and the `provenance.json` of `export-data` carry it too (name, version, kind, file, SHA-256). A project that enables nothing writes the same bytes as before.
  - **Honest about the risk.** A trusted plug-in runs with the rights of the user who trusted it. The tool has no sandbox and does not claim one: the trust store decides whether code runs, not what it does (`docs/SECURITY.md`, `docs/developer/extensions.md`).
  - Unchanged: still 39 built-in design rules (an extension's rules are its own, `x-`); the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical; a project without an `extensions/` folder, or with files it does not enable, gives the same findings and the same files byte for byte (a test). New: `core/extensions/` (registry, builtins, expr, jsonpos, schema, declarative, fields, plugins, pluginapi, trust, manager), `cli/extensions_cmd.py`, `gui/extensions_dialog.py`, `api.list_extensions` (the API snapshot gains that name; `API_VERSION` stays 1), `extensions DIR --docs` (the rules of a project as a table in the form of `docs/RULES.md`). Examples in `docs/examples/extensions/` (a rule *minimum wire gauge for power*, a table with a lookup table, a Python rule), the page `docs/user-manual/extend-the-tool.md`, `docs/developer/extensions.md`.
  - Tests: `tests/test_extensions_expr.py`, `test_extensions_declarative.py`, `test_extensions_plugins.py`, `test_extensions_cli.py`, `test_extensions_examples.py`, `test_extensions_gui.py`, `test_extensions_docs.py`.
- **Everything the tool does can be scripted: a Python interface and `--json` (transparency work, step 7, requirement R11, D-191).** The audit found no documented Python API, no JSON output and no output-folder option. Now:
  - `harness_design_studio.api` (new, no Qt, prints nothing, never ends the program) wraps the functions the commands call: `load_project`, `create_project`, `list_templates`, `validate`, `run_checks`, `list_rules`, `verify`, `generate`, `export` (with an `out` folder), `explain`, `explain_all`, `explain_finding` (the calculation of `core.derive.explain_finding` that the editor's *Explain* shows), `write_derivations`, `get_config`, `get_settings` (the settings of `settings`, with the value in force and where it comes from) and `set_config` (a value is checked as the editor and `config` check it). It adds no behaviour: the tests compare each function with its command, file by file and finding by finding. Results are small frozen dataclasses with `to_dict()`; errors are one tree under `HarnessError` (`ProjectDamagedError`, `ReadOnlyProjectError`, `ConfigValueError`, `NoOutputsError` are new, the rest are the tool's own). Writing functions take the same project lock as the editor.
  - `--json` on every command that has a result in data form (`new`, `templates`, `schema`, `validate`, `check`, `drc`, `verify`, `generate`, `export`, `merge-fix`, `config`, `library`, `import-parts`, `import-lengths`, `import-build-data`, `import-netlist`, `import-pinout`, `import-devices`, `import-subsystem`, `export-pinout`, `export-devices`, `export-subsystem`, `review`, `release`, `revise`, `diff`, `compare`, `log`, `migrate`, and `explain --all`). One document, the same exit code, no time, user name or absolute path, a fixed order, the same bytes for the same design; the keys of each are in `docs/CLI.md` (made from `SHAPES` in `cli/jsonout.py`). The documents of `validate`, `drc`, `verify`, `generate`, `export` and `config` are the `to_dict()` of the interface's results. Without `--json` no byte of any command's output changes.
  - `export --out FOLDER` writes the outputs to FOLDER instead of `<project>/outputs`, byte for byte the same files.
  - Three worked examples in `docs/examples/scripting/` (a batch over several projects with one summary table, a trade study that varies the bundle derating and tabulates gauges, findings and the worst voltage drop, and every derivation as files) and the page `docs/user-manual/script-the-tool.md`. The examples may import only names of `api.__all__`.
  - The interface is kept stable on purpose: `tests/fixtures/api_snapshot.json` holds the names, signatures, result fields and exception bases (`python -m tools.gen_api_snapshot`), the rules are in `docs/developer/api-stability.md`.
  - New: `api.py`, `core/records.py` (the result types, shared by the interface and the command line), `core/validation.py` (what `validate` and `check` look at, moved out of the command, used by both), `cli/jsonout.py`, `tools/gen_api_snapshot.py`. No design rule is added (still 39), no number and no written file changes: the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical.
  - Tests: `tests/test_api.py`, `tests/test_api_stability.py` (the snapshot, and the interface in a process where Qt cannot be imported), `tests/test_cli_json.py` (every command, the keys, the exit codes, the same bytes twice, no absolute path), `tests/test_scripting_examples.py` (the examples run and use only the interface).
- **Explanations for everything else (transparency work, stage S1, D-191, D-193).** `explain` now reaches the numbers and decisions that had no derivation, with the same mechanism (the code that makes the result is the code that explains it; the records are `Derivation`, `Input`, `Step`, `Check`): **(1)** *Ready to build* and each of the eight checks of the build workbook (which checks block, by `model.readiness_blocking`; how many are open; the open wires, pins and parts) with `explain DIR HARNESS`; **(2)** the test limits (continuity, insulation, test voltage): the key of `config/generation.json`, the unit, the tests that use it, *TBD (placeholder)* and what is missing, with `explain DIR HARNESS` or `explain DIR HARNESS.T001`; **(3)** pin allocation (why this signal is on this pin: the order `model.pin_allocation_categories`, the power group and its gaps, first fit, locked and fixed pins, the lines the generator recorded) with `explain DIR CONNECTOR.PIN`, the choice of a connector, and segmentation (why these interfaces share a harness: the mode, chain, class and zones) with `explain DIR HARNESS` or `explain DIR INTERFACE`; **(4)** the labels of a wire and of a connector, the colour of a wire (from the table `wire_colour_by_signal`, typed, or none) and the shield groups with their ends (`explain DIR WIRE`, `explain DIR GROUP`); **(5)** the limit of every design rule: for each rule that compares a number with a limit (spare pins, connector and wire voltage, temperature, mating cycles, the gap between power and return pins, bundle current, wires on a pin, bundles of one EMC class) the finding's object has a derivation with the equation, inputs, steps and the check (actual, limit, margin, verdict), and a rule with no numeric limit says *this rule is a yes/no check: it compares X with Y* and shows the statement, the configuration it reads and what it saw. **The Explain button of the Checks tab now works for every rule** (`EXPLAINABLE_RULES` is the whole registry, the independent verification and the logical rules), and the **Why** tab explains a harness, wire, interface, connector and pin. The findings and every written file are unchanged (the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical). `derivations.json` is format 2 (a `text` key for a result that is a text) and `derivations.csv` has the column *Text*; `explain --all` adds the derivations of the harness, its wires and the rules of each finding. Nineteen assumptions with IDs (A-READY-CHECKS to A-BUNDLE-CURRENT). New pages in `docs/models/`: readiness, test-limits, pin-allocation, segmentation, labels-and-colours, shields, rule-limits; `core/derive_kinds.py` lists every kind of derivation with its page and a test fails for one without. New: `core/derive_build.py`, `core/derive_layout.py`, `core/derive_rules.py`, `core/derive_kinds.py`, `core/generate/shields.py`; `tools/gen_rule_limit_docs.py`. The generator and the build workbook call the same functions as the explanations (`buildsheet.open_items`, `tables.wire_label_texts`, `wirecolours.signal_colour`, `generate.shields.plan`): no output changes.
- **`explain`: how the numbers of a wire were made (transparency work, step 1, D-191).** `harness-design-studio explain DIR WIRE` prints the current the wire's gauge may carry after derating and its voltage drop, each with the equation, the inputs with their units and where each comes from (a key of `config/*.json`, a field of the design, or the program), the intermediate results, what the result is checked against (actual, limit, margin) and the assumptions built into the program that it rests on; `--json` prints the same as data. A value that is not set is named, never made up. The numbers are produced by the functions the generator and the design rules call, so a derivation cannot disagree with the number it explains (`tests/test_derivations.py` compares them wire by wire in seven scenarios, to the last digit). New: `core/derive.py`, `core/assumptions.py`, `docs/models/` (the first page: wire sizing, with a worked example). Nothing that is written changes.
- **Settings and the Assumptions page (transparency work, stage S2, D-191, D-193).** `harness-design-studio settings DIR` lists every setting a result depends on (the keys of the eight configuration files) with the value in force, its unit, where it comes from (the project; the tool's own default, which is the behaviour before the setting existed; or not set), what depends on it and its source, then the assumptions built into the tool with the settings that parameterise them, and the fixed definitions (the AWG formula, the tolerance of a limit comparison, the rounding of the data files). `--json` prints the same as data; `--set FILE.KEY=VALUE` changes a value and refuses one outside its range; **Help > Assumptions…** opens the same page in the editor. The choices that were built into the program are now settings of the new optional file `config/model.json`, each with today's behaviour as its default: `return_path_categories`, `return_path_conductors`, `single_path_conductors` (the voltage drop of a power or ground wire counts the way out and back) and `service_loops_per_wire` (two loops on a generated wire). The explanations follow the settings (*0 × service loop*). `core/settings.py` is the one table; a test checks that every key of every file is described there and in `docs/CONFIG.md`, and a test walks the calculation modules and fails on a number that is neither a setting, a definition nor a listed exception, so a model constant cannot be hidden in the code again. A project that has no `model.json` is written as before and gives the same numbers (the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical). New page `docs/user-manual/see-what-the-tool-assumes.md`.
- **Selectable models (transparency work, stage S3, D-191, D-193).** The remaining calculation choices that were built into the program are settings of `config/model.json`, each with the behaviour of version 1.11 as its default: `bundle_basis` (`all_wires`, `loaded_wires` or `busiest_segment`: which wires the bundle factor K is looked up for), `length_model` (`path_plus_loops` or `path_loops_allowance_twist`, with `cut_allowance_m` and `twist_takeup_fraction`, both 0 by default), `drop_model` (`dc_solid` or `dc_at_ambient`, with `resistivity_temperature_coefficient` and `resistivity_reference_temperature_c`), `mass_model` (`connectors_and_wires` or `full_bom`), `pin_allocation_categories` (the order interface categories get pins) and `readiness_blocking` (which checks of the build workbook turn *Ready to build* into no). Sizing, the rules, the build workbook, the release check and `explain` read the same setting, so the explanation names the model in force. Nothing changes unless a project chooses another value: the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical. Bundle counting moved to one function (`core/generate/bundle.py`). New page `docs/models/selectable-models.md`; `tests/test_selectable_models.py` checks each model against a hand calculation.
- **Overrides with a reason, a change journal and recorded deviations (transparency work, stage S4, D-191, D-193).** A gauge, wire type, colour or length that a person sets on a generated wire is now an **override** that needs a **reason**: the model (`Wire.overrides`, a sparse field: field name to reason, never empty), the Edit wire tab (a *Reason* box that appears when such a value is changed; no Apply without it; the same in *Edit all wires*), `core/wires.py` (`plan_wire_edit(..., reason=)`, `plan_override`, `plan_clear_override`) and the new command `harness-design-studio override DIR WIRE FIELD VALUE --reason TEXT` (`--clear FIELD` gives one value back) refuse an override without one. Generation keeps the values an override names and gives the others the design's value; a wire locked in an earlier version keeps all four as before and is shown as *Locked, no reason recorded*. `explain` starts with *Overridden by the engineer*: the value set, the reason, the value the design would give and what the set value does to the current limit and the voltage drop (actual, limit, margin); the overrides are flagged in the wire table, in the wire list (a last column *Overrides*), in the cut list and *Start here* sheet of the build workbook, beneath the drawing, in `derivations.json` and `.csv`, and in the release record, and only for a project that has an override. **The change journal** (`core/journal.py`, the file `journal.json`, `harness-design-studio journal DIR [--json] [--since SEQ] [--object ID] [--start]`, Help > Change history…) records who changed what, when, from what to what and why: an entry for each changed field of a design input, a configuration value, an override, a waiver, a part approval and a release, and one entry for each harness a generation changed. It is written when the project is saved, from the files whose bytes differ from the ones on disk, so every way of changing a project is covered and saving stays as fast; it is append-only (each entry carries a chain value; a changed, removed or inserted entry makes `validate` report a damaged journal and the editor refuse to save over it); it is a file of its own that no hash and no written output holds, so the same design still gives the same bytes; a project has a journal if and only if it has the file, `new` and the editor's New project start one (`new --no-journal` does not), `journal --start` starts one for an existing project. The author is `--author`, `HARNESS_AUTHOR`, the name Git has, the user of the computer, `unknown`. Waivers and part approvals thereby carry author and date, shown next to a waiver in the Problems list and the Checks panel. **Deviations (D-191 (3)):** an error of an electrical rule (`current-over-wire`, `current-over-contact`, `voltage-drop`, `bundle-current`, `connector-voltage`, `wire-voltage`, `temperature-margin`) can be accepted with a reason (`harness-design-studio deviation`, *Accept as deviation…* on the error card): the finding stays on every list and output marked DEVIATION (the Problems list, the Checks panel, `drc`, `drc_findings.csv`, `drc_report.md`, the drawing, the build workbook and the release record) and no longer blocks a release; it does not meet the requirement. No design rule was added. A project that has no override, no deviation and no journal is opened and saved byte for byte as before: no file changes, `schema_version` stays 1 and there is no migration; the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical. Existing tests that edit a generated wire now give a reason. New page `docs/user-manual/override-and-keep-a-history.md`; `docs/FILE_FORMAT.md` describes `overrides` and `journal.json`. `journal`, `override` and `deviation` take `--json` and have functions in the scripting interface (`get_journal`, `set_override`, `clear_override`, `get_overrides`, `list_deviations`, `accept_deviation`, `remove_deviation`, `start_journal`); `create_project` starts a journal like `new` (`journal=False` opts out). An import is an entry of the journal (kind `import`), and `imports/records.json` is left out of the hash of the journal's files like `journal.json`. The journal and the run record (`export --run-record`) now resolve the person in one place (`journal.resolve_author`: `--author`, HARNESS_AUTHOR, the name Git has, the user of the computer, `unknown`). `export-data` says in `provenance.json` only whether a journal is kept (`design.journal`), and `settings.json` has a row `overrides.wires` when a wire has an override. On the screen a gauge override shows its cross-section; the files keep the plain number. The journal also records a change to `extensions/enabled.json` or an `extensions/*.json` file (kind `config`, object `extension:<file>`, the SHA-256 before and after), and one list (`layout.outside_the_hash`) says what is left out of the model hash: the journal, the import record and the folders `extensions/` and `asbuilt/`.
- **A finding opens its calculation (transparency work, step 1d, D-191).** In the Checks tab, for the rules *Wire current*, *Voltage drop* and *Contact current*, an **Explain** button next to **Show** opens a window with how the limit of the selected finding was calculated: the equation, the inputs with units and sources, the steps, the check (actual, limit, margin) and the assumptions, the same text as `explain` and the Why tab. New: `core.derive.explain_finding` and `EXPLAINABLE_RULES`, and the window `gui/explain_dialog.py`. The findings themselves, and every written file, are unchanged.
- **Every derivation as data (transparency work, step 5a, D-191).** `harness-design-studio explain DIR --all --out FOLDER` writes how every number of the project was made to `derivations.json` (the format number, the tool version, and every derivation with its equation, inputs with units and sources, steps, check, assumptions and what is missing) and `derivations.csv` (one row each: kind, subject, quantity, value, unit, equation, actual, limit, margin, verdict, assumptions, missing). For the flatsat that is 516 derivations. There is no time, user name or path in them, so the same design gives the same bytes; a file can be handed to a colleague, filtered in a spreadsheet (*Verdict* `fail`, *Missing*) or read by a script. Nothing in the project is changed. New: `core/derive_export.py`; the section *Every derivation as files* in `docs/models/README.md`.
- **The contact-current check is explained (transparency work, step 1c, D-191).** `explain DIR INTERFACE.CONNECTOR` (the object name of a *Contact current* finding, for example `IF-001.GPSU1-J01`) shows the limit the rule `current-over-contact` used: the contact rating of the connector part (and the key of the library it was read from) times the contact current factor, the current of the interface and the margin, with what is missing named. The assumption the audit found is written down: A-CONTACT-SHARED (the whole current is compared with the rating of the contacts at each end; it is not split over pins; one factor for all connectors). The rule and the derivation read the rating through the same functions (`drc.rules.contact_rating`, `contact_rating_key`); nothing that is written changes. New page `docs/models/contact-current.md`.
- **The bill of materials is explained (transparency work, step 1c, D-191).** `explain DIR PART` (a part ID of the library, as well as a wire or a harness ID) says why `system/bom.csv` has the quantity it has for the part, in each unit it is counted in: for each harness, how many connectors, contacts, wires and accessories add to it, and for a wire part in metres the sum of the lengths over the conductors of the part. What a line leaves out is stated: A-BOM-CONTACTS (a contact part is counted once for each pin that has a wire) and A-BOM-NO-ALLOWANCE (no spares, overage, cut allowance or waste; a wire with no length adds nothing and is counted in *Wires without length*). To make this possible the BOM function was split into one that lists what is counted (`tables.bom_contributions`) and one that adds it up, in the same order: every written BOM is byte-identical (the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical). New page `docs/models/bom.md`.
- **The mass is explained (transparency work, step 1c, D-191).** `explain DIR OBJECT` now takes a harness as well as a wire. For a harness it itemises the mass: every connector with its part and mass, the wires grouped by part with their total length, the total of what is known, the margin and the mass with margin; for a wire it ends with the wire's share (mass per metre × length / conductors of the part). **Show the calculation** in the Why tab shows the same for a harness. Two assumptions the audit found are now written down with IDs: A-MASS-SCOPE (only connectors and wires; no contacts, backshells, accessories, splices, solder or shield terminations) and A-MASS-CONDUCTORS (a twisted pair part's mass is split between its two wires). New page `docs/models/mass.md`. The per-wire arithmetic moved into one function, `mass.wire_mass_g`, that the harness mass and the derivation both call; no mass changes.
- **The length of a wire is explained (transparency work, step 1c, D-191).** `explain` and the **Show the calculation** section of the Why tab now start with the length: the segments of the wire's path with their lengths, the service loops that generation added (2 × `service_loop_m`), and the total, or, when the length was typed on the wire or does not match its path, a plain statement of that. A length is never of unknown origin, and a missing segment length is named. Two assumptions are listed with IDs: A-PATH-UNIQUE and A-SERVICE-LOOP. New page `docs/models/wire-length.md`. The path function was split so that the derivation uses the very path the length uses (`lengths.path_trail`); no length changes (the numbers snapshot and the output hashes are identical).
- **The Why tab shows the calculation of a wire (transparency work, step 1b, D-191).** Select a wire and press **Show the calculation**: the current its gauge may carry and its voltage drop appear below the recorded reasons, with the equation, the inputs with units and sources, the steps, the check and the assumptions built into the program. It is the text of `explain`, produced by the same code, and closed until asked for, so the tab stays short. Nothing that is written changes.
- **The numbers are kept on purpose (for developers).** `tools/numbers_snapshot.py` and `tests/fixtures/numbers/snapshot.json` hold, for eleven scenarios, the gauge, length, part, colour and lock of every wire, every finding with its text and the SHA-256 of every written file; `tests/test_numbers_snapshot.py` fails and names the wire, finding or file that moved. `docs/changes.md` records every change that moves a result, with before and after.
- **Every import is recorded in the project (transparency work, step 6, D-191, D-193).** An import that is applied writes `imports/records.json`: the kind and name of the file, its SHA-256, the rows read, the rows dropped with the reason, the values converted (as written, the unit, as stored), the columns read and left out, the unit (given or assumed) and the tool version. The path of the file is kept only when it lies inside the project folder, relative to it; there is no time, no operator and no absolute path, so the same import gives the same bytes. Importing the same file again replaces its record; one undo step takes the import and its record back together. A project that never imported has no such file and keeps byte-identical files; the record is not in the model hash and in no output file. The JSON Schema `import-records` is exported by `schema`; the format is in `docs/FILE_FORMAT.md`. `tests/test_import_records.py`, `tests/test_ui_import_records.py`.
- **`imports`: has the source changed? (transparency work, step 6, D-191, D-193).** `harness-design-studio imports DIR [--json] [--check] [--source FILE]` lists the records and compares the SHA-256 of every source that can be reached (its path inside the project folder, or a file named with `--source`) with the recorded one: `unchanged`, `source changed`, `source not found` or `not checked (no path)`. `--check` ends with 1 when a source has changed. In the app, the first page of the import wizard lists the earlier imports and marks a changed source with the words *source changed*, and the Next step page says it once in a quiet line; nothing shows while the project has no record.
- **Units on import (transparency work, step 6, D-191, D-193).** `import-lengths --unit` takes `mm`, `cm`, `m`, `in` and `ft` (the default is still `mm`); `import-build-data --unit` reads the strip and accessory lengths in the same units; `import-parts --mass-unit` reads the masses in `g`, `kg`, `lb` or `oz` (the default is still `g`). Every import says which unit it used and whether it was given or assumed. `docs/models/import-units.md`.
- **The parts import reads ratings (transparency work, step 6, D-191, D-193).** A column headed with the name of a rating (`contact_current_a`, `rated_voltage_v`, `dielectric_withstand_v`, `max_temp_c`, `mating_cycles`, `shield_insulated`, `conductive_finish`, or the key `contact_rating_key` names) is read into the ratings of the part; before, these columns were left out without a word. A file without such a column imports as before.
- **KiCad: the pin type and the name of the schematic are kept (transparency work, step 6, D-191, D-193).** The import record keeps the KiCad pin type of every imported pin and the file name of the schematic the netlist was made from (never its folder). `import-netlist --pin-direction` also fills the direction of a pin that has none from the types `input`, `output` and `bidirectional`; without it no direction is set, as before.

## 1.11.1 (2026-11-01; not a statement of compliance)

### Changed

- **The documents were brought up to date with the program (release 1.11.1, D-190; pull request 139).** A page-by-page check of the hand-written documents against the code and against real runs of the commands they show. Wrong or stale statements were corrected: the package names in the root README, the menus and navigation rail of the Systems engineer role, a menu path, the number of files `templates` writes (19, not 18), the mass of the flatsat example (2.25 kg since the pair-part count of 1.11.0), buttons that the program does not have, and sample outputs that no longer matched (`pinouts.csv`, `config_invalid`, `invalid_json`, the refusal for a damaged project). Features of 1.8.0 to 1.11.0 that pages left out were added: pin direction, the connection list, `conductors` in the BOM and the mass, a waiver tied to its finding, the coax rule, `release_distinct_roles`, the Requirement and Signal/Interface columns, *Number of these devices* and *First connector*, the library and subsystem commands. The reference pages (file format, imports, outputs, configuration, architecture, requirements) and the notes for developers were checked against the code. Fifteen pictures of the manual were made again with `tools.tab_screenshots`; the Add device window, the Link connectors window, the Devices tab and the Next step page now show the fields and buttons that the old pictures lacked. Nobody looked at the program on a real display: steps in the app were checked against the program's texts, and the pictures are drawn without a screen.
- **The guide in the program (F1) says what the program does now.** The guide is built from `docs/guide/USER_GUIDE.md` and is inside the packages, so it is the one document that a user of 1.11.0 cannot get from the repository. Fifteen corrections: which role opens on the Next step page, the menu entries the Systems engineer role hides, a button that does not exist, the status chips *Outputs changed* and *Made by hand*, the files the export writes, the new columns of the pinout tables and the wire list, the coax rule, waivers that no longer fit, `release_distinct_roles`, three command options and the `merge-fix` entry under *When something goes wrong*.
- **No change to what the program does.** The program, the file format, the design rules (still 39) and the written files are as in 1.11.0, apart from the tool version in the stamp and the model hash that follows from it. `docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json` is not written again (`1.11.0` and `1.11.1` have the same length; the check prints *identical*). A docstring of `tools/tab_screenshots.py` was corrected.

## 1.11.0 (2026-10-30; not a statement of compliance)

### Changed

- **A twisted pair is counted once in the bill of materials and the mass (release 1.11, D-188).** A wire part can say how many conductors one metre of it carries (`conductors`, 2 for a twisted pair; the parts import reads a *Conductors* column). Every wire that names the part is one conductor of it, so the BOM and the mass now count the sum of the wire lengths divided by that number: the metres of the cable. Before, a pair of two 3 m wires showed 6 m of cable (and 2× the mass). A part without the field counts as before, and the field is left out of the files while it is 1. The starter part `EX-WIRE-TWISTED-SHIELDED` has `conductors` 2; a `quad` made from that part counts two pairs. **In your own projects** set `conductors` on your pair parts (a *Conductors* column in the parts import) — the tool does not guess it. Of the files the shipped examples write, only the BOM, the mass table and `export.json` change (7 BOM files and 7 mass tables, system and harness level); every other file differs only in the model hash, which includes the parts. `tests/test_pair_part_counts.py`.
- **The Problems panel says why a waived problem is open again (release 1.11, D-187).** When a waiver no longer fits its finding (the finding changed after it was waived), the card names the earlier reason and says that the waiver no longer applies, and the *Waiver* column of the Checks panel reads "Waiver no longer fits". Before, the finding simply showed as open. `tests/test_gui_drc.py`.
- **A waiver is tied to the finding it was made for (release 1.11, D-187).** A waiver now records `finding_hash`, a short hash of the rule and the statement of the finding. While the finding says the same, nothing changes. When it says something else (a number or a name in its text changed), the waiver no longer applies: the finding is open again, and the DRC report lists it under *Waivers that no longer fit* with the old reason, so a changed problem is not silently still accepted. Waiving it again records the new hash. A waiver without the field (made before, or written by hand) applies as before, and the field is left out of the file while empty, so projects that have no waiver, and waivers made earlier, are written exactly as before. `tests/test_waiver_finding_hash.py`.
- **The documents no longer quote a model hash (D-186).** The transcripts in the tutorial, the manual, the examples page and the troubleshooting page show `<hash>`: the hash changes with the tool version, so every quoted value was stale after each release (four manual pages still showed 1.8.1 values). Nothing changes in the program or its files.

## 1.10.0 (2026-10-29; not a statement of compliance)

### Changed

- **The outer conductor of a coax is bonded at both ends (release 1.10, plan `docs/ait/RELEASE_1.10_PLAN.md`, D-182).** Generation gave every shield group the two ends of the project-wide grounding concept (`shield_end_a`, `shield_end_b`), so a coax got `floating` at one end, or `floating` at both while the concept was a placeholder. A coax group now always gets `backshell_360` at both ends, whatever the concept says; a coax that a person groups (**Group wires**) gets the same. Twisted pairs, shielded pairs, quads and overall shields still follow the concept. The rule `shield-wrong-end` no longer compares a coax with the concept (a coax is bonded at both ends by construction); `shield-unterminated` and `shield-bonding` read the new ends, so a coax that was reported only because of the concept is no longer reported, and a coax that a person set to `floating` still is. No file format change. In the shipped examples only `flatsat` has a coax (harness W005): its saved harness file and its drawing now show `backshell_360` at end B. Regenerating a project that has a coax changes that group's ends in a harness that is not released; a released harness is frozen and does not change. The model hash of `flatsat` changes with its saved harness and is quoted in every file it writes, so the stored comparison of the written files moves from the files of release 1.2.0 to a baseline (`docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_BASELINE.json`, `tools/output_hashes.py`) that is renewed on purpose; the 1.2.0 file stays as a record (D-183). Of the 612 files of the four examples only the 120 of `flatsat` differ from what 1.9.0 wrote. A coax group that was generated cannot have its ends set by hand (only groups a person made can); if your practice needs another end for a coax, tell the owner: it would need a setting of its own.

- **Signal and interface on every cable pin; the requirement in the wire list and the traceability matrix (release 1.10, W2).** In `pinouts.csv` the *Signal* and *Interface* cells of a cable pin were empty unless the pin had a signal of its own, so a technician reading a connector could not see what a wire carried. A cable pin without its own value now shows the signal and the interface of the wires that end on it (different values sorted and joined with `;`); a pin of its own keeps its value, a spare pin stays empty, box pinouts are unchanged. While any interface has a requirement ID, the wire list gets a *Requirement* column after *EMC class* and `traceability.csv` one after *Routed* (an interface row shows its ID, a connection row stays empty); a project without requirement IDs keeps both tables. The independent output check mirrors both. No file format change. In the shipped examples only the pinout tables and the per-harness workbooks change (90 of the 612 files; the examples have no requirement IDs), so the stored baseline of the written files was renewed again (D-183, D-184).
- **`CLAUDE.md` is split (release 1.10, W1, D-182).** The file that gives an agent its instructions was 50 kB; it is 8.5 kB now (core rules, status, commands) and six area notes in `docs/developer/agent/` hold the rest, word for word. `tests/test_claude_md_split.py` keeps the file under 20 kB and every note linked both ways. No change for users.

### Fixed

- **The program no longer aborts when it ends while the autosave journal is still being written.** Since 1.9.0 the journal is written by a background thread; a process that ended without closing the window (a script, a test, any other way out) could end with signal 6 and the message `QThread: Destroyed while thread '' is still running` when the writer was slow. Ending the program now waits for the autosave writer and for a running design check (at most five seconds each, as closing the window does). The same exit no longer prints `Internal C++ object (MainWindow) already deleted` when a panel is hidden while the window is being deleted. Closing the window was not affected. `tests/test_exit_with_worker.py`.

## 1.9.0 (2026-10-28; not a statement of compliance)

### Added

- **A direction on a pin.** A pin can say which way its signal runs (`in`, `out` or `bidirectional`; empty means not set). It is optional and left out of the files while empty, so an existing project is written exactly as before. Where it is set it decides before any name does: the connection review reports output against output and input against input from the two directions (also for analog, discrete, LVDS, pyro and I2C clock signals, which the review otherwise leaves alone), and the pairing proposal pairs two equal `TX` names when the pins say `out` and `in`. The pinout tables and `export-pinout` get a last column *Direction* only while some pin has one; `import-pinout` reads a *Direction* column (a word that is not in, out or bidirectional stops the import and names the row; `n/a` and `passive` leave it unset). The JSON Schema of the connector files lists it. The Devices tab shows it (the edit column comes with the screens of this release).
- **The transmit and receive words are a setting.** The table of word pairs the connection review and the pairing proposal use (`TX`/`RX`, `MOSI`/`MISO`, `DOUT`/`DIN` ...) is the key `signal_counterparts` of `config/naming.json`; while it is not set the table of 1.8.1 is used, so nothing changes for an existing project. It is checked like the other configuration values (rule `config-invalid`, `config`, generation), and `harness-design-studio config DIR --counterparts "TX=RX, OUT=IN"` sets it (`default` clears it, `none` pairs equal names only). See `docs/CONFIG.md`.
- **A connection list as output (release 1.9, W2; audit findings F-02 and F-08).** A project that has connections (the links made with **Link connectors…**) now writes two more files, `system/connections.csv` and `system/connections.xlsx`, in the style of an interface control document:
  - One row for each pair of pins: connection, name, and for each end the device, connector, pin, signal and role (`power`, `return`, `ground`, `signal`, `shield`, `spare`), then the interface type the pair belongs to, the harness made from the connection and its note. The workbook has the same rows as a printable sheet, a **Summary** sheet with a line for each connection, and the stamp. Columns: `docs/OUTPUTS.md`, *Connection list*.
  - The connections are drawn with their IDs in `system/block_diagram` and listed in `system/traceability.csv`, whose first heading then reads *Interface or connection* and which gets a last column *Kind*.
  - **They need no harness.** `harness-design-studio export` no longer stops with "there are no harnesses to export" for a project that has connections, the Export window opens on it and plans the connection list in its tree (a project that was never saved first asks where to save it), the empty Outputs area offers **Export the connection list…**, and the banner *Outputs out of date* also shows for a project that only has connections. A project with neither a harness nor a connection still cannot be exported.
  - The independent output check reads the new files cell by cell (`out_connections`), checks that they exist exactly when the project has connections, that the block diagram shows every connection and that the traceability matrix lists them; the manifest and the checksums list them.
  - A project without connections writes none of this: every file keeps its bytes (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` prints *identical*). No design rule and no field was added.
- **The Systems engineer path in the screens (release 1.9, W1; audit of 1.8.0, section 3.2).** No new design rule and no file-format change: a project that does not use these features writes the same files, and `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` prints *identical*.
  - **A first connector when you add a device.** The window *Add device* has an optional *First connector* (family, number of pins, gender, from the connector parts of the library). *No connector yet* stays the default.
  - **Add a connector inside Link connectors.** Under each device of the window; the connector is made with the connection in one undo step, and not at all if the window is cancelled.
  - **Pair by pin number** and **Paste a pairing…** (two columns from a table) in the window *Link connectors*. Both show a proposal with what was left out, and wait for *Accept the pairs*; the pairs join the table, never replace it, and nothing is saved before *Link connectors*.
  - **A card with the first open problem on the Next step page**: what is wrong, one sentence on what to do, *Show it*, *Waive…* (where the check allows a waiver) and *Approve …* for an unapproved part (after a question that says what approving means; one undo step; refused for a part marked *not approved* or locked by a release).
  - **The Devices tab shows the Role of each pin** (the role you gave, or the one taken from the signal name marked *(auto)*) and you can change it in the table.
  - **A tour of its own for the Systems engineer role** (the Next step page, the first button, Connect, the bar on the left).
- **Two people who both press Generate can merge (release 1.9, audit finding F-05).** No new design rule and no file-format change; every output keeps its bytes (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` prints *identical*).
  - **`.gitattributes` in a new project.** `harness-design-studio new` (and the New project window of the app) writes it once, like `.gitignore`; the five example projects ship it, and `ci/gitattributes` in the templates is the same text for a project made earlier. It has one effect: Git treats `generated/generation.json` as a binary file, so the merge still stops on it when both branches ran Generate (the record holds a hash of the whole design), but Git writes no conflict markers into it, the file stays valid JSON and keeps the version of the branch you are on. Every other file stays text and merges line by line. A save never rewrites or removes it.
  - **New command `harness-design-studio merge-fix DIR`.** It finds the files of the project that are in conflict (files with conflict markers, and the files that the index of Git still lists as unmerged; the tool reads the repository's own files and does not start `git`, new module `core/vcs/gitindex.py`). If `generated/generation.json` is the only one, it keeps this branch's version of it, runs `generate` and then `check`, and tells you to commit. If any other file is in conflict, it names the files and changes nothing (exit 1); `generate` or `check` blocked, and the record is put back as it was found. It writes nothing itself except that record, and a harness made by hand is never touched. `tests/test_merge_fix.py` makes real two-branch merges with `git` (with and without the `.gitattributes`, `merge-fix` against the recipe of the manual byte for byte, a refusal, the index reader against `git ls-files -u` for index versions 2, 3 and 4).
  - **The documents say what is true.** `docs/user-manual/use-git-and-ci.md`, CONCEPTS, TROUBLESHOOTING, FAQ, TIPS, the cheat sheet, FILE_FORMAT, the user guide and the command reference describe `merge-fix`, the `.gitattributes` and the files that still conflict by design: `logical/links.json` (every new connection gets the next number, so two people who each add one both create the same ID and the merge stops; sharding the file or a random part in the ID is a change of the file format and is not part of this change), `changelog.json`, `baselines/`, `layout.json` and, if kept in Git, `outputs/`.

- **Different people in a release, an optional setting (release 1.9, audit finding F-07).** A project can switch on `release_distinct_roles` in `config/naming.json` (`harness-design-studio config DIR --distinct-roles on`; `off` takes it out again and the file is as before). Off, which is the default, nothing changes: one name can still author, check and release a harness, and every file and output stays byte-identical. On: the author (who submitted the harness for review), the checker (`--checker`, required) and the person who releases must be three different people, in plain sentences (*The checker and the person who releases it must be different people, but both are 'Z'.*; letter case, width and extra spaces do not make a different person); a review, release or new revision may not be dated earlier than the latest entry of the change log or the latest baseline of any harness of the project (the rule for the same harness holds with or without the setting), nor more than one day after today; and `--by` may be left out, then the name from Git (`user.name`, read from the Git configuration files without starting Git, and never made up) is used and the command says so. The release dialogs show the rule, start with the Git name when no name was saved, ask for the checker as required and say in the hint which two names are the same. Switching the setting does not make the harness plans out of date. `docs/CONFIG.md`, `docs/user-manual/release-a-harness.md`; the editor has no switch for the setting yet.

### Changed

- **The README starts with a 10-minute tour, not with release history (audit findings F-13 and E-F4).** `HarnessDesignStudio/README.md` is 65 lines and about 700 words instead of 123 lines and 2,800 words: what the tool is, the honest limits, a numbered tour of five steps whose commands and quoted output lines a test runs (`tests/test_docs_claims.py`; the screens it names are checked against `gui/strings.py`), a short table of documents and the developer setup. The status, the release history, the feature list and the other text that stood there are in the new `docs/STATUS.md`, word for word (only relative links adjusted); the root `README.md` no longer carries the 3,300-character list of releases either and links there. `CLAUDE.md` (51 KB, now 49 KB) loses its 4,900-character status paragraph the same way: it keeps a short status with the facts an agent needs, and the paragraph is in `docs/developer/CLAUDE_HISTORY.md` word for word. `docs/developer/release.md` names `docs/STATUS.md` as the page to update at a release. No code, file format or output changed.
- **An edit, the autosave journal, the export and an undo step cost far less on a big project (release 1.9, R3; audit of 1.8.0, findings A-8, A-9, A-12 and B-08).** Nothing a person sees changes, no file format and no output byte: `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` prints *identical*, and the 612 files of the four examples (the build workbooks included) were compared byte by byte before and after. Measured with `python -m tools.bench_incremental` on a project of 200 devices (2,000 interfaces, 150 harnesses with 20,000 wires, three 40-pin connectors on every device, 200 connections), median of three runs of five; the machine is shared and noisy, so read the ratios (numbers in `docs/ui/PERF_BASELINE.md`):
  - **One integrity check per edit, and only where the edit reached (A-8).** `History.execute` ran the whole check twice and started over whenever any connector changed. An `IntegrityTracker` now compares the project with the state it last looked at and checks again only the harnesses, connectors and other groups that changed or that read something that changed. Renaming a device 38 ms to 1.9 ms, editing a device connector 84 ms to 14 ms, editing one of 20,000 wires 43 ms to 2.5 ms. The first edit after a project is opened still pays for one full check (about 120 ms). The answer is the one the whole check gives: `tests/test_integrity_tracker.py` compares the tracker and the check with a verbatim copy of the 1.8.1 check (`tests/reference/integrity_181.py`) on 40 generated projects with 30 random edits each, after undoing edits, and after changes made without `History`.
  - **The autosave journal no longer stops the window (A-9).** The journal was built and written on the GUI thread after every pause: 0.41 s at 200 devices, 0.79 s the first time. The GUI thread now only copies the project (0.4 ms; entities are immutable) and a worker thread writes the journal; `serialize` rebuilds only the files whose objects changed (215 ms to 6.5 ms after one edit) and only those files are encoded again for the journal. An edit during a write asks for one more write when it ends. A save, a discard or opening another project stops a pending write first, so a late write cannot bring back a journal that was cleared. The journal file has the same bytes as before and restoring it gives the same project (`tests/test_journal_incremental.py`).
  - **The export reuses cell styles (B-08).** The build workbooks made 28,726 font objects for the flatsat; a cell style is now made once for each combination in a workbook (24 fonts), and the export of the flatsat without drawings takes 1.84 s instead of 3.73 s. Per-harness incremental export was not built: every written file carries the model hash of the whole project (the first line of each CSV, the footer of each sheet), so after any edit every file of every harness has other bytes anyway.
  - **An undo step holds the change, not a second copy of the harness (A-12).** `evolve` keeps the objects of the fields it does not change instead of dumping and validating them again: a step that edits one wire of a flatsat harness kept 682 kB, now 7 kB (dense project: 15 kB to 7 kB).
- **Making a harness from a connection, the pairing proposal and the review of all connections work in one pass.** A harness from a connection of 1,000 pairs took 31.7 s in 1.8.1 (each wire read every ID of the project and rebuilt the harness) and now takes about 0.1 s; the review of all connections no longer reads every connection again for each one, and the proposal finds the candidates of a pin by name instead of comparing every pair of pins. The results are the same as in 1.8.1: tests compare them with a copy of the 1.8.1 code on generated projects.
- **A warning no longer holds the Next step page** on *Fix what is marked*. Only a failed check that is an error does; with warnings left the page goes on to the next step and says *n warnings are left. They do not stop you.* (before, a new project with starter parts stayed on *Fix what is marked* for good and offered no fix).
- **The Systems engineer role stays on the Next step page or the diagram** after adding a device, generating, creating a harness from a connection or adding library devices, and its menus leave out the engineering entries (wire, harness, zone and alignment tools, the interface-first way, diagram drawing options, revisions of a harness, engineering values, the detail views). Every one is still in the command palette (Ctrl+K) and on its key; the Electrical / AIT engineer role lists them all.
- **Wording that follows what the project has.** A release blocker on the screen names the place in the program instead of a command-line command (the command line keeps its own words); Generate is the primary button only when the project has interfaces; the status bar says *Made by hand* for a project of connections or hand-made harnesses, not *Not generated*; the tour step about connecting mentions pin numbers and accepting; the message for a device with no connector says *Press Add a connector under it*.
- **Problem counts agree.** The Problems list says *Problems (14 of 24)* while a filter hides some, the Problems tab of the inspector explains in its tooltip why its number is smaller, and the chip of the independent check in the Harness tab says *Independent check passed* (and *Independent check passed; n design checks failed* while a design check fails) instead of a bare *Check passed*.

- **Python 3.14 or newer only.** The tool and the WireViz program at the repository root no longer support older Python versions (`requires-python = ">=3.14"`, `python_requires=">=3.14"`). The packages (`.deb`, `.tar.gz`, Windows setup and `.zip`) bring their own Python, so nothing changes for people who install those; installing from source needs Python 3.14 (Ubuntu 24.04 does not have it in its archive). CI, the release workflow and the build container use 3.14 only; `ruff` and `mypy` target 3.14, the code is modernised with `ruff` (`UP` rules; quoted annotations, `Optional`/`Union`/`List`, the `# -*- coding` lines), and the development tool `pip-audit` moves from 2.9.0 to 2.10.1 because 2.9.0 needs a version of `lxml` that has no 3.14 wheel. No file format, rule, output or behaviour changes; `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` still prints *identical*.
- **Package build.** `tomli`, which the new `pip-audit` pulls into the build environment, is left out of the packages (`EXCLUDES` in `tools/build_installer.py`); the build still stops if any other undeclared package ends up inside.
- **Deeply nested files are refused on purpose.** Python 3.14 reads JSON nested hundreds of thousands of levels deep, where 3.13 and older stopped at about a thousand with a `RecursionError`. A file nested deeper than 1,000 levels (a real one has about ten) is now refused with the same message as before ("nested too deeply"), so that the answer does not depend on the Python version (`core/io/canonical.py`, `MAX_NESTING`).
- **Faster tests.** Eleven test files that rebuilt the same generated outputs or the same released project in every test now build it once per module and give each test a copy (no test, test name or assertion changed; the summed time of the non-GUI tests went from about 1,730 s to about 1,090 s on a loaded machine). New marker `slow` for the 11 tests of about 10 s or more: pull requests skip them (`-m "not perf and not slow"` in `harness-ci.yml` and `harness-platforms.yml`), the run on `master` and `tools.release_check` run them.
- **A release freezes the device pins that its wires end on (audit finding A-2).** A harness made from a connection (**Link connectors…**) or by hand has no interface to say which pins of the device connectors it uses, so the signal and the other fields of those pins were not frozen: `import-pinout --replace` changed them after the release and ended with exit 0. Now the pins that a released harness reaches with a wire are frozen the way the pins of a released interface already were (the same check, the same sentence, naming the harness), in the editor, in the data commands and in `harness-design-studio check` (a change made behind the tool is reported as `released_modified`); the other pins of the same connector stay editable. A project whose released harness has had such a pin changed since its release now gets that report from `check`. `docs/FILE_FORMAT.md`.

### Fixed

- **One limit for the wire current and the bundle current.** The rule `bundle-current` left the temperature factor out and `current-over-wire` took it in, so with a temperature factor of 0.5 the same wire was allowed 2.5 A by one and 1.25 A by the other. Both now use one function (`sizing.wire_current_limit`: ampacity times the bundle factor K times the temperature factor, the same as the wire sizing). `bundle-current` still runs while the temperature factor is not set (it counts as 1 there); both rule ids stay, so waivers and the lists keep working. No shipped example sets a table of bundle factors, so no example output changes.

## 1.8.1 (2026-10-27; not a statement of compliance)

Fixes from the audit of 1.8.0 (`docs/AUDIT_1.8.0.md`, decision D-177): no new feature, no file-format change, the design rules stay 39, and the files and outputs of a project that does not meet one of these cases are unchanged (`python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` prints *identical*). The one written text that changes for projects on placeholder configuration is the line *Ready to build* of the build workbook.

### Fixed

- **Connections.**
  - Connections between analog, discrete, LVDS, pyro and I2C clock pins are no longer reported as "two outputs drive against each other", and the pairing proposal now pairs them.
  - The pairing proposal recognises vendor-style names (`UART_TX` and `UART_RX`, `SPW_DOUT+` and `SPW_DIN+`, `TX_P` and `RX_P`, `MOSI` and `MISO`). It never pairs two equal transmit names or two equal receive names; a pair of that kind that you make yourself gets a warning.
  - A harness made from a connection is now checked against it: if a wire is moved, added, removed or relabelled, `validate`, `check`, `verify`, the design rules and `export` stop with a plain message.
  - A device connector with two cable plugs on it, or a pin wired by two harnesses, is reported when you check the project, and Generate reports a second plug. Making a connection or harness onto a connector that already has a plug, or onto a pin an interface uses, is refused with a reason.
  - A device joined only by a connection is no longer reported as "not connected to anything yet", and a heater connection (`HTR+`, `HTR-`) is no longer reported as "power without return".
- **Silence is not a pass.**
  - A design rule that is ready to run but has nothing to look at (no interface with a current or voltage, no EMC class) now says "not checked" with a plain note instead of "pass".
  - Wires that have no interface (harnesses drawn by hand or made from a connection) get a note saying that the current, voltage-drop and bundle checks did not run for them; the manual says so too.
  - The build workbook no longer says a bare "Ready to build: yes" when the values rest on placeholder configuration: it says "yes (values rest on placeholder configuration: ...)" and lists the placeholder files.
  - `export --drawings` no longer fails for a harness that has a connector and no wires.
- **Library.**
  - A device whose pins use a cable contact part can be added from the library (and as part of a subsystem), and a renamed device can be saved and added again.
  - One damaged or hand-edited file in the library folder no longer hides the others; it is listed with a plain sentence, with line and column for JSON errors. A key that appears twice in a library file is refused.
  - "Save to library" no longer replaces a different device of the same ID without asking: Replace, Save as a new ID or Cancel. `export-devices` refuses unless `--replace` is given. A part's approval in the library is never lowered.
  - The library list shows the file of each entry, and the counts of damaged files use words instead of "(s)".
- **Keyboard and screen.**
  - Tab now leaves every table. Before, a keyboard user with a row selected in the pair table of the Link connectors window, in the library list or in the main-window tables could not reach the buttons below. Tables where Tab moves from cell to cell (Devices pins, Units, Interfaces) keep that and leave with Ctrl+Tab.
  - Connection lines on the diagram can be reached with Tab, Space selects them and Enter opens the Link connectors window.
  - Editing in a project with many devices is faster: the diagram legend no longer rebuilds the list of all connectors for every harness connector (about 30 % faster per edit at 150 devices, over 40 % at 300).
  - The Problems panel heading reads "Waived" again, not the long toast sentence; `gui/strings.py` no longer defines a name twice (a test guards it), and 67 unused texts and 4 unused functions are gone.
  - The Inspector heading says "Device", not "Unit". Words that told people with connections to "Press Generate" now fit what the project has: the first-start tour, the empty Harness and Lists views and the project tree. A harness made from a connection or by hand is no longer labelled "Not generated".
- **Command line and files.**
  - The command line writes UTF-8 when its output is redirected or piped, so `drc project > report.md` no longer fails on names with characters such as Ω or ✓ on Windows.
  - Importing data the project already holds (pinouts, parts, KiCad netlists, build data) says "unchanged", prints "Already up to date." and exits 0, so a build can import the same file every time. A file with nothing in it is still an error.
  - `import-lengths` refuses two rows that give the same segment different lengths, and says "too large" for a huge value.
  - Open in the Outputs list and in the export window opens only files the program wrote and of a known kind; any other file is not opened, and the folder is offered instead.
  - A command on an empty or wrong folder no longer leaves a hidden lock file there. `export-pinout` writes tab-separated text for `.tsv` and refuses endings it does not know; `export-devices` and `export-subsystem` no longer make a missing folder; `config` and `library` on a read-only project exit with 2 like the other commands; paths with spaces are quoted in the "Next:" hint; a device named like `A<B and C>D` is shown as typed in the delete question.
- **Documents and tests.**
  - Six stale statements are corrected: interface types are in `logical/interface_types.json`, not `library/`; a new project ships no engineering numbers but `config --apply-profile` writes the ECSS table values; two people editing different parts merge cleanly except for `generated/generation.json` (the manual now gives the way out); the README says which role opens on the Next step page; the Getting Started "Why" excerpt shows the real four lines; the command line can add devices through the import commands.
  - The speed tests use a time factor of 2 by default, so they pass on an idle machine, and a slow run prints the measured value, the limit and the factor. Seven tests that asserted nothing now assert; the README quick-start test now runs its commands (it ran none before). `tools.trace` reports the requirements verified by file only as a separate count. The package metadata names Python 3.12 and 3.13, and the dev extra includes pytest-timeout.

## 1.8.0 (2026-10-26; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.8.0 answers the workshop of 2026-10-23, where a systems engineer, an AIT engineer and an electrical engineer used the tool (milestones M1 to M5 of decision D-172; plan `docs/ait/WORKSHOP_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`; decision D-173 pinout first, D-174 harness and connector editing, D-175 the library, D-176 the roles). The tool now starts from the pinout: two connectors are joined pin by pin (**Link connectors**), **Check my connections** reviews them and **Create harness** makes a harness only when you press it. A connector, a group and all wires of a harness can be edited. The library can be a folder on a shared drive with devices and subsystems, and a device can be asked for in a number. Two **roles**, Systems engineer and Electrical / AIT engineer, replace the Simple and Full workspaces of 1.7.0. The quick fixes of the first milestone are in too: a new device asks for its name, the screen says *device* instead of *unit*, problem notices can be switched off, export asks where to save, RTN is explained.

What did not change: nothing that exists is changed in the files. Every new field is left out of the files while it is empty (schema version 1), so a project that uses none of the new things keeps byte-identical files, and every generated file of an existing project is byte-identical apart from the tool version it records (checked file by file, `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` prints *identical*). The design rules grow from 35 to 39 (four rules that read connections; the report says *Rules run: 39*); a project without connections gets no new finding. The command line gains `--device` as another name of `--unit`, and `export-subsystem` and `import-subsystem`.
