Not in this release: stage S4 of the simple workspace (a blank project that asks one thing at a time); command-line versions of the new edits (no command makes a connection, edits a connector, a group or all wires); a lock for each pin (an edited connector of a generated harness is locked as a whole); a measurement of the roles with test users, so nothing here claims that the tool is easier to use. Not done, as in earlier releases: no screen was watched on a real display by a person (the screens were checked in a window started without a screen), signing the packages, a screen-reader pass and the clean-machine installation test (waived by the owner, `compliance/DEVIATIONS.md`); the owner has not signed this release off.

### Added

- **A new device asks for its name first** (workshop point 1). **Edit > Add device > Device…** (Ctrl+Shift+N), the button of the empty diagram, the **Next step** button and the **Add device** tool of the Systems engineer role open a small dialog, **Add device**, before the device exists, instead of making `DEV1` named "Device 1". **Name** is required (hint: *for example Star tracker*). **ID** is suggested as soon as a name is typed: the first word, letters and digits, upper case, at most 8 characters, and the first free number (*Star tracker* gives `STAR1`, then `STAR2`; *Gyro 3* gives `GYRO3`); it can be edited, and once it has been edited it no longer follows the name. **Subsystem** offers the subsystems of the project. A reason shows under the fields while the device cannot be added (*A device needs a name.*, *A device needs an ID.*, *ID X is already used by another device.*) and the button **Add device** stays off until it can. The new device is one undo step and opens in the Devices tab. The **New device…** of the pinout import wizard makes its ID from the name typed. The kinds in **Edit > Add device** (computer, power unit …) still add a device at once.

- **Connections between connectors, pin by pin: the pinout first** (workshop points 10 to 16, D-173, milestone M2). A *connection* joins a connector of one device to a connector of another and needs no signal type. **Edit > Link connectors…** (Ctrl+Shift+K), the **Connect** tool and the key **C** (click two devices) open the window **Link connectors**: the program proposes which pins go together **from the signal names on the pins, never from their position** (`TX` meets `RX`, `TX+` meets `RX+`, `PWR` meets `PWR`, `RTN` meets `RTN`; an output is not paired with an output); you look at the pairs, change, add or remove any of them, give a pin its **role** (Power, Return, Ground, Signal, Shield, Spare) and press *Link connectors*; one undo step. What the connection carries (*CAN, 2 wires*) is worked out from the pins and shown under the connector names on the diagram; nobody includes an interface. The older way, by interface type, stays under **Edit > Connect with an interface type…** for projects that use it, and Generate keeps working for them. There is a **Connections** group in the project tree, the connection lines on the diagram, **Edit > Selected connection** (edit the pairs, rename, create harness, delete), and a **Connections** filter in the Problems panel.

- **Check my connections** (D-173). **Design > Check my connections** reviews every connection and changes nothing. Four new design rules, `link-unpaired` (a pin with a signal that no pair uses), `link-direction` (output against output, input against input), `link-signal-mismatch` (the two names of a pair differ) and `link-power-return` (power paired and no return), join the existing 35 (39 rules); the integrity checks gain `dangling_link`, `link_pin_missing`, `link_pin_reused` and `link_same_device`. A pin with the role *Spare* is never asked about.

- **Create harness, only when you press it** (D-173). From a connection, **Create harness** shows what it will add (the harness, the cable connectors, one wire for each pair), and makes it only after you agree: a harness you own, which Generate leaves alone, with length, gauge and colour empty until you enter them in *Edit wire*. The program never creates a harness on its own. The **Next step** page counts connections as *Connect* done and offers **Create harness** next.

- **The files** (D-173). `logical/links.json` (written only while the project has a link) and the optional `role` of a pin (see `docs/FILE_FORMAT.md`, *Links and pin roles*). A link of a released harness is locked with it, is a diffable object and is recorded in the baseline. Deleting a device deletes its connections. `harness-design-studio validate`, `check` and `drc` read projects with connections; there is no command-line command to make one.

- **The library: a folder that your team shares** (workshop points 17 to 19, D-175, milestone M4). **File > Library folder…** (and **Settings > Project**) names the folder, for example on a shared drive or in a Git checkout; it is a setting of the person, and the program stays offline: it reads plain files. The folder holds device files (the library file of 1.4.0, unchanged) and the new **subsystem files**. Nothing is written into it unless you press **Save to library**. A folder that is missing or cannot be read is explained in words (*Is the drive connected?*), and a damaged file is named without hiding the others.
- **A subsystem: devices and the connections between them** (D-175). A new file kind, `harness-subsystem-library` (`docs/FILE_FORMAT.md`, *Subsystem library file*): a name, a description, the devices with their connectors and pins, and the connections between them. Adding one to a project adds all of it in **one undo step**, sets the subsystem of each device to its name, gives a taken ID the next free one (`OBC` becomes `OBC-2`, and the connections follow), puts the devices side by side in one lane, never replaces anything, and refuses as a whole when one device or connection cannot be added. The command line has `export-subsystem` and `import-subsystem` (`--show`, `--dry-run`).
- **The library window** (D-175). **File > Library…** (Ctrl+Shift+O; also a button on the Home page) opens a window of its own beside the editor, not modal: a search field and a filter for devices and subsystems on the left, a preview on the right (the connectors and pins of a device, or the devices and connections of a subsystem), **Add to project** and **Save selection to library…** (the devices selected in the project, saved as devices or as a subsystem with a name; a name that is taken is refused under the field). The window follows the theme, works from the keyboard (Ctrl+F searches, Enter adds, F5 reads the folder again, Esc closes) and remembers its own size and place. The buttons **Save to library…** and **Add from library…** of the Devices tab work as before.
- **Redundancy is a number of devices** (workshop point 20, D-175). **Number of these devices** in the **Add device** window, in the **Devices** tab (with **Add copies**) and in the library window makes the device in that number, in one undo step. Two devices are the device and `ID-R`, exactly what **Create redundant copy** has always made, so existing projects and outputs do not change; three or more add `ID-3`, `ID-4` and so on, on the redundant chain, with the same connectors and the links mirrored where the other end has a free connector. The number is counted from the devices that exist and only grows (delete a copy to have fewer); at most 10. **Create redundant copy** and the *Chain* field stay.
- **Two roles: Systems engineer and Electrical / AIT engineer** (workshop point 23, "the tool generally is just too overcrowded", D-176, milestone M5). A first start asks once, in a small window, *Who are you working as?* with two big buttons and the line *You can change this later in View > Role.* **View > Role** (and the row **Role** of **Settings > Editing**) changes it at once, with no restart, and each role remembers its own layout. The **Systems engineer** (devices, the connections between them, subsystems, the library, the checks) gets the lean window: a project opens on the **Next step** page, the side panels, the minimap, the legend, the status counts and the problem notices are off until asked for, the tools above the diagram are **Select**, **Connect**, **Add device** and **More**, and the rail lists Next step, Model, Checks, Revisions and Settings (**Home**, **Drawings** and **Outputs** are left out of it; the Go menu, the keys, the command palette and the buttons of the Next step page still reach them, and the rail lists the area that is open). The Next step page has the buttons **Library…** and **Show everything** for this role. The **Electrical / AIT engineer** (connectors, pinouts, harnesses, wires, build sheets, tests) sees every area and panel, as the editor always did. The roles **replace** the *Simple* and *Full* workspaces of 1.7.0 and the words are gone from the screen and the documents; the saved layouts of both are kept (the Systems engineer has the keys of Simple, the Electrical / AIT engineer those of Full). A settings file that already holds values and no role becomes **Electrical / AIT engineer** without a question, and a choice of *Simple* in 1.7.0 becomes **Systems engineer**. A role is a view, never a permission: it locks nothing, is not stored in a project, and the command line has none. The tour has a last step about the role. Nothing written changes. Page: `docs/user-manual/choose-your-role.md` (the old `use-the-simple-workspace.md`).
- **Show problem notices** (workshop point 3). A switch in **View** and in **Settings > Editing**, remembered for each role: on for the Electrical / AIT engineer, **off for the Systems engineer**. Off hides what appears without being asked for: the **Problems** button and its count in the status bar, the count on the inspector tab, the status chip of the top bar, the chip in the corner of the diagram, the problem, warning, pending and not-checked badges on the diagram, the link to pending values and the dismissible *out of date* banner. It never hides what says why something cannot be done or what you asked for: the read-only, locked, damaged-file and sample banners, the **Check** button and its result, the **Checks** area, the Problems panel (**View > Problems panel**, Ctrl+Shift+M), the **Next step** page and the error texts of dialogs. Nothing written changes.

- **RTN is explained where it is read** (workshop point 5). The pin tables of the Devices tab and the Harness tab show *Return line: where the current of the signal comes back* when the pointer rests on a signal named `RTN` (`GND`: *Ground*), and the glossary has the entry *RTN (return)*.

- **Edit a connector of a harness** (workshop point 6, D-174, milestone M3). In the **Connectors** tab, **Edit connector** sets the **Part**, **Gender**, **Keying** and **Name** of a cable connector (one undo step; the pins are not touched and a part with another number of pins is said under the fields), and **Pin of a connector** gains **Signal** and **Role** next to the function, termination, contact size and contact; **Pin ID** with **Add pin**, **Rename pin** (the wires on the pin follow) and **Delete pin** (refused while a wire uses it) change the pins of a connector of a harness you made. On a harness that **Generate** made the tab still adds no connector, but the part, gender, keying and the details of a pin can be set: the connector then belongs to you (`Connector.locked` in the harness file, written only then), Generate keeps those values and says *Edited by you*, the pins, their number, the name and the contact of each pin keep following the device, **Give back to Generate** returns it, and the warning `locked_data_lost` names an edited connector that cannot be kept. A project that edits no connector keeps byte-identical files and outputs.

- **Change a group** (workshop point 7, D-174). **Change group…** in the **Edit wire** tab, for a group you made: the **Kind** (twisted pair, shielded pair, quad, coax, overall shield), the **wires** (ticks) and the **shield ends A and B** (360 degree backshell, pigtail, floating), one undo step, with the count rule said in a line when it is broken. A group of the interface type is refused with the reason. Generate keeps the change.

- **Edit all wires** (workshop point 8, D-174). **Edit all wires…** (also **Edit > Edit all wires of the harness…**, Ctrl+Shift+B) sets the same **gauge, type, colour and length** on all wires of the harness, on the selected wires or on the wires of one signal, optionally only where a value is still empty, in one window and one undo step; the wires are locked like an edited wire (Generate keeps the values, **Reset to generated** gives them back).

- **Connectors at the ends of a connection, and the harness path** (workshop point 9, D-174). In **Expert** mode, and in Guided mode with **View > Show connectors on connections** (also on the Settings page, remembered, off by default), a small box at each end of a connection line shows the connector name and how many of its pins the connection pairs. The tooltip of a connection adds **Path** (`OBC-J01 - W001-P1 - W001-P2 - RW1-J02`) once a harness was made from it, and **Show the harness** (Edit > Selected connection, the project tree and the right-click menu of the line) opens the Harness tab on that harness. The right-click menu of a connection line, which was empty, now lists its actions.

### Changed

- **The Simple and Full workspaces are the two roles** (D-176). `View > Simple` and `View > Full` are `View > Role > Systems engineer` and `View > Role > Electrical / AIT engineer`; the setting *Workspace* of Settings is *Role*; the Next step link **Show everything** switches to the Electrical / AIT engineer role. The rail of the Systems engineer leaves out Drawings and Outputs as well as Home. The picture files `simple-workspace.png` and `full-workspace.png` are now `systems-engineer.png` and `electrical-engineer.png`.
- **Export asks where to save first** (workshop point 4). Export from a project that has harnesses and no folder yet (the sample project that is held in memory, or a project that was never saved) first opens a folder chooser, *Choose an empty folder to save the project in. The files are then exported next to it*, and then the Export window opens on the new outputs folder; cancelling the chooser stops there. This holds for every way in: the menu, the top bar, the Harness tab, the **Next step** button and the banner. The notice for a project with damaged files lists up to 5 of the file names (*and N more*) instead of saying only that a file could not be loaded. The **Export** button of the Harness tab says why it is off or what it will do beside it (*Read-only: save a copy first*, *Asks where to save first*), and the note of the Next step page says *Export first asks where to save the project.* instead of promising a folder chooser that did not exist.

- **A "unit" is now a "device" in everything you read** (workshop point 2, `docs/ait/WORKSHOP_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`). The editor (menus, tabs, tooltips, empty states, the tour, the messages), the messages of the command line, the user guide and the other documents say *device* where they said *unit*. The table of all devices, which was the *Units* tab, is now the **Device list** (the **Devices** tab with the connectors and pins of one device is unchanged). On the command line the option `--unit` of `import-netlist`, `import-pinout`, `export-pinout`, `export-devices` and `import-devices` is now `--device`; `--unit` still works as another name, so no script breaks (the option `--unit mm` of `import-lengths` is a unit of measure and is unchanged). An interface table with the headings *From unit* and *To unit* still imports; the headings *From device* and *To device* are read too. What did not change: the project files (`units`, `logical/units/`, `unit_id`), every written output file (checked file by file, `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json`), the design rule check texts that appear in the written reports, and the word *unit* in its sense of a unit of measure. The glossary has the entry *Device* (called *unit* in version 1.7.0 and earlier, and still *unit* in the project files); the left side panel is 40 pixels wider because the tab name *Device list* is longer than *Units*. Still open: the texts of findings that reach a written output file (the cross-strap, isolated and look-alike findings and the descriptions of the design rules) still say *unit*, because changing them would change generated files.

## 1.7.0 (2026-10-22; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.7.0 answers the test users of 1.6.0 (plan: `docs/ait/TEST_USER_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`, decisions D-169, D-170 and D-171). They did not like the harness plan PDFs and asked for a clear, actionable spreadsheet to build production harnesses from, and they said the tool felt overwhelming. The tool now writes a **build workbook** for every harness (`<ID>_build.xlsx`; the PDF drawings become optional), takes the **build data** a technician needs (strip lengths, the contact and crimp tool, accessories; `harness-design-studio import-build-data`) and has a **simple workspace** (stages S1 and S2: a Next step page and fewer things on screen). The owner decided on 2026-10-22 that all of this ships as one release, version 1.7.0 (the number 1.8.0 will not be used for this work), and that stage S3 of the simple workspace (plain words) is discarded.

What did not change: the file format (schema version 1; every new field is left out of the files while it is empty, so a project saved by 1.6.0 opens as it is and a project that uses none of the new fields keeps byte-identical files), the 35 design rules, and every other generated file of an existing project apart from the tool version it records (checked file by file, `python -m tools.output_hashes --check docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json` prints *identical*; the one thing it lets through is the new kind of file, `_build.xlsx`). What does change by default: `harness-design-studio export` no longer writes the PDF drawings of the harnesses unless `--drawings` is given. Not built: stage S4 of the simple workspace (a blank project that asks for one thing at a time; it remains open and would go in a later version) and the measurement with the testers, so nothing here claims that the tool is easier to use. Not done, as in earlier releases: signing the packages, a screen-reader pass and the clean-machine installation test (waived by the owner, `compliance/DEVIATIONS.md`). Still open: what soldering needs beyond the termination column in the lists is for the AIT engineers to say; a command line equivalent of *New harness* is not built.

### Added

- **A build workbook for every harness** (D-169). `outputs/harnesses/W001/W001_build.xlsx`, a document to build from: **Start here** (ready to build yes or no, what is open: wires without length, gauge, colour or part are marked TO FIX, pins in use without a termination and parts that are not approved are marked NOTE; who authored, checked and approved the design; a sign-off block), **Cut list** (one row per wire in cut order, thick before thin, then colour, then length; the colour as a coloured cell with its name and code beside it; a value the model does not have is shown as *to fill in*), **Connectors** (one block per connector, pin by pin: signal, function, wire, colour, gauge, where it goes, termination, and tick columns for terminated, inserted and checked), **Parts** (with the approval state), **Labels**, **Tests** (the continuity and insulation tests with empty columns for the measured value, the result, the initials and the date) and **About**. Header rows stay on screen, the tables have filters, every sheet prints on A4 landscape with the version and model stamp in the footer; there are no formulas; the same design gives the same bytes. The independent output check reads the workbook cell by cell (`out_build`).

- **The build data a technician needs** (D-170). Optional fields, left out of the files while empty, so an existing project keeps byte-identical files and outputs: the **strip length** at each end of a wire, the **contact** (a library part) on a pin and the **crimp tool** of a contact part, and **accessories** on a harness: sleeving, heat shrink, labels and backshells with where they go in words, a length and a quantity. Generate keeps what a person entered. The build workbook shows them all, and a value that is missing is marked *to fill in*; the tables show them only when they are used. `harness-design-studio import-build-data` reads them from a table; the editor has fields for them.

- **A simple workspace, first stage: one next step** (D-171). The test users also said the tool felt overwhelming. A new rail area **Next step** shows one page, *What to do next*: the next step in plain words, **one** button (*Add a device*, *Connect two devices*, *Generate the harnesses*, *Check the design*, *Show the problems*, *Export the build sheets*, *Go to Releases*), the six steps of the work with an icon and a word for each (*done*, *next*, *to do*) and a link **Show everything**. The setting *Workspace: Simple or Full* (View menu and Settings): a first run opens projects on the Next step page; anyone whose settings file already holds values keeps the full editor and can switch in View. Nothing written changes (no file, rule, option or output). The plain-words stage (S3) was discarded by the owner on 2026-10-22; the first-project stage (S4) and the measurement with the testers are not built.

- **A simple workspace, second stage: fewer things on screen** (D-171). In the Simple workspace the project tree, the inspector and the Problems panel start closed, the minimap and legend are off, the status bar keeps only what matters (saved or not, the running job, offline, the path), and the diagram tool row shows *Select*, *Connect*, *Add device* and a **More** menu with every other tool. The rail leaves out Home (the Next step page does its job). **Show panels** (top bar, and **Show side panels** first in the View menu) brings the tree and the inspector back together. Every hidden thing keeps its menu entry, shortcut and palette entry. The Full workspace is unchanged, and each workspace keeps its own layout. Nothing written changes.

### Changed

- **The PDF drawings are optional** (D-169). `harness-design-studio export` no longer writes `drawing_A3.pdf`, `drawing_A4.pdf` and `drawing_A3_s<n>.svg` of the harnesses; add `--drawings` to write them. The system diagrams and every other file are written as before. A release writes the outputs again with the same set the last export had. In the editor the Export window has a check box **Include the PDF drawings**; it is checked only when the previous export in the folder had them, and the quick Export button keeps whatever the folder had. A set with the drawings of every harness or of none is valid. The existing per-harness workbook `W001.xlsx` and all CSV files are unchanged.

## 1.6.0 (2026-10-21; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.6.0 **removes macOS** (decision D-168, on the owner's instruction: "please remove macOS completely and entirely, nobody needs it") and makes a **blank project say where to start**. The tool is supported on Ubuntu 24.04 and newer and on Windows 10 and 11.

What did not change: the file format (schema version 1; no new field, so a project saved by 1.5.0 opens as it is), the 35 design rules, the command line and every generated file of an existing project apart from the tool version it records (checked file by file, `tools/output_hashes.py`, `tests/test_output_hashes.py`). The releases 1.2.0 to 1.5.0 still carry their two macOS disk images on GitHub; they are not maintained or tested any more. Not done, as in earlier releases: signing the packages, a screen-reader pass and the clean-machine installation test (waived by the owner, `compliance/DEVIATIONS.md`). Still open: what soldering needs beyond the termination column in the lists is for the AIT engineers to say; a command line equivalent of *New harness* is not built.

### Fixed

- **A blank project says where to start.** The empty diagram told the user to add a unit "from the palette on the left", which the redesign of 1.3.0 had removed. It now has the button **Add a device** (a unit with its own connectors and pins; the same as Edit > Add unit > Device…, Ctrl+Shift+N; hidden once there is a unit and in a read-only project), and the empty diagram, the empty link and unit lists, the narrow-window notice and the name of the left tab name controls that exist. No file format, rule, command line or generated file changes.

### Removed

- **macOS (D-168).** The tool is supported on Ubuntu 24.04 and newer and on Windows 10 and 11 only. The workflows no longer build, test or publish anything for macOS (the release makes the `.deb`, the `.tar.gz`, the Windows setup program and `.zip`, and one `SHA256SUMS`), and `tools/build_macos.py`, `packaging/macos/`, the macOS mode of `tools/smoke_package.py`, the `.icns` of `tools/make_icons.py`, the macOS tests and the macOS parts of the documents are gone. No file format, model, rule, command line or generated file changes. The releases 1.2.0 to 1.5.0 still carry their two macOS disk images on GitHub; they are not maintained or tested any more.

## 1.5.0 (2026-10-21; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.5.0 answers a second request of the AIT engineers, to **create a project completely from scratch**: a harness can now be made by hand, with its connectors, pins and wires, and no interface behind it (decision D-166). It also makes the Linux package say what is missing in plain words instead of failing silently, and proves in CI that no package needs a Python on the machine (D-167).

What did not change: the file format (schema version 1; no new field, so a project saved by 1.4.0 opens as it is and a project that uses nothing new keeps byte-identical files), the 35 design rules, the command line, and every generated file of an existing project apart from the tool version it records (checked file by file, `tools/output_hashes.py`, `tests/test_output_hashes.py`). Not done, as in earlier releases: signing the packages (so Windows SmartScreen and macOS Gatekeeper still ask once), a screen-reader pass and the clean-machine installation test (waived by the owner, `compliance/DEVIATIONS.md`). Still open: what soldering needs beyond the termination column in the lists is for the AIT engineers to say; a command line equivalent of *New harness* is not built.

### Changed

- **Installers: nothing to install first, and a clear message when the machine lacks something.** The Linux, Windows and macOS packages already carry their own Python and Qt (checked: the Windows package holds `python312.dll`, the Linux one `libpython3.12`; the Linux self-test passes with no Python reachable). The CI smoke tests of the packages now run them in an environment without any Python (no `PYTHON*` variables, no virtual environment, no folder of the runner's on the PATH that holds a Python; the system folders stay, because the launcher of the macOS app needs `dirname` from `/usr/bin`), and the self-test has a step that fails when a package imports from outside its own folder. On Linux the editor now checks, before it starts, the three libraries it cannot carry (`libgl1`, `libegl1`, `libxcb1`) and whether there is a desktop session; if not, it says what is missing and the `sudo apt install` line, in the terminal and in a small window (zenity, kdialog or xmessage, when there is one) instead of a Python traceback or silence. `harness-design-studio-gui --check-system` runs only that check and `install.sh` runs it after installing and warns. See D-167.

### Added

- **A harness from nothing (answer to the AIT engineers: create a project completely from scratch).** A harness no longer has to come from Generate. **New harness…** (Drawings area, Edit menu, Ctrl+Shift+H) makes an empty draft harness; the new **Connectors** tab adds its cable connectors (a part of the library, or the other half of a unit's connector with its pins, gender and keying taken from it; pin names of your own if you like), sets the function, termination and contact size of a pin, and deletes a connector that has no wires; the wires are drawn in *Edit wire* as before. From there it is a harness like any other: checked, drawn, listed, reviewed and released. Generate leaves it, its connectors and its wires as they are; a harness that Generate made takes no connector by hand. No file format change (every field existed) and no change to any generated file. See `docs/user-manual/build-a-harness-by-hand.md` and D-166.

### Documentation

- **Documents brought up to 1.4.0.** The user manual (edit a wire, define a device and reuse it, import data with InvenTree files, export, Git, naming, wire colours, generate), the FAQ, glossary, concepts, cheat sheet, learning path, troubleshooting and tips, the user guide, both READMEs, the architecture, design and UX pages, the developer pages (tests of the 1.4.0 features, a picture script), the compliance records and the AIT plan now describe what 1.4.0 does; statements that were no longer true (the app has no button to lock a wire, a harness is never drawn by hand, there is no editor for interface types, the root changelog's latest version) were corrected, quoted model hashes and version stamps renewed, and two pictures added (`docs/img/edit-wire-tab.png`, `docs/img/devices-tab.png`, made by `python -m tools.tab_screenshots`). No code or output changes.

## 1.4.0 (2026-10-20; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.4.0 answers the feedback of the AIT engineers (plan: `docs/ait/AIT_FEEDBACK_PLAN.md`, decisions D-160 to D-165): every wire can be edited and drawn by hand, wires can share a pin and be twisted or shielded together, pins have a function, connectors and harnesses have labels, the last commit is shown, and devices can be reused across projects.

What did not change for existing work: every new field is optional and is not written to a project file while it has its default value, so a project that does not use a new feature keeps byte-identical files and outputs (the file format stays at schema version 1). All 567 output files of the four example projects have the same SHA-256 as with release 1.2.0 apart from the tool version they record (`tools/output_hashes.py`, `tests/test_output_hashes.py`). There are 35 design rules (one new, *Several wires on one pin*). A project that uses a new field cannot be read completely by 1.3.0 (an older tool says which fields it does not know). Not done, as in earlier releases: signing the packages, a screen-reader pass and the clean-machine installation test (waived by the owner, `compliance/DEVIATIONS.md`). What soldering needs beyond the termination column in the lists is for the AIT engineers to say.

### Added

- **Edit a wire (1.4.0, M1).** The *Edit wire* tab beside *Why* in the Drawings area sets the gauge, type, colour and length of one or several selected wires, a label (printed on the wire labels instead of the signal name) and a note. Gauge, type, colour and length lock the wire, so Generate keeps them; label and note are always kept; *Reset to generated* gives the wires back to generation. One undo step; a released harness is refused with the reason. The wire list gets the columns *Label* and *Note* only when a wire has one, so every existing project keeps byte-identical files and outputs. See `docs/user-manual/edit-a-wire.md` and D-161.
- **Wires that you draw, several wires on one pin, twisting (1.4.0, M2).** In the same tab, *New wire* adds a wire between a connector and pin of the harness at each end; Generate keeps it, you can move its ends or delete it. Two wires may share a pin: the new design rule *Several wires on one pin* (`pin-shared`, 35 rules now) names each such pin, and the independent check says *pin_shared* (a warning) instead of *pin_reused* when a wire you drew is one of them. *Group* twists or shields selected wires together as a twisted pair, shielded pair, quad, coax or overall shield; Generate keeps the group; *Ungroup* takes a group you made apart. See D-162.
- **A function on every pin, labels on connectors and the harness, crimp or solder in the lists (1.4.0, M3).** A pin has a free-text *function* (Devices tab column *Function*, pasted blocks and the pinout import: a *Function* column beside a *Signal* column; the cable end copies it). A box connector has a *Label* on its card, a harness and each of its cable connectors have one in the *Labels* section of the *Edit wire* tab. `labels.csv` prints them (a cable end without a label prints the one of the box connector it plugs into; a harness with a label gets a row). The pinout tables get the columns *Function* and *Termination* only when a pin has one, so existing projects keep byte-identical lists. See D-163.
- **Who made the last change (1.4.0, M4).** The Revisions area shows a line with the last commit of the Git repository around the project folder: author, date, first line of the message, short id and branch. It is read from the repository files without starting `git` and without a network (nothing new for `docs/SECURITY.md`); only the author's name is kept. It is the last commit of the whole repository, and changes since then are not shown. No project file and no output changes. See D-164.
- **Devices for reuse, and parts lists from InvenTree (1.4.0, M5).** The Devices tab has *Save to library…* and *Add from library…*: a device with its connectors, pinouts (signal, function, contact size, termination), parts and interface types goes into a device library file (JSON), and devices from a file are added to another project as one undo step. Nothing in the project is replaced: a taken ID gets the next free one (`GYRO1-2`), parts and interface types you already have are kept. The same on the command line: `export-devices` and `import-devices` (`--list`, `--dry-run`). The parts import now also reads `.tsv` and `.json` files and the column names of an InvenTree export (the IPN is the part ID; a category path such as `Connectors/D-Sub` is read as `connector`); no connection to a server is made. Approval status is still never guessed. See D-165.

## 1.3.0 (2026-10-20; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.3.0 is 1.2.0 with a **new user interface** (the UI overhaul, decisions D-150 to D-159, brief and plan in `docs/ui/`): a navigation rail, one top bar with the three steps Generate, Check and Release, light and dark themes that switch at once with the bundled Inter and JetBrains Mono fonts, tables that work like a spreadsheet, a window that does not freeze, one import wizard, a list of the exported files with their checksums, and a Settings area. Before and after pictures are in `docs/ui/BEFORE_AFTER.md` and `docs/ui/screenshots/`.

What did not change: the design model, the file format (a project saved by 1.2.0 or earlier opens as it is), the 34 design rules and their results, the command line, and the generated files. Every file the four example projects write was compared with the files of release 1.2.0 (`tools/output_hashes.py`, `docs/ui/OUTPUT_HASHES_1.2.0.json`, `tests/test_output_hashes.py`): 567 files, identical apart from the tool version that they record (the model hash of a generated project includes the version of the generation record). The packages for Ubuntu, Windows and macOS are made as before. Not done, as in earlier releases: signing the packages, a screen-reader pass and the clean-machine installation test (waived by the owner, `compliance/DEVIATIONS.md`).

### Changed

- **UI overhaul, M9 (Settings).** Settings is now a list of sections: Appearance (theme, interface size and a new *Reduce motion*), Editing (mode, snap to grid, all link labels), Project, Keyboard shortcuts (every key of the window, with a filter), About (version, Python and Qt versions, where your settings are kept) and Licences (the fonts, icons and Qt, with their texts). Every setting is applied at once and kept for the next start; the page follows the menus and Ctrl+K. Help > About opens the About section instead of a message box. Changing the interface size still takes about a second.
- **UI overhaul, M8 (Outputs and Revisions).** The Lists tab shows every exported file with its state (matches the export, changed since, missing, not in the export), SHA-256, document, harness, format, size and time written, and offers Open, Reveal in folder and Copy to folder; the checksums are worked out in the background. In Revisions, the rows of the Differences table are tinted by the kind of change and a line says what the released harness locks. The files an export writes are byte-identical to 1.2.0.
- **UI overhaul, M7 (Import).** File > Import > Interfaces (Ctrl+Shift+I) opens the same wizard as the other five kinds, with *Paste table text instead…* for the older dialog. A file of more than 1,500 rows is read in the background with a progress line and Cancel, so a 20,000-row table no longer freezes the window, and long tables size their columns much faster. See D-156.
- **UI overhaul, M6 (Drawings).** The drawing now has the width and most of the height of the Drawings area, with the pin table, the wire table and the reasons under it, so the sheet is large and its buttons are no longer cut off; picking a wire on the sheet selects its link for the inspector. The files the tool writes are unchanged: all 567 output files of the four packaged examples have the same SHA-256 as with release 1.2.0 (`tools/output_hashes.py`, checked by a test). See D-155.
- **UI overhaul, M5 (Checks and Problems, and a long freeze fixed).** In a project with a very large rule check result (tens of thousands of findings) every edit froze the window for about 11 seconds; two lookups that searched every wire for every finding are now instant, and the same edit takes under 0.1 s. The Checks tab says *Out of date* the moment the design changes, a row in the Problems panel goes to parts and value files as well as to units, links and harnesses, and *Details and fixes* (right-click or Shift+Enter) opens it as a card. See D-154.
- **UI overhaul, M4 (model tables as spreadsheets).** The Interfaces table and a new Units table (Ctrl+Shift+J) work like a spreadsheet: Enter edits, Esc cancels, Ctrl+V pastes a block from Excel as one undo step, one edit applies to every selected row, right-click the header to choose columns; numbers are right-aligned with their unit in the header. See D-153.
- **UI overhaul, M3 (Home and the workflow).** The top bar shows three steps, Generate, Check and Release, each with its state and what is next (a Release button is new); a banner says when what you are looking at is part of a released harness and offers a new revision; Home lists recent projects with their state and the examples (the flatsat example opens as a copy of its own); empty tables say what to do. See D-152.
- **UI overhaul, M2 (the shell).** A navigation rail (Home, Model, Checks, Drawings, Outputs, Revisions, Settings), one full-width top bar (Undo, Redo, mode and search moved up from the second tool bar, with the revision and where you are), a job indicator with Cancel and an Offline marker in the status bar, a Problems panel at the bottom (Ctrl+Shift+M), Home and Settings pages, and a layout that is remembered per project. See D-151.

- **UI overhaul, M1 (foundation; the screens themselves follow in M2 to M9).** A theme package (`gui/theme/`) replaces IBM Carbon: one token source, Inter and JetBrains Mono (bundled, SIL OFL) instead of IBM Plex, rounded controls, a calmer light and dark palette, tuned for WCAG 2.2 AA. Switching light and dark is now instant (about 0.1 s instead of 0.5 to 1 s). No change to the design model, the file formats, the command line or any generated file. See D-150 and `docs/ui/UI_OVERHAUL_PLAN.md`.

- **UI overhaul, final milestone (checks).** One test file (`tests/test_ui_final.py`) walks every area, the tabs, the sections of Settings and the Problems panel in both themes: no Qt warning, every control has an accessible name, every icon-only button a tooltip, the Tab key visits every control and returns to where it started, and keyboard focus can be seen on every kind of control. The performance numbers are in `docs/ui/PERF_BASELINE.md` ("After the final milestone"); changing the interface size still takes about a second (D-158, D-159).

### Fixed

- **A keyboard trap in every area.** The tab widget that holds the inspector (and the one of an area with a single tab) has no visible tab bar, but stayed a Tab stop that hands the focus to its hidden bar, so Tab from the project panel's tab bar did nothing. Such a tab widget is no longer a Tab stop.
- **The left panel did not return to its width.** Going from the Interfaces tab to the Units tab took the widened panel for the normal one, so the Project tab never got its width back; it does now.
- `python -m tools.ui_screenshots` makes each theme and part in a process of its own (making two themes in one process crashed Qt's offscreen platform).

## 1.2.0 (2026-10-19; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.2.0 is 1.1.0 plus **packages for Windows and macOS** and the changes that make the tool and its whole test suite run there. The tool is supported on Ubuntu 24.04 and newer, Windows 10 and 11 (64-bit) and macOS 13 and newer (Apple Silicon and Intel), decision D-149. The suite of about 2,800 tests passes on all three systems in CI.

What did not change: the design model, the file format (a project saved by 1.1.0 or 1.0.0 opens as it is), the 34 design rules and their results, the command line, and the generated outputs (every generated file is the same as in 1.1.0 except for the tool version and the model hash it records; the model hash includes the tool version). The tool still ships no derating or EMC values and no approved parts list (D-11, D-12): each project supplies them. The packages are not signed. This release has not been signed off by the owner (`docs/RELEASE.md` step 14, `compliance/SIGNOFF.md`).

### Added

- **Windows and macOS packages (D-149).** The release workflow now builds, for Windows 10 and 11 (64-bit), a per-user setup program made with Inno Setup (no administrator rights, a Start menu entry, `harness-design-studio` and `harness-design-studio-gui` on the user's `PATH`, an uninstaller) and a portable `.zip`, and, for macOS 13 and newer, `Harness Design Studio.app` in a `.dmg` for Apple Silicon (`arm64`) and one for Intel (`x86_64`), next to the Ubuntu `.deb` and `.tar.gz`; one `SHA256SUMS` covers all of them. Built by `tools/build_windows.py` and `tools/build_macos.py`; tested on the finished files (install, run, uninstall; mount, copy, run; the first-steps commands give byte-identical files to a source install) by `tools/smoke_package.py` in `.github/workflows/harness-packages.yml` (branches `pkg/**` and manual runs; publishes nothing) and in `release.yml`. The packages are not signed; the workflows have signing and notarization steps that run only when the repository secrets exist (`docs/RELEASE.md`, `docs/SECURITY.md`). Nothing is downloaded when a package is installed or run. Install steps: `docs/INSTALL.md`, ways D and E.
- **The test suite runs on Windows and macOS.** `.github/workflows/harness-platforms.yml` runs the whole suite on `windows-latest` and `macos-latest` for every change, with a time limit for each test so that a hang names the test.
- The packaged editor accepts `--selftest --selftest-out FILE` and writes `selftest ok` (or what failed) to the file, because the Windows editor has no console; the exit code is the result as before.

### Fixed

- **Windows: the "project is already open" protection.** The lock file that stops two copies of the tool from changing one project now works on Windows, where the earlier code (which relied on `fcntl` and on asking whether a process number is alive) did not run at all. The holder keeps the lock file open with a byte-range lock that the system drops when the process ends; a second copy is refused, and the lock of a program that was killed is taken over by exactly one new program. Linux and macOS are unchanged.
- The rule check helper process no longer stops at `os.nice` on Windows, which has no such function.
- The bundled IBM Plex fonts are TrueType files on every system (unpacked unchanged from the WOFF2 files; same licence), because Qt on Windows cannot read WOFF2 and the editor showed a fallback font there.
- Keys work the same on every system: Return or Enter on a row of a table does what a double-click does, F2 edits the cell of the pin table, and Tab reaches every button (on macOS the system setting for full keyboard access decided that before). The editor uses the Fusion widget style on every system; its look comes from the tokens anyway.
- The output verifier reported a crash instead of a finding for a workbook whose zip header names a newer zip version than Python reads (`NotImplementedError`); it is now the finding `out_unreadable`, with a regression test (found by the randomised damaged-outputs test).

### Changed

- The platform decisions D-110 and D-127 (Ubuntu only) are superseded by D-149 (Ubuntu 24.04 and newer, Windows 10 and 11, macOS 13 and newer).
- Text files are checked out with LF on every system (`.gitattributes`), so the byte-for-byte golden files match on Windows.
- `tools/build_installer.py` is split into pieces that the three platform builds share (the program builds and the allow-list check); the Linux packages are unchanged. The licence texts of the GPL-3 and LGPL-3 are kept in `packaging/licences/` for builds on systems without Debian's `common-licenses` folder; `tools/gen_scf.py` lists `.exe`, `.zip` and `.dmg` files too; `tools.release_notes complete` fails a release that lacks a package of any kind.
- The text of a GitHub release page is now a short page: what the tool is, a table with one row for each package that is attached (what it is for and how to install it), the highlights of the version, the full changelog section folded away, install steps and checksum commands for the systems whose packages are there, and the SHA-256 table. The highlights are written by hand in `docs/releases/<version>.md`; `python -m tools.release_notes check` fails without that file. The release workflow has a "notes only" run (`workflow_dispatch` with `notes_only`) that writes the text of an existing page again from the tag's changelog and the published checksums, without building or uploading anything.

## 1.1.0 (2026-10-18; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.1.0 is 1.0.0 plus the changes listed below: the editor rebuilt against the new UX standard (a token-driven theme, the Harness, Lists, Checks, Releases and Devices tabs, the project tree and the inspector, state chips that never rely on colour alone, "Fix all", the Ctrl+K overlay, clearer link labels and pictograms); **the Devices tab, with which a device is defined down to each pin** (connectors, pins, the interface each pin carries, import of a pinout from CSV or XLSX, `import-pinout` and `export-pinout`); the rename of the folder and the commands (`harness-design-studio`, `harness-design-studio-gui`; `harness` and `harness-gui` stay as aliases); and a test run that takes about 30 minutes in CI instead of up to three hours.

What did not change: the design model, the file format (a project saved by 1.0.0 opens as it is), the 34 design rules and their results, the generated outputs (every generated file is the same as in 1.0.0 except for the tool version and the model hash it records; the model hash includes the tool version), and the command line apart from the two new commands. The tool still ships no derating or EMC values and no approved parts list (D-11, D-12): each project supplies them. This release has not been signed off by the owner (`docs/RELEASE.md` step 14, `compliance/SIGNOFF.md`).

### Added

Look and feel
- Theme: View > Theme offers System (the default, follows the operating system's light or dark scheme), Light and Dark; the choice is stored in the settings.
- Vector icons (from Lucide, ISC licence, drawn in code) for the toolbar and panels: zoom in, zoom out, fit, search, import, undo, redo and the panel close buttons have an icon and a tooltip that names the shortcut.
- State chips for pending, not checked, locked, released, out of date, pass and fail: icon, word and, for pending (dotted) and not checked (hatched), a pattern, so colour is never the only sign.
- Shared table code (`gui/tables.py`): right-aligned numbers, units in the header, sorting, filtering, Ctrl+C copies the selected cells as tab-separated text, column widths are remembered.

Diagram
- Several items can be selected (Shift-click, Ctrl-click, a rubber band, Ctrl+A); Properties shows what they have in common, and they are moved, deleted, aligned, spaced out and duplicated as one undo step (Ctrl+D, Edit menu, right-click menu).
- Find with Ctrl+F marks units and links; a right-click menu offers the same actions as the menus, with the reason for each greyed entry, and "Add unit here"; a grid and View > Snap to grid (Alt places freely).
- Marks on every unit card and link label: problems, warnings, values still to enter and "not checked yet" as a badge (icon and count); units, links and connectors that a release locks show a lock and "W001 A" and a faint released tint; the legend lists only the marks that are on the diagram. A click on a badge selects the object and asks the window to show its problems (the window does not act on that yet).
- A corner chip says whether the harnesses and the outputs are out of date, not generated, not exported or up to date.
- The "n auto" chip and the tooltip of the Auto badge say why the tool chose a connector and that it is not confirmed yet. "Why?" in the right-click menu asks the window for the same (not connected to a panel yet).
- Cards show a picture of their kind of unit, the number of connectors ("12 conn"), "+3" where connector pictures do not fit, and the "n auto" count in Guided mode too; FLIGHT or GROUND can be set per zone from the unit's menu (a viewer setting, not in the project file).

New views
- Harness tab (`gui/harness_view.py`): a header with the harness, its release state, its interfaces, the hash Generate last read and chips for design, outputs and independent check; a connector and pin table (locked pins carry a lock and name the release that locks them; pending values and auto-filled rows have their own chips), a wire table, a zoomable drawing and a Why pane for the selected wire, connector or pin, all linked by hover and selection.
- Lists tab (`gui/lists_panel.py`): the wire list, pinouts, BOM, mass and length, test tables, labels and the system BOM as sortable, filterable tables with units in the headers, Ctrl+C as tab-separated text, a banner when the design changed after Generate, and export of the visible rows as CSV or Excel ("Export table").
- Checks tab (`gui/checks_panel.py`): one row for each of the design rules with its result (pass, fail, not checked, pending), a summary strip ("34 rules: 20 passed · 2 failed · 12 not checked · 4 pending values"), the requirement IDs of each rule, the offending objects (Enter or double-click shows one), and the exact configuration value a pending rule waits for. A check that is running can be cancelled, and a failed check leaves a banner until the next one succeeds.
- Releases tab (`gui/releases_panel.py`): the timeline of reviews, releases and new revisions of every harness with state chips, the differences between two revisions or a revision and the working design grouped by kind with counts (values read "(none)", never `None`), and Submit for review, Release and New revision per harness.
- Project tree (`gui/project_tree.py`, left panel): Units, Interfaces, Harnesses with their release state, Parts, Interface types, Configuration with its placeholder and pending values, and the import entry points; problem counts and a lock on every object, a filter, keyboard navigation and an "Add" menu. It updates only the rows a change touches.
- Inspector (`gui/inspector.py`, right panel): tabs Inspector, Why and Problems; a banner that says why an object is locked and offers "Start new revision"; fields a release forbids changed are disabled with the reason; "Used in" lists the links, harnesses and wires that use a unit, link, part or interface type; a configuration file lists its engineering values with a status chip each.
- Why panel (`gui/why_panel.py`): the recorded reasons for a unit, link, harness, wire, connector or pin as "rule: sentence" lines, what is still pending and the placeholder files generation used.
- Devices tab (`gui/devices_view.py`, Ctrl+6): one unit down to each pin: its state (draft, in use, locked, read-only), its connectors and their cards, a pin table with signal, mode, interface, direction and class taken from the interface type (never guessed), wire and state, notes about the pins, and the pinout as exported with Export pinout (CSV or Excel). The unit selected on the diagram or in the tree is the device shown.
- Devices tab, editing: cells of the pin table are edited in place (pin name, signal from the project's interface types or free text, mode, contact size, termination) with F2 or Enter, Tab, Insert, Delete, Shift+Delete, Space and paste from a spreadsheet; each action is one named undo step ("Edit pin J01.A2", "Paste 9 pins into J01"). Add > Device… (Ctrl+Shift+N) makes an empty device; connectors are added from a part picker with New part…; "Fill from interface type…" (Ctrl+L) and "Assign to interface…" (Ctrl+Enter) put signals and interfaces on selected pins. A part or interface type made here is marked pending or unverified; the tool never approves it. A released device shows the lock banner with Start new revision; the pin set of a connector that a released cable mates with cannot be changed.
- Changing the part of a connector now also gives it the part's pin count and gender (the Inspector does it at once, the Devices tab asks first); before, the pins and the gender stayed as they were.
- Device pinout import: **File > Import > Import device pinout…** and `harness-design-studio import-pinout` define the connectors and pins of a device from a CSV or XLSX table (one pin per row; headings matched by name; a bad row stops the whole import; one undo step "Import pinout (2 connectors, 36 pins)"). `harness-design-studio export-pinout` writes one device's pinout as CSV or XLSX, and what it writes can be imported again. Columns the project cannot store (a description, remarks, a voltage, a direction) are named in a warning and left out.
- User manual page "Define a device" (`docs/user-manual/define-a-device.md`), the guide and the cheatsheet name the Devices tab, its keys and the two new commands.
- A table for the generate preview (`gui/plan_table.py`): each harness with whether Generate creates, changes, removes or leaves it, and the locked pins that stay. It is not in the Generate preview window yet.

Dialogs
- Export window (`gui/export_dialog.py`): output folder, a harness by document tree, progress with Cancel, and the list of files written with Reveal in folder, Open, a picture of the selected drawing and Copy selection to folder. A failed independent check or write error is a notice in the window that stays. The export still writes the full set.
- Import wizard (`gui/import_wizard.py`): approved parts, segment lengths, the ampacity table and a KiCad netlist (interfaces too, though Ctrl+Shift+I still opens the older window) in four steps (file, columns with a live preview, conflicts and placeholders with the exact reason for each row, apply); every import is one undo step called "Import n rows". File > Import lists every kind.
- New project window (`gui/new_project_dialog.py`): folder, name and example in one dialog with the problems said next to the field.
- Engineering values form (`gui/config_dialog.py`): **Supply value…** on a pending rule (or Design > Engineering values…) opens every engineering value grouped by config file, with units, the placeholder flag, inline reasons for a refused value and locked values disabled with the reason; Apply sets all changes as one undo step and the checks run again.

Window
- Top bar (`gui/topbar.py`): project name, short model hash in mono, a dirty marker, **Generate** (the one primary button), Check, Export… and a status chip (up to date, plans out of date, outputs out of date, generating, exporting, checking). Ctrl+G generates, Ctrl+E exports, F5 runs the checks.
- Persistent banners: when the plans or the exported files no longer match the design a notice stays under the top bar with Generate and Export buttons; a failed generate, export or check stays as a notice until the next success.
- Main tabs Diagram, Harness, Lists, Checks and Releases (Ctrl+1 to Ctrl+5), the project tree on the left, the inspector with its Why and Problems tabs on the right; a link double-click opens the Harness tab.
- A status-bar link "n values pending · n rules not checked" that opens the Checks tab filtered; the status bar also shows the project folder (monospace, cut in the middle when narrow), the number of rules and the tool version, "Generated at model xxxxxxxx" and the zoom level.
- F6 and Shift+F6 move the keyboard focus between the diagram, the project panel and the inspector; Ctrl+I adds an interface; the Connect tool is in the Edit menu; every shortcut is in a menu and in the tooltip ("text (shortcut)").
- View > Reset layout; the dock layout is saved with the window.
- Fix all (n) in the Checks tab applies the one-click fix of every open problem that has one as one undo step; it is greyed out with the reason when there is nothing to fix or the project is read-only, and a fix that a released harness locks is left out and named. It never confirms a connector the tool chose (a person does that, one problem at a time).

### Changed

- **Names** (D-147): the folder `harness/` is now `HarnessDesignStudio/`; the command line tool is `harness-design-studio` and the editor `harness-design-studio-gui` (pip and packages alike; in the packages the editor was called `harness-design-studio`). `harness` and `harness-gui` still work as aliases. Project files, settings and the output of `--version` are unchanged.

Look and feel
- Text is 13 px at 100 % (it was 14) and follows the 12 / 13 / 16 / 20 px type scale; spacing follows 4 / 8 / 12 / 16 / 24 / 32 px; all buttons, combo boxes and fields are 32 px high.
- Hashes, IDs, requirement IDs, pin and wire numbers and the drawing preview use IBM Plex Mono; the drawing preview takes its page and background colours from the theme.
- The Guided, Expert, Select and Connect toggles use a neutral selected style (bold with a bar under), so Generate is the only accent-coloured button; the Fix buttons on problem cards are no longer primary.
- The editor window and the taskbar entry show the Harness Design Studio logo (drawn in code, so the packaged program needs no SVG support).
- The first interface size follows the screen (200 % on a 4K screen at ratio 1); the View menu item "UI scale" is now "Interface size".

Diagram
- Navigation: Ctrl+wheel zooms, the wheel scrolls, the middle button or Space with a drag pans; F fits, Shift+F zooms to the selection, Ctrl+0 shows 100 % (it used to fit); the zoom level is shown. Unit names stay readable when zoomed out, and zoomed out links of different kinds differ by dash pattern and a small picture, with a readable label on the selected or hovered link.
- Double-click or Enter on a unit or link opens it in the Harness tab (or says it is not generated yet).
- The legend is an overlay you can fold or hide (View > Show legend) and lists only what the diagram uses; the overview map can be dragged and is smaller or hidden in a small window.
- A missing voltage or current on a power link reads "V ?" or "A ?" in the pending style instead of staying blank; the link tooltip lists harness, wire count, state and lock; NOMINAL is shown only where the project has a redundant chain.
- Deleting an interface asks first and names both ends and the harness wires it affects; the Delete key works only from the diagram and the tables; a read-only project says why a drag, Delete or arrange did nothing.
- Arrange diagram has a shortcut (Ctrl+Shift+L) and a toolbar button, and big diagrams are arranged in the background; dragging a unit among many is smooth (straight lines while dragging, routes on release); a click on a link label selects its own link.

Views and dialogs
- The Problems header no longer says "all 34 rules checked" while rules were not checked: it reads "34 rules: n passed · n failed · n not checked", and the notes about rules that could not run are never cut off by the 25 card limit.
- The drawing preview is a zoomable view: the wheel zooms to the cursor, drag pans, `+` `-` `0` zoom and fit, Page Up and Page Down change sheet; hovering a wire or pin marks it. The written SVG and PDF files are unchanged.
- The independent check and the state of the outputs are worked out in the background and cached, so the Harness tab no longer waits for them on every change.
- A refused edit (an object held by a released harness) puts the stored value back into the form instead of leaving the rejected one showing; a refusal stays on screen with a "Start new revision…" button until the next change goes through.
- The release dialog lists what the release will lock (wires, pins, interfaces, parts, interface types, config files, with placeholder values shown as pending); the new-revision dialog says what happens and what becomes editable instead of "Nothing blocks this release"; both say in words what is still missing for the button to work (the 10-character comment, a name, a reason).
- New project and New project from an example are one dialog (folder, name, example).
- Export opens the export window.

Window
- Ctrl+K lists every command (Generate, Check, Export, Release, Revise, Reset layout, the tabs, the import kinds, engineering values, go to harness), ranks a name that starts with what you typed first and no longer stops at 14 results; it floats over the window instead of blocking it (Up, Down and Enter, Esc or a click elsewhere closes it).
- Undo and Redo name the step ("Undo Move PDU2").
- A panel can no longer make the window wider than the screen.
- The bottom panel (Problems, To-do, Interface table, Harness plans, Outline) and the Palette tab are gone, and nothing they did is lost (the table is in `docs/ux-audit.md`, "Build status"): Problems and a To do list are in the Problems tab on the right; the interface table is the Interfaces tab on the left (Ctrl+Shift+T, Go menu); the harness list, check, outputs and wires are in the Harness tab, whose More menu has Changes…, Change log… and Delete harness; the project tree replaces the Outline and takes Shift and Ctrl for a multiple selection and Delete; units are added with Add in the project tree or Edit > Add unit; the arrow beside Connect lists the interface types. A short window no longer hides a panel, and the tour has the steps Add, Connect and Problems at their new places. Per-harness Generate is not built (see `docs/ux-audit.md`, "Build status").
- In a window shorter than 800 px the bottom panel is hidden so the diagram keeps at least 55 % of the height at 1280 x 720; a panel can no longer make the window wider than the screen.
- Developers: the test suite is several times faster. GUI tests no longer leave their windows alive for the rest of the run (`tests/conftest.py` deletes them), and the suite runs in parallel with `pytest -n auto` (pytest-xdist 3.8.0, dev only; D-148). Tests that assert a wall-clock limit carry the marker `perf` and run alone afterwards (`pytest -m perf`); CI does both and applies the same 90 % core coverage gate.

### Fixed

- LVDS and RS-422 no longer share one picture: LVDS shows a differential pair closed by a termination resistor (a test fails if two kinds of link share a picture, apart from primary and secondary power, which differ by colour and name).
- A link's label in the diagram keeps clear of the other links: it is placed where no other link runs through it, and where there is no such place a smaller label (the picture alone) is used. On the flatsat example 10 of 44 labels still touch another cable (before: 28).
- A review, release or new revision cannot be dated before the last change-log entry of the same harness (`date_before_last`).
- `harness new --name` with a blank name is refused, and a name is stored without surrounding spaces. A link (also a dangling one) is not accepted as the folder of a new project, with the usual "not an empty folder" message.
- `import-lengths` help, `IMPORTS.md` and the templates README say that its three columns are read by position. The exit code table in `CLI.md` names the imports and `config` under exit code 1.
- `FILE_FORMAT.md` and `TROUBLESHOOTING.md` describe the empty `.harness.lock.takeover` file.
- **Fixed: outputs, low findings** (audit D-12, D-14, D-17). Wires with both ends on one side of a cable block (loop-back plugs) are counted when the drawing is split into sheets, so their loops no longer run into the title block. A damaged output manifest that lists a folder no longer makes every export fail; the empty folder of a removed harness is removed; the deep output check treats a linked output file as changed and the output reader skips links. The routing function names a missing gap instead of raising a key error.
- The package self-test (`--selftest`) no longer leaves a temporary folder behind, names the step that failed, works when `QT_QPA_PLATFORM` is set but empty, and only reacts to its flag as the first argument.
- The `.deb` no longer carries the tarball-only `README.txt`, icon and desktop template under `/opt`.
- The CI run on a merge to `master` is no longer cancelled by the next merge; the documents-only job also runs the flatsat walkthrough test; a missing `.deb` or `.tar.gz` fails the build job.
- The wheel carries the `.gitignore` files of the example projects; `tools.vendor` no longer downloads an unpinned `wheel`; a licence such as "GPL-3.0 with LGPL exception" is rejected by the licence report.
- `harness verify` on a project without wires says to run `harness generate` first.
- `harness config` no longer names decision-log numbers (D-10, D-15).
- Documents: locking a wire or pin is done by editing the harness file (the app has no button); `emc.classes` is documented as read by no rule.
- Build tools: a failing example builder keeps the old example folder; `tools.metrics --coverage` and `tools.usability_summary` give a message instead of a traceback; `tools.extract_requirements` checks that all PDFs exist before it rewrites any list; the user-guide converter keeps code spans and link targets intact.
- A two-colour wire written `yellow/green` or `green-yellow` shows both codes (`YE/GN`) in the drawing instead of a cut-off word; it is still drawn grey (audit B-19).
- `forbid_nominal_with_redundant: false` now lets nominal and redundant interfaces share a harness, and `category_pairs_to_separate` separates only the pairs it lists (audit B-17).
- The mass table header says it covers wires and cable connectors; backshells, shielding, sleeving and tie-downs are not in the mass (audit B-21).
- The Explain tab no longer says "(X drives)" for power and other single-path signals, names the connector on each pin line, and shows the "stays on pin ... because the pin is locked" line; a missing mating part is no longer written as "mates with itself" (audit B-16).
- Arranging the diagram twice changes nothing more, also at stress size; a fixed pin with no signal is never given to an interface; a branch name cannot equal a cable connector ID (audit B-22).
- **Fixed: editor, small items** (audit F1-26, F1-27, F2-10). A number typed with other scripts' digits (Arabic-Indic, full-width) is refused in the number fields and the table. An unknown chain typed into the interface table is refused with a plain message. The names of the steps in the Undo menu, the table headings and a few words on the diagram moved into `strings.py`. The accessible-name test no longer accepts a tooltip as a name.
- Loading a project with many set-aside objects reads and scans each file once (it was quadratic: 32000 bad units took 30 s). Audit A-13.
- The undo history keeps the last 200 steps, and an edit with no operations is no longer an undo step (it also cleared redo). Audit A-14.
- Names refuse line and paragraph separators, the byte-order mark and private-use characters; dates (library, release, change log) must be exactly `YYYY-MM-DD`; zone names in `layout.json` are checked like other names. Audit A-16.
- The integrity check reports a part that mates with itself or a missing part, a baseline of a missing harness or with an ID that is not `<harness>.<revision>`, baselines whose IDs differ only by case, and (as warnings) a connector for a missing interface type and a change log entry of a deleted harness. The integrity cache follows the generated flag of the owning harness. Audit A-15, A-17.
- The connector-manufacturer rule ignores case and spaces; emc-class-split also sees interfaces named only by wires; wire-spec and mating-cycles findings can be shown; `config --apply-profile` says when the plans became out of date. The verifier no longer imports the generator. Audit C-22, C-23.

## 1.0.0 (2026-10-12; released on the owner's instruction, see compliance/SIGNOFF.md; not a statement of compliance)

Version 1.0.0 is 0.1.0 plus the changes listed below: the fixes of the October 2026 pre-release audit (the findings and what was done about each are in `AUDIT.md` at the root of the repository), the release freeze of parts, interface types and configuration, two more examples (`minimal-satellite`, `flatsat`), a design worksheet template and the wiring-diagram drawing. The open engineering decisions were recorded as defaults on 2026-10-12 (`docs/DECISIONS.md` D-142 to D-145); the tool still ships no standard or part data. **What is still true**: the configuration files are placeholders and the parts are example parts, so results rest on placeholders; no compliance with any standard is claimed; the packages are not signed; the upstream WireViz code under `src/wireviz/` is unchanged.

- **Fixed: a release freezes the parts, interface types and configuration it uses** (audit E-5, I-2). The baseline now also records the parts, interface types, configuration values and waivers of the released harness (old baselines without them still load and are written back unchanged). While a harness is released, the editor and the data commands refuse to change or delete a part, interface type or configuration value it uses, and `harness check` reports a hand edit as *released-modified*. Adding parts, editing parts no released harness uses, notes and names stay allowed. The exact list is in `docs/FILE_FORMAT.md` ("What a release freezes").
- **Fixed: impossible numbers** (audit A-6, D-9, B-18, K-19). A wire gauge outside AWG 0 to 40, a length below 0 or above 10000 m, a current or voltage of 0 or less, a pin count outside 1 to 1000, a negative mass or rating, a harness number below 1 and a schema version below 1 are refused by the editor and set aside on load (reported, kept), instead of reaching the outputs.
- **Fixed: locks and saving** (audit A-7 to A-12). A project lock from before a restart, or whose process number was reused, is taken over; the lock error names the lock file and, for another computer, how to remove it. Saving no longer copies the content behind a link into a `.bak` or the migration backup; a migration backup that is a link is refused and the backup is built under a temporary name; new files follow the umask; a folder that is not a project gets no lock helper file; temp files left by a killed save are removed by the next save. The editor schemas: `library/manifest.json` has its own schema only, and IDs carry their pattern.

- **Fixed: outputs, medium findings** (audit D-3 to D-18, G-7, I-4). A pin ID is drawn whole (smaller, or in a wider pin column) instead of cut to `SHIELD~`. The CSV guard against formulas can be undone exactly, so a signal that starts with `'` plus `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` exports and verifies. `titleblock.fields` is checked (unknown or repeated names and non-text entries are a configuration error) and `sheet` may be left out. Characters a PDF cannot show are named in a warning. A shield keeps its end A on every sheet. The box pinouts, the mating matrix and the JSON export no longer depend on the order the harnesses were created in. The output check now compares every cell of the pinouts, BOM, tests, labels, mass table, mating matrix, traceability, box pinouts and DRC findings, the YAML values, the pins and signals in the drawing, the PDF texts, the XLSX sheets and the provenance values. A workbook that cannot be built (a full temporary folder) gives a message that says why. The harness overview draws every pair of units of a harness, a routing sketch no longer cuts a segment length, and a signal name keeps its double spaces in the labels. XLSX files are written without lxml, so they are identical on every machine. The SVG drawings changed (pin texts carry a label), so the output golden digests were regenerated.
- **Fixed: diagram editor** (audit F2-4 to F2-14). The key under the diagram follows the kinds of link in use. A unit cannot be dragged past the top or left edge, and the scene grows to hold units placed far out. The Connect tool no longer drags a unit on screen without moving it in the project. Switching to Expert grows the scene. Link labels are placed again when their numbers change. The outline clears its highlighted row when nothing is selected. Long unit IDs and connector names end in an ellipsis. A line break in an interface name typed into the table is refused. The tour skips steps whose panel is hidden and does nothing on a closed window.
- **Fixed: installer scripts** (audit G-2, G-3, G-4, G-13). `install.sh` writes a list of what it installed (`.harness-design-studio-install`); `uninstall.sh` removes only the folders on that list and refuses to touch a `cli`, `LICENSES` or `_internal` folder that it did not install (before, it deleted them and said "Your projects are not touched"). `install.sh` refuses to install into the folder that holds the unpacked package (it deleted the package), copies to a temporary name before replacing the old install, and makes the files belong to root in a root install. The tarball is built with owner root, sorted entries and fixed times. A link in the bin folder that points to another program is not replaced; `--prefix ""` is refused; `uninstall.sh --help` works; one install's uninstall keeps the launcher of another.
- **Fixed: release gate and packages** (audit G-5, G-6, G-8, G-9, G-11, G-12). The release check fails when the version is already a git tag or `## Unreleased` still has text, runs the tests with `HARNESS_REQUIRE_BROWSER=1`, prints the number of skipped tests and fails on any skip except the permission tests that need a non-root user (when it runs as root). The release image has poppler and Chromium. The `.deb` is reproducible without `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` and depends on `libc6 (>= 2.39)`. `gen_scf` fails when there is no `.deb` or `.tar.gz` and leaves `*.egg-info` out of the source manifest. The soak test's undo-then-redo check now compares the model.
- **Fixed: CI template and documents** (audit G-10, G-14, G-17, K-6 to K-8, K-13, K-15, K-16). The GitHub Actions template runs `harness drc` like `build.sh`; the tool's own workflow has read-only token rights. The in-app guide no longer breaks a table row with an escaped bar. The tests compare the output quoted in `GETTING_STARTED.md` and `docs/examples/README.md` with what the tool prints, and cover all Markdown under `docs/` for links; the flatsat export count is 112, the library list in the install notes has all 14 packages, and a few wrong sentences in the CLI reference, cheat sheet and guide are corrected.
- **Fixed: release record, damaged files and locks** (audit of 2026-10-10). A release or new revision that was saved can no longer be undone (undo is off for that step; before, Release, Save, Undo, Save removed the baseline and the change-log entry from disk). A project file that is a link, over 64 MB or unreadable now counts as damaged: the project is "recovered" and cannot be saved over its folder (it was moved to `.bak` and regenerated). A lone surrogate escape (`\ud800`) in a project file makes the file invalid, instead of loading clean and crashing every save. New check `wire_to_itself` (a wire that starts and ends on the same pin). `harness check` and the rule *released-modified* also compare the units and box connectors stored in the baseline; the design fields of a unit of a released harness are locked like those of its interfaces.
- **Fixed: generation on bad input** (audit B-1, B-2, B-4, I-1). A generation result that holds only errors is no longer "nothing to change" (`empty` is false and the summary lists the errors). Generation checks the configuration first: invalid values (a derating factor above 1, an ampacity table that falls with wire size or has a gauge outside 0 to 40, a negative or fractional pin gap, a number given as text, a wrong `default_gauge_awg` or `wire_colour_by_signal`) are reported as errors and treated as unset instead of crashing or being used. A naming template that raises, gives a reserved name (`COM1`), or repeats itself no longer crashes or hangs; it falls back to the default with a warning, shown after the names are made. A locked pin that already carries a signal is kept on a connector with a fixed pinout, and a locked fixed pin keeps its interface (it used to be cleared and the harness dropped).
- **Fixed: generation and editing, medium findings** (audit B-5 to B-15, B-20, B-22).
  - A harness naming template whose default fallback names would be too long is refused with a warning (it used to crash).
  - Locked power pins now count for the power-to-signal pin gap. Pins of an interface that could not be placed are given back to the next one.
  - The generation status turns stale when a manual harness takes over an interface. Releasing a harness still does not.
  - A locked wire or an entered segment length that regeneration cannot carry over is named in a warning (`locked_data_lost`). In per_unit_pair mode a renamed unit keeps its harness IDs, locked wires and lengths when the interfaces are the same.
  - A redundant copy of a unit no longer copies the pin assignments of the original (locked and fixed pins keep their signal).
  - Deleting a unit is blocked by manual harnesses that plug into it.
  - Interfaces with both ends on one connector, with fewer than two ends, or of a type with a repeated signal name are skipped with a finding instead of being wired onto one pin.
  - Zone names are checked; a project with no zones can be edited (one implicit lane).
  - Editing operations raise a plain `EditError` for unknown IDs, bad IDs, a repeated interface ID and a wrong end number. The compatibility checks no longer scan all interfaces for every connector.
- **Fixed: verifier and design rules** (audit of 2026-10-10). A wire with the same pin at both ends no longer stops `verify` and `drc` with an internal error: it is a verifier error (`wire_same_pin`; a wire between two pins of the same end is `wire_same_end`).
